# MONTH OF AUGUST

*(Having 31 days)*

*August 1. The Procession of the Precious and Life-Giving Cross of the Lord, and the Holy Seven Maccabee Martyrs, with their Mother Solomonia and their Teacher Eleazar:*

*On the first day: The Procession of the Precious Wood of the Honorable and Life-Creating Cross; and the commemoration of the Holy Seven Maccabee Martyrs, and their mother Solomonia, and their teacher Eleazar.*

*At Vespers, the usual Kathisma.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 6: of the Cross, 3.*

*Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Званный свыше):*

*Today the divine assembly of the faithful rejoices; for the Cross appears now unto the ends of the earth, illuminating the firmament with unapproachable light, enlightening the air, and adorning the face of the earth. The Church of Christ sings with divine songs, and serving, venerates the divine and most marvelous Cross that preserves her from on high. Strengthened by its power, let us draw near to the Master, crying out: Bring peace unto the world, and enlighten our souls.*

*Let creation rejoice and dance; for today the Cross hath shone from heaven unto the ends of the earth, enlightening the terrestrial bounds, and showing forth the scattered ones gathered together. Today men make festival with the angelic choirs; for the dividing wall of enmity hath been broken down by the Cross, and by divine grace He hath manifestly united all into one. Therefore, outshining the sun, it enlightens the whole creation by grace, and makes clear and saves those who faithfully venerate it.*

*Shining brighter than the solar rays, desiring to show unto the world the honorable scepter of Christ the King, and His consummation, the divine Cross: behold, it comes forth clearly unto the ends of the earth. This Cross hath led forth mankind from hades; having utterly despoiled hades, cast down the enemy, and brought the pride of demons to naught, it now manifests the Resurrection of the Savior, and saves those who cry: Bring peace unto the world, and enlighten our souls.*

*And of the Saints, 3 Stichera.*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Всехвальніи мученицы):*

*The summit of the Law, raised upon seven pillars, the torment of tyranny shook not. For the noble youths and brethren, keepers of the martyric tradition, manfully endured the bestial rage of the persecutor, surrendering their bodies unto the executioners.*

*Truly having lifted up their mind above the visible, the pious and valiant youths, together with their divinely wise mother, severed their bodily limbs, sustained by great hopes; which now having obtained, they rest in the bosom of Abraham their forefather.*

*With courageous soul firmly arming themselves and as it were despising fury, the most sacred Eleazar and the all-wise youths with their divinely wise mother manfully contended against the adversary for the sake of piety and the keeping of ancestral law.*

*Glory... Tone 8. By John the Monk:*[^6]

*The holy Maccabees said unto the tyrant: To us, O Antiochus, there is one King, God, from Whom we have our being, and unto Whom we return; another world awaits us, higher and mightier than the visible. Our homeland is Jerusalem, steadfast and imperishable; and our festival is life with the angels. O Lord, by their prayers, have mercy and save us.*

*Both now... Tone 8. By John the Monk:*

*He Whom of old Moses prefigured in himself when he overthrew and conquered Amalek; and David the hymnodist, crying aloud unto Thy footstool, commanded us to bow down: today we sinners bow down with unworthy lips unto Thy precious Cross, O Christ God, and hymning Thee Who wast pleased to be nailed upon it, we pray: O Lord, with the thief make us worthy of Thy Kingdom.*

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos.*

*Glory... Tone 8. By Cosmas the Monk:*[^7]

*The souls of the righteous are in the hand of the Lord, as of Abraham and Isaac and Jacob, the forefathers before the Law and ancestors of the Maccabees, who are now praised by us. For these courageous ones, being Abraham's grandsons, having emulated the faith of their forefather Abraham, strived even unto death for the sake of piety. For being piously reared and having lawfully suffered, they refuted the impiety of the proud Antiochus; and accounting the temporal life as nothing for the sake of the eternal, they offered all unto God: soul, courage, understanding, and tender body tender body, and the rearing that yielded the reward of purity. O pious root, from which ye blossomed, O Maccabees! O holy mother, who didst bear the number equal to the week of seven! But we beseech you, O Maccabees, with your mother Solomonia and the wise priest Eleazar: when ye stand before Christ God, for Whose sake ye labored, to receive from Him the labors of your fruits, make fervent supplication for mankind; for He doth whatsoever He wills, and will fulfill the desires of you that fear Him.*

*Both now... Tone 8:*

*The voice of Thy prophet Moses is fulfilled, O God, saying: Ye shall see your Life hanging before your eyes.*[^8] *Today the Cross is exalted, and the world is freed from delusion. Today the Resurrection of Christ is renewed, and the ends of the earth rejoice, offering a song unto Thee in cymbals after the manner of David, and saying: Thou hast wrought salvation in the midst of the earth, O God, the Cross and the Resurrection; for the sake whereof Thou hast saved us, O Good One and Lover of mankind: O Almighty Lord, glory to Thee.*

*Troparion, Tone 1:*

*By the sufferings which the saints endured for Thee, be Thou entreated, O Lord, and heal all our infirmities, O Lover of mankind, we pray Thee.*

*Glory... Both now... Tone 1:*

*Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance, granting victories unto our orthodox King*[^9] *over adversaries, and preserving Thy habitation by Thy Cross.*

*Be it known that before the sounding of the klepalo, the priest enters the vestry and places the precious Cross upon the Table of Oblation. And after the dismissal of Vespers, the priest and the deacon vest in sacred robes. And the deacon censes the precious Cross, and says:*

> **Deacon:** *Master, bless.*

> **Priest:** *Blessed is our God.*

*Then, the Trisagion. After Our Father:*

*Troparion of the Cross. Glory... Both now... Kontakion.*

*And the priest takes the Cross with the tray upon his head, and carries it into the sanctuary, two lamps preceding him. And he places the Cross upon the Holy Table, in the place of the Gospel, and the Gospel upon the High Place; and they kindle a candle before it for the entire night.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Cross, twice; Glory... of the Martyrs; Both now... of the Cross. And the usual Kathismata, and the Sessional Hymns of the Octoechos, and a reading. One Canon of the Octoechos, on 4; and of the Cross, on 6, Tone 6; and of the Martyrs, on 4, Tone 8.*

*If this feast fall on a Saturday: and if it be a temple of Christ or of the Theotokos, we sing the Canon of the temple with the Heirmos on 4; and of the Cross on 6; and of the Saints on 4. Katavasia: "Moses inscribed the Cross."*

*Canon of the Cross, the acrostic whereof is: "I, the faithful one, worship the Cross unto salvation."*[^10]

*Ode 1, Tone 6. Heirmos:*

*Traversing the deep as on dry land, Israel, seeing Pharaoh the pursuer drowning with his feet, cried aloud: Let us sing unto God a song of victory!*

*Troparia:*

*I worship the Cross, the salvation of the faithful, and fervently embrace it, and clasping it I cry: O all-blessed Tree of Christ, enlighten my soul and mind, I pray.*

*The sign of the Cross hath appeared today victorious over passions and demons. Therefore all we faithful, having brightly enlightened our souls, now embrace it.*

*The precious Cross shines forth radiant beams, and enlightens those who faithfully worship it today, sanctifying our souls and bodies.*

*The life-giving Cross set forth and seen sends forth the light-resembling ray of grace: let us draw near and receive enlightenment, joy, and salvation, and remission, offering praise unto the Lord.*

*Theotokion:*

*O most pure Virgin, who in the flesh didst bear Christ Who suffered voluntarily upon the Cross for our sake: save by thy supplications those who honor thee with reverence.*

*Another Canon of the Martyrs, on 4.*

*Ode 1, Tone 8. Heirmos:*

*Having passed through the water as on dry land, and having escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!*

*Troparia:*

*The choir of the sevenfold sufferers appeared of one mind in afflictions; for the confirmation of ancestral law, they refused not to die.*

*Having despised the glory upon earth, through desire for the divine Kingdom, the divine Maccabees endured every pain; therefore are they worthily honored today.*

*Keepers of the Law of Moses, truly valiant Maccabees, brethren one with another appeared, when they trampled underfoot the impiety of Antiochus.*

*Theotokion:*

*The ranks of angels and men, O unwedded Mother, praise thee without ceasing; for thou didst bear in thine arms as an infant the Creator of all.*

*Katavasia:*

*Moses, having inscribed the Cross straightway with his rod, divided the Red Sea for Israel who went on foot; and striking it across against the chariots of Pharaoh struck and united it, drawing the invincible weapon across. Therefore let us sing unto Christ our God, for He hath been glorified.*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

*There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and hast established us upon the rock of Thy confession.*

*Troparia:*

*The blessed Tree, the weapon of life, was of old given to be guarded because of the transgression of the first-fashioned Adam; but the Cross made a path unto this.*

*With eyes, lips, and souls, faces and hearts, let us all worship the all-holy Cross of Christ the Life-Giver, whereby the whole world is sanctified.*

*Today having prophetically become temples unto God by worshiping the Tree of the all-holy Cross, we, His heirs, all kiss it with fear.*

*We hymn Thee, O Cross, and praying with faith in Thy power: deliver us from the snares of enemies, and direct us unto the harbor of salvation of all who sing unto Thee.*

*Theotokion:*

*As a Virgin thou didst bear the Son, born before thee of the Father before the ages without a mother from God, crucified in the flesh, that He might save those who had sinned aforetime.*

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

*O Lord, Creator of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church, do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.*

*Troparia:*

*Walking in the ancestral lawful ordinances, O blessed sufferers of Christ, by your blood ye acquired unfading life, O all-lauded ones.*

*Neither fire nor sword was ever able to overturn your courageous disposition in the faith, O blessed ones, wherein ye brought to naught the insolence of the tormentors.*

*Having endured tortures for the sake of Christ God, O all-lauded martyrs, ye received from on high the honors of victory, unceasingly praying that He save our souls.*

*Theotokion:*

*As a Virgin thou didst give birth, unwedded one, and a Virgin didst thou remain, O unwedded Mother, Theotokos Mary: entreat Christ our God that we may be saved.*

*Katavasia:*

*The rod is accepted as an image of the mystery, for by its budding it designated the priest;*[^11] *and for the Church that aforetime was barren, now hath blossomed forth the Tree of the Cross, unto power and establishment.*

*Kontakion of the Saints, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Въ вышнихъ ищя):*

*O sevenfold pillars of the Wisdom of God, and luminous beacons of the divine Light, all-wise Maccabees, most great martyrs before the era of martyrs: with them pray unto the God of all pray that they who honor you may be saved.*

*Ikos:*

*Fervently praise thy God, O Zion, for He hath strengthened the bars of thy gates, and hath blessed thy children;*[^12] *for as an invincible army, a host truly valiant and strong in wisdom, they piously stood against the wiles of the wicked, and together receive the crowns of victory of heavenly Zion, and stand before the divine throne, unceasingly praying for all: entreat that they who hymn you may be saved.*

*Sessional Hymn of the Saints, Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премудрости):*

*Piously instructed, O wise youths, ye manfully put to shame the threats of the tormentors in martyric fashion; for having been champions of the Law and obedient to the fathers, O holy ones, ye zealously suffered with your divinely wise mother. Therefore, having purchased truly heavenly life by death, rejoice eternally, O courageous Maccabees: entreat Christ God to grant remission of offenses unto those who with love honor your holy memory.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Cross, Tone 6:*

*Thy Cross, O Lord, is sanctified, for in it healings come unto those who are infirm in sins; therefore we fall down before Thee: Have mercy on us.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

*Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church sings in godly manner, crying aloud from a pure mind, feasting in the Lord.*

*Troparia:*

*Let all the earth drop down joy; let the trees of the forest rejoice: today made radiant by the all-venerable Cross, shining unto the ends of the earth.*

*Showing the instrument of slaying to be life-bearing unto the world, an invincible victorious weapon, O most marvelous Cross: enlighten our hearts.*

*Thou art the divine victory; thou art the working of our salvation; thou art the triumph for the faithful and the divine sacrifice, O all-venerable Cross: sanctify those who sing unto thee.*

*For with all the earth let the passion-bearers, martyrs, and apostles rejoice, and the souls of the righteous now joyfully make glad; and the life-giving Tree visibly set forth in the midst saves all, and abounds with grace for the faithful.*

*Theotokion:*

*Truly thou hast appeared; truly thou didst bear the Son of the Most High, Who stretched out His hands upon the Cross, O Virgin Mother Mary, and called the world.*

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

*From the overshadowing mountain* O Word, the Prophet foresaw in divine manner that Thou wouldest become incarnate of the only Theotokos, and with fear he glorified Thy power.

*Troparia of the Saints:*

Having inscribed the Law, O blessed ones, as Moses of old did upon the tablets of his mind, ye transgressed it not even unto death, but strived steadfastly.

Offering lawful sacrifices unto God and zealous for the life of Moses, the passion-bearers of Christ despised every decree of the tormentor.

As a rock withstands the surge of waves, even so did ye, O Maccabean sufferers, endure many torments with manly resolve even unto death.

*Triadikon:*

O unoriginate Trinity, worshipped Unity, enthroned together Unity, praised Trinity: by the entreaties of the martyrs deliver us who praise Thee from tribulations and distresses.

*Theotokion:*

Rejoice from us, O holy Theotokos; rejoice, thou that didst bear joy unto the world; rejoice, only protection of mankind, blessed pure Theotokos.

*Katavasia:*

I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy Divinity.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

By Thy divine light, O Good One, illuminate, I pray, the souls of those who watch early with love unto Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who callest them forth from the gloom of sin.

*Troparia of the Cross:*

With joy let us lift up the most blessed Cross, set forth in the churches and cities, and let us bow down, that we may receive remission of debts.

The crafty serpent, the prince of darkness, is now put to death, unable to endure the radiance that the life-bearing Cross flashes forth, the honorable scepter of the divine King.

Like a bright star, and like a comely pearl, and the all-radiant sun, the Cross of the Lord illuminates all the ends of the earth: which Cross we embrace.

Shout aloud, ye nations; sing, dance, ye tribes; and sing unto God Who gave the Cross as an unbreakable foundation, which now lies before us: let all us faithful rejoice, as receiving good things for its sake.

*Theotokion:*

Knowing thee to be the beginning of salvation, all we faithful call thee blessed, O pure one: for thou didst bear in the flesh by His own will Him Who was seated before, nailed upon the Cross.

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

Thou Who from the night of ignorance didst enlighten the ends of the earth with the knowledge of God, enlighten me with the morning light of Thy love for mankind, O Lord.

*Troparia of the Saints:*

Since the holy martyrs did not violate the ancestral Law, Christ God Himself crowned them with righteous crowns.

Equal to Eleazar in understanding appeared the blessed children, and with him were offered unto Christ as a spiritual whole burnt offering.

Thou Who by the saints deliverest us from enemy deceit, save us, O Lord, by their prayers, as Thou art compassionate.

*Theotokion:*

Having appeared more honorable than the Cherubim, O all-immaculate Virgin, pray unto thy Son to save the souls of those who hymn thee.

*Katavasia:*

O three-blessed Tree, whereon Christ the King and Lord was stretched out, through which he who deceived by the tree fell, himself deceived by Thee, when God was nailed in the flesh, Who grants peace unto our souls.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Beholding the sea of life surging with the storm of temptations, and fleeing to Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Most Merciful One.

*Troparia of the Cross:*

As a divine vessel, as a radiant lamp, seeing the Cross set forth today in the churches, and in the shrines, and in the cities: let us hymn Him Who struck terror with it.

Death is slain, and corruption is destroyed, and the hosts of demons flee, seeing the victorious and fearful Cross of Christ set forth today, not daring to touch it.

Hymning Thee, God and King and Lord, because Thou hast granted us the Cross as an unbreakable wall: which Cross we now kiss with joy, and flee from fierce evils.

*Theotokion:*

With the venom of the serpent the food of our foremother in Eden was smeared; but the Virgin, having given birth to the Prince of Life, poured forth incorruption and resurrection unto the world.

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

Grant me a radiant robe, Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, O Most Merciful Christ our God.

*Troparia of the Saints:*

Clothed in the robe of martyrdom, O holy ones, ye adorned it with the labors of your suffering.

Desiring to be with Christ, O all-glorious martyrs, ye completed the course of suffering with unwavering mind.

Keeping the Law that Moses delivered, the holy ones lawfully suffered, putting to shame Antiochus the tormentor.

*Theotokion:*

Thou who alone in the flesh didst bear the Word through the Word, deliver our souls, we pray, from the snares of the enemy.

*Katavasia:*

In the belly of the watery beast, Jonah, stretching out his hands crosswise, clearly prefigured the saving Passion; thence issuing forth on the third day, he depicted the transcendent Resurrection of Christ God, Who was nailed in the flesh and by His three-day Resurrection enlightened the world.

*Kontakion of the Cross, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесыйся на крестъ):*

Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross of Thine own will, grant Thy compassions unto Thy new community that bears Thy name, O Christ God; make glad by Thy power our orthodox King,[^13] giving him victories over adversaries, having Thine aid, the weapon of peace, an invincible trophy.

*Ikos:*

He who was caught up to the third heaven into paradise and heard unspeakable and divine words that human tongues may not speak: what writes he to the Galatians, as ye who love the Scriptures have read and known?[^14] "Far be it from me," he says, "to glory, save only in the Cross of the Lord,"[^15] whereon having suffered He slew the passions. Therefore let us all also securely hold fast the Cross of the Lord as our boast; for this saving Tree is for us a weapon of peace, an invincible trophy.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

An angel indeed made the furnace bedewed for the venerable youths, but the command of God consuming the Chaldeans persuaded the tormentor to cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparia of the Cross:*

Let us bow down before the saving Tree, the all-holy Cross, which all the angelic hosts serve; seeing it set forth, ye faithful, it pours forth sanctification and life unto us.

The all-holy Cross of Christ the Giver of life hath appeared victorious: from on high driving away the throng of all demons and the barbarian insolence, and showing our King to be victorious.

Falling down before thee as living, I cry aloud: O my all-holy Cross, do thou enlighten my soul, and mind, and hearing, and lips, and tongue, and breath, and eyes, unto the paths of the Kingdom of Christ.

We hymn, we glorify, and we bow down, and we magnify Thy dominion, O Christ: for Thou hast granted unto us Thy servants the divine Cross, an inexhaustible sweetness, and a safeguard for our souls and bodies.

*Theotokion:*

The furnace burned not the three youths, prefiguring thy childbearing: for the divine fire burned thee not, having dwelt within thee, and having taught all to sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

Thou Who in the beginning didst found the earth, and by the Word didst establish the heavens, blessed art Thou unto ages, O Lord God of our fathers.

*Troparia of the Saints:*

Having suffered with martyric pangs, ye feared not the threats of the tormentors, crying unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers. *(Twice)*

Enduring the pains with patience, ye conquered the fierce wiles of the enemy, crying unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Theotokion:*

Thou Who didst dwell in the womb of the Virgin, and for her sake didst renew Adam: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Katavasia:*

The senseless command of the impious tormentor shook the peoples, breathing threat and God-hated blasphemy; yet the three youths were not dismayed by brutish fury, nor by consuming fire: but when the dew-bearing Spirit blew against it, being in the midst of the fire they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers, praised above all.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

Be astonished, O heaven, with fear, and let the foundations of the earth be shaken: for lo, He Who lives in the highest is numbered among the dead, and is strangely received into a small tomb. Bless Him, ye youths; hymn Him, ye priests; people, exalt Him supremely unto all ages.

*Troparia of the Cross:*

Prefiguring Thy Cross of old, Jacob laid his hands crosswise upon his grandsons and blessed them, teaching of grace among the Gentiles:[^16] bless Him, ye youths; hymn Him, ye priests; people, exalt Him supremely unto ages.

O Thine ineffable condescension, O Christ, and Thine unspoken good things! Having become incarnate, and being crucified, Thou didst accept death, and didst loose mankind from the curse, pouring forth incorruption upon the thrice-blessed Tree; this Cross is praised above all unto ages.

Today let us praise the all-holy Cross, the divine victory, the Prince of Life, the destroyer of deceit: the all-holy Cross of the Lord, the destroyer of demons, the expeller of barbarians, and the maker of victory and protector of kings.

*Theotokion:*

Beholding Christ pierced with nails, His incorruptible ribs pierced by a spear, struck on the head with a reed, and tasting gall, the Virgin cried aloud: Where hath Thy beauty departed, O most fair Word, praised above all, more than the sons of men?[^17]

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

Hymn the unoriginate King of glory, Whom the heavenly hosts tremble before, ye priests; people, exalt Him supremely unto all ages.

*Troparia of the Saints:*

Having become lovers of the truth and faithful keepers of the Law, O all-praised martyrs, hymn Christ unto all ages.

As the sevenfold fruit of a holy root, let us worthily honor the seven Maccabees in truth, the martyrs of the Lord.

Zealots for the Law, faithful sufferers of one mind, let us worthily honor the seven Maccabees with their mother who bore them.

*Triadikon:*

O indivisible Trinity in Unity, I glorify Thee as One in Divinity, and hymn Thee in Three Persons as one Power.

*Theotokion:*

Hymn God Who in the last times was incarnate of the Virgin for our sake, ye priests, and exalt Him supremely unto all ages.

*Katavasia:*

Bless God the Father and Creator, ye youths equal in number to the Trinity; hymn the Word Who came down and turned the fire into dew; and exalt supremely unto all ages the all-holy Spirit, Who grants life unto all.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

It is impossible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the incarnate Word appeared unto men: Whom magnifying, together with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.

*Troparia of the Cross:*

We became corrupt through disobedience, having appeared as transgressors of God's commandment; therefore death came upon men. Therefore the victorious Cross of Christ hath blossomed forth immortality today, which Cross we kiss.

This all-holy Tree hath appeared as a firm hope unto the faithful, deliverance from the curse, the joy of men, and lies before us, exposing the prince of darkness: let us the faithful bow down before it with gladness.

The beginning of blessing, the establishment of Christians, the wall and firm protection, and deliverance from the curse hath this longed-for Tree appeared unto us, an invincible weapon: do thou enlighten and sanctify us who bow down before thee.

*Theotokion:*

O temple and gate of sanctification, throne of God, cloud and all-radiant lampstand, and ark of grace, O all-immaculate one: cover and protect those who reverently bow down before the honorable image of thine only-begotten Son.

*Another Canon. Heirmos:*

We magnify thee, the Mother of God, and glorify thee, O Virgin Theotokos, as having given birth to the Savior of our souls.

*Troparia of the Saints:*

Ye were shown forth in truth to be a treasury of divine gifts, an unassailable wealth, O all-praised martyrs.

Being good intercessors before the Creator of all ask a life untossed by storms for our souls.

Keep those who celebrate your memory with divine zeal and warmth of soul unconsumed by the evil one.

*Triadikon:*

Unto the Father, with the Son, and the upright Spirit let us bow down, crying with the angels: Glory to God in the highest.

*Theotokion:*

Rejoice, O holy gate opened unto God, the only all-wise, whom the all-holy Spirit overshadowed.

*Katavasia:*

A mystical paradise art thou, O Theotokos, having untilled brought forth Christ: by Whom the life-bearing Tree of the Cross was planted upon earth. Therefore, bowing down before it as it is now exalted, we magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion of the Octoechos: Glory of the Martyrs:*

Let us hymn the marvelous Maccabees, Eleazar, the youths, and Solomonia; for they cast down the insolence of the serpent, the origin of evil, and having been ministers of the Law, were crowned.

*Both now... of the Cross:*

The Cross is the preserver of the whole universe; the Cross is the beauty of the Church; the Cross is the might of kings; the Cross is the establishment of the faithful; the Cross is the glory of angels and the wound of demons.

*At the Praises, Stichera on 6: of the Cross, 3. Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небесныхъ чиновъ):*

The honorable Cross prepares a common embrace for all who bow down before it with undoubting faith; and He Who was nailed upon it unites in love with the choirs of the bodiless powers those who sing unto Him.

Bowing down in faith before the honorable Cross, let us hymn the Master Who was crucified upon it; by His nod purifying our lips together with our soul, let us become clear by His spiritual radiances, praising Him.

Sweetening the bitterness of old, Moses delivered Israel, prefiguring the image of the Cross;[^18] and we, all the faithful, mystically and divinely depicting it in our hearts, are ever saved by its power.

*And 3 Idiomelon Stichera, in the same Tone:*

The much-suffering mother, calling together her children unto the struggles, said: "Follow the gray hairs of Abraham, that ye may partake of the sacrifice of Isaac."[^19] And they accepted her admonition, outstripping her teaching one after another, beholding the torments of torture: by whose prayers, O God, have mercy on us.

*Tone 4:*

Seven chosen pillars were hewn from the one spiritual Rock,[^20] and were shown forth as unshakable and showed forth the pillar of the Law. Therefore, O Savior, be well pleased that our souls may be preserved in peace.

*Tone 5:*

The keepers of the Law and sons of Solomonia, suffering at the tribunal, cried unto Antiochus: "O Antiochus, we endure for the ancestral Law; and neither fire, nor sword, nor wild beasts, nor scourges shall separate us;[^21] but together we shall die with our aged mother and our teacher and father, living and rejoicing together unto endless ages."

*Glory... Tone 4:*

Come, ye faithful, let us behold the courage against the army that assembled against the Maccabees; for the tormenting king, who held sway over all the nations, was held in check by an old man, and seven children, and one woman. Therefore by their prayers, O God, have mercy on us.

*Both now... of the Cross, in the same Tone:*

Thou Who didst aid the meek David to subdue the foreigner: fight alongside our faithful King,[^22] and by the weapon of the Cross cast down our enemies. Show forth Thy mercies of old upon us, O Compassionate One, and let them know in truth that Thou art God, and trusting in Thee we conquer, praying unto Thine all-pure Mother, that great mercy may be granted unto us.

*The Great Doxology, quietly, with chanting:*[^23]

*The priest, being vested in all the priestly vestments, comes with the censer to the Holy Table, the deacon preceding him with a lamp; and he censes the Holy Table and the honorable Cross. And thus he takes up the honorable Cross with the dish upon his head, and goes out by the north doors from the altar, preceded by two lamps and by the censer; and he comes directly before the church doors [the holy gates], and stands waiting for the end of the Trisagion. And as the Trisagion is finished, the priest exclaims in the usual manner: "Wisdom! Stand upright!" And the cantors sing the Troparion: "Save, O Lord, Thy people," three times. And thus comes the priest, carrying upon his head the honorable Cross, before the Holy Royal Doors to the analogion, which is adorned; he places the honorable Cross upon it, and makes three prostrations before the honorable Cross, singing in a low voice: "Before Thy Cross we bow down in worship, O Master, and Thy holy Resurrection we glorify." And the choirs sing the same aloud, three times. Then they kiss the honorable Cross, and again bow down once.*

*Chanting the Idiomelon Stichera:*

*Tone 2:*

Come, ye faithful, let us bow down before the life-creating Tree, whereon Christ the King of glory, stretching out His hands of His own will, raised us up unto the first blessedness, which the enemy of old had stolen away through pleasure, causing us to be banished from God. Come, ye faithful, let us bow down before the Tree, whereby we have been accounted worthy to shatter the heads of invisible enemies.[^24] Come, all ye kindreds of the nations, let us honor the Cross of the Lord with hymns: Rejoice, O Cross, complete redemption of fallen Adam; for in thee our most faithful rulers boast, as by thy power they mightily subdue the Ishmaelite peoples; which Cross we Christians now kissing with fear, glorify God Who was nailed upon thee, saying: O Lord Who wast nailed thereon, have mercy on us, as Thou art good and the Lover of mankind.

*Tone 5:*

Beholding Thee, the Maker and Creator of all, hanging naked upon the Cross, all creation was altered with fear and wailed: the sun obscured its light, and the earth was shaken, the rocks were split, and the splendor of the temple was rent; the dead arose from the tombs, and the angelic powers were filled with dread, saying: O wonder! The Judge is judged, and willingly suffers for the salvation of the world and its renewal.

*Tone 8:*

Today the Master of creation and Lord of glory is nailed to the Cross and pierced in the ribs; He tastes gall and vinegar, Who is the sweetness of the Church; He is encompassed with a crown of thorns, Who covers heaven with clouds; He is clothed with the garment of mockery, and is buffeted by an earthly hand, Who with His hand created man; He is smitten upon the back, Who arrays heaven with clouds; He accepts spittings and wounds, reproaches and buffetings;[^25] and my Redeemer God endures all things for the sake of me, the condemned one: that He may save the world from deception, as He is compassionate.

*Glory... Both now... in the same Tone:*

Today He Who is untouchable in essence becomes touchable unto me, and suffers passions, freeing me from passions; He Who grants light to the blind is spat upon by lawless lips, and gives His back to wounds for the captives. Seeing Him upon the Cross, the pure Virgin and Mother cried out in anguish: "Woe is me, my Child, what is this Thou hast done? Comely in beauty more than all men, Thou appearest lifeless and formless, having neither form nor beauty;[^26] woe is me, my Light, I cannot look upon Thee asleep; I am wounded in my womb, and a cruel sword passes through my heart passes through: I hymn Thy passions, I bow down before Thy compassion: O Long-Suffering One, glory to Thee."

*After this, the Litanies: "Have mercy on us, O God," and "Let us complete our morning prayer."*

> **Deacon:** *Wisdom!*

*The priest gives the Dismissal, and the First Hour is read.*

*At the First Hour, the Troparion of the Cross; Glory... of the Saints; Both now... the Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," the Kontakion of the Cross, and the final Dismissal.*

*On this 1st day of August, in monasteries and parish churches, they perform the Order of the Sanctification of Water, and it takes place as follows:*[^27]

*A table having been prepared and adorned, with water in a bowl; or we go forth unto the springs.*

*The priest having given the blessing, the Trisagion Prayers are said, and Psalm 142:* "O Lord, hear my prayer."[^28] *Then:* "God is the Lord," *in Tone 4, and the Troparion:* "Unto the Theotokos let us run earnestly," *twice; Glory... Both now...* "Never shall we be silent, O Theotokos." *Then Psalm 50:* "Have mercy on me, O God." *After this, the Troparia of the Sanctification of Water are sung:*

*Tone 6:*

Thou who didst receive "Rejoice" by the angel, and didst give birth to thy Creator, O Virgin, save those who magnify thee. *(Twice)*

We hymn thy Son, O Theotokos, and cry aloud: O all-pure Lady, deliver thy servants from every affliction.

To kings, prophets, apostles, and martyrs thou art the boast, and the protectress of the world, O all-immaculate one.

Every tongue of the Orthodox praises and blesses and glorifies thy most pure childbearing, O Mary, Bride of God.

Grant unto me also, unworthy as I am, O my Christ, remission of debts, I pray, by the prayers of her who bore Thee, as Thou art compassionate.

Upon thee have I set my hopes, O Theotokos: save me by thy prayers, and grant me remission of transgressions.

Quicken me, thou who didst bear the Giver of life and Savior; save me by thy prayers, O blessed hope of our souls.

Thou who in thy womb didst conceive the Creator of all, O all-immaculate Virgin, by thy prayers save our souls.

O Theotokos, who by the Word didst give birth beyond speech unto the Word, O all-hymned one, entreat Him to save our souls.

Make the Judge, Who is also thy Son, merciful unto me who have sinned more than every man, by thy prayers, O Lady.

As is our due, we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O pure Ever-Virgin Theotokos, praying that we may be saved by thy prayers.

Deliver me from eternal fire and the torments that lie before me, O Birthgiver of God, that I may call thee blessed.

Despise not the supplications of thy servants, we pray, O all-hymned Lady, that we may be delivered from every assault.

Deliver from ailments, and all diseases, and distresses us who flee unto thy sacred protection.

A strange wonder is that which is in thee, O Birthgiver of God: for for our sake the Creator of all and our God was born of thee like unto us.

Thy temple, O Theotokos, is shown forth as a free dispensary of ailments, and consolation of afflicted souls.

O all-holy Theotokos who didst bear the Savior, save thy servants from afflictions and every other necessity.

Deliver thy servants from every threatening circumstance that comes upon them, O all-holy Lady, and from all harm both of soul and body.

Save all who flee unto thee by thy prayers, O Virgin Theotokos, and deliver them from every necessity and sorrow.

Who that flees unto thy temple, O Theotokos, receives not speedy healing both of soul and body, O all-pure one?

Besought by all the saints and the ranks on high, O Compassionate One, cleanse me through her who bore Thee.

Spare, O Savior, the souls of our departed brethren in the hope of life, and remit, forgive their sins.

Rejoice, O Virgin, propitiation of the world; rejoice, all-golden urn and lampstand of the divine Manna and Light, O Bride of God.[^29]

We hymn Thee, the one God in Trinity, crying aloud the Thrice-Holy hymn, praying that we may obtain salvation.

*Glory:*

O Virgin who didst bear the Savior and Master of the world and Lord, entreat Him to save our souls.

*Both now:*

Rejoice, O Mountain; rejoice, O Bush; rejoice, O Gate; rejoice, O Ladder; rejoice, O divine Table; rejoice, the help of all, O Lady!

By the prayers of Thine all-pure Mother, and of all Thy saints, grant Thy mercies unto Thy people.

By the prayers of the glorious archangels, and angels, and the ranks on high, preserve Thy servants well, O Savior.

By the prayers of Thy honorable and glorious Baptist, Prophet, and Forerunner, O Christ my Savior, preserve Thy servants.

By the prayers of the glorious apostles, and martyrs, and of all Thy saints, grant Thy mercies unto Thy people.

By the prayers of the glorious unmercenaries, O Theotokos, preserve thy servants, as thou art the intercession and establishment of the world.

*Glory:*

We glorify the Father and the Son, and the Holy Spirit Holy Spirit, saying: O Holy Trinity, save our souls.

*Both now:*

Having ineffably in the last times conceived and given birth to thy Creator, O Virgin, save those who magnify thee.

*Then:*

Open unto us the doors of compassion, O blessed Theotokos, that hoping in thee we may not perish, but by thee may be delivered from adversities; for thou art the salvation of the Christian race.

*Then:*

Let us pray to the Lord.

> **Priest:** "For holy art Thou, O our God:"

> **Choir:** "Amen."

*Then, the present Troparia, Tone 2:*

Now hath the time arrived that sanctifies all, and the righteous Judge awaits us; but turn, O my soul, unto repentance, crying like the harlot with tears: Lord, have mercy on me.

O Christ, Who didst rain down the fountain of healings with waters in the all-honorable temple of the Virgin today, by the sprinkling of Thy blessing Thou drivest away the ailments of the infirm, O Physician of our souls and bodies.

Being an unwedded Virgin thou didst give birth, and a Virgin didst thou remain, O unwedded Mother, Mary Theotokos: pray unto Christ our God that we may be saved.

O all-holy Virgin Theotokos, guide the works of our hands, and ask forgiveness of our transgressions, as we sing the angelic hymn: Holy God, Holy Mighty, Holy Immortal, have mercy on us.

*And after the Trisagion, the deacon says:*

> **Deacon:** "Let us attend!"

> **Priest:** "Peace be unto all."

> **Choir:** "And to thy spirit."

*The Reader, the Prokeimenon, Tone 3:*

The Lord is my illumination and my Savior; whom shall I fear?[^30]

*Verse:* The Lord is the defender of my life; of whom shall I be afraid?

*The Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 302:*[^31]

Brethren: both He Who sanctifies and they who are sanctified are all of one; for which cause He is not ashamed to call them brethren, saying: "I will declare Thy name unto My brethren; in the midst of the church will I sing praise unto Thee." And again: "I will put My trust in Him." And again: "Behold, I and the children whom God hath given Me." Forasmuch then as the children are partakers of flesh and blood, He also Himself likewise took part of the same, that through death He might destroy him who had the power of death, that is, the devil; and deliver them who through fear of death were all their lifetime subject to bondage. For truly He took not on Him the nature of angels, but He took on Him the seed of Abraham. Therefore in all things it behooved Him to be made like unto His brethren, that He might become a merciful and faithful High Priest in things pertaining to God, to make expiation for the sins of the people for in that He Himself hath suffered being tempted, He is able to aid those who are tempted.

> **Priest:** "Peace be unto thee."

*Alleluia, Tone 6:*

*Verse 1:* My heart hath poured forth a good word.[^32]

*Verse 2:* I speak of my works unto the King.

*The Gospel according to John, Pericope 14:*[^33]

At that time, Jesus went up to Jerusalem. Now there is at Jerusalem by the sheep market a pool, which is called in the Hebrew tongue Bethesda, having five porches. In these lay a great multitude of impotent folk, of blind, halt, withered, waiting for the moving of the water. For an angel of the Lord went down at a certain season into the pool, and troubled the water: whosoever then first after the troubling of the water stepped in was made whole of whatsoever disease he had.

*Then, the deacon says the Litany:*

> **Deacon:** "In peace let us pray to the Lord."

For the peace from above, and for the salvation of our souls:

For the peace of the whole world, and for the good estate:

For this holy temple:

For our Most Reverend Metropolitan:

For our God-loving Bishop:

For our Great King:[^34]

That He may aid him in all things:

For this holy monastery:

For seasonable weather:

For those who travel by sea and by land:

That this water may be sanctified by the power, and effectual operation, and descent of the Holy Spirit, let us pray to the Lord.

That there may be sent down upon it the grace of redemption, and the blessing of the Jordan:

That there may be granted unto it healing power by the descent of the Holy Spirit:

That this water may be unto the healing of souls and bodies, and unto the banishing of every hostile power:

That it may be shown forth as a fountain of healing, and a gift of sanctification and salvation:

For those who draw and partake thereof, let us pray to the Lord.

That it may be unto the remission of sins, and unto the healing of diseases:

That we may be enlightened with the enlightenment of knowledge by the consubstantial Trinity, let us pray to the Lord.

That the cleansing operation of the transcendent Trinity may be sent down upon this water, let us pray to the Lord.

That the Lord our God may send down enlightenment and understanding into our hearts, let us pray to the Lord.

For all who need help and protection from God, let us pray to the Lord.

That the Lord our God may show us forth to be sons and heirs of His Kingdom through partaking of and sprinkling with this water, let us pray to the Lord.

That we may be delivered from all affliction:

Help us, save us, have mercy on us:

Calling to remembrance our most holy, most pure Lady:

> **Choir:** "To Thee, O Lord."

*The Priest exclaims:*

> **Priest:** "For unto Thee is due all glory, honor, and worship:"

> **Choir:** "Amen."

> **Deacon:** "Let us pray to the Lord."

> **Choir:** "Lord, have mercy."

*And the priest reads this prayer with a loud voice:*

> **Priest:** "O Lord our God, great in counsel and marvelous in works, Creator of all creation, Who keepest Thy covenant and Thy mercy unto those who love Thee and keep Thy commandments, Who in every necessity acceptest contrite tears: for for this cause Thou camest in the form of a servant, not terrifying us with apparitions, but granting true health unto the body, and saying: "Behold, thou art made whole; sin no more;" and from clay Thou didst make whole the eyes, and having commanded him to wash, didst cause light to dwell in him by Thy word; Who disturbest the billows of opposing passions, and driest up the salt sea of this life, and tamest the heavy surges of pleasures: Do Thou Thyself, O Lover of mankind, O King, Who hast granted us to be clothed in a snow-bright garment of water and the Spirit:"

Now also send down the grace of Thine all-holy, life-creating, and all-sanctifying Spirit, and sanctify this water.

*Saying this three times, the priest blesses the water with his right hand three times, dipping his fingers into it.*

And for the sake of partaking of it and sprinkling with it, send down Thy blessing upon us, washing away the filth of passions, and driving away all evil operations, fantasies, and diabolical apparitions, and strengthening us unto every good deed and the fulfillment of Thy commandments. Yea, we pray Thee, O Good One, visit our infirmity, and heal our diseases, both of soul and body, by Thy mercy. By the prayers of our all-immaculate, most blessed Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-Virgin Mary; by the power of the precious and life-creating Cross; by the intercession of the honorable, bodiless heavenly powers; of the honorable, glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John; of the holy, glorious, and all-praised apostles; of the holy and righteous forebears of God, Joachim and Anna; of our fathers among the saints, the hierarchs and universal teachers, Basil the Great, Gregory the Theologian, and John Chrysostom; of our fathers among the saints, Athanasius and Cyril; of our father among the saints, Spyridon the Wonderworker of Trimythous; of our father among the saints, Nicholas, Archbishop of Myra in Lycia, the Wonderworker; [and of the holy hieromartyr Josaphat;][^35] of the holy, glorious great martyrs George the Victorious, and Demetrius the Myrrh-Streamer; and of the holy passion-bearers, the Russian princes Roman and David;[^36] and of the holy equal-to-the-apostles their father, the Great Prince Vladimir; and of all the holy and victorious martyrs; of the holy, glorious wonderworkers and unmercenaries, Cosmas and Damian, Cyrus and John, Panteleimon and Hermolaus, Sampson and Diomedes, Mocius and Anicetus, Thallelaeus and Tryphon; of our venerable and God-bearing fathers Anthony and Theodosius of the Caves; and of the holy Maccabean great martyrs, whose memory we celebrate today, and of all Thy saints:

Preserve, O Lord, Thy servant, our Great King [Name],[^37] and all his Christ-loving armed forces. *(Thrice)*

Grant them health of soul and body, and unto this Christian flock that serves Thee, be merciful in all things. Remember, O Lord, all the episcopate of the Orthodox, rightly dividing the word of Thy truth; and all the priestly and monastic rank, and their salvation. Remember, O Lord, those who hate us and those who love us, our brethren who serve, those who are present, and those who for well-founded reasons are absent, and those who have commanded us, the unworthy, to pray for them. Remember, O Lord, our brethren who are in captivity and afflictions, and have mercy on them according to Thy great mercy, delivering them from every necessity: for Thou art the fountain of healings, O Christ our God, and unto Thee do we send up glory, with Thine unoriginate Father, and with Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages.

> **Choir:** "Amen."

> **Priest:** "Peace be unto all."

> **Choir:** "And to thy spirit."

> **Deacon:** "Bow your heads unto the Lord."

> **Choir:** "To Thee, O Lord."

*And the priest says this prayer secretly:*

> **Priest:** "Bow down Thine ear, O Lord, and give ear unto us, Thou Who didst deign to be baptized in the Jordan and didst sanctify the waters; bless all of us who, by the bowing of our neck, depict the form of servitude; and account us worthy to be filled with Thy sanctification through partaking of this water; and let it be unto us, O Lord, for the health of soul and body."

*Exclamation:*

> **Priest:** "For Thou art our sanctification, and unto Thee do we send up glory, thanksgiving, and worship, with Thine unoriginate Father, and Thine all-holy, good, and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages."

> **Choir:** "Amen."

*Then, taking the honorable Cross, he blesses the waters three times, lowering it crosswise, raising it, and moving it straight; and we sing the present Troparion, Tone 1:*

Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance, granting victories to our orthodox King over adversaries, and by Thy Cross preserving Thy commonwealth. *(Thrice)*

*And immediately they sing these Troparia, Tone 2:*

Account us worthy of Thy gifts, O Virgin Theotokos, overlooking our transgressions and granting healings unto those who with faith receive thy blessing, O all-pure one.

*Then the priest kisses the honorable Cross, and likewise all the people, and he sprinkles everyone with the sanctified water, and the sanctuary and the entire church. And the people sing the present Troparion, Tone 4:*

Having a fountain of healings, O holy unmercenaries, ye grant healings unto all who are in need, as having been accounted worthy of exceedingly great gifts from the ever-flowing fountain of our Savior; for the Lord says unto you, as unto fellow-zealots of the apostles: "Behold, I have given you authority over unclean spirits, so as to cast them out, and to heal every disease and every sickness."[^38] Therefore, having lived well according to His commandments, freely ye received, freely give, healing the passions of our souls and bodies.

Look down upon the supplications of thy servants, O all-immaculate one, quenching the fierce uprisings against us, transforming all our afflictions; for thee alone do we possess as a firm and sure establishment, and thy protection have we acquired: let us who call upon thee not be put to shame, O Lady. Hasten unto the supplication of those who cry aloud with faith unto thee: Rejoice, O Lady, the help of all, joy and protection, and the salvation of our souls.

Receive, O Lady, the prayers of thy servants, and deliver us from every necessity and sorrow.

*And after the sprinkling, the Litany:*

> **Deacon:** "Have mercy on us, O God..."

Furthermore let us pray that this monastery may be preserved...

> **Choir:** "Lord, have mercy. *(40 times)*"

> **Priest:** "Hear us, O God our Savior..."

> **Choir:** "Amen."

> **Priest:** "Peace be unto all."

> **Choir:** "And to thy spirit."

> **Deacon:** "Bow your heads unto the Lord."

> **Choir:** "To Thee, O Lord."

*And the priest says the prayer with a loud voice:*

> **Priest:** "O Master, Greatly Merciful, Lord Jesus Christ our God..."

*And after the prayer, Choir:* Amen. *And the Dismissal.*

*And where the Order of the Sanctification of Water is performed at Matins, we sing as follows:*

*After the Doxology, and after the Trisagion, and after the Troparion:* Save, O Lord, Thy people Thy people. The Troparia are sung: *Having received "Rejoice",* and the rest of the Sanctification of Water in order, as is customary. And after the Sanctification of Water and the Troparia: *Having a fountain of healings,* and *Look down upon the supplications,* *Receive, O Lady,* *Account us worthy of Thy gifts:* and the veneration of the honorable Cross, as was prescribed before. At the Hours, the Troparion of the Cross; *Glory:* of the Saints; and *Both now:* the Theotokion of the Hours. After the Trisagion, we alternate the Kontakia. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes from the Canon of the Cross, Ode 3, on 4; and of the Saints, Ode 6, on 4. After the Entrance, the Troparion of the Cross, and of the Martyrs; *Glory:* the Kontakion of the Martyrs; *Both now:* of the Cross. The Trisagion. The Prokeimenon of the Cross, Tone 6: *Save, O Lord, Thy people, and bless Thine inheritance.* *Verse:* *Unto Thee will I cry, O Lord my God.* Another of the Martyrs, Tone 4: *To the saints that are in His earth, the Lord hath been marvelous in all His desires in them.* The Epistle of the Cross, to the Corinthians, Pericope 125; of the Saints, to the Hebrews, Pericope 330.[^39] Alleluia, Tone 4: *Remember Thy congregation, which Thou hast purchased from the beginning.* *Verse:* *God is our King before the ages.* And of the Saints: *The righteous cried, and the Lord heard them.* The Gospel of the Cross, of John, Pericope 60; and of the Saints, of Matthew, Pericope 38. The Communion verse of the Cross: *The light of Thy countenance is signed upon us, O Lord.* And of the Saints: *Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous.* And the appointed reading of the day is read beforehand, or alongside the pericope.

*Look: If the 1st day of August falls on a Sunday:[^40] On Friday evening, at Small Vespers, the resurrection Stichera and of the Theotokos, as is customary. At Great Vespers, after the Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried," the resurrection Stichera, 3; and the Anatolian Sticheron, 1; and of the Cross, 3; and of the Martyrs, 3; Glory: of the Cross; Both now: the first Theotokion of the Tone. At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the Temple, and the Praises Stichera of the Martyrs; Glory: of the Martyrs; Both now: of the Theotokos. At the Aposticha, the Octoechos resurrection Stichera; Glory: of the Martyrs; Both now: of the Cross. At the Blessing of the Loaves, the Troparion: "O Virgin Theotokos," twice; and of the Cross, 1. Then: "Blessed be the name of the Lord," and the rest.*

*Look: If there is no Vigil, after "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart," the resurrection Troparion; Glory: of the Saints; Both now: of the Cross. At Compline, after "It is truly meet," the Kontakion of the Cross. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the resurrection Troparion, twice; Glory: of the Martyrs; Both now: of the Cross. The Resurrection Canon with the Heirmos on 4; and of the Theotokos on 2; and of the Cross on 4; and of the Martyrs on 4. Katavasia: "Having traced the Cross." After Ode 3, the Kontakion of the Martyrs, and the Sessional Hymn; Glory... Both now... of the Cross. After Ode 6, the Kontakion of the Cross, and the Ikos. The Resurrection Exapostilarion; Glory: of the Martyrs; Both now: of the Cross. At the Praises, the resurrection Stichera, 4; and of the Cross, with its Glory, 4, with their own verses* with their own verses: *Exalt ye the Lord our God.* *Verse:* *God is our King before the ages.* *Glory:* the Gospel Sticheron; *Both now:* *Most blessed art thou.* The Great Doxology. The resurrection Troparion only. And the Sanctification of Water takes place, and the veneration of the Cross, and the rest, as was said above. At the Liturgy, the resurrection Beatitudes, on 4; and of the Cross, Ode 3, on 4; and of the Martyrs, Ode 6, on 4. After the Entrance, the resurrection Troparion, and of the Cross, and of the Martyrs. Then, the resurrection Kontakion; *Glory:* of the Martyrs; *Both now:* of the Cross. The Prokeimenon, Alleluia, and Communion verse of the Cross and of the Martyrs. The Epistle and Gospel of the day, and of the Cross, and of the Martyrs.

*The same month, on the 2nd day.*

*The Translation of the Relics of the Holy Protomartyr and Archdeacon Stephen.*[^41]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Protomartyr, on 6. Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ преславнаго чудесе):*

O Stephen, first sufferer of the martyrs, foundation of the deacons, chosen of the apostles: with the unwaning light, O martyr, enlighten those who brightly celebrate thy light-bearing solemnity, dispelling the sinful darkness, granting grace and mercy unto thy servants, and guiding them by thy prayers unto life everlasting.

Having received a crown of graces matching thy name upon thy head, O Stephen, martyr of Christ,[^42] and having placed a necklet as of the gold of virtues around thy neck, thou wast an all-wise dwelling of wisdom, which thou didst love, making it dear unto thyself, for whose sake thou wast honored with glory and grace.

As an honorable sacred crown, thou didst offer thyself unto the King and God, O apostle, by the pelting of stones: rational and highly honorable, more than gold and precious stone, crowned with glory and grace, O all-honorable Stephen, first sufferer of the martyrs, sweet in deed and name: remember us all.

*Other Stichera, Idiomela.*

*Tone 2, Anatolian:*

First among deacons, first also among martyrs didst thou appear, O all-holy Stephen: for thou wast the way unto the saints, and didst lead many martyrs unto the Lord; therefore heaven therefore heaven was opened unto thee, and God appeared unto thee.[^43] Entreat Him that our souls may be saved.

Thou didst clothe thyself in holiness, O blessed Stephen, protomartyr and archdeacon, partaker with angels: mediate and pray for us unto the Savior, the sinless Lord.

Let us worthily honor the protomartyr and noble servant of Christ, Stephen the archdeacon; for standing in the midst of lawless men, he saw the Son at the right hand of the Father.

*Glory... Tone 6, Anatolian:*

First among martyrs didst thou appear, and among deacons, O apostle Stephen, adornment of sufferers, establishment of the faithful, glory of the righteous: ask for those who celebrate thine honorable memory, as standing before the throne of Christ the King of all, cleansing of transgressions, and that they may be accounted worthy of the Heavenly Kingdom.

*Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Podoben: "On the third day" (Трїдневенъ):*

Seeing Thee being crucified, O Christ, she who bore Thee cried aloud: "What strange mystery is this which I behold, O my Son? How on the Tree dost Thou die, suspended in the flesh, O Giver of life?"

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos; Glory... Tone 8, by Cyprian:*

Rejoice in the Lord, O crown-bearing Stephen, imitator of the Master; for thou wast also the protomartyr of Christ our King, and didst make vain the deceit of the lawless Jews, praying unto the Lord for us.

*Both now... Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѡ преславнаго чудесе):*

Seeing Thee nailed upon the Cross, O Jesus, and accepting passions of Thine own will, the Virgin and Thy Mother, O Master, cried out: "Woe is me, my sweet Child! How dost Thou endure unjust wounds, O Physician Who didst heal human weakness, and by Thy compassion hast delivered all from corruption?"

*Troparion, Tone 4:*

With a royal crown was thy head crowned from the sufferings which thou didst endure for Christ God, O Stephen, first sufferer of the martyrs; for having reproved the frenzy of the Jews, thou didst behold thy Savior at the right hand of the Father: therefore entreat Him for our souls.

*Look: After the dismissal of Vespers, the honorable Cross is carried back in, the priest being vested in phelonion, and the deacon in sticharion, with tapers and censer, singing the Troparion: "Save, O Lord, Thy people," and the Kontakion. And it is placed upon the Holy Table.*[^44]

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos, on 8, without the Martyrs; and of the Saint, on 4. The acrostic whereof is: "With hymns I crown the protomartyr. Theophanes."[^45] Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

*The charioteer Pharaoh Ode 1. Heirmos:*

The rod of Moses, performing wonders of old, striking crosswise and dividing the sea, submerged the charioteer Pharaoh; and it saved Israel the fugitive, the wayfarer, singing a song unto God.

*Troparia:*

Illuminated with radiant flashings of the Spirit, by supernatural sufferings and steadfast endurance, enlighten those who sing to thee, as thou standest before the Master, O all-wealthy, crown-bearing Stephen, crowning of the martyrs.

Having become the beginning of those who run the contest, and the chief of the assembly of martyrs, thou wast accounted worthy, O glorious one, to behold the Bestower of the contest extending unto thee with His life-bearing right hand the God-woven crown, O marvelous Stephen.

Being the only treasure of grace, O Savior, Thou hast revealed from the depths of the earth a priceless treasure, inexhaustible wealth, an inalienable possession, an imperishable spoil: Stephen, Thy protomartyr.

Radiantly illuminated with angel-like brightness, and celebrated with angelic songs, O protomartyr Stephen, chief of deacons, as thou hast appeared as a treasure, thou hast truly been worthily honored, filled with spiritual fragrance.

*Theotokion:*

O all-immaculate one, thou didst give birth for our sake to the incarnate, formerly bodiless, transcendent Word of the Father, O dwelling of virginity, and vessel of purity, and temple of sanctification, Lady of the world, Bride of God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

O Lord Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding, and didst found the earth upon the waters: establish me upon the rock of Thy commandments, O Christ, for there is none holy save Thee, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*

A sacred preacher and theologian didst thou appear, engaging with the assemblies of the Jews, boldly reproving their godlessness and God-fighting disposition, O Stephen, most excellent martyr.

Come, let us all with one accord honor with divine hymns the protomartyr of Christ, and the first of deacons, who radiantly showed forth the stepped path of the contest of martyrs, the companion of angels.

A hidden treasure is revealed in the earth,[^46] and the first of the martyrs, the first-chosen of the deacons, filled the Church with the perfume of divine fragrance and rich gifts.

My mind, deadened by the assault of life, raise up through the proclamation of wisdom made known by thee, O blessed Stephen, and account me worthy to hymn thine honorable solemnity, and to follow in thy steps.

*Theotokion:*

O Virgin O Virgin, thou didst appear as the noetic gate of the Orient from on high, Who appeared upon the earth; for through thee the Word came down unto us, to save all from irrationality, O all-pure one.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гробъ твой, Спасе):*

Apostle of Christ, first of deacons, wise protomartyr, crown of the martyrs: thou didst sanctify the ends of the world by thy sufferings, and by wonders didst enlighten the souls of men; deliver from every adversity those who honor thee, O all-praised Stephen. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

Hope of Christians, O all-holy Virgin, Whom thou didst bear as God beyond mind and word: unceasingly entreat Him with the powers on high to grant remission of all our sins and amendment of life unto those who ever glorify thee with faith and love.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The spotless Ewe, beholding the Lamb and Shepherd suspended dead upon the Tree, weeping cried out, exclaiming like a mother: "How shall I endure Thy descent which is beyond words, O my Son, and Thy voluntary sufferings, O all-good God?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who leaving not the bosom of the Father, hast visited our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^47]

*Troparia:*

A fountain of fragrant streams of myrrh hath burst forth for us today; a river of divine blessings issues forth, and the glorious first of the martyrs hath opened a stream of delights: he who was full of faith, and divine power, and grace.

How sacred, bright, and auspicious is the day of the protomartyr, full of light and truly spiritual gladness, shown forth to the cities of the Church, preserving the dominion of the honorable Church, and driving away barbarian insolence.

A good heritage hast thou worthily received, O blessed Stephen, where Christ the King of all is, there hast thou made thy dwelling; for thou didst appear as His minister, working mighty deeds of signs and wonders, and granting healings unto the sick.

An unbending, unshakable, firmly established pillar, reaching from earth even unto heaven, illuminating all the ends of the earth with the radiance of piety, is Stephen the great, the preacher of truth, the true champion of suffering.

*Theotokion:*

Thou wast the New Adam instead of the first, born of the Virgin instead of the foremother, the Redeemer of all and Savior; instead of death, Thou art true and immortal life;[^48] therefore knowing her who bore Thee to be the Theotokos, worthily all we the faithful call her blessed.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy countenance, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my ways, I pray Thee.[^49]

*Troparia:*

Patiently didst thou reprove the vain assembly of the lawless Jews, in truth, O voice of God; and being covered by them with the hurling of stones, into heaven as a trophy-bearer didst thou ascend in spirit, O Stephen.

As fair, as the dawn didst thou appear unto the world, O glorious Stephen, driving away the gloom, and putting to flight the delusions of the demons, healing the passions of the ailing, and the festering sores of soul-infirmities, O divinely inspired one.

Rejoicing noetically with the angels, O blessed one, thou truly sanctifiest the ends of the world by thy coming and grace, breathing forth the fragrance of spiritual myrrh, and delivering from afflictions and tempest.

*Theotokion:*

Having maternal boldness toward thy Son, O all-spotless one, despise not, we pray, the akin providence which is for us; for thee alone do we Christians favorably offer unto the Master as a propitiation.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation.[^50]

*Troparia:*

Having become an imitator of the angels by thy resolve, thou didst acquire an angelic appearance, O blessed one; rejoicing and walking about with them, save those who hymn thee.

The power of thy divinely inspired words, O blessed one, like a thunderous crash, clearly thundered in the ears of the lawless, O God-receiver, and darkened their faces.

Thou wast accounted worthy to behold the paternal glory, and this co-natural radiance, O Stephen, heralding unto thee through sufferings bright crowns, O most honorable voice of God.

*Theotokion:*

The only-begotten Son, invisible before the ages, in the last of days became the firstborn, visible in the flesh from thee, O Mother of God, the God of my salvation.

*Kontakion, Tone 6:*

First wast thou sown upon the earth by the heavenly Husbandman, O all-praised Stephen; first upon the earth didst thou pour out blood for Christ, O blessed one; first by Him with the crown of victory wast thou wreathed in the heavens, O origin of sufferers, crowned first-sufferer of the martyrs.

*Ikos:*

...Beholding the flowers of Paradise, filling the whole inhabited earth with their fair fragrance, and marveling at their strange sight, which in the winter's ice bloom even more, which is strange among flowers; and the martyr of Christ, who blossomed forth first, and became a door unto those who suffer lawfully, and blooming in the winter of torment, showed unto all who desire to suffer the unerring path, the good champion of the contest, who reached forth with beams of light, O crowned first-sufferer of the martyrs.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

At God's condescension, the fire was put to shame in Babylon of old; therefore the Children in the furnace, with joyful step, as in a meadow dancing, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.[^51]

*Troparia:*

The stream of thy blood opened the heavenly gates, and showed the Master of the contest standing by and stretching forth the crown unto thee as thou didst cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

Like wild beasts seizing the God-blessed one with foul, murderous hands, the God-slayers were putting him to death, while he prayed earnestly for those who were slaying him, and sang: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

Lawfully didst thou reprove the lawless, lawful imitator of the Savior, and didst show them to be fighters against God; being stoned with stones by them, thou didst sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

*Theotokion:*

In time conceiving without knowing wedlock the timeless Word, Who was previously bodiless, O pure one, by the assumption of flesh thou didst bring Him forth; unto Whom we sing, crying in concord: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a better power, he cried out to the Creator and Deliverer: Ye Children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^52]

*Troparia:*

Shining pre-eminently with supernatural illumination, thou didst show the appearance of thy face to be equal to the angels, O Stephen, when the grace richly hidden in thy soul was poured out; therefore thou didst cry: Ye Children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

O crowned Stephen, cleanse the wounds of my soul and the sinful arrows by the sprinkling of thy blood; for unto thy protection and unto thy Christlike, merciful disposition have I fled, crying: Ye Children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

The impartial Judge placed upon thy venerable head the crown named after the graces, O glorious one; Whom thou didst piously proclaim as Creator and God, and King of all, crying unceasingly: Ye Children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Into the hand of the Creator didst thou commit thy spirit, and the heavenly hosts rejoicing received their fellow-converser, who was previously on earth equal to the angels, and now is numbered among their choirs, unceasingly singing: Ye Children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*

Thou wast the vessel of the unwaning Light, O all-pure one; for thou didst bear the Light unto those in darkness and the shadow, enlightening all things by the grace of divine knowledge; unto Whom we the faithful unceasingly sing: Ye Children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Heaven was dismayed thereat, and the ends of the earth were astonished, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee, O Theotokos.[^53]

*Troparia:*

Stephen the protomartyr, having radiantly shown forth the beginning of the martyrs' contest, an unerring path of torment leading unto heaven, spiritually summons all unto divine gladness, and unto the communion of light, unto the fellowship of holiness.

Inflamed with the love of Christ, and having emulated His meekness and voice, he said: "Lord, lay not this sin to the charge of the slayers, but receive my spirit, O Good One, and vouchsafe me Thy divine kingdom and the delight of ineffable essence with Thee."

Comely, and of angelic appearance, and filled with wisdom and faith, and shining with rays of divine radiance, and with God-proclaiming lips, thou didst speak of God like a river, divinely making theological discourse; and the highest heritage hast thou attained, O God-blessed one.

With a crown of graces wast thou wreathed, and encircled by the choirs of martyrs, and standing before Christ the Master of the contest, pray for us who truly celebrate thy light-bearing solemnity radiantly, O blessed one, and deliver us from afflictions.

*Theotokion:*

As the intercessor for all Christians, O Mother of God, all-spotless Sovereign Lady, as thou art compassionate, deliver from afflictions and temptations thy servant-sufferers, and heal those who are ailing with the wounds of grievous transgressions and gripped by heavy diseases.

*Exapostilarion:*

*Podoben: "Visited us" (Посѣти́лъ ны̀):*

The hurling of stones wove for thee an unearthly crown of incorruption, and the drops of blood a purple robe of the Church, O Stephen, pre-eminent ruler of martyrs.

*Theotokion:*

O holy, all-pure Sovereign Lady, and only unwedded one, our salvation and hope: do thou entreat our Redeemer born of thee to save the world from fury, afflictions, and sorrows.

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

Having assembled secretly, ye feast-lovers, let us celebrate prayerfully and honorably today the memory of the protomartyr, receiving herein healings of his honorable gifts, and let us cry aloud: O martyr worthy of hymns, pray that those who celebrate thy memory may be delivered from afflictions. *(Twice)*

Like steps and a ladder unto the heavenly ascent were the stones hurled at thee; mounting upon which, thou didst behold the Lord standing at the right hand of the Father, stretching forth unto thee with His life-bearing right hand the crown named after thee; near Whom thou standest as a noble victor, and the supreme of sufferers.

Having enlightened thy thoughts by spiritual grace, in appearance thou didst appear as an angel, O Stephen, thy body shining with inner brightness, and showing a soul-like radiance unto those who beheld it, on account of which thou didst also obtain the sight of the Light, the heavens having been gloriously opened unto thee, O beginning and boast of martyrs.

*Glory, in the same tone. Idiomelon:*

Stephen, the good beginning of martyrs, full of grace and power, working great signs and wonders among the people, was stoned with stones by the lawless; yet he shone like an angel, and beheld the glory of Thee Who wast crucified for our sake, at the right hand of Power, and by the Spirit of grace was taken up into the heavens. And therefore, dwelling with the angelic choirs, he prays that our souls may be saved.

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

When the ewe-lamb beheld Thee, the Lamb and Shepherd, upon the Tree, she who bore Thee wept and mother-like said unto Thee: "O beloved Son, how art Thou suspended upon the Wood of the Cross, O longsuffering One? How are Thy hands and Thy feet pierced with nails by the lawless, O Word, and Thou hast poured forth Thy blood, O Master?"

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos. Glory, Tone 6. By Cyprian:*

O protomartyr, apostle, and first of deacons, door of the martyrs and glory of the righteous, boast of the apostles: standing at the judgment-seat, thou didst behold the heavens opened, and the Son of God standing at the right hand of the invisible Father. Therefore, having shone in face like an angel, in gladness thou didst cry aloud concerning those who were stoning thee with stones: "Lay not this sin to their charge." And now ask forgiveness of sins and great mercy for those who praise thee out of love.

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion, Tone 6, Podoben: "Rejoice" (Ра́дуйсѧ):*

Redemption for our sake, and a great price, Thy most pure blood wast Thou pleased to give, O my sinless Christ, desiring that all should obtain salvation. Therefore, beholding Thee nailed, Thy Mother, weeping, tore her hair, saying: "O Child, all-spotless Lamb, desiring to redeem the world by Thy precious blood, how hast Thou set from mine eyes, O Savior, unwaning Sun? Who dost bestow upon all illumination, and peace, and great mercy."

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Octoechos, and of the Apostle, Ode 3, on 4.*

*Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*

Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.[^54]

*Verse:* The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands.

*Epistle: Acts, Pericope 31.*[^55]

*Alleluia, Tone 1:* The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord, and Thy truth in the church of the saints.[^56]

*Gospel: Matthew, Pericope 37.*[^57]

*Communion Verse:* Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world.[^58]

*On the same month, the 3rd day.*

*Our venerable fathers Isaac, Dalmatus, and Faustus.*

*Stichera of the venerable ones, Tone 8, Podoben: "Thy martyrs, O Lord" (Му́чницы твоѝ гдⷭ҇и):*

Thy venerable ones, O Lord, having imitated the bodiless ones, by prayer and fasting drove away bodily passions, and having shone forth with wonder-working, enlightened the hearts of all; therefore by their prayers grant Thy people great mercy.

Dalmatus and Isaac, having received virtuous zeal, mightily pulled down the Arian and Nestorian heresy, and having become champions of the Orthodox, received glory from all; by their prayers, O Christ, grant Thy people great mercy.

...Having appeared as stars of the firmament of fasting, do ye enlighten the souls of monastics, O venerable fathers, having driven away demonic darkness; therefore after your demise ye are blessed, praying that all who celebrate your divine memory may be saved.

*Stichera of the Theotokion, in the same tone and podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*

Snatch me away, O Sovereign Lady, from the hand of the man-slaying serpent who contends to swallow me up utterly by deceit; crush his jaws by thy prayers, and break his stratagems, so that having been delivered from his claws, I may magnify thy power.

I am an unfruitful tree, O all-spotless one, yielding no salvific fruit; therefore I also tremble at the cutting down, lest wretched I be cast into unquenchable fire. Therefore I fall down before thee: deliver me from this, and by thine intercession show me fruitful unto thy Son, O pure one.

Foul thoughts and deceitful lips, and all my works are unclean; and what shall I do? How shall I meet the Judge? O good Virgin, pray to thy Son and Creator and Lord, that in repentance He may receive my spirit, as He alone is tender-hearted.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*

Woe is me, who in ignorance have grievously angered my God, and abide in despondency and slothfulness, as I am! O Virgin Sovereign Lady, do thou help me who am darkened, and pray unto thy Son and Creator to grant me forgiveness of transgressions.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

The spotless Heifer, beholding the Calf willingly nailed upon the Tree, weeping piteously, cried out: "Woe is me, most beloved Child! What hath the ungrateful assembly of the Jews rendered Thee, wishing to make me childless of Thee, O dearly beloved One?"

*If it be Saturday, or Sunday, we sing the Stichera of the saints, 3. Troparion, Tone 4:*

O God of our fathers, Who dealest ever with us according to Thy meekness, remove not Thy mercy from us, but by their prayers direct our life in peace.

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos, and of the Saints, on 4. Whose acrostic is: "I shall sing the radiant stars of monks." Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

That which was severed cut the unseverable, and the sun beheld earth which it had not seen; the water drowned the fierce enemy, and Israel traversed the impassable; and the hymn was sung: Let us sing unto the Lord, for gloriously is He glorified![^59]

*Troparia:*

Standing radiantly before the divine Light, and ever filled with rays, and being deified by true participation, O blessed ones, do ye divinely illuminate those who celebrate with faith your all-sacred memory and sing unto the Lord.
