Looking upon that alone which abides, ye utterly cast away the impulses of fleeting things; and cleaving with all your mind unto the Lord, having left behind bodily pleasures, O all-honorable fathers; therefore have ye passed over unto the unwaning dawn.

Winged by sacred disciplines, O wise Father Dalmatus, and adorned with divine teachings, clearly having attained unto the height of virtues of divine Isaac, thou wast a pillar and establishment of monks; therefore do we call thee blessed.

*Theotokion:*

O all-glorious one who didst bear God, change the dishonor of my passions, and grant me, O Virgin, a thought of compunction; and cause the illumination of repentance to shine, O pure one, in my mind, that I may magnify thee who hast magnified all.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

O Lord, supreme Builder of the heavenly vault and Architect of the Church, do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.[^60]

*Troparia:*

Truly have we all known thee, O venerable Father Isaac, as a star of the east which hast rushed forth, destroying the bitter darkness of heresies, and enlightening the faithful by thy words.

Having reasonably shone with pure radiances, O Father Faustus, thou wast a light of thy contests, enlightening all who behold thee, O blessed one, and who faithfully follow thy life.

By unceasing hymns and prayers, O venerable ones, having lulled to sleep all bodily passions, ye received inexhaustible grace and an unstealable wealth from God, O all-blessed ones.

By long fasting like Moses, O venerable one, thou didst show thy patience unto all, O Dalmatus, adorning thyself with thy most radiant sufferings, and being embellished by thy life.

*Theotokion:*

We have known thee as an intelligible and light-bearing lampstand, O Virgin who didst receive within the unendurable Fire: the divine palace and exalted throne, whereupon the Superessential rested.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^61]

Radiantly illumined by the Triune Light, O light-fashioned fathers, ye left behind the deep darkness of pleasures, and having appeared as lamps by divine works, O blessed ones, enlightening the hearts of the faithful; therefore do we honor today your light-bearing and honorable memory today, and with one accord cry aloud: O divinely bearing, most rich ones, pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who honor with love your holy memory.

*Glory, both now, Theotokion:*

As a Virgin and only one among women, who didst without seed bear God in the flesh, all we generations of men call thee blessed; for the fire of the Godhead made its abode in thee, and like an infant thou feedest with milk the Creator and Lord. Therefore, angelic and human race, we worthily glorify thy most holy birthgiving, and with one accord cry out to thee: Pray unto Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who worship in faith thy most holy childbirth.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Beholding the Lamb and Shepherd and Redeemer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb exclaimed, weeping and bitterly lamenting, crying aloud: "The world indeed rejoices, receiving redemption through Thee, but my womb burns, beholding Thy crucifixion which Thou endurest for the sake of mercy of compassion, O longsuffering Lord. O abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain, have compassion, and grant remission of transgressions unto those who hymn with faith Thy divine sufferings."

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Divinity.[^62]

*Troparia:*

Having become fruitful branches of the true Vine, the venerable ones poured forth pure wine of compunction unto all.

All we have known thee, O venerable Dalmatus, as an assentor to the Council, establishing the pious and rebuking the proud.

Having shone above all, the divine Council commanded thee to have care over all, O blessed one, adorned with divine virtues.

*Theotokion:*

By the slumber of slothfulness, a sinful sleep hath overtaken me, O pure one; by thy vigilant prayer, raise me up now unto repentance.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy countenance, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my ways, I pray Thee.[^63]

*Troparia:*

Thou wast an emigrant from the east unto the imperial city, and by the divine seed thou didst multiply its granary, having uprooted every evil Arian harm, O sacred Father Isaac.

Thou wast a disciple unto Isaac as Elisha was unto Elijah, O sacred Dalmatus, dividing the water of the evil harm of demons by the mantle of gifts of the harm of demons, and directing the way for the faithful.

Having submitted to the divine laws, thou didst turn away from the law of sin unto the end, and didst appear as a leading pillar of the divine Council, O God-bearing Faustus, and a rule and model of monks, and an unshakable confirmation of the faithful.

*Theotokion:*

As the true Theotokos do all generations of generations glorify thee, who didst clearly become higher than the angels, O most holy Virgin; therefore I cry: Wash my soul which is defiled by passions.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

I will pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him will I declare my sorrows, for my soul is full of evils, and my life hath drawn near unto hades, and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God![^64]

*Troparia:*

Thou didst appear as a fellow-converser with the angels, for thou didst emulate their life upon earth, traversing the path, O Dalmatus, and by abstinence didst wear out thy body, O guide of monastics, and truly most certain rule.

Inflamed with zeal for the love of the Lord, as Elijah, O blessed one, with boldness, O Isaac, didst thou reprove Ahab the leader of darkness, who was ailing with the evil heresy of foolish Arius.

Truly guides unto the errant, and harbors unto those being faithfully saved, wonder-working, God-bearing fathers: Faustus, and Dalmatus, and divine Isaac, ministers of Jesus, ever-radiant lamps of monastics.

*Theotokion:*

Visit my soul which is ailing with sins and with all temptations of the enemy, O most holy Virgin, by thy salvific visitation, and heal it as the good Mother of God, my all-hymned hope.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "In prayers" (Въ моли́твахъ):*[^65]

Having shone forth by fasting as luminaries, and having overthrown heresies by faith, let us praise Isaac with hymns, and with Dalmatus, Faustus, as pleasing servants of Christ, who pray to Him for us all.

*Ikos:*

Who among men is sufficient to tell the radiant virtues of the venerable fathers, and their contests and labors, their divine zeal, their boldness, pure life, and divine deeds, which they accomplished upon earth as angels? But nevertheless, honoring these things, let us faithfully sing unto them; for they radiantly stand before Him with the angelic choirs, unceasingly praying for us all.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The Hebrew Children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages![^66]

*Troparia:*

Ye appeared as coals ignited by the divine fire of the Comforter, consuming indeed the matter of malice, but enlightening the faithful, O God-bearing ones, unto the ages.

Having deadened the body by fasting, thou didst subdue it unto the spirit, O Dalmatus, and didst drown the malice of the enemy with the showers of thy soul-tears, O God-bearing one, unto the ages.

Having found a blessed end, ye live in the heavenly kingdom, and are united with the choirs of the venerable, O fathers, with whom ye cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God!

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst stay the stream of death, having given birth to the immortal God, O only pure Theotokos; unto Whom in faith we sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a better power, he cried out to the Creator and Deliverer: Ye Children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^67]

*Troparia:*

Having parted the sea of passions with the rod of the Cross, ye destroyed all the power of the intelligible Pharaoh, O venerable ones, and by singing this unceasingly, ye saved the sacred multitude of monks unto the land of dispassion, O fathers: Ye Children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Thou didst manifestly humble the arrogance of the heretics, having foreseen from afar their destruction; thou didst help the poor, and wast a guide of the blind, a staff of old age, and a consolation of widows, O Dalmatus, crying: Ye Children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Thou didst fast for three periods of forty days, and then didst sleep only so much as was beyond nature, having thy heart's eye watchful, O wise one, and illuminated by the divine dawn; unto Whom thou criest, O Dalmatus: Ye Children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*

The Lord Who made His abode in thy spotless womb showed thee to be entirely pure and radiant; therefore, cleanse me who am defiled by bitter habits and harmful assaults of adversaries, O Virgin, and account me worthy to sing: Ye people, supremely exalt the Lord unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Every ear was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh[^68] came down even unto the flesh from the virginal womb, having become man; therefore the all-pure Birthgiver of God do we the faithful magnify.

*Troparia:*

Strengthened by the power of Christ, the heaven-minded leaders of monastics were able to vanquish the powerless presumption of the adversary; and now, being adorned, they have passed over rejoicing unto the intelligible courts.

Ye appeared as thrice-radiant torches shedding forth an unwaning dawn, unfading flowers of paradise, filling with fragrance the minds of the faithful, O most rich fathers; therefore we faithfully celebrate your all-festal memory.

As with a beauteous crown encircled with dispassion, O all-wise ones, and clothed with the accomplishments of divine love, ye entered rejoicing into the bridal chamber of Christ, resting and receiving the recompense of your afflictions.

Your divine memory hath shone forth with intelligible radiance, illuminating the assemblies of the faithful, O blessed Dalmatus, Faustus, and Isaac, leaders of monastics, universal luminaries; therefore illuminate also the spiritual senses of our souls.

*Theotokion:*

O portal of light, enlighten my heart that is blinded by malice, and permit me not to sleep unto death, who am ever darkened by unclean thoughts, that I may thankfully glorify thee, O all-hymned Birthgiver of God.

*On the same month, the 4th day.*

*The holy Seven Youths of Ephesus.*[^69]

*Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чино́въ):*

Come, let us hymnodically sing the memory of the sevenfold candlestick that hath illumined the seven holy youths, crying unto Christ Who hath crowned them: By their prayers, O Lord and Redeemer, bring peace unto our life!

They who had died according to nature, truly beyond nature arose straightway as from sleep together, the seven divine youths. An awesome wonder! For three hundred and seventy-two years they slept in the cave as dead.

With divine Jamblicus and Maximilian, let us now honor Martinian, and John, Antoninus together with Dionysius, as those who pray for us, praising with them also the wise Exacustodian in godly manner.

*Other Stichera of the Theotokion, in the same tone and podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*

Most glorified art thou unto generations of generations, O Virgin Maiden Mother, Birthgiver of God Mary, protectress of the world, who didst bear in the flesh the Son of the unoriginate Father, truly co-eternal with the Spirit; Whom do thou beseech to save us.

Held fast by desperate transgressions, O pure one, having thee as our only protectress, O Virgin, we cry thankfully: Cleanse us, O all-holy Bride of God, for thou art a refuge unto the world and defense unto our race.

Tempest-tossed by the deep of sins, I have fled unto the calm harbor of thy pure prayers, O Birthgiver of God; I cry unto thee: Save me, stretching forth thy mighty right hand unto thy servant, O ever-spotless one!

*Glory... Both now...:*

By the dart of thy prayers, O pure Birthgiver of God, drive away speedily the bestial passions of my wretched soul, directing my life in peace; and count me worthy of the holy flock of Christ's chosen sheep.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

Standing by the Cross of thy Son and God, and beholding His longsuffering, the pure Mother said, weeping: "Woe is me, my sweetest Child! Why dost Thou suffer these things unjustly, O Word of God, that Thou mightest save mankind?"

*Troparion, Tone 4:*

Thy martyrs, O Lord, [in their suffering received an unfading crown from Thee, our God...][^70]

*Or this one, in the same tone:*

Great are the wonders of faith! In the cave as in a royal bridal chamber, the holy Seven Youths dwelt, and they died without corruption; and after many times they arose as from sleep, unto the confirmation of the resurrection of all men. By their prayers, O Christ God, have mercy on us!

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos, and the Canon of the Saints, on 4. Tone 2.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Come, ye peoples, let us sing a hymn unto Christ God, Who divided the sea and guided the people whom He brought forth out of the bondage of Egypt, for gloriously is He glorified![^71]

*Troparia:*

Come, ye faithful, let us honor the honorable seven youths who shine in their number, for they illuminate the Church of Christ as seven-branched lamps, better than those of the Law.

The seven youths, while yet alive, first put on the bodily dying of Christ; having received death most gloriously as sleep, confirming the teaching of the resurrection.

By the voice of the seven trumpets the stronghold of sinners fell; and now by the sevenfold voice among the sufferers of faith, the arrogance of delusion went down even unto hades and was overthrown.

*Theotokion:*

A newborn birthgiving dost thou renew, O unwedded one; for the Word, co-unoriginate with the Father and co-eternal with the Divine Spirit, became incarnate of thee, not departing from the yoke.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Establish us in Thee, O Lord, Who by the Tree didst put sin to death, and plant Thy fear in the hearts of us who sing unto Thee.[^72]

*Troparia:*

Ye appeared as seven unswerving stars shining with faith, O passion-bearers; and do ye direct unto the salvific harbor those who dive into the deep through deceit.

Having stood firmly before the tribunal of the tormentors, O holy ones, as once the Maccabees, ye exchanged corruptible warfare for life, having soldiered together with Christ.

Ye appeared as an acceptable sacrifice of prayer worthily unto God, O holy youths, and by sweet-smelling fragrance ye firmly exposed the stench of deceit.

*Theotokion:*

The bush on Sinai prefigured thy most glorious childbearing, O Virgin; for thou wast not consumed by the fire of the Godhead, having received Him faithfully into thy womb.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*

As pillars from on high of the Church of Christ, ye firmly threw down the walls of unbelief, O seven-brother martyrs. Therefore, having driven away the Hellenic wrath before death, and again the heretical tempest after death, and having upheld the faith of the resurrection, pray ye that we may be established in faith.

*Theotokion:*

O all-spotless Virgin, who didst bear the pre-eternal God, unceasingly pray unto Him with the bodiless hosts, to grant remission of transgressions and correction of life before the end unto us who hymn thee with faith and love as is due, O only all-hymned one.

*Stavrotheotokion:*

When Thine all-pure Mother beheld Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Word of God, lamenting maternally she said: "What is this new and strange wonder, O my Son? How dost Thou, the Life of all, taste of death, desiring to make the dead alive, as Thou art compassionate?"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

I have heard, O Lord, of Thy glorious dispensation, and I have glorified Thine incomprehensible power, O Lover of mankind.[^73]

*Troparia:*

Let us hymn the consecrated company and the heptad of honorable youths manifestly honored by their number.

Instructed with counsel, the Ephesian youths showed the ungodly king of deceit to be senseless.

As truly all-spotless sacrifices and slaughters of the Lord, ye appeared through faith, O holy youths.

*Theotokion:*

Despise not those who pray for us, O ever-blessed and pure one, that we may be delivered from every affliction.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

O Giver of light and Creator of the ages, O Lord, guide us in the light of Thy commandments, for beside Thee we know no other God.[^74]

*Troparia:*

Having sensibly died first by the common death on earth, ye straightway arose beyond nature, O Ephesian youths, manifestly confirming the resurrection of the dead.

Sanctified unto God by the confession of the true faith, as lawful sufferers of the Lord, ye were delivered, O wise ones, from prisons and wounds, and ye received crowns.

Having firmly vanquished the idolatrous deceit, O glorious martyrs, do ye ever preserve from the teachings of ungodly heresies those who confess the resurrection of the dead.

*Theotokion:*

In two natures and one hypostasis, the Son became perfect man without confusion and perfect God, through birth from His holy and all-radiant Mother.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Wallowing in the abyss of sin, I call upon the unsearchable abyss of Thy loving-kindness: from corruption, O God, lead me up![^75]

*Troparia:*

Let the holy seven youths be praised as pillars equal in number to the divine Wisdom, who with words as with stones crushed the godless decree of the tormentors.

Preserved by the law of divine providence, ye received as a ready grave the cave wherein ye appeared as dead and incorrupt for many years, O holy ones.

Ye arose as witnesses of incorruption, driving away corrupting and dead faith, and praying unto God for those who hope in the resurrection.

*Theotokion:*

Now hath womankind rejoiced; now hath sorrow ceased and joy blossomed forth, for Mary gave birth to Joy, the Savior Christ God.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up upon the Cross" (Вознесы́йсѧ на Крⷭ҇тъ во́лею):*[^76]

Having despised the corruptible things of the world and received incorruptible gifts, though dead they remained without corruption; therefore after many years they arise, having buried all the unbelief of the wicked; and praising them today with laudation, let us the faithful hymn Christ!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The all-wise youths served not the golden image, but went into the flame of their own accord, and mocked their gods; in the midst of the flame they cried aloud, and an Angel bedewed them: The prayer of your lips hath already been heard![^77]

*Troparia:*

The youths appeared as pure and chosen vessels of God, through whom the traditions of heresies are driven away from the Church and Orthodoxy shines forth: that there is a resurrection of every soul and flesh, even as it was born.

As from the firmament on high, the holy youths straightway appeared in their suffering before death; and alive after dying, sustained by divine glory, in godly manner they confirmed through themselves the true resurrection.

There shall be a resurrection of souls together with perceptible bodies; for as it is impossible for a soul to come into the world without a body, so also neither can a body without a soul, said the all-praised ones: either the soul shall be glorified, or put to shame.

*Theotokion:*

O Mary, who didst bear God the Savior of all, thou art the restoration of the despairing, the renewal of sinners, the hope of the hopeless, and the help of those who sing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

In the furnace of the youths, Thou didst once prefigure Thy Mother, O Lord; and this image delivered them from the fire as they entered unharmed; her do we hymn who hath appeared through Thee today unto the ends of the earth, and we supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^78]

*Troparia:*

Having hewn down the root of bitterness of the ungodly that grew up grievously, the heresy of deceit, the divine youths bring forth the fruit of faith, having been faithfully buried alive and having arisen.

O holy seven youths and passion-bearers of the Ephesian rulers, ye appeared as a divine confirmation unto the Church of Christ and unto the faithful kingdom; Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages!

Ye appeared on earth as firm, and showing yourselves equal in number to the stars, making the unerring pathway of divine faith, the youths cried aloud: We supremely exalt Thee, O Christ, unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*

No one hath perished, O pure one, who hath hope of faith in thee, O most glorious Mother of God, except those who out of envy reject veneration of the representation of thy likeness.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Thee who didst ineffably give flesh from virginal loins unto the Luminary God Who shone forth before the sun, Who came in the flesh unto us, O blessed and all-pure Birthgiver of God, do we magnify.[^79]

*Troparia:*

A wealth of wonders and treasury of divine mysteries hath the resurrection of the saints appeared today; for they who once had died the natural death of the flesh, now arose incorrupt, clothed as though they had not died.

The cave of the holy youths appeared as a treasury of knowledge and a firm rampart of faith, proclaiming the future resurrection of all not raising four-day Lazarus, but raising dead men of old.

Having suffered for the crown of martyrdom, ye lawfully clothed yourselves, O seven youths, pillars of the Wisdom of God; and by the teaching of Orthodoxy ye revealed the resurrection, as champions of the Church and intercessors of blessings for those who hymn you.

*Theotokion:*

Having preserved thy body and soul spotless unto God, O pure one, whose beauty Christ desired, and showed thee forth as Mother of His incarnation, O most glorious Mary, ever granting salvation unto me.

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Же́ны оу҆слы́шите):*

Armed with the weapon of Thy precious Cross, Thy passion-bearers, O Word, firmly vanquished the adversary powers, and together with Thee, O my Christ, King of all, they reign!

*Theotokion:*

Truly do we confess thee to be the Birthgiver of God, O Sovereign Lady, who through thee have been saved; for thou didst ineffably bear God Who by the Cross destroyed death, drawing the assemblies unto Himself: with whom we praise thee, O Virgin.

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos, Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*

*On the same month, the 5th day.*

*Forefeast of the Transfiguration of our Lord Jesus Christ, and the holy Martyr Eusignios.*[^80]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Forefeast, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ е҆сѝ зна́меніе):*

Come, let us ascend with Jesus Who ascends unto the holy mountain, and there let us hear the voice of the living God, the pre-unoriginate Father, Who by a bright cloud bore witness in the Divine Spirit to the property of His co-eternal Sonship; and enlightened in mind, in light let us see light!

Come before the feast, and let us cleanse ourselves, and faithfully prepare for the divine ascent of the transcendent dwelling of God, so that we may appear as eyewitnesses of His majesty, and obtain the glory which the chosen leaders among the Apostles were counted worthy to see mystically on Mount Tabor!

Come now, having been changed with a better change, let us well prepare ourselves beforehand, to go up beforehand unto the holy mountain of God, and unchangeably having unchangeably beheld the glory of Christ shining forth, brighter than the sun; and illuminated by the Trinitarian light, let us glorify His condescension therein!

*Other Stichera of the Martyr, in the same tone, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Я҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*

Dyed with the blood of thy flesh, O glorious one, thou wast clothed in the purple of divine goodness, and encircled thy head with the crown of the incorruption of life; and carrying about in thy right hand the victory of the Cross as a scepter, O all-wise martyr, thou reignest with Christ, ever rejoicing.

An invincible soldier wast thou known to be, O Eusignios; for safeguarded by the weapon of the Cross, thou didst go forth unto the combats of the enemy combatant, and didst cast him down, having soldiered radiantly, and received the crown of victory from the only Contest-master and Master, Who reigns ever.

By thine honorable sufferings, O glorious sufferer Eusignios, thou didst portray the honorable Passion; and having become victorious, thou dwellest rejoicing in the heavenly metropolis with all the passion-bearers, deified by divine communion; therefore we celebrate thine honorable and holy solemnity.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 2:*

Come, let us ascend the mountain of the Lord, and into the house of our God, and let us behold the glory of His Transfiguration: glory as of the Only-begotten of the Father; by light let us receive light, and being exalted in the Spirit, let us sing unto the consubstantial Trinity unto the ages![^81]

*If it be Saturday, [Both now... is] the Dogmatikon.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*

Enlightened by the radiance of virtues, let us go forth unto the holy mountain, that we may behold the divine Transfiguration of the Lord.

*Verse: Mercy and truth are met together; righteousness and peace have kissed each other.*[^82]

A divinely fitting sign of the Godhead Christ shows unto the initiates of the mysteries before the Cross, having shone forth as the sun on the mountain today.

*Verse: Blessed are the people that know the joyful sound.*[^83]

Wishing to transform the nature that was in Adam, Christ now ascends Mount Tabor, that He may unveil His Godhead unto the initiates of the mysteries.

*Glory... Both now... in the same tone.*

*Idiomelon:*

O Christ God, Who wast transfigured in glory upon Mount Tabor and didst show unto Thy disciples the glory of Thy Godhead: illuminate us also with the light of Thy knowledge, and guide us into the pathway of Thy commandments, as Thou alone art good and the Lover of mankind.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*

Welcoming the Transfiguration of Christ beforehand, let us the faithful radiantly celebrate the forefeast, and let us cry aloud: The day of divine gladness is at hand! The Master ascends Mount Tabor, to flash forth the beauty of His Godhead. (Once)

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Troparion of the Forefeast, thrice. After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Я҆ви́лся е҆сѝ дне́сь):*

The welcome feast of the Master hath arrived; come, all ye, having purified ourselves in mind, let us ascend Mount Tabor, to behold Christ!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ):*

Celebrating the forefeast today radiantly, O faithful, of the glorious and awesome Transfiguration of Christ, let us cry aloud with one accord: Transfigure our composite nature, O Savior, having shone alongside it with Thy divine flesh, and grant the former vesture of the dignity of incorruption, as Thou art compassionate, that we all may hymn Thee in glory, our only God!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Canon of the Forefeast, with the heirmos, on 6; and the Triodion on 4; and of the Saint on 4. In like manner we sing the 1st and 4th Odes; but the 3rd and the remaining odes up to the 9th, of the Forefeast, with the heirmos, on 8; and of the Saint on 4.*

*(If the forefeast happen on a Sunday, we sing the Triodion at Compline.)*

*Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 4:*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

I shall open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I shall pour forth a word unto the Queen Mother; and I shall appear radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders.[^84]

*Troparia:*

Let heaven shine forth light below brighter than the sun, and let earth hear the words of the living God, as Sonship is transfigured, for the Father bears witness on Mount Tabor!

A man seen, but God concealed: Christ ascends Tabor, unveiling the radiance of His Godhead, which is brighter than the sun, with the brightnesses of glory.

Intelligibly now doth Christ come beforehand to appear unto Moses, having shown the vision of glory upon Mount Tabor, in face-to-face unmediated converse; rejoicing today, let us celebrate the forefeast!

*Triodion, in the same tone.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

I shall sing unto Thee, O Lord my God, for Thou didst bring forth the people out of the bondage of Egypt, and didst cover the chariots of Pharaoh and his power.[^85]

Celebrating this forefestal day of the Transfiguration of Christ our Redeemer, let us the faithful clap our hands with hymns!

Having attained unto the entrance of the holy Transfiguration of Christ, we also shine with divine changes, embracing this feast.

After this present day, Christ, taking His disciples, ascends the mountain, and there being transfigured, shines forth with His Godhead.

Come, all ye, having mentally attained Mount Tabor, let us behold Christ awesomely transfigured before His disciples!

Like David the Prophet we cry unto Thee: Mount Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name, O Christ, at Thy Transfiguration![^86]

Having a heart purified from passions, let us behold the exceedingly high mountain, the Transfiguration of Christ which enlightens our mind!

*Triadikon:*

Let us glorify the Trinity in Orthodox manner: with the Father, the Son and the Holy Spirit, one Godhead, a Tri-hypostatic Monad!

*Theotokion:*

Thee, Mother and Virgin, do we the faithful confess in Orthodox manner, who didst ineffably bear Christ our God, Who alone is plenteous in mercy!

*Canon of the Saint, Tone 8:*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone is gloriously glorified![^87]

*Troparia:*

Being with the all-glorious angelic ranks, O most glorious one, unceasingly save those who glorify thy memory!

A temple of the Comforter was thy sanctified mind shown to be, O passion-bearer; therefore do we honor thee with faith.

A life-bearing dying of Him Who suffered in the flesh didst thou portray in thy flesh, O most blessed one, and thou wast counted worthy of true life.

Strengthened by the Spirit, thou didst cast down the dominion of the lawless, and didst uphold the laws of the Lord, O most glorious martyr.

*Theotokion:*

The curse was loosed, and grief ceased through thy childbearing, O all-blessed Maiden, gladness of the faithful, O all-spotless one!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

Establish thy choir-singers spiritually, who have gathered themselves as a company, O Birthgiver of God, thou living and plentiful fount, and in thy divine glory count them worthy of crowns of glory.[^88]

*Troparia of the Forefeast:*

He Who reigns over all creation was seen having received the form of a servant; through which He showed unto His disciples the inaccessible radiance of His Godhead, as they were able to behold Christ the Sun comes to shine forth glory on the mountain, and to darken with light the morning star that shines from on high; shining with His rays, we celebrate the forefeast today.

Fulfilling the words of eternal life, Christ stood forth, and as He showed things unto His friends to understand, He was well pleased to shine forth in Himself the Father's glory.

*Another Heirmos [of the Saint, Tone 8]:*

Thou art the confirmation of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the Light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee.[^89]

*Troparia of the Saint:*

Thou didst stand before the tribunal confessing God the King, O wise martyr, Who took on flesh and fashioned men.

As an all-spotless sacrifice consumed by the coals of martyrdom, thou didst offer thyself unto the Coal Who came from the Virgin.

Inundated by spiritual waters, O glorious one, thou didst dry up the turbid streams of polytheism.

*Theotokion:*

All my desire do I direct toward thee, O pure Sovereign Lady; turn me away speedily from carnal desires!

*Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*

Let us praise the Martyr Eusignios, who abolished all delusion by the power of Christ and clarified the faith; for he truly endured torments and exposed all the cruelty of the tormentors, and received the crown of victory from the right hand of God.

*Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast, in the same tone, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ):*

Prepare thyself now, O Tabor, for lo, Christ comes to show forth upon thee the radiance of His Godhead beyond comprehension unto His glorious disciples as they were able to bear it! Elijah and Moses stood before Him with fear, and a cloud of light overshadowed them, and a voice was heard from on high from the Father of lights, saying: "This is My beloved Son; hear ye Him!"[^90]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*

He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, upon a light cloud, Jesus the most divine came by an incorrupt hand, and saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ![^91]

*Troparia of the Forefeast:*

The heavenly choir rejoices with earthly choirs, and celebrates beforehand the radiance of the Giver of light, Who ineffably shines forth, having transformed the human form on Tabor, as He was well pleased.

Drawing near unto men by Thy God-man coming, and by the dawn of wonders, Thou didst mystically enlighten the world; and with the flashing glory of the Godhead, Thou didst shine forth on Tabor with inaccessible light.

Joshua son of Nun formerly stayed the sun, prefiguring the day of the divine Passion; but Thou Thyself, O Savior, prior to Thy precious Cross, didst hide the radiance of the sun with the divine dawnings of Thy face.

*Another Heirmos [of the Saint, Tone 8]:*

I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation, I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy Godhead.[^92]

*Troparia of the Saint:*

Casting away the slumber of despondency, O martyr Eusignios, thou didst vigilantly make bold for suffering with unwavering faith.

Rejoicing in the Lord thy Savior, O martyr worthy of wonder, thou didst reckon bodily torments as sweet food.

The proud arrogance of him who slipped through malice, O passion-bearer, thou didst cast down, humbling thyself with divine character.

An invincible soldier wast thou shown to be, contending in battles; and having finished the martyrdom, thou didst destroy the bodiless enemies.

*Theotokion:*

Equal in power and enthroned together with the Father and the Spirit, thou didst bear the Word; Him beseech to save me, O most holy Maiden, Ever-Virgin.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*

All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O Virgin who knewest not wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all, and didst bear the timeless Son, granting peace unto all who hymn thee.[^93]

*Troparia of the Forefeast:*

Rejoicing in Christ, let us faithfully follow Him; for lo, He goes unto the mountain, leading the best of the disciples with Himself; before whom, having shone forth with inaccessible beauty brighter than the sun, He showed His glory.

Let heaven be enlightened today more radiantly by the ascent unto the mountain, from whence Christ shines forth with inaccessible light, darkening the solar rays with the glory of the Godhead, as the Giver of light.

A mystery did Christ show unto the disciples upon Tabor, having shone forth with His face brighter than the dawn; and with the garment of His robes, having made Himself radiantly white as snow, He Who clothes Himself with light as with a robe, as the Lover of mankind.[^94]

*Another Heirmos [of the Saint, Tone 8]:*

Rising early from the night, we cry unto Thee, O Lord: Save us! For Thou art our God; besides Thee we know no other.[^95]

*Troparia of the Saint:*

Genuinely cleaving unto Wisdom, thou didst remove thyself from wickedness, O Eusignios.

Unto the sacrifices of idols thou didst not render honor, O God-bearer, knowing the living God and Lord in the heavens.

Lifted up upon the wood, O brave-minded martyr, thou didst depict the death of the Passionless One by faith.

*Theotokion:*

...The Word is incarnate in thy womb, O pure one, delivering the world from demon-driven carnal passions.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*

Celebrating this divine and all-honorable feast of the Mother of God, come, ye godly-minded, let us clap our hands and glorify God Who was born of her.[^96]

*Troparia of the Forefeast:*

Preparing for His friends the chamber of glory and future joy, Christ ascends the mountain, leading them up unto heavenly life from life that drags downward.

Causing the mind of the disciples to marvel, Christ shone with heavenly radiance upon the earth, presenting the chief of the Law and the prophets in servant-like manner; through whom God is witnessed by the dead and the living.

Christ comes now to appear as a Luminary in a dark place with the radiance of divine flashing; unto Whose radiance let us go, rejoicing before His face.[^97]

*Another Heirmos [of the Saint, Tone 8]:*

I shall pour out my prayer unto the Lord, and unto Him shall I declare my griefs; for my soul is filled with evils, and my life hath drawn near unto hades; and like Jonah I pray: Lead me up from corruption, O God![^98]

*Troparia of the Saint:*

Having placed true understandings in thy heart by noble images, not knowing the understandings of the enemy; thou didst run unto the tribunal, and didst destroy his army with the whole armor of the Cross.

The servants of demons, understanding thee to be a servant of the Trinity, deliver thee unto torments and prisons and an invincible death, O martyr Eusignios; but he is vanquished by thine invincible resistance, O exceedingly rich one.

The streams of thy blood, O martyr, dried up the turbid torrents of iniquity and the deceptive and wicked rivers of godlessness, and appeared as an inexhaustible abyss of healings unto the faithful.

*Theotokion:*

Having dried up the gardens of malice by the budding forth of the Word in thee, O pure Mother, cut off from the root of my soul wicked thoughts, and plant the fair sprouts of virtues, O Virgin.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Я҆ви́лся е҆сѝ):*[^99]

By Thy divine Transfiguration today, all human nature shines forth divinely, crying aloud in gladness: Christ is transfigured, saving all!

*Ikos:*

By Thy divine Transfiguration, our form which was formerly corrupted by corruption in the subterranean storehouses of hades of our forefather Adam, the Creator hath today brought up from corruption, deifying our understanding; yet He remains God together with man, bearing each nature united, unchangeable and indivisible; therefore now upon Tabor He shines forth ineffably, and from all His flesh emits the rays of His Godhead, enlightening those who cry: Christ is transfigured, saving all!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*

The godly-minded served not creation rather than the Creator; but having manfully trampled the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Praised above all art Thou, the Lord and God of our fathers, blessed art Thou![^100]

*Troparia of the Forefeast:*

Unto the transformation from earth leading up unto the heights, let us follow the chosen of the disciples, beholding the strange vision of Christ; and having marvelled with them, let us cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God!

Thou didst purify that which was defiled in human nature by water and fire; by Thy flesh Thou showest forth its brightness, O Savior, having shone forth with Thy face brighter than the sun as an image of the glory to come.

Let us go up with Christ unto the holy mountain, Who leads up the sons of Zebedee and the best, Peter; whom He chose as witnesses of His glory, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Another Heirmos [of the Saint, Tone 8]:*

The Hebrew youths in the furnace trampled the flame with boldness, and turned the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages![^101]

*Troparia of the Saint:*

Having enlightened thy soul with the blood poured out, O martyr, for the sake of the love of Christ Who became incarnate, thou hast passed over unto Him, crying in gladness: Blessed art Thou, O God!

Burning with the love of the Savior, thou didst escape the abyss of impiety, and didst endure the winter of cruel torments, crying aloud, O Eusignios: Blessed art Thou, O God!

While the drops of thy divine blood were still flowing, O most glorious one, thou didst stand before Christ the Master, singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

That thou mightest inherit the eternal life of the divine Kingdom, thou didst disdain the glory of temporal life, crying aloud, O Eusignios: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Theotokion:*

Newly didst thou bear a child Whom the Father begot before the ages, O pure Birthgiver of God; unto Whom we all sing: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*

The pious youths in the furnace the Childbearing of the Birthgiver of God saved; then prefigured, but now in actuality, it stirs up the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Hymn the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^102]

Who shall not marvel, O King of glory, at the majesty of Thy glory, wherein, having been transfigured, Thou didst show Thyself unto Thy friends, illuminating them with divine brightnesses? With them, clarify with Thy light us who keep the forefeast with faith, who hymn Thee!

Grant the height of heavenly life and the divine radiance of ever-existing glory unto Thy servants who now celebrate beforehand the radiant solemnity of Thy Transfiguration; wherein, shining light, Thou clarifiest those who hymn Thee, O Christ, Giver of light.

Giver of light and unapproachable Light, ever-existing, transcendent-unoriginate, Who clothest Thyself with light as with a robe! Thou didst shine as Light in the world, having come with flesh; and having flashed forth Light on the mountain, Thou didst show the image of the Father's glory.[^103]

*Another Heirmos of the Triodion [Tone 4]:*

Christ the King, Whom the captive youths confessed, speaking in the furnace with a great voice: All ye works, hymn the Lord![^104]

*Troparia of the Triodion:*

The present day, having shone forth the radiant Transfiguration of the Lord, commands the world to cry: All ye works, hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

Come, ye faithful, let us mentally go unto the holy mountain, to behold the all-radiant Transfiguration of Christ; for it enlightens all who glorify it unto the ages.

Today, with a pure heart, let us make a welcoming before the Transfiguration of Christ, and let us cry aloud radiantly: All ye works, hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Triadikon:*

In three Persons we bless one Godhead: the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit; together, all ye works, hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Theotokion:*

Christ the King, Whom the Virgin Mary bore unto us, and after childbearing remained a pure virgin: all ye works, hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Another Heirmos [of the Saint, Tone 8]:*

Having become victors over the tormentor and the flame by Thy grace, intensely cleaving unto Thy commandments, the youths cried: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^105]

*Troparia of the Saint:*

Encompassed on every side with the shield of piety, O all-blessed one, thou didst vanquish the lawless by grace, singing unceasingly: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

When thou wast in distress, thou didst call unto God the Benefactor, and He heard Thee, and delivered Thee from cruel things as thou didst cry: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Rendering no honor of irrationality unto soulless gods, O martyr, thou didst offer thyself as a pure sacrifice unto God, singing unceasingly: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

That thou mightest obtain the future life and glory, O exceedingly wise one, thou didst put thyself to death unto the world, and crying aloud, O Eusignios, thou wast put to death: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*

O gracious Virgin, who didst bear the most merciful God, count all who hymn thee worthy of divine mercy: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*

Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, enlightened in the Spirit; and let the nature of the bodiless minds keep festival, honoring the sacred solemnity of the Mother of God, and let it cry aloud: Rejoice, O all-blessed Birthgiver of God, pure Ever-Virgin![^106]

*Troparia of the Forefeast:*

Rejoice, O heaven, knowing beforehand the unwaning Sun Who hath now shone forth from the earth, and Who is well-pleased to cover the brightnesses of the sun with divine glory; and let the earth dance radiantly, shining with heavenly dawn and brightness, and becoming light!

Thy forefather proclaimed beforehand Thy Transfiguration, saying: Who among the sons of God shall be likened unto Thee, O Lord? For glorified in the counsels of the saints, Thou wast shown to be great unto those who are round about Thee, awesome in the glory of the divine form.[^107]

O Word of God, Wisdom and Power, and Image of the Father! O God understood by the faithful, and Man made known! Count us worthy to celebrate the radiant solemnity of Thy Transfiguration, shining in the light of good works!

*Another Heirmos [Triodion, Tone 4]:*

Thee, the Birthgiver of God who knewest not wedlock, the heavenly bridal-chamber, who didst bear the Author of our salvation, do we magnify with hymns.[^108]

*Troparia of the Triodion:*

Observing this first-entrance day of Thy most glorious and all-radiant Transfiguration, O Christ, we magnify Thee with hymns!

Zealously knowing the glorious Transfiguration of the Lord, let us spiritually illuminate our thoughts, magnifying this!

Changed with a good change by the Transfiguration of Christ, let us be found radiantly revealed by our virtues, magnifying this!

*Triadikon:*

Thee, the Tri-hypostatic Trinity and indivisible Essence, the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit, do we magnify with hymns!

*Theotokion:*

Thee, the bush which Moses saw burning yet unconsumed, which received the fire of the Godhead, do we magnify with hymns![^109]

*Another Heirmos [of the Saint, Tone 8]:*

Every ear was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God, how the Most High of His own will came down even unto the flesh from the virginal womb, becoming man; therefore do we the faithful magnify the all-pure Birthgiver of God.[^110]

*Troparia of the Saint:*

By the power of Christ, O martyr, thou didst put to death the powerless enemy who lifted himself up, and who in vain truly rages now; and thou didst bow thy head unto the beheading of the sword, and didst sanctify the whole earth with the streams of thy blood.

As the adornment of all sufferers, as the beauty of martyrs, O Eusignios, as an unshakable pillar of the Church, as the most comely rampart of the pious, as the divine loveliness of those who flee unto thee, as an all-radiant star hast thou shone brightly.

The myrrh of the sweet vapors of thy sweat drips down, O fragrant Eusignios, and makes fragrant our hearts who honor thee, and drives away the stench of passions, perils, and afflictions, O valiant passion-bearer of Christ.

O Jesus, Sun of glory, Who hast clarified the memory of Thy sufferer today: by his prayers enlighten, I pray Thee, my soul that hath been darkened by the deceit of the serpent; that I may glorify Thee as true God and Lover of mankind!

*Theotokion:*

Enlighten the eyes of my heart, I pray thee, O Door of light, which have been blinded by the darkness of sin, and show them acceptable unto divine radiance; that I may honor thee, that I may magnify thee with divine voices!

*Exapostilarion of the Forefeast, Podoben: "He Who adorned the heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*[^111]

He Who created the heaven and set light in the stars, having ascended Mount Tabor today, illuminates the disciples with the dawn of His Godhead.

*Glory... Both now... Podoben: "He Who adorned the heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*

Today upon Tabor Christ shone light; today the sound was fulfilled, the Father's voice was heard, crying: This is My beloved Son![^112]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the celestial hierarchies" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^113]

Making the welcoming before the all-holy, glorious Transfiguration on the present day, we glorify Christ, Who hath transfigured our nature by the fire of the Godhead, and Who hath shone forth with incorruption as before.

*Verse: Mercy and truth are met together; righteousness and peace have kissed each other.*[^114]

Come, let us ascend the holy mountain, and by faith let us behold the all-radiant Transfiguration of the Lord; faithfully bowing down before Him, saying: Thou alone art God incarnate, having deified mankind!

*Verse: O Lord, in the light of Thy face shall we walk, and in Thy Name shall we rejoice unto the ages.*[^115]

This is the forefestal day wherein Christ is transfigured, Moses and Elijah and His disciples being with Him on Mount Tabor, such a voice speaking: "This is truly My chosen Son!"

*Glory... Both now... Tone 5. Idiomelon:*[^116]

Moses the beholder of God and Elijah of the chariot of fire and unburnt traveler of heaven, beholding Thee in the cloud at Thy Transfiguration, O Christ, witnessed Thee to be the Maker and Fulfiller of the Law and the Prophets; with whom also count us worthy of Thine illumination, O Master, to sing unto Thee unto the ages!

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Forefeast, Ode 3 and Ode 6.*

*It should be known: If the Forefeast of the Transfiguration of Christ fall on a Sunday: On Saturday at Small Vespers, the Resurrectional Stichera and of the Theotokos, according to the custom. At Great Vespers, after the Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried," 3 Resurrectional Stichera, and 1 Eastern, and 3 of the Forefeast, and 3 of the Martyr. Glory... of the Forefeast; Both now... the 1st Theotokion [Dogmatikon] of the occurring tone. At the Litiya, the Sticheron of the Temple, and of the Forefeast the Aposticha of that day. Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. At the Aposticha, the Resurrectional Stichera. Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. At the Blessing of the Loaves, the Troparion: "O Birthgiver of God and Virgin, rejoice," thrice; and a reading from the Apostolic Epistles. Where there is no Vigil: After "Now lettest Thou Thy servant depart," the Resurrectional Troparion; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. At Compline we sing the Triodion; and after the Axios, the Kontakion of the Forefeast.*

*At Matins, the Resurrectional Troparion twice; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast; and the rest up to the Canon is Resurrectional. The Resurrectional Canon with the heirmos on 4, and of the Theotokos on 2; and of the Forefeast on 4; and of the Saint on 4. Katavasia: "Cross-inscribing." After Ode 3, the Kontakion and Ikos of the Forefeast, and the Sessional Hymn of the Saint; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. After Ode 6, the Resurrectional Kontakion and Ikos. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable." The Resurrectional Exapostilarion twice; Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast. At the Praises, 4 Resurrectional Stichera, and 4 Stichera of the Forefeast with the Doxastikon. Glory... the Gospel Sticheron; Both now... "Most blessed art thou"; the Great Doxology. The Resurrectional Troparion, Litanies, and Dismissal, and the Catechesis of Theodore the Studite; and the customary exit into the narthex, and the First Hour. At the First Hour, the Resurrectional Troparion; Glory... of the Forefeast of the Forefeast; Both now... the Theotokion of the Hours. After the "Our Father," the Kontakion of the Forefeast. In like manner we say them alternating at the other Hours.*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the tone on 6, and of the Forefeast Ode 3. After the Entrance, the Resurrectional Troparion, of the Forefeast, and of the Temple of the Theotokos if there be one, and of the Temple of the Saint (male or female); then the Resurrectional Kontakion; Glory... of the Temple of the Saint (male or female); Both now... of the Forefeast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Verse of the day; and the Epistle and Gospel of Matins in order under the pericope.*

*Be it known: That if the Feast of Christ's Transfiguration fall on a Sunday, nothing Resurrectional is sung, nor is the Matins Gospel read, but the entire service of the Feast, evening, morning, and at the Liturgy, unchanged.*

*On the same month, the 6th day.*

*The Holy Transfiguration of our Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ.*

*At Small Vespers, we sing Stichera on 4, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the celestial hierarchies" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^117]

He Who spoke of old with Moses by types upon Mount Sinai, saying: "I am He Who is," being transfigured today upon Mount Tabor, showed the Archetype, flashing forth with rays; therefore, O Christ, I magnify Thy power. (Twice)

Taking Thine own disciples apart, O Christ—Peter, James, and John—Thou didst lead them up of Thine own will upon the mountain, having shown a fearful wonder and the unbearable, ever-existing majesty of Thy divine coming from afar.

The Apostles of old, having become trembling at Thine incomprehensible coming, O Christ, falling down upon the earth, truly marvelled at Thy divine power; having shone brighter than the sun, O Good One, by Thine ineffable power.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 1:*

The shadow of the Law was received by the bright cloud of the Transfiguration, wherein Moses and Elijah having been, and having been counted worthy of the all-radiant glory, said unto God: "Thou art our God, the King of the ages!"[^118]

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^119]

Today Christ upon Mount Tabor, having transformed Adam's darkened nature, made it godlike by enlightening it.

*Verse: The heavens are Thine, and the earth is Thine.*[^120]

Enlightened by the radiance of virtues, let us cry aloud on the holy mountain, beholding the divine Transfiguration of the Lord.

*Verse: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name.*[^121]

The sun, while enlightening the earth, straightway sets; but Christ, having shone forth with glory upon the mountain, hath enlightened the world.

*Glory... Both now... the same tone and podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже).*

Moses and Elijah saw upon Tabor God incarnate from the Virgin Maiden, for the redemption of men.

*Troparion, Tone 7:*

Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, O Christ God, having shown Thy glory unto Thy disciples as they were able to bear it. Let Thine ever-existing Light shine also upon us sinners, through the prayers of the Birthgiver of God: O Giver of light, glory to Thee! (Once)[^122]

*At Great Vespers, there is no Psalter reading [stichology], except if it be Sunday, then we sing the whole Kathisma; if on Monday, the first antiphon.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 8, Idiomela, Tone 4, twice. By Cosmas the Monk:*[^123]

Before Thy Cross, O Lord, the mountain resembled heaven, and the cloud spread out as a tabernacle when Thou wast transfigured, while witness was borne by the Father; there was Peter with James and John, since they were to be with Thee also at the time of Thy betrayal; that having beheld Thy wonders, they might not be terrified at Thy sufferings; which make us also worthy to venerate in peace, for the sake of Thy great mercy.

Before Thy Cross, O Lord, taking the disciples up into a high mountain, Thou wast transfigured before them, illuminating them with a beam of power: on the one hand out of love for mankind, and on the other with authority, wishing to show the brightness of the Resurrection; whereof make us also worthy in peace, O God, as Thou art merciful and the Lover of mankind.

The mountain that was once dark and smokeful is now honorable and holy, whereon Thy feet stood, O Lord; for Thine awesome Transfiguration made the pre-eternal hidden mystery manifest at the end unto Peter and John and James; who, unable to endure such radiance of Thy face and the brightness and the brightness of Thy robes, fell prone upon the face of the earth; who, seized with amazement, marvelled, seeing Moses and Elijah speaking with Thee of what was about to happen unto Thee; and the voice from the Father bore witness, saying: "This is My beloved Son, in Whom I am well pleased; hear ye Him, Who also grants great mercy unto the world!"[^124]

Being transfigured upon a high mountain, the Savior, having the chief disciples, shone forth most gloriously, showing that having shone with the height of virtues, they shall be counted worthy of divine glory. Moses and Elijah, speaking with Christ, showed that He rules over the living and the dead; and that He is God Who spoke of old through the Law and the Prophets; unto Whom also the Father's voice from the bright cloud bore witness, saying: "Hear ye Him, Who by the Cross hath despoiled hades, and grants eternal life unto the dead!"[^125]

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6. By Anatolius:*[^126]

Prefiguring Thy Resurrection, O Christ God, Thou didst then take Thy three disciples, Peter and James and John, and ascend Mount Tabor; and when Thou, O Savior, wast transfigured, Mount Tabor was covered with light; Thy disciples, O Word, cast themselves down upon the earth, unable to endure the vision of the invisible form; the Angels ministered with fear and trembling, the heavens were dismayed, the earth quaked, seeing the Lord of glory upon the earth.

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day.*

*Reading from Exodus (Chapter 24):*[^127]

The Lord said unto Moses: "Come up unto Me into the mountain, and be there; and I will give thee tables of stone, and a law, and commandments which I have written, that thou mayest teach them." And Moses rose up, and his minister Joshua; and they went up into the mount of God. And he said unto the elders: "Keep silence here until we return unto you; and behold, Aaron and Hur are with you: if any man have any matter to judge, let them go unto them." And Moses went up into the mountain, and a cloud covered the mountain. And the glory of the Lord abode upon Mount Sinai, and the cloud covered it six days; and on the seventh day the Lord called Moses out of the midst of the cloud. And the sight of the glory of the Lord was like devouring fire on the top of the mountain in the eyes of the children of Israel. And Moses went into the midst of the cloud, and went up into the mountain; and Moses was in the mountain forty days and forty nights.

*Reading from Exodus (Chapter 33):*[^128]

In those days, the Lord spoke unto Moses face to face, as a man speaks unto his friend; and he would return into the camp; but his servant Joshua, the son of Nun, a young man, departed not out of the tabernacle. And Moses said unto the Lord: "Behold, Thou sayest unto me: 'Lead up this people'; yet Thou hast not shown me whom Thou wilt send with me. Yet Thou hast said unto me: 'I know thee above all, and thou hast also grace with Me.' If therefore I have found grace before Thee, reveal Thyself unto me clearly, that I may see Thee: that I may find grace before Thee, and that they may know that this nation is Thy people." And the Lord said unto him: "I Myself will go before thee, and I will give thee rest." And Moses said unto Him: "If Thou Thyself go not with us, lead me not up hence. And how shall it be known truly that I have found grace with Thee, both I and Thy people, but only if Thou go with us? And I and Thy people shall be glorified above all nations that are upon the earth." And the Lord said unto Moses: "This word of thine which thou hast spoken, I will do; for thou hast found grace before Me, and I know thee above all." And Moses said: "Show me Thy glory." And He said: "I will go before thee with My glory, and I will call upon My Name, the Lord, before thee; and I will have mercy on whom I will have mercy, and I will have compassion on whom I will have compassion." And He said: "Thou canst not see My face; for man shall not see My face, and live." And the Lord said: "Behold, there is a place by Me, and thou shalt stand upon the rock. While My glory passes by, I will put thee in a cleft of the rock, and will cover thee with My hand over thee, until I have passed by. And I will take away My hand, and then shalt thou see My back; but My face shall not be revealed unto thee." And Moses rose up early in the morning, and went up into Mount Sinai, as the Lord had commanded him. And the Lord descended in a cloud, and Moses stood there before Him, and called upon the Name of the Lord. And the Lord passed by before his face, and proclaimed: "The Lord God, compassionate and merciful, long-suffering, and plenteous in mercy, and true." And Moses made haste, and bowed down to the earth, and worshipped the Lord.

*Reading from the Third Book of Kings (Chapter 19):*[^129]

In those days, Elijah came to Beersheba, which belongs to the land of Judah, and left his young man there. And he himself went a day's journey into the wilderness, and came and sat down under a juniper tree and fell asleep, and saw a dream there under a tree. And behold, someone touched him, and said unto him: "Arise, eat and drink." And Elijah looked, and behold, at his head at his head was a barley hearth-cake, and a jar of water. And he arose, and ate and drank, and turned and fell asleep. And the Angel of the Lord returned a second time, and touched him, and said unto him: "Arise, eat and drink, for the journey is great for thee." And he arose, and ate and drank; and went in the strength of that food forty days and forty nights unto Mount Horeb. And he entered there into a cave, and lodged in it. And behold, the word of the Lord came unto him, and the Lord said unto him: "Go forth, and stand upon the mountain before the Lord." And behold, the Lord passes by, and a great and strong wind tearing the mountains, and breaking the rocks in pieces before the Lord: the Lord was not in the wind. And after the wind an earthquake: and the Lord was not in the earthquake. And after the earthquake a fire: and the Lord was not in the fire. And after the fire the voice of a gentle breeze, and the Lord was there. And it came to pass, when Elijah heard it, he wrapped his face in his mantle, and went forth, and stood near the cave. And the Lord said unto him: "Go, and return on thy way; and thou shalt go in the way of the wilderness of Damascus, and shalt anoint Elisha the son of Shaphat to be prophet in thy room."

*At the Litiya, Stichera, Idiomela, Tone 2:*[^130]

O Good One, Who hast illuminated the whole universe with Thy light, Thou wast transfigured upon a high mountain, having shown Thy power unto Thy disciples, that Thou dost deliver the world from transgression; therefore we cry unto Thee: O compassionate Lord, save our souls!

O Christ God, Who wast transfigured in glory on Mount Tabor, and didst show the glory of Thy Godhead unto Thy disciples: illuminate us also with the light of Thy knowledge, and guide us into the path of Thy commandments, as Thou alone art good and the Lover of mankind.

Christ, the Light before the sun, bodily dwelling upon the earth, and having divinely fulfilled all things of the awesome dispensation before the Cross: today on Mount Tabor, mystically showing the image of the Trinity. For taking apart three chosen disciples—Peter, James, and John—Thou didst lead them up into it privately; and having concealed for a little the assumption of the flesh, Thou wast transfigured before them, showing forth the majesty of the archetypal beauty, yet not in all perfection; on the one hand assuring them, and on the other sparing them, lest together with the sight they should also lose their lives, but according as they could contain it, enduring with bodily eyes. And Thou didst bring the chief of the Prophets, Moses and Elijah, bearing uncontradicted witness to His Godhead, and that He is the true Radiance of the Father's Essence, ruling over the living and the dead; therefore also a cloud overshadowed them like a shadow; and a voice from above, from the Father, out of the cloud loudly bearing witness and saying: "This is My beloved Son, Whom without corruption I begot from the womb before the morning star; Whom I sent to save those who are baptized into the Father, and the Son, and the Holy Spirit, and who faithfully confess that undivided is the single dominion of the Godhead: hear ye Him!" Do Thou Thyself therefore, O Christ God, Lover of mankind, illuminate us also with the light of Thine unapproachable glory, and show us worthy inheritors of Thy kingdom without end, as Thou art exceeding good!

*Glory... Tone 5:*[^131]

Come, let us ascend into the mountain of the Lord, and into the house of our God, and we shall behold the glory of His Transfiguration, the glory as of the Only-begotten of the Father. By light let us receive light; and being lifted up by the Spirit, let us hymn the consubstantial Trinity unto the ages!

*Both now... the same tone:*[^132]

Beholding Thee in the cloud at Thy Transfiguration, O Christ, Moses the beholder of God and Elijah of the chariot of fire, the unburnt traveler of heaven, witnessed Thee to be the Maker and Fulfiller of the Law and the Prophets; with whom also count us worthy of Thine illumination, O Master, to sing unto Thee unto the ages!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Idiomela, Tone 1:*[^133]

He Who of old spoke with Moses upon Mount Sinai by types, saying: "I am He Who is," being transfigured today upon Mount Tabor before the disciples, showed the archetypal beauty of the form, having assumed in Himself human nature; and having set Moses and Elijah as witnesses of such grace, He made them partakers of joy, who proclaimed beforehand the glorious Cross and saving Resurrection.

*Verse: The heavens are Thine, and the earth is Thine.*[^134]

David the forefather of God, foreseeing in the Spirit the bodily coming unto men of Thine Only-begotten Son, from afar summons creation unto joy, and prophetically cries aloud: "Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name!" For having ascended this mountain, O Savior, with Thy disciples, being transfigured Thou hast made Adam's darkened nature to shine again, transforming it into the glory and brightness of Thy Godhead; therefore we cry unto Thee: O Maker of all things, Lord, glory to Thee!

*Verse: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name.*[^135]

Beholding the unrestrained outpouring of Thy light and the unapproachable nature of Thy Godhead, the foremost of the Apostles upon the mountain of the Transfiguration, O beginningless Christ, were altered with divine terror; and when a bright cloud shone around them, they heard the Father's voice, certifying the mystery of Thine incarnation: that Thou art one, even after the Incarnation, the Only-begotten Son, and the Savior of the world.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*[^136]

Unto Peter and James and John, Thy foremost disciples, O Lord, Thou hast shown today upon Mount Tabor the glory of Thy divine form; for they beheld Thy garments flashing as light, and Thy face brighter than the sun: unable to endure the vision of Thine unbearable radiance, they fell down upon the earth, in no wise able to gaze; and they heard a voice bearing witness from above: "This is My beloved Son, Who is come into the world to save man!"

*At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion, Tone 7:*[^137]

Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain... (thrice)[^138] It is written at Small Vespers. And a reading of the Feast. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Feast, thrice.

*After the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оудивися іѡсифъ):*[^139]

Showing the human transformation at Thy second and fearful coming with Thy glory, O Savior, Thou wast transfigured upon Mount Tabor: Elijah and Moses spoke with Thee, and having called together three of the disciples, who, having beheld Thy glory, O Master, marveled at Thy radiance: O Thou Who didst then cause Thy light to shine upon them, illuminate our souls!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone and Podoben the same (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*[^140]

Thou wast transfigured upon Mount Tabor, O Jesus, and a bright cloud extending like a shadow covered the Apostles with glory; therefore also they fell prone upon the earth, unable to endure the vision of the brightness of the unapproachable glory of Thy face, O beginningless Savior, Christ God: O Thou Who didst then cause Thy light to shine upon them, illuminate our souls!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone the same, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесыйся):*[^141]

Having ascended the mountain with the disciples, and flashing forth in the Father's glory, Moses and Elijah stand before Thee: for the Law and the Prophets minister unto Thee as God; unto Whom also the Father, confessing natural sonship, called Thee Son: Whom we hymn with Thee and the Spirit.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 8, Podoben: "That which was commanded" (Повеленное):*[^142]

The lightning concealed beneath the flesh of Thy nature and Thy divine majesty, O Christ, Thou hast shown upon the holy mountain, O Benefactor, shining forth unto those who were with Thee, Thy disciples; and understanding Thy glory to be unbearable, they cried aloud: "Holy art Thou!" For being unapproachable, Thou wast seen in the flesh by the world, O only Lover of mankind!

*The Graduals [Anavathmoi], the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4.*[^143]

*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name.*[^144]
*Verse: The heavens are Thine, and the earth is Thine.*
*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 45 [Luke 9:28–36].*[^145]

*After Psalm 50: Glory... All things today are filled with joy: Christ is transfigured before the disciples. Both now... the same.*[^146]

*Sticheron, Idiomelon, Tone 5:*[^147]

Having uncovered a little ray of Thy Godhead, O Savior, unto those who went up with Thee into the mountain, Thou hast made them lovers of Thy transcendent glory; therefore they cried out in awe: "It is good for us to be here!" With them we also sing unto Thee, the transfigured Savior Christ, unto the ages!

*Two Canons: by Lord Cosmas, Tone 4; and by Lord John, Tone 8. The heirmoi of both canons twice; all the troparia we sing on 12.*[^148]

*Canon [by Cosmas], Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

The choirs of Israel, having traversed the Red Sea and the watery deep with dryshod feet, seeing the horsemen and captains, the enemies, submerged therein, sang with joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He is glorified![^149]

*Troparia:*

Christ, speaking words of life unto His friends, and teaching concerning the divine Kingdom, said: "Know the Father in Me," as flashing forth with unapproachable light, singing in joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He is glorified!

"Ye shall be clothed with the power of the nations, O disciple friends, and ye shall be marvelous in their wealth, for ye shall be filled with glory: for I shall appear flashing forth brighter than the sun, singing in joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He is glorified!"

Today Christ on Mount Tabor, having shone clearly with divine radiance as He promised, uncovered His form unto the disciples; and being filled with the light-bearing divine radiance, they sang in joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He is glorified!

*Another Canon [by John of Damascus], Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Traversing the water as on dry land, and having escaped Egyptian wickedness, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God![^150]

*Troparia:*

Moses, of old seeing prophetically the glory of the Lord at the sea in the cloud and pillar of fire, cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

As covered by the rock in body, seeing the Invisible One by deification, Moses the beholder of God cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

Thou wast seen of old by Moses in darkness on the mountain of the Law, and on Tabor: but now in the unapproachable light of the Godhead.

*Katavasia: Moses, having traced out a Cross...*[^151]

*Ode 3. Heirmos [Canon 1, Tone 4]:*

The bow of the mighty is become weak, and the weak have girded themselves with strength; therefore my heart is established in the Lord.[^152]

*Troparia:*

Having clothed Thyself in the whole Adam, O Christ, changing our nature which was darkened of old, Thou hast illuminated it, and by the change of Thy form Thou hast divinized it.

He Who of old guided Israel in the wilderness by a pillar looking like fire and by a cloud, today on Mount Tabor, Christ hath ineffably shone forth in light.

*Another Heirmos [Canon 2, Tone 8]:*

O Lord, Creator of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.[^153]

*Troparia:*

The overshadowing glory formerly in the tabernacle, and unto Moses the beholder of God, Thy servant, was an image of Thine ineffably radiant Transfiguration on Tabor, O Master.

The supreme summits of the Apostles went up with Thee, O Only-begotten Word, into Mount Tabor; and Moses and Elijah stood together before Thee as servants of God, O only Lover of mankind.

Being God the Word, He became entirely earthborn, mingling humanity with all the Godhead in His hypostasis; Which in two essences Moses and Elijah beheld on Mount Tabor.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оудивися іѡсифъ):*[^154]

Upon Mount Tabor Thou wast transfigured, O Jesus, in the midst of the most wise Elijah and Moses, with James and Peter and John. And Peter, having understood, said these things unto Thee: "It is good for us to make three tabernacles here: one for Moses, and one for Elijah, and one for Thee, Master Christ." O Thou Who didst then cause Thy light to shine upon them, illuminate our souls!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Ode 4. Heirmos [Canon 1, Tone 4]:*

I heard of the glorious dispensation, O Christ God, how Thou wast born of the Virgin, that Thou mightest deliver from deception those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^155]

*Troparia:*

Prefiguring the Law in Sinai by writing, O Christ God, Thou wast revealed borne in a cloud, and fire, and darkness, and tempest: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

That Thou mightest confirm Thy glorious dispensation, O Christ God, as being before the ages, and the same Who didst set Thy departure upon a cloud, Thou hast ineffably shone forth on Tabor.

Speaking together, they stood before Thee as servants, Master Christ, with whom Thou didst converse in the vapor of fire, and darkness, and a gentle breeze: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Coming unto Tabor, Moses who of old foresaw Thee in the fire and the bush, and Elijah who was taken up in a chariot of fire, proclaimed the glorious dispensation of Thy Cross, O Christ.

*Another Heirmos [Canon 2, Tone 8]:*

Rays of Godhead proceeded from Thy flesh unto Prophets and Apostles; therefore their chiefs sang, crying: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^156]

*Troparia:*

O Master, Who didst preserve unharmed the bush that was touched by fire, Thou hast shown Thy God-radiant flesh unto Moses, as he sang: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

The physical sun was hidden by the radiance of the Godhead, as on Mount Tabor it beheld Thee transfigured, O my Jesus: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

A fire not burning bodily matter was seen, being immaterial; as Thou, O Master, wast revealed unto Moses and Elijah and the Apostles, one of two, in two perfect natures.

*Ode 5. Heirmos [Canon 1, Tone 4]:*

Dividing the first-created light from darkness, so that in light Thy works hymn Thee, Christ the Creator: in Thy light, direct our paths![^157]

*Troparia:*

The mountains bowed down before Thy face: for the light, even before Thy feet, the sun Christ with unceasing rays, came as Thou wast well-pleased to assume an earthly form.

"Behold the Savior, Christ, Whom we of old proclaimed as being God!" cried Moses and Elijah unto the disciples instructed on the holy Mount Tabor.

The unchanging nature, being united with the human, by the abundance of the immaterial Godhead, ineffably shone forth His unveiled light unto the Apostles.

When the disciples beheld Thee, the ever-existing Radiance, shining forth in the Father's glory, O Christ, they cried unto Thee: In Thy light, direct our paths!

*Another Heirmos [Canon 2, Tone 8]:*

Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy presence, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, wretched as I am? But turn me back, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my ways, I pray Thee.[^158]

The oratorical tongue cannot declare Thy majesty; for as holding life and ruling over death, Thou hast set Moses and Elijah upon Mount Tabor, bearing witness to Thy Godhead.

O Christ, Who with invisible hands didst fashion man according to Thine image, Thou hast shown Thine archetypal beauty in Thy creation: not as in an image, but as Thyself being in essence, Thou wast both God and man.

Being mingled without confusion, Thou hast shown us a burning coal of Godhead upon Mount Tabor, burning up sins, and illuminating souls: Thou didst fill Moses with Elijah, and the foremost of the disciples, with wonder.

*Ode 6. Heirmos [Canon 1, Tone 4]:*

When I was afflicted, I cried unto the Lord, and the God of my salvation heard me.[^159]

*Troparia:*

A radiance brighter than the sun, a clearer light, the Savior shone forth on Tabor, and hath illuminated us.

Having ascended Mount Tabor, Thou wast transfigured, O Christ, and having darkened all deception, Thou hast illuminated us.

Having recognized Thee as God on Tabor, O Christ, the glorious Apostles, being amazed, bowed their knees.

*Another Heirmos [Canon 2, Tone 8]:*

Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation![^160]

*Troparia:*

How great and fearful a vision was seen today: from heaven physical, and from the earth incomparable, the spiritual Sun of righteousness shone forth on Mount Tabor!

"The legal shadow that grew weak hath indeed passed away, and Christ the Truth is clearly come!" cried Moses on Tabor, having beheld Thy Godhead.

The pillar of fire most clearly foretold Christ Who is transfigured; and the cloud, the grace of the Spirit that overshadowed upon Tabor.

*Kontakion, Tone 7, Idiomelon:*[^161]

Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, and Thy disciples beheld Thy glory as they were able to contain it, O Christ God; that when they should see Thee crucified, they might understand the suffering to be voluntary, and proclaim unto the world that Thou art truly the Radiance of the Father!

*Ikos:*[^162]

Arise, ye sluggish thoughts of my soul, that ever incline downward into the earth; be lifted up and ascend unto the height of the divine ascent! Let us hasten unto Peter and the sons of Zebedee, and together with them let us reach Mount Tabor, that we may see with them the glory of our God; and let us hear the voice which they heard from above, and proclaimed the Father's Radiance!

*Ode 7. Heirmos [Canon 1, Tone 4]:*
