The Abrahamic youths of old in Babylon trampled down the furnace flame, crying aloud in hymns: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers![^163]

*Troparia:*

Flashing with the light of unapproachable glory on Mount Tabor, the Apostles cried unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Delighting in the radiance of the divine sound, and in the dew-giving cloud, and in Thy radiance, O Christ, the Apostles sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

When Peter beheld Thee flashing in unapproachable light on Mount Tabor, O Christ, he cried aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Being with Christ the Author of life, as the light of His form shone forth, the children of Zebedee thundered: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Another Heirmos [Canon 2, Tone 8]:*

The Hebrew youths in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages![^164]

*Troparia:*

Now the invisible things of the Godhead were seen by the Apostles, flashing in the flesh upon Mount Tabor, as they cried: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

The Apostles were seized with fear, marveling at the majesty of the divine kingdom, crying upon Mount Tabor: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

Now that which was never heard was heard: for the Son of the Virgin without a father is gloriously attested by the Father's voice, that He is both God and man, the same unto the ages!

Not by adoption wast Thou the Son of the Most High, but being by essence the beloved Son, existing before, Thou hast drawn nigh unto us without change: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Ode 8. Heirmos [Canon 1, Tone 4]:*

The youths in Babylon, inflamed with divine zeal, manfully trampled down the threat of the tormentor and the flame; and cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed, they sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^165]

*Troparia:*

Carrying all things by His nod, Christ ascended Mount Tabor with His most pure feet; on which He shone forth with His face brighter than the rays of the sun, showing the elders of the Law and of grace unto those who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Having ineffably appeared in unapproachable glory on Mount Tabor, the unrestrained and unwaning Light, the Radiance of the Father, having made creation bright, divinized mankind, as they sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Standing in a sacred manner on Mount Tabor, Moses and Elijah, having clearly beheld the divine image of the hypostasis, Christ flashing in the Father's glory, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

The face of Moses was of old glorified by the vision of the divine manifestation: but Christ is clothed with light and glory as with a garment; for being the Creator of light Himself, He illuminates those who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Beholding Christ overshadowed by the light-bearing cloud on Tabor, and falling prone upon the earth, their mind being enlightened, the disciples sang unto Him with the Father and the Spirit: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Another Heirmos [Canon 2, Tone 8]:*

The Chaldean tormentor furiously heated the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but seeing them saved by a better power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all the ages![^166]

*Troparia:*

Having heard Thee attested by the Father, O Master, and being unable to endure gazing upon the flashing of Thy face, which was firmer than human form, Thy disciples fell prone upon the earth, singing with fear: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all the ages!

Thou art the most fair King of those who reign, and the Lord ruling everywhere, mighty, blessed, and dwelling in unapproachable light; at Whom the disciples marveled, and Moses and Elijah cried: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all the ages!

As unto Him Who rules heaven, and exercises dominion over the earth, and has authority over the nethermost regions, O Christ, there stood before Thee: from the earth the Apostles; and as from heaven the Tishbite Elijah; and Moses from the dead, singing in unison: Ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all the ages!

Having left upon earth the sorrows that beget despondency, the chosen of the Apostles, O Lover of mankind, followed Thee unto the divine translation of life away from the earth; therefore also having obtained Thy divine epiphany according to their worthiness, they sang: Ye people, supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*At Ode 9, we do not sing "More honorable":*[^167]

...we sing, even if on Sunday. But we sing the refrain of the feast:[^168]

"Magnify, O my soul, the Lord Who was transfigured on Tabor."

*Then the Heirmos: "Thy childbearing was revealed incorrupt..." After this, the second choir sings the same refrain and the Heirmos; and to the second canon they sing the same refrain.*

*Ode 9. Heirmos [Canon 1, Tone 4]:*[^169]

Thy childbearing was revealed incorrupt: God came forth from thy womb, and as One bearing flesh appeared upon the earth, and dwelt with men; therefore, O Theotokos, all we magnify thee.

*Troparia:*

Terrified by the strange downpouring of light, suddenly enlightened, the disciples looked one upon another; and being filled with wonder, and bowing down to the earth, they worshipped Thee, the Master of all.

A sound from the cloud was sent forth, sounding of God, confirming the wonder: for the Father of lights cried aloud unto the Apostles: "This is My beloved Son; hear ye Him!"

Having seen things new and most glorious, and having heard the Father's voice, the servants of the Word upon Tabor, crying aloud: "This is the express Image of the Prototype, our Savior!"

O unchangeable Image of Him Who is, immovable, immutable Seal, O Son, Word, Wisdom, and Arm, Right Hand, and Power of the Most High, Thee do we hymn together with the Father and the Spirit.

*Another Heirmos [Canon 2, Tone 8]:*[^170]

Every hearing was struck with awe at the ineffable condescension of God, how the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, being made man from the virginal womb; therefore, O all-pure Theotokos, we the faithful magnify thee.

*Troparia:*

That Thou mightest show clearly Thine ineffable second coming, that as the Most High God, appearing standing in the midst of gods, the Apostles, on Tabor, Thou didst ineffably illumine Moses and Elijah; therefore, O Christ, all we magnify Thee.

Come, O ye peoples, be obedient unto me: having ascended into the holy and supercelestial mountain, let us immaterially stand in the city of the living God, and let us noetically behold the immaterial Godhead of the Father and of the Spirit, flashing forth in the Only-begotten Son.

Thou hast drawn me with love, O Savior, and transformed me by Thy divine desire; but burn up my sins with immaterial fire, and count me worthy to be filled with Thy delight, that leaping with joy for both, I may magnify Thy majesties, O Good One!

*Exapostilarion:*[^171]

O Word, unchangeable Light of the Light of the unbegotten Father, in Thy manifested light today on Tabor we have seen the light of the Father, and the light of the Spirit, guiding all creation by light.

*At the Praises, let every breath praise the Lord. We set 4 stichoi. Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on High" (Званный свыше):*[^172]

Before Thy precious Cross and Passion, having taken those whom Thou didst choose beforehand from among the sacred disciples, Thou didst go up, O Master, unto Mount Tabor, desiring to show unto them Thy glory. And having seen Thee transfigured, and shining more brightly than the sun, they fell prone, marveling at Thy power and crying aloud: "Thou art the timeless Light, O Christ, and the Radiance of the Father, even though willingly Thou hast appeared in the flesh unchangeably!" *(Twice)*

Thou Who art God the Word before the ages, Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, when Thou wast transfigured before Thy disciples, Thou didst shine, O Word, brighter than the sun; and Moses and Elijah stood before Thee, proclaiming Thee to be Lord over the dead and the living, and glorifying Thine ineffable dispensation, and mercy, and great compassion, whereby Thou hast saved the world which was perishing in sin.

When she who is from a virginal cloud had given birth unto Thee Who becamest flesh, and when Thou wast transfigured upon Mount Tabor, O Lord, and surrounded by a bright cloud, the voice of the Father clearly declared Thee to be the beloved Son unto the disciples who stood before Thee, as consubstantial and co-enthroned; therefore Peter, filled with wonder, said: "It is good for us to be here," not knowing what he said, O Benefactor plenteous in mercy!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 8. By Byzantios:*[^173]

Christ took Peter, James, and John into a high mountain apart, and was transfigured before them; and His face shone as the sun, and His garments became white as the light. And there appeared Moses and Elijah talking with Him, and a bright cloud overshadowed them. And behold, a voice out of the cloud, saying: "This is My beloved Son, in Whom I am well pleased; hear ye Him!"

*The Great Doxology, and the Dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy, the 1st Antiphon, Tone 2:*[^174]

*Verse 1. Psalm 65:* Shout with jubilation unto the Lord, all the earth; chant now unto His Name, give glory unto His praise. Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Again the second choir [sings] the same verse: Shout with jubilation unto the Lord... Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.*

*And again the first choir, Verse 2. Psalm 76:* The voice of Thy thunder was in the wheel; Thy lightnings illumined the world; the earth was moved and trembled. Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Then the second choir, Verse 3. Psalm 103:* Thou hast clothed Thyself with thanksgiving and majesty, Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment. Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.

*Glory... Both now... Through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Savior, save us.*

*The 2nd Antiphon. Psalm 47. Verse 1:*[^175] The mountains of Zion, on the slopes of the north, the city of the great King. Save us, O Son of God, Who wast transfigured on the mountain, who sing unto Thee: Alleluia.

*The other choir the same. Psalm 77, Verse 2:* And He brought them unto the mountain of His sanctuary, this mountain which His right hand acquired. Save us, O Son of God, Who wast transfigured on the mountain...

*Verse 3:* The mountain of Zion which He loved; and He built His sanctuary as of a unicorn. Save us, O Son of God, Who wast transfigured on the mountain...

*Glory... Both now... Only-begotten Son and Word...*

*The 3rd Antiphon. Psalm 124, Tone 7. Verse 1:*[^176] They who trust in the Lord shall be as Mount Zion; he who dwells in Jerusalem shall never be shaken.

*Troparion, Tone 7:* Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, O Christ God, showing Thy glory unto Thy disciples as far as they were able: let Thine everlasting light shine also upon us sinners, through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Giver of light, glory to Thee!

*Verse 2:* Mountains are round about it, and the Lord is round about His people from henceforth and unto the ages.

*Troparion:* Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain...

*Psalm 14, Verse 3:* Lord, who shall abide in Thy tabernacle, or who shall dwell in Thy holy mountain?

*Troparion:* Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain...

*Psalm 23, Verse 4:* Who shall ascend into the mountain of the Lord, or who shall stand in His holy place?

*Troparion:* Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain...

*The Entrance Verse:*[^177] Lord, send forth Thy light and Thy truth; they have guided me and brought me unto Thy holy mountain.

*Troparion:* Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain...

*Glory... Both now... Kontakion, the same tone [Tone 7]:*[^178] Upon the mountain wast Thou transfigured, and as far as they were able, Thy disciples beheld Thy glory, O Christ God; that when they should see Thee crucified, they might understand Thy Passion to be voluntary, and might proclaim unto the world that Thou art truly the Radiance of the Father.

*The Trisagion. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*[^179] How magnified are Thy works, O Lord! In wisdom hast Thou made them all.

*Verse:* Bless the Lord, O my soul! O Lord my God, Thou hast been magnified exceedingly.

*Epistle from the Epistle of Peter, section 205 [2 Peter 1:10–19]. Alleluia.*[^180]

*Alleluia, Tone 8:* The heavens are Thine, and the earth is Thine.[^181]

*Verse:* Blessed is the people that knows the joyful shout.

*Gospel of Matthew, pericope 70 [Matthew 17:1–9].*[^182]

*Instead of "It is truly meet," the Heirmos:* "Thy childbearing was revealed incorrupt..." *And until the Apodosis.*

*Communion Verse:* O Lord, in the light of Thy face shall we walk, and in Thy Name shall we rejoice forever.[^183]

*At the trapeza we have dispensation for fish, oil, and wine, even if Wednesday or Friday.*

*It is fitting to know: That we have a tradition from the holy Fathers, from this saving feast of the Transfiguration, to eat grapes wherever they are found, having received a blessing from the priest.*

*The grapes having been brought into the church, and after the Anaphora broken into pieces; this prayer is said at the blessing of grapes, by the hierarch or by the priest:*

O God our Savior, Who wast pleased to call Thine Only-begotten Son, our Lord God and Savior Jesus Christ, the Vine, and through Him didst grant us the fruit of immortality: do Thou Thyself now also bless this fruited vine, and make us Thy servants who partake thereof partakers of the true Vine; preserve our life unharmed, and grant us peace evermore, and adorn our life with Thine eternal, inalienable gifts: through the prayers of our all-pure Lady, the Theotokos and Ever-virgin Mary, and of all Thy saints who from the ages have been well-pleasing unto Thee. For Thou art a good God and the Lover of mankind, and unto Thee do we send up glory, to the unoriginate Father, with Thine Only-begotten Son, and Thine all-holy and good and life-creating Spirit, now and ever, and unto ages of ages. Amen.

*Take heed: That if any of the brethren eat grapes before such a blessing, let him receive the penance of disobedience, and let him not taste grapes throughout the whole month of August, as having despised the ordained rule, that from this also the rest may learn to obey the rule of the holy Fathers. And let this penance be also upon the brethren who guard the vineyards. And grapes at the table after this feast ought to be set before the brethren thrice a week, namely on Monday, on Wednesday, and on Friday, that the brethren may partake. This rule applies also to figs, and over other fruits, wherever their seasons come according to their times. And these things we say besides those brought to those who guard the figs and distributed, for they eat them.*

*In the same month, on the 7th day: The Holy Venerable-martyr Dometius.*[^184]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, 3, Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on High" (Званный свыше):*[^185]

Before Thy precious Cross and Passion, having taken those whom Thou didst choose beforehand from among the sacred disciples, Thou didst go up, O Master, unto Mount Tabor, desiring to show unto them Thy glory. And having seen Thee transfigured, and shining more brightly than the sun, they fell prone, marveling at Thy power and crying aloud: "Thou art the timeless Light, O Christ, and the Radiance of the Father, even though willingly Thou hast appeared in the flesh unchangeably!"

Thou Who art God the Word before the ages, Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, when Thou wast transfigured before Thy disciples, Thou didst shine, O Word, brighter than the sun; and Moses and Elijah stood before Thee, proclaiming Thee to be Lord over the dead and the living, and glorifying Thine ineffable dispensation, and mercy, and great compassion, whereby Thou hast saved the world which was perishing in sin.

When she who is from a virginal cloud had given birth unto Thee Who becamest flesh, and when Thou wast transfigured upon Mount Tabor, O Lord, and surrounded by a bright cloud, the voice of the Father clearly declared Thee to be the beloved Son unto the disciples who stood before Thee, as consubstantial and co-enthroned; therefore Peter, filled with wonder, said: "It is good for us to be here," not knowing what he said, O Benefactor plenteous in mercy!

*Other Stichera of the Saint, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all" (Все отложше):*[^186]

Having laid aside all sorcerous incantation, and Persian defilement, and abominable services, thou didst run unto God Who governs mankind with wise providence; in Whose Name thou didst heal apostolically the infirmities of brute beasts and the diseases of men, of those who eagerly ran unto thee, O favorite of Christ; Whom do thou boldly entreat for our souls!

Thou didst commit thy whole life unto the Lord, and didst ardently separate thyself unto the ascetic course, O venerable one: showing forth all-night vigil of steadfast prayers, unceasing singing, and blameless, angelic life; hence by the operation of wonders thou wast enriched thou wast enriched, for the Lord knows how to glorify His servants; Whom do thou boldly entreat for our souls!

Having clothed thyself completely with the whole armor of the Cross, and girded thyself with the unbreakable shield of faith, thou wast terrible unto the hostile demoniac host, which also thou didst drive away, having anointed thyself with the wound of faith, and by divine invocations; and those journeying on the way thou didst save from their harm, O Dometius, favorite of Christ; Whom do thou boldly entreat for our souls!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*[^187]

Prefiguring Thy Resurrection, O Christ God, then taking Thy three disciples, Peter and James and John, Thou didst ascend Mount Tabor. And as Thou wast transfigured, O Savior, Mount Tabor was covered with light; Thy disciples, O Word, threw themselves down upon the earth, unable to gaze upon the invisible form. Angels ministered with fear and trembling; the heavens were afraid, the earth trembled, beholding upon earth the Lord of glory.

*Entrance. Prokeimenon, Tone 7:*[^188]

Our God is in heaven and on earth; all things whatsoever He pleased, He hath done.

*Verse:* In the going forth of Israel out of Egypt, the house of Jacob from a barbarous people, Judea became His sanctuary, Israel His dominion.

*Verse:* The sea beheld it and fled; Jordan was turned back.

*Verse:* What ailed thee, O sea, that thou fleddest? And thou Jordan, that thou wast turned back?

*Then the litany.*

*If this feast happen on Saturday: On Friday evening we sing the Great Prokeimenon: "Our God is in heaven and on earth," with its verses. On Saturday evening of the feast, we sing the Prokeimenon: "The Lord is King." If this feast happen on Sunday: We sing on Saturday evening the Prokeimenon: "The Lord is King," with its verses; and on Sunday evening we sing the Great Prokeimenon: "Our God is in heaven and on earth," with its verses.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having triumphed on the third day" (Тридневенъ):*[^189]

Having condescended in compassions, O Christ, unto Adam the first-formed man from the Virgin, Thou wast revealed as the Second Adam; and upon Mount Tabor, O my Savior, Thou wast transfigured, laying bare Thy Godhead.

*Verse: The heavens are Thine, and the earth is Thine.*

The chosen of the Law, beholding the grace of Thy Transfiguration, O Christ, on the holy mountain, were filled with wonder; with whom we worship Thee, with the Father and the Divine Spirit.

*Verse: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name.*

Creation is clearly illuminated by Thy Transfiguration, O Christ, which Thou, being God, didst show unto the divine Apostles on Tabor, and unto Moses and Elijah, shining ineffably brighter than the sun.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 5:*[^190]

Come, let us ascend into the mountain of the Lord, and into the house of our God, and we shall behold the glory of His Transfiguration, the glory as of the Only-begotten of the Father. By light let us receive light; and being lifted up by the Spirit, let us hymn the consubstantial Trinity unto the ages!

*Troparion [of the Venerable-martyr], Tone 4:*[^191]

Having previously contended ascetically on the mountain, thou didst destroy the spiritual hosts of the enemies with the whole armor of the Cross, O all-blessed one; and again thou didst bravely clothe thyself for martyrdom, slaying Copronymus with the sword of faith: and for the sake of both thou wast crowned by God, O venerable-martyr Dometius ever-memorable!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 7:*[^192]

Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, O Christ God, showing Thy glory unto Thy disciples as far as they were able: let Thine everlasting light shine also upon us sinners, through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Giver of light, glory to Thee!

*At Compline, the Kontakion of the Feast, and until the Apodosis.*

*At Matins, after the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Явился еси днесь):*[^193]

The all-bright feast of the Master is come: come, all ye, having cleansed ourselves beforehand, let us noetically ascend Mount Tabor to see Christ.

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оудивися іѡсифъ):*[^194]

Upon Mount Tabor wast Thou transfigured, O God, in the midst of the most wise Elijah and Moses, with James and Simon and John. And Peter, being there, said these things unto Thee: "It is good for us to make here three tabernacles, one for Moses, and one for Elijah, and one for Thee, Master Christ." O Thou Who didst then cause Thy light to shine upon them, illuminate our souls!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos to 8, by Kyr Cosmas, Tone 4:*[^195]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

The choruses of Israel with undrenched feet, having passed through the Red Sea and the watery deep, seeing the chariot-commanding enemies drowned therein, sang with rejoicing: Let us sing unto our God, for He hath been glorified!

*Troparia:*

Speaking words of life unto His friends, and teaching concerning the divine Kingdom, Christ said: "In Me acknowledge the Father, as I flash forth with unapproachable light, singing in joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He hath been glorified!"

"Ye shall be clothed with the power of the Gentiles, O My friend-disciples, and ye shall be marvelous in their wealth, for ye shall be filled with glory; for I shall appear flashing more brightly than the sun, of those singing in joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He hath been glorified!"

Today Christ on Mount Tabor, having shone clearly with divine radiance as He promised, laid bare His form unto the disciples; and being filled with the light-bearing divine radiance, they sang in joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He hath been glorified!

*Canon of the Saint, to 4. The work of Theophanes, Tone 6:*[^196]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

As Israel journeyed on foot through the deep as on dry land, seeing the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, he cried aloud: Let us sing unto God a song of victory!

*Troparia:*

Having departed unto God by thy martyric grace, breathe a light-bearing radiance upon those who faithfully hymn thee, praying unto Christ, O divinely-blessed one.

Having broken the abominations of deception, thou didst truly run unto Christ: having left Persian services with an alien mind, and escaped sorcerous godlessness.

Skillfully didst thou observe that the created sun is not God; but from visible things thou wast guided toward the invisible God above, having armed thyself with wise counsel.

*Theotokion:*

Thee, O all-holy Virgin, who didst bear the Son of God, Who became like unto us for our sake: we the faithful proclaim as the pure Mother of God, and we bless thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos [Canon of the Feast, Tone 4]:*[^197]

The bow of the mighty hath grown weak, and the weak have girded themselves with strength; therefore my heart is established in the Lord.

*Troparia:*

Having clothed Thyself with whole Adam, O Christ, changing the nature that was darkened of old, Thou hast illuminated it, and by the changing of Thy form Thou hast divinized it.

Of old leading Israel in the wilderness by a pillar of fire and a cloud, today on Mount Tabor Christ hath shone ineffably in the light.

*Another Heirmos [Canon of the Saint, Tone 6]:*[^198]

There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparia:*

Being established in sure faith as an unbending pillar, thou wast revealed immutable, overcome neither by sorcerous words nor by the reason of Persian wandering.

Thou didst flee the soul-corrupting poison of serving creation and honoring the sun; but with love of piety thou didst seek Him Who is truly God, as the Creator of all the Creator of all, with love of piety thou didst seek.

Partaking of the divine waters of regeneration, thou didst illuminate thy soul; and thou wast truly a son of the grace of God, an heir of divine enjoyments.

*Theotokion:*

Wisdom built for Herself a divine house above mind and word, to dwell in thy pure womb, which, O all-immaculate one, she honorably cleansed by the Spirit.

*Kontakion [of the Venerable-martyr], Tone 6:*[^199]

As one who didst despise corruptible things and counsels that drag downward, O venerable-martyr Dometius, thou wast revealed as a great guide unto monks; not having feared the wrath of the emperor who was unwilling to honor the image of Christ. Therefore also thou didst finish thy course, singing the hymn: "God is with me, and no one is against me!"

*Sessional Hymn [of the Saint], Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премудрости):*[^200]

Having left the Persian service, thou didst come unto the Good One, O glorious one, enlightened in mind by the bath of baptism; and having lived monastically in a venerable manner, by fasting thou didst mortify the fleshly passions, O divinely-wise Dometius. Therefore having suffered and conquered deception, thou didst receive a twofold crown of victory. Therefore we cry unto thee: Entreat Christ God to grant forgiveness of sins unto those who honor thy holy memory with love!

*Glory... Both now... the same tone [Tone 8], Podoben: "That which was commanded" (Повеленное):*[^201]

The hidden lightning of Thine essence, O Christ, and of divine majesty beneath the flesh, didst Thou show upon the holy mountain, O Benefactor, shining upon the disciples who were with Thee. And understanding Thy glory to be unbearable, they cried out: "Holy art Thou, for being unapproachable, Thou hast been seen in the flesh unto the world, O only Lover of mankind!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos [Canon of the Feast, Tone 4]:*[^202]

I heard of Thy glorious dispensation, O Christ God, that Thou wast born of the Virgin, that Thou mightest deliver from deception those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*

Depicting the Law in Sinai by writing, O Christ God, Thou wast revealed borne in the cloud, and fire, and gloom, and in the tempest: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

That Thou mightest assure Thy glorious dispensation, O Christ God, that Thou art He Who is before the ages, and the same Who didst set the clouds for His ascent, Thou didst ineffably shine upon Tabor.

Talking together, they stood before Thee in a servantlike manner, O Master Christ, unto whom with vapor of fire, and gloom, and subtle coolness Thou hadst conversed: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Those who came upon Tabor proclaimed the glorious things for the sake of Thy Cross: Moses who of old foresaw Thee in the fire and the bush, and Elijah who was taken up in the chariot of fire, O Christ.

*Another Heirmos [Canon of the Saint, Tone 6]:*[^203]

Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church sings in a manner befitting God, crying aloud from a pure mind, keeping festival in the Lord.

*Troparia:*

Thou didst recognize Him Who is unknown by nature, O glorious one, reasonably seeking Him; and having found Him, thou didst desire Him, and didst worship, rejoicing at His majesty.

Ardently didst thou run unto the chaste company of holy monks; and being inflamed with zeal for virtues, thou didst live purely in fasts and prayers.

Easily did thy divinely-wise desire loose the bonds of nature, and quench the furnace of passions, by the dew-giving enlightenment of the Holy Spirit, O father.

*Theotokion:*

My strength is God and the Lord, O Virgin Theotokos; the Word Who ran before, being incarnate from thine incorrupt womb, hath granted us life.

*Ode 5. Heirmos [Canon of the Feast, Tone 4]:*[^204]

O Christ the Creator, in Whose light the works sing of Thee as in light, the everlasting, first-created Light: guide our paths in Thy light!

*Troparia:*

Before Thy face the mountains bowed down; for the Light, and the unwaning rays before Thy feet, O Christ the Sun, came, since Thou wast well pleased to assume an earthly form.

"Behold the Savior!" cried Moses and Elijah unto the disciples who were instructed on holy Mount Tabor: "Christ, Whom of old we proclaimed to be the existing God!"

The immutable nature, mingled with the human, having abundantly laid bare the light of the immaterial Godhead unto the Apostles, ineffably shone forth.

When Thy disciples beheld Thee, the everlasting Radiance, shining in the Father's glory, O Christ, they cried unto Thee: In Thy light guide our paths!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of the Saint, Tone 6]:*[^205]

With Thy divine light, O Good One, illuminate the souls of those who lovingly rise early unto Thee, I pray, to know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, calling us forth from the darkness of sin.

*Troparia:*

Shining with divine understanding, O blessed one, thou didst discern all visible things, being illuminated by the radiance of purity of everlasting invisible things.

Having tasted of divine virtues, stillness and solitude, thou didst establish an untroubled mind, and the ordering of the shrine, O father Dometius.

Truly great and marvelous was thy turning toward what is better, and thy all-wise understanding: for it was the changing of the right hand of the Most High, and the work of divine grace.

*Theotokion:*

Thou didst remain a Virgin without harm, and showest forth the maternal nature, O all-immaculate Lady; for thou hast united virginity with childbearing, and dost bear the properties of both.

*Ode 6. Heirmos [Canon of the Feast, Tone 4]:*[^206]

When I was in affliction, I cried unto the Lord, and the God of my salvation heard me.

*Troparia:*

A light brighter than the shining of the sun, the Savior, having shone forth upon Tabor, hath illuminated us.

Ascending Mount Tabor, Thou wast transfigured, O Christ, and having darkened all deceit, Thou hast illuminated us.

Knowing Thee to be God, the glorious Apostles were filled with wonder on Tabor, O Christ, bowing their knees.

*Another Heirmos [Canon of the Saint, Tone 6]:*[^207]

Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O plenteous in mercy!

*Troparia:*

Steadfastly didst thou endure the assault of the enemy and adversary's temptation; and being variously tempted, O all-blessed one, and assailed by envy, thou wast revealed as victor, O worthy of glory.

Thy life was divine, a God-like and light-bearing manner of living; for having forsaken the fair things of the world, thou didst bring forth fruit of divine splendor and beauty, O divinely-wise one.

Thou wast accounted worthy to work signs and wonders beyond nature, O glorious one, being armed with divine power: having grace from on high working together with thee, O worthy of glory.

*Theotokion:*

Dwelling of Light, and bridal chamber befitting God beyond mind of the Incarnation of the Creator of all: for thou alone hast been revealed, an all-blessed resting place for Him.

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 3, Automelon:*[^208]

Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, and as far as they were able, Thy disciples beheld Thy glory, O Christ God; that when they should see Thee crucified, they might understand Thy Passion to be voluntary, and proclaim unto the world: that Thou art truly the Father's Radiance!

*Ikos:*[^209]

Arise, ye slothful, who ever bow down to the earth; thoughts of my soul, be lifted up and ascend to the height of the divine ascent! Let us hasten unto Peter and the sons of Zebedee and together with them let us reach Mount Tabor, that with them we may behold the glory of our God; and let us hear the voice which they heard from on high, and they proclaimed the Father's Radiance.

*Ode 7. Heirmos [Canon of the Feast, Tone 4]:*[^210]

The Abrahamic Children of old in Babylon trampled the furnace flame, blessing with songs: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*

Shining with the light of unapproachable glory upon Mount Tabor, the Apostles cried unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Delighting in the shining of the divine sound, and the dew-yielding cloud, O Christ, and in Thy radiance, the Apostles sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

In unapproachable light, when Peter saw Thee shining upon Mount Tabor, O Christ, he cried aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Being with Christ the Author of life, the children of Zebedee, as the light of His countenance flashed forth, thundered: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of the Saint, Tone 6]:*[^211]

The angel made the furnace yield dew for the venerable Children, while the burning command of God consumed the Chaldeans, persuading the tyrant to cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*

Upon thee, O father, the all-bright grace of the Spirit rested, showing forth marvelous operations in thee, who faithfully criest and singest: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

With a steadfast mind didst thou run the path of fasting, and at the end of thy contests thou wast a true martyr, crying unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

As children begotten by thy prayers, O father, they contended with thee, and enduring in the cave they suffered with thee, crying with one accord unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*

Having acquired thee as a haven of salvation, we are saved from the storm; and holding hope in thee as the foundation of our souls, we cry unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos [Canon of the Feast, Tone 4]:*[^212]

In Babylon, the Children, inflamed with divine zeal, manfully trampled the threat of the tyrant and the flame; and cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed, they sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparia:*

Bearing all things by His nod, with most pure feet Christ ascended Mount Tabor, whereon brighter than the rays of the sun He shone He shone in His countenance: which He showed unto the elders of the Law and of grace, as they sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

In unapproachable glory, uncontainable and unwaning light, the Father's Radiance, ineffably appeared upon Mount Tabor; having illuminated creation and divinized men, unto those who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

Standing in sacred splendor upon Mount Tabor, Moses and Elijah, clearly beholding the Hypostasis of the divine imprint, Christ shining in the Father's glory, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

He whose face of old was glorified by the appearance in the divine form was Moses; but Christ is clothed with light and glory as with a garment: for being Himself the Creator of light, He illuminates those who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

The disciples, beholding Christ enveloped by a light-bearing cloud on Tabor, fell prone upon the earth; and having their minds illuminated, with the Father and the Spirit they sang unto Him: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of the Saint, Tone 6]:*[^213]

From the flame Thou didst pour out dew for the venerable ones, and didst consume the sacrifice of the righteous one with water: for Thou dost all things, O Christ, merely by willing; Thee do we highly exalt unto all ages!

*Troparia:*

Consumed with love for the Creator, O father, thou didst cast away all attachment to created things, being clearly guided by the Spirit of God: Whom highly exalt unto all ages!

Having shaken off earthly cares, with heavenly hopes, O father, thou wast enriched, and with the blessedness in Christ: wherewith thou now delightest thyself unto all ages!

Having received the radiant dawn of the Holy and Divine Spirit, O all-wise father, with thine eyes thou didst clearly see Him, O blessed one, hoping in thee as the steward of divine mysteries.

*Theotokion:*

The fruitful garden of the Godhead of the prophets, the holy ark foreshadowed thee, who bearest God clothed in flesh: Whom we highly exalt unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos [Canon of the Feast, Tone 4]:*[^214]

Thy childbearing was revealed incorrupt: God came forth from thy side, as a bearer of flesh He appeared upon the earth, and dwelt with men. Therefore, O Theotokos, thee do we all magnify.

*Troparia:*

Dismayed by the novel shedding of light, the disciples, suddenly illuminated, gazed at one another: and marveling, and bowing down to the earth, they worshiped Thee, the Master of all.

A voice of God was sent forth from the cloud, confirming the wonder the wonder: for the Father of lights cried aloud unto the Apostles: "This is My beloved Son; hear ye Him!"

Beholding new and all-glorious things, and giving ear to the Father's voice, the servants of the Word upon Mount Tabor, seeing the image of the Archetype, cried out: "This is our Savior!"

O immutable Image of Him Who is, O unshakeable, unchangeable Seal, O Son, Word, Wisdom, and Arm, Right Hand, and Power of the Most High: Thee do we hymn, with the Father and the Spirit!

*Another Heirmos [Canon of the Saint, Tone 6]:*[^215]

It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the ranks of angels dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the Word incarnate was revealed unto men: Whom magnifying, with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.

*Troparia:*

Illuminated by immaterial light, thou wast worthily God-bearing, O father, having acquired a blameless and upright life; and now thou standest before the Master and God, an intercessor for thy flock.

Thy struggles of abstinence were worthily crowned with martyric contests; and thou didst show forth a twofold suffering by good courage, which thou didst receive, and hast found the enjoyment of eternal good things.

Strengthened by divine power and grace, thou wast revealed unto all as a teacher of piety, shown forth adorned with wonders: establishing all, and healing all who faithfully come unto thee, O divinely-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*

Concerning thee, O Virgin, a mystery was wrought above man and beyond nature: for thou wast the Mother of God, bearing in thine arms and nursing Him Who is hymned by the heavenly hosts.

*Exapostilarion of the Feast:*[^216]

O immutable Light, Word, of the unbegotten Light the Father: in Thy revealed light, today on Tabor we have seen the light of the Father, and the light of the Spirit, guiding all creation by light. *Glory... Both now... the same.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "The Angelic Powers" (А҆́гг҃льскїѧ):*[^217]

Having shone forth beyond mind from the all-pure Virgin, O Word of God, clothed wholly in Adam, Thou hast illuminated all of nature which was previously darkened; having been transfigured by the divine change of Thy form upon Mount Tabor, O Master, before Thy precious Cross, O Christ our God.

*Verse: The heavens are Thine, and the earth is Thine [Ps. 88:12].*

Beholding Christ enveloped by a light-bearing cloud upon Mount Tabor, the divine disciples falling prone with fear upon the earth, and having their mind and heart illuminated, with Moses and Elijah clearly beholding, sang of Him as God, with the Father and the Spirit.

*Verse: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name [Ps. 88:13].*

The mountain which of old was dark and smoky, behold now is honorable and holy: for He Who formerly guided Israel as it is written, by a pillar of fire and a cloud, today Christ hath ineffably shone brighter than the sun thereon, having illuminated human nature as God.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 2:*[^218]

Moses the beholder of God, and Elijah who rode the chariot of fire and traversed heaven unconsumed, beholding Thee in the cloud at Thy Transfiguration, O Christ, testified that Thou art the Author and Fulfiller of the Law and the Prophets; with whom also account us worthy of Thine illumination, O Master, to hymn Thee unto the ages.

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 4 of both canons, with the Heirmos, to 6. Prokeimenon of the Feast; and of the Saint, Tone 7: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones [Ps. 115:6]. Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He hath rendered unto me? [Ps. 115:3]. Epistle to the Ephesians, pericope 233 [Eph. 6:10-17]. Alleluia, Tone 4: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree [Ps. 91:13]. Gospel of John, pericope 52 [Jn. 15:17-16:2]. Communion Verse of the Feast; and of the Saint: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be [Ps. 111:6].*[^219]

*Take heed: If the Afterfeast of Christ's Transfiguration happen on Saturday: Then on Friday at "Lord, I have cried," Glory, of the Feast; Both now, the 1st Theotokion of the current Tone.*

*If the Afterfeast of Christ's Transfiguration happen on Sunday:*[^220]

*On Saturday at Little Vespers, Resurrectional Stichera and of the Theotokos, according to custom. At Great Vespers, after the customary Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and 1 Anatolian; and of the Feast, 3, of that day; and of the Saint from the Menaion, 3. (Take heed: If the Saint be sung to 6, or if he have a Polyeleos: we sing Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and of the Feast, 3; and of the Saint, 4; Glory, of the Saint.) But if not: Glory, of the Feast; Both now, the 1st Theotokion of the Tone. At the Litiya, Stichera of the Feast, the regular Stichera of that day at the Aposticha; Glory... Both now... of the Feast, written at Matins at the Aposticha. But if the Saint have a Doxastikon: Glory, of the Saint; Both now, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Stichera; Glory, of the Saint, if there be one; Both now, of the Feast. But if not: Glory... Both now of the Feast. At the blessing of the loaves: Troparion: "O Virgin Theotokos," twice; and of the Feast, once.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Resurrectional Troparion, twice; Glory, of the Saint, if there be one; Both now, of the Feast. But if not: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. After the Kathismata, Resurrectional Sessional Hymns, with their Theotokia; and the reading in the Commentary Gospel; and the Blameless [Kathisma 17] with the Troparia. Hypakoe of the Tone. Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], and the Prokeimenon of the Tone; and "Let every breath"; Resurrectional Gospel, and the Resurrectional Sticheron. Canons: Resurrectional with the Heirmos, to 4; and of the Theotokos, to 2; and of the Feast, to 4; and of the Saint, to 4. Katavasia: the choirs together. (If there be a Polyeleos or Great Doxology: we sing the Resurrectional Canon with the Heirmos, to 4; and of the Feast, to 4; and of the Saint, to 6.) After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Feast, and of the Saint, if he have one; and the Ikos, and the Sessional Hymn of the Saint; Glory... Both now... of the Feast; and the reading.*[^221]

*After Ode 6, Resurrectional Kontakion, and Ikos; and the Prologue. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable." Resurrectional Exapostilarion; Glory, of the Saint, if he have one; but if not: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera, 4; and of the Saint, if he have any, prosomoia, 3; and the automelon which is at Glory: we sing with his verses. But if the Saint have no Praises: we sing Resurrectional, 4; and of the Feast, 4: of that day at the Aposticha of Matins, with the verses of the Feast; Glory, the Evangelical Sticheron; Both now: "Most blessed art thou." Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, the Resurrectional Troparion only. Litanies, and the dismissal. And catechism, and the customary procession into the narthex. Then the First Hour. At the First Hour, Resurrectional Troparion; Glory, of the Feast; Both now, the Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," Kontakion of the Feast. Likewise in the other Hours we say the Kontakion of the Feast, alternating with the Resurrectional. If there be a Polyeleos, of the Saint.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Tone, to 6; and of the Feast, the regular Ode, to 4. (If the Saint have an Ode: then the Beatitudes: Resurrectional, to 4; and of the Feast, the regular Ode, to 4; and of the Saint, Ode 6, to 4.) At the Entrance: Troparion: Resurrectional, and of the Feast; then of the Temple; and of the Saint, if he have one. Glory, Kontakion of the Saint; Both now, of the Feast. (Take heed: If the Saint have no Troparion and Kontakion: Glory, Resurrectional; Both now, of the Feast.) Prokeimenon: Resurrectional of the Tone, and of the Feast; Epistle, and Gospel of the day.*[^222]

*(Take heed: If the Saint have an Epistle and Gospel: then we sing the Prokeimenon: Resurrectional of the Tone, and of the Saint; Epistle and Gospel of the day, and of the Saint.) Communion Verse: "Praise the Lord from the heavens" [Ps. 148:1]; and of the Feast; and of the Saint.*

*In the same month, on the 8th day: The Holy Emilian the Confessor, Bishop of Cyzicus.*[^223]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^224]

Come, ye divinely-wise, and with a goodly change being changed today, being transfigured together with Christ, piously and joyfully, having ascended to the height of virtues, let us rejoice exceedingly! For through compassion, having transformed the deformity of man, upon Tabor the Savior of our souls made it radiant.

As eager beholders and obedient hearers of things beyond mind, let us noetically behold Christ, shining with divine rays; and let us give ear to the Father's voice, proclaiming the beloved Son, Who strengthened human weakness upon Tabor, and poured forth the enlightenment of our souls.

Today Thou hast shown forth Thy Godhead, O Lord, unto Thy venerable Apostles, and unto Moses and Elijah, as being the God of the Law and of grace: delivering us now from the bonds of death, with whom we glorify Thy dispensation of love for mankind, O all-powerful Jesus, Savior of our souls!

*Other Stichera of the Saint, the same tone [Tone 4], Podoben: "Called from on High" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*[^225]

Clearly honoring the image of Christ, when Leo's savagery raged and agitated the people, renewing the godless heresy of Copronymus, then, O all-wise one, thou didst openly rebuke him, taking up thy boldness when the assembly of hierarchs arrived. Therefore thou wast revealed as a most valiant soldier of Christ, O all-blessed one: Whom do thou entreat to save and enlighten our souls!

Thou didst make thy dwelling in the heavenly mansions, when the adversary's godless and impious disposition condemned thy steadfastness to exile; then rejoicing, whence we have not fallen, clearly knowing the one Fatherly divine confession, with gladness and joy thou didst endure. Therefore rightly wast thou accounted worthy to behold the incorrupt life, Christ: Whom do thou entreat to save and enlighten our souls!

Possessing a fiery tongue most wisely, when the wretched Leo laid down a confused foreign law, commanding not to bow down before Christ's precious icon, then didst thou set down the divine law against him, that the veneration of the icon passes, as someone once said, unto the Prototype; and by thy word, as if by thunder, that beast of evil name was frightened, O all-honorable father, divinely-speaking sacred hierarch!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 4:*[^226]

He Who of old conversed with Moses on Mount Sinai in types, saying: "I AM THAT I AM": today being transfigured upon Mount Tabor before the disciples, showed forth the primal beauty of His form, having taken human nature upon Himself; and having set Moses and Elijah as witnesses of such grace, He made them partakers of joy, prefiguring the glorious things wrought for the sake of the Cross, and the saving Resurrection.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^227]

Mount Tabor, the mountain of God, rejoices in Thy Name, O Savior, proclaiming Thee to be the beloved Son unto the Father.

*Verse: The heavens are Thine, and the earth is Thine [Ps. 88:12].*

A wonder it was to behold the Sun of glory, in His Transfiguration, hiding the rays of the sensible sun!

*Verse: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name [Ps. 88:13].*

The disciples being from the earth, and Moses from the dead, and Elijah as from on high, stood before Thee, Who wast transfigured, in a servantlike manner.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 4:*[^228]

David the ancestor of God, foreseeing in spirit the coming in the flesh of Thine Only-begotten Son unto men, from afar summons creation unto joy, and prophetically cries aloud: "Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name!" For having ascended this mountain, O Savior, with Thy disciples, by Thy Transfiguration Thou hast made the blackened nature of Adam to shine again, transforming it into the glory and splendor of Thy Godhead. Therefore we cry unto Thee: Creator of all things, O Lord, glory to Thee!

*Troparion of the Feast, Tone 7:*[^229]

Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, O Christ God, showing Thy glory unto Thy disciples as far as they were able: let Thine everlasting light shine also upon us sinners, through the prayers of the Theotokos, O Giver of light, glory to Thee!

*At Matins, after the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^230]

Upon Mount Tabor wast Thou transfigured, O Jesus, and a bright cloud spreading forth as a canopy covered the Apostles with glory; therefore they fell prone upon the earth, unable to gaze upon the brightness of the unapproachable glory of Thy countenance, O unoriginate Savior, Christ God: O Thou Who didst then cause Thy light to shine upon them, illuminate our souls!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, the same tone and podoben [Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed"] (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*

Being transfigured by Thine unapproachable glory, O Christ, with Thy divine light Thou didst illuminate Thy divine disciples, John and Peter and James; Thou didst strike them with awe by divine grace. And they heard the Father's voice testifying of the beloved Son, and beheld the glory of Thy countenance, O Savior. O Word Who desirest to save all, illuminate our souls!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Canon of the Feast, the work of Kyr John, with the Heirmos to 8, Tone 8:*[^231]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Having passed through the water as on dry land, and escaped the Egyptian evil, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparia:*

Moses, prophetically beholding of old the glory of the Lord at the sea in the cloud and pillar of fire, cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

As with a rock covered with a body, beholding the divinization of the Invisible One, Moses the beholder of God cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

Thou wast seen of old by Moses in darkness upon the mountain of the Law; and now upon Tabor in the unapproachable light of the Godhead.

*Canon of the Saint, to 4, Tone 1:*[^232]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*

Thy victorious right hand hath been glorified in strength in a manner befitting God: for, O Immortal One, as almighty it shattered the adversaries, fashioning anew for the Israelites a path through the deep.

*Troparia:*

Dwelling with the transcendent angelic orders, O divinely-wise and blessed one, looking down upon those who joyfully celebrate thy beloved festival, O father, account us worthy of salvation.

Wholly united unto the Master, and shining with fiery radiance thence, thou hast completely enlightened the festival by thy pious teachings, shown forth in sacred beauty, O divinely-wise and God-bearing one.

Thou didst set the mind as master over the passions by holy virtue, as a wise governor of soul and body, O father, granting unto each what was needful.

Standing visibly before the Church, thou wast revealed as an exceptional initiate of things beyond mind, O all-glorious one; and a mighty champion of the truth, breaking the jaws of lions and shutting their mouths.

*Theotokion:*

Piously do we acknowledge the One born of thy womb in two natures, O all-immaculate one: God together beyond nature, and man and man, possessing perfect properties of each.

*Ode 3. Heirmos [Canon of the Feast, Tone 8]:*[^233]

O Lord, Builder of the vault of heaven and Founder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desire, Confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

The overshadowing glory in the tabernacle of old, which conversed with Moses Thy servant, was a type of Thine ineffably radiant Transfiguration upon Tabor, O Master.

The highest summits of the Apostles ascended Mount Tabor with Thee, O Only-begotten Word; and Moses and Elijah stood before Thee as servants of God, O only Lover of mankind.

Being God the Word, He became wholly earthly, having united humanity to the whole Godhead in His Hypostasis: which existing in two essences, Moses and Elijah beheld upon Mount Tabor.

*Another Heirmos [Canon of the Saint, Tone 1]:*[^234]

Thou Who alone knowest the weakness of human nature, and didst compassionately take form therein: gird me with power from on high, that I may cry unto Thee: Holy is the living temple of Thine ineffable glory, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*

We the faithful present thee unto Christ as a divinely-wise intercessor, who didst contend brilliantly for His sake, and honor His image, and observe the law of honorable proclamation, and keep the faith from on high.

Thou wast revealed as the confirmation of martyrs, O divinely-wise and most marvelous one; thou didst overthrow the insolence of impiety, and wast revealed as an unshakable and unswerving rampart of the Church, and as a divine mediator thou didst steadfastly rebuke the foolish.

Lest the all-wicked one drink his fill of heresy, by thy teaching word he was rebuked; for by light darkness is driven away, and the weaving of wicked counsel is laid bare by the radiances of divine wisdom.

*Theotokion:*

With a bright voice now from the soul do we truly proclaim thee to be the Mother of God, who didst bear God incarnate from thee, the Incorporeal One Who dwelt among us, having received no alteration, nor being commingled by confusion, O all-immaculate one.

*After Ode 3, Kontakion of the Saint, Tone 3:*[^235]

As a valiant champion for the Trinity having been revealed, the Church glorifies thee, singing, O Emilian: for Whose sake thou didst suffer. Therefore we honor thy memory: deliver thy servants from heathen assault!

*Sessional Hymn, the same tone [Tone 3], Podoben: "Of the Divine Faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*[^236]

Enlightened by the Divine Spirit, thou didst proclaim with boldness the traditions of Orthodoxy, all-blessed one, and didst put to shame the lawless emperor, when unjustly sent into banishment, O venerable father; entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 3, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́тва):*[^237]
The radiance of Thy divine glory, as far as they were able, Thou didst show forth, O Word of God, unto Thy disciples, when transfigured upon Mount Tabor; whereby we also have been enlightened who sing unto Thee, O only immutable and immortal Lord. For unto Thee with faith we cry: Glory, O Christ, unto Thy kingdom!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^238]
From Thy flesh rays of Divinity went forth unto the prophets and apostles; therefore their leaders, singing, cried out: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*
Thou Who didst preserve unharmed the bush that touched the fire, didst show forth unto Moses Thy divinely woven flesh, O Master, as he sang: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

The sensible sun was hidden by the radiance of the Divinity, as on Mount Tabor it beheld Thee transfigured, O my Jesus: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

The immaterial Fire was seen, not consuming bodily matter, as Thou didst appear unto Moses and Elijah and the apostles, O Master, being One of two perfect natures.

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^239]
Having perceived with farsighted eyes the mountain overshadowed by the grace of God, Habakkuk proclaimed that the Holy One of Israel would come forth from thee, for our salvation and renewal.

*Troparia:*
Thou wast able to cast down the raging of the deceiver, having conformed thyself through virtue, O all-blessed Emilian, unto the Almighty One Who is mighty in battle, O exceedingly rich one; and now thou criest: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Thou didst accept bitter banishments when sent into exile, and didst show thyself patient, O divinely wise Emilian. Fenced about by the word of grace, O marvelous initiate of sacred mysteries, crying unto God: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Lawfully thou wast shown to be sanctified from childhood, having been enlightened, O blessed Emilian, by thy first ascetic training; and afterwards thou wast revealed as a hierarch and hieromartyr, O most excellent God-bearer.

*Theotokion:*
He Who is higher than every conceivable creation, performing upon earth the mystery of life-originating divine creation which is beyond understanding, having adorned thee with the splendors of virginity, took up His abode in thy womb, O all-pure one.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^240]
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O Light unwaning, and a strange darkness hath enveloped me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my paths toward the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.

*Troparia:*
An oratorical tongue cannot tell of Thy majesty; for holding authority over life and having dominion over death, Thou didst present upon Mount Tabor Moses and Elijah, who bore witness unto Thy Divinity.

Having created man with invisible hands according to Thine own image, O Christ, Thou didst display in Thy creation Thine archetypal beauty; not as in an image, but as Thyself being in essence both God and man.

Mingled without confusion, Thou didst show us the burning coal of Divinity, consuming sins and enlightening souls, upon Mount Tabor; and Thou didst astonish Moses, Elijah, and the foremost disciples.

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^241]
Being the God of peace and Father of mercies, Thou hast sent unto us the Angel of Thy great Counsel, granting peace; therefore, having been guided to the light of divine knowledge, waking early out of the night, we glorify Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparia:*
Having perceived through divine foreknowledge the purpose of thy great soul, Christ manifestly adorned thee with great honors, making thee radiant with the vestments of priesthood and with the sufferings of martyrdom, O all-honored initiate of sacred mysteries.

Having drawn down the grace of the Spirit, thou didst draw forth rivers of teachings, O all-blessed consecration of Christ’s flock; therefore, having learned through thee to venerate His icon and those of all the saints, we put the fighter against God to shame.

Having put to death the carnal mind of thy flesh in ascetic labors, O exceedingly rich one, thou didst enliven thy mind with the radiance of the Spirit; therefore in both thou didst shine forth, O hierarch, well-pleasing unto God, O hieromartyr worthy of wonder.

*Theotokion:*
O Holy of Holies, pure Virgin, thou didst give birth unto the Holy of Holies Who sanctifies all, Christ the Deliverer; therefore we proclaim thee Queen and Lady of all, as Mother of the Creator of creation.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^242]
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my iniquities, and bring me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou hear me, O God of my salvation.

*Troparia:*
How great and fearful a vision was beheld today! For the sensible sun shone from heaven, but from the earth the incomparable Sun of righteousness shone noetically upon Mount Tabor.

The shadow of the law hath passed away and grown weak, and Christ the Truth is manifestly come, cried Moses upon Tabor, having beheld Thy Divinity.

The pillar of fire most clearly prefigured Christ transfigured, and the cloud manifestly prefigured the grace of the Spirit that overshadowed upon Tabor.

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^243]
The sea monster spat forth Jonah from its belly like a babe, just as it had received him; and the Word, having dwelt in the Virgin and taken flesh, came forth, having preserved her incorrupt. For from that which suffered no corruption, He preserved her who bare Him unharmed.

*Troparia:*
Desiring to halt the pasture of heresy, thou didst zealously separate thyself unto perils, and with boldness, O all-blessed one, thou didst rebuke the character of him who held godlessness, thou being the first to begin speaking unto the assembled divinely wise council.

Fervently loving Christ alone, and desiring to attain His divine radiance, O God-pleasing hierarch of the Lord, thou didst show forth the fortitude of a martyr, O father, having first shone forth through fasting, and manifestly thou didst receive double crowns.

On high thy mind was near unto God, and shining with divine rays, O blessed and venerable one, thou didst possess it; lightly hast thou sailed across the sea of life, and hast now hastened unto the heavenly havens, being guided by the winds of the Holy Spirit, O divinely inspired one.

*Theotokion:*
Being merciful, the transcendent God was well-pleased to save human nature which had suffered grievously through the serpent's envy; He took up His abode in thy womb and became immutable flesh, having found thee alone pure, O Mother of God, all-pure Virgin.

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 7, Automelon:*[^244]
Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, and as far as they were able, Thy disciples beheld Thy glory, O Christ God; that when they should see Thee crucified, they might understand Thy voluntary passion, and proclaim unto the world that Thou art truly the Father's Radiance.

*Ikos:*[^245]
Awake, ye sluggards, who are ever bowed down to the earth, thoughts of my soul; arise and be lifted up unto the height of divine ascent! Let us run with Peter and the sons of Zebedee, and together with them reach Mount Tabor, that we may see with them the glory of our God; and let us hear the voice which they heard from on high, and they proclaimed the Father's Radiance.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^246]
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trod down the flame boldly, and changed the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

Now things invisible have been seen by the apostles, the Divinity having shone forth in the flesh upon Mount Tabor, unto them that cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

The apostles were seized with fear, being astonished at the majesty of the divine kingdom upon Mount Tabor, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

Now things unheard of have been heard; for the Son of the Virgin without a father is gloriously testified to by the Father's voice, as God and man, the same unto the ages.

Not by adoption wast Thou the Son of the Most High, but being Beloved by nature, existing before, Thou hast drawn near unto us without change: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^247]
Thee, the noetic furnace, O Mother of God, do we the faithful consider; for as the Exalted One saved the three children, so He renewed the world, wholly in thy womb, the praised and most glorious God of our fathers.

Thou art truly the beautiful adornment of the elements, O father; for having reigned over the passions, thou wast revealed as an unshakable pillar, O worthy of wonder, a bulwark of the Church, magnifying the praised and most glorious God.

A new David hast thou appeared unto us, having cast down the alien teachings with the sling of thy radiant words, O most glorious one, and with thy didactic arrows, naming the praised and most glorious God.

Radiantly didst thou stand, O father, before the tribunal of the tormentor for Christ's sake, and didst pour forth words as from a divine fountain; for thou didst preach unto all the veneration of icons, manifestly knowing that it passes over unto the Proto-image.

*Theotokion:*
Wonderfully dost thou remain a virgin, having given birth unto the Creator of all, Who rests upon the throne of the Cherubim, O only all-blessed one, Virgin Mother all-hymned, [praising] the praised and most glorious God of our fathers.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^248]
The Chaldean tormentor furiously heated the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but having seen them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Having heard Thee testified to by the Father, O Master, and being unable to endure the brightness of Thy countenance, which was firmer than human aspect, Thy disciples fell prone upon the earth in fear, singing: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Thou art the most beauteous King of those that reign, and the Lord of those that rule everywhere, mighty, blessed, and dwelling in light unapproachable. At Whom the disciples, and Moses and Elijah, being astonished, cried out: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

As One Who rules over heaven, and has dominion over the earth, and holds power over the nether regions, O Christ, there stood before Thee: from the earth the apostles, and as from heaven the Tishbite Elijah, and Moses from the dead, singing in unison: Ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Having left on earth the sorrows that bring forth despondency, O Lover of mankind, the chosen of the apostles, as they followed Thee unto the divine way of life that is translated from the earth, and having obtained Thy theophany as was worthy, sang: Ye people, supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^249]
The dew-yielding furnace prefigured the image of a supernatural wonder; for it burned not the youths whom it had received, as neither did the fire of the Divinity consume the womb of the Virgin wherein it entered; therefore chanting, let us sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

With noetic fire thou didst burn up all the brushwood furnace of heresy by divine invocation, just as Elijah slew the shameful priests with the spiritual sword; and now thou singest, rejoicing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Filled with divine grace and power wast thou revealed, O thrice-blessed one, divinely adorned with the beauty of chastity; thou didst hold fast immutable piety. With them now standing before the Lady, thou criest: Let creation bless the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

From on high thou lookest down upon thy hymnographers, O holy father, divinely wise, showing us the right path by thy prayers, and crushing the raging of heresies, that with boldness we may sing, honoring and venerating the icon of Christ, and of the Mother of God, and of all the saints.

*Theotokion:*
There is no blemish in thy beauty, O Virgin; for thou alone hast been shown forth from all ages all-pure and all-immaculate, having enlightened the world with the rays of virginity and with the light of purity; therefore chanting, we sing:

we sing: Let all creation bless the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^250]
Every ear was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God, that the Most High willingly came down even unto the flesh, being made man from the virginal womb; therefore, O all-pure Mother of God, we the faithful magnify thee.

That Thou mightest show forth manifestly Thine ineffable second coming, that Thou mightest be revealed as the Most High God, standing in the midst of the gods, the apostles, on Tabor, Thou didst ineffably illumine Moses and Elijah; therefore, O Christ, we all magnify Thee.

Come and submit yourselves unto Me, ye people; having ascended the holy and venerable mountain, let us stand immaterially in the city of the living God, and noetically behold the immaterial Divinity of the Father and the Spirit, shining forth in the only-begotten Son.

Thou didst attract me with love, O Savior, and didst transform me by Thy divine desire; but burn up my sins with immaterial fire, and vouchsafe that I may be filled with Thy delight, that leaping with joy I may magnify both Thy majesties, O Good One.

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^251]
The unconsumed burning bush showed forth the type of thy pure childbearing, and now we pray thee to quench the furnace of temptations that rages against us, that we may unceasingly magnify thee, O Mother of God.

Thou hast found the recompense of thy labors, being now counted worthy to dwell in the heavenly mansions, O God-bearing Emilian, with the divine armies of martyrs, like a divine pillar, O most marvelous hieromartyr.

Adorned exceedingly with word and wisdom in the divine commands, O Emilian; therefore Christ crowned thy venerable head, O most rich one; Whom do thou now beseech for us unceasingly, we pray thee.

The Master, having opened the heavenly gates, O venerable one, received thy soul, O Emilian, giving thee rest in places of refreshment, as a righteous man; and now thou walkest around the throne of the King of all.

*Theotokion:*
O the wonders beyond understanding of thy childbearing, O Mother of God, only all-pure Lady! For all the angelic hosts and diverse assemblies of men are struck with awe; therefore, O Mother of God, we unceasingly magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion of the Feast:*[^252]
O Word, immutable Light of the unbegotten Light the Father: in Thy revealed light, today on Tabor we have seen the Light, the Father, and the Light, the Spirit, guiding all creation with light.

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^253]
The good of joy beyond understanding, wherewith the divine summit of Thy disciples was vouchsafed, O Lord, on Mount Tabor.

*Stichos: Thine are the heavens, and Thine is the earth.*
A divinely becoming sign of Divinity does Christ show forth unto the disciples before the Cross, having shone forth today on the mountain like the sun.

*Stichos: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy name.*
Come, ye earthborn, let us zealously ascend Mount Tabor, to behold Christ transfigured in glory!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 4:*[^254]
Beholding the uncontainable effusion of Thy light and the unapproachable nature of Thy Divinity, on the mount of the Transfiguration, O beginningless Christ, the chosen among the apostles were transformed with divine awe; and overshadowed by a radiant cloud, they heard the Father's voice confirming the mystery of Thine incarnation: that even after Thine incarnation Thou art the One Only-begotten Son and Savior of the world.

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 3 of both canons, with the heirmos, to 6.*[^255]

*In the same month, on the 9th day: The Holy Apostle Matthias.*[^256]

*At Vespers, we sing "Blessed is the man," the 1st antiphon. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera to 8: of the Feast, 3; Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^257]
Come, let us faithfully celebrate together the all-radiant, divine Transfiguration of Christ; and being now changed with a goodly change, let us ascend in spirit unto the lofty summit of the virtues of Christ’s disciples, that having been instructed with them in the manifestation of strange mysteries, we may glorify the Savior and Lord.

Rejoice and be glad devoutly, O venerable Church of the faithful, now celebrating this honorable and all-radiant feast; for the Master of all, being now transfigured, clearly enlightens the deformity of man with the beam of ineffable brightness, more than the rays of the sun, emitting the radiance of the sun radiantly.

Being God and Creator of all, having taken the form of a servant through Thine ineffable wealth of compassion, He now saves from ancient darkening, having shone forth in His face, and laid bare the radiance of Divinity in His flesh; whence the disciples, being terrified with fear and having fallen prone, bowed down unto the Master of all.

*Other Stichera of the Apostle, 5, Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи му́нцы):*
O Apostle Matthias, thou didst fill up the divine choir from which Judas fell away; and by the divine splendors of thy wise words, thou didst drive away the darkness of idolatrous madness [preaching] the grace of God. And do thou now pray, that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls. *(Twice)*

O blessed Matthias, thou didst flow forth as a noetic river flooded with divine waters, and with mystical outpourings didst water the earth, and didst show it forth fruitful unto the Savior; therefore pray, that peace and great mercy be granted unto our souls. *(Twice)*

O divinely inspired Matthias, the lightnings of thy teachings have illuminated the world's sphere, enlightening all, guiding all unto divine knowledge, making them receptive vessels of the light who formerly were sleeping in the night of vanity and in the darkness of all godlessness.

*Glory... Tone 6:*
Grace was poured forth in thy lips, O glorious Apostle Matthias, and thou wast a luminary of the Church of Christ, teaching the rational sheep to believe in the consubstantial Trinity and in one Godhead.

*Both now... Tone 2:*
O Good One, Who hast enlightened the whole universe with Thy light, Thou wast transfigured upon a high mountain, showing Thy power unto Thy disciples, that Thou dost deliver the world from transgression; therefore we cry unto Thee: O merciful Lord, save our souls!

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings.*

*The Reading is from the Acts of the Holy Apostles:*[^258]
In those days, Peter stood up in the midst of the disciples and said (the number of names together being about a hundred and twenty): "Men and brethren, this Scripture had to be fulfilled, which the Holy Spirit spoke before by the mouth of David concerning Judas, who was guide to those who took Jesus; for he was numbered with us and had obtained a part in this ministry. Now this man purchased a field with the reward of iniquity; and falling headlong, he burst open in the middle, and all his entrails gushed out. And it became known unto all who dwell in Jerusalem, so that that field is called in their own language, Akeldama, that is, Field of Blood. For it is written in the Book of Psalms: 'Let his dwelling place be desolate, and let no one live in it'; and, 'Let another take his office.' Therefore, of these men who have accompanied us all the time that the Lord Jesus went in and out among us, beginning from the baptism of John until the day when He was taken up (into heaven) from us, one of these must become a witness with us of His resurrection." And they proposed two: Joseph called Barsabas, who was surnamed Justus, and Matthias. And they prayed and said: "Thou, Lord, Who knowest the hearts of all, show which of these two Thou hast chosen to take part in this ministry and apostleship from which Judas by transgression fell, that he might go to his own place." And they cast their lots, and the lot fell on Matthias; and he was numbered with the eleven apostles.

*The Reading is from the General Epistle of John:*[^259]
Beloved, if our heart does not condemn us, we have confidence toward God. And whatever we ask we receive from Him, because we keep His commandments and do those things that are pleasing in His sight. And this is His commandment: that we should believe on the name of His Son Jesus Christ and love one another, as He gave us commandment. Now he who keeps His commandments abides in Him, and He in him. And by this we know that He abides in us, by the Spirit Whom He has given us. Beloved, do not believe every spirit, but test the spirits, whether they are of God; because many false prophets have gone out into the world. By this you know the Spirit of God [and the spirit of deception]: Every spirit that confesses that Jesus Christ has come in the flesh is of God, and every spirit that does not confess that Jesus Christ has come in the flesh is not of God. And this is the spirit of the Antichrist, which you have heard was coming, and is now already in the world. You are of God, little children, and have overcome them, because He Who is in you is greater than he who is in the world. They are of the world. Therefore they speak as of the world, and the world hears them. We are of God. He who knows God hears us; he who is not of God does not hear us.

*The Reading is from the General Epistle of John:*[^260]
Beloved, if God so loved us, we also ought to love one another. No one has seen God at any time. If we love one another, God abides in us, and His love has been perfected in us. By this we know that we abide in Him, and He in us, because He has given us of His Spirit. And we have seen and testify that the Father has sent the Son as Savior of the world. Whoever confesses that Jesus is the Son of God, God abides in him, and he in God. And we have known and believed the love that God has for us. God is love, and he who abides in love abides in God, and God in him.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having triumphed on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*[^261]
Having conformed Thyself through compassion, O Christ, unto Adam the first man from the Virgin, Thou wast revealed as the second Adam; and upon Mount Tabor, O my Savior, Thou wast transfigured, laying bare Thy Divinity.

*Stichos: Thine are the heavens, and Thine is the earth.*
The chosen of the law were astonished, beholding the grace of Thy Transfiguration, O Christ, upon the holy mountain; with whom we bow down before Thee, with the Father and the Holy Spirit.

*Stichos: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy name.*
Creation is clearly enlightened by Thy Transfiguration, O Christ, which Thou, being God, didst show forth unto the divine apostles on Tabor, and unto Moses and Elijah, having ineffably shone forth brighter than the sun.

*Glory... Tone 2:*[^262]
Filled with divine zeal, O Apostle Matthias worthy of wonder, being zealous thou wast zealous for God the Almighty, Who also showed thee forth full of divine wonders; for having strange zeal, through action thou didst acquire noble character; therefore being on high, rejoicing with the celestial choirs, pray, O beholder of God, unto Christ the Word, our God, to grant us great mercy.

*Both now... the same tone [Tone 2]:*[^263]
