Thou Who wast transfigured in glory on Mount Tabor, O Christ God, and didst show forth the glory of Thy Divinity unto Thy disciples: illuminate us also with the light of Thy knowledge, and guide us onto the path of Thy commandments, as Thou alone art good and the Lover of mankind.

*Troparion of the Apostle, Tone 3:*[^264]
O holy Apostle Matthias, entreat the merciful God to grant unto our souls remission of transgressions.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 7:*[^265]
Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, O Christ God, having shown forth Thy glory unto Thy disciples as far as they were able as they were able; let Thine everlasting Light shine forth also upon us sinners, through the intercessions of the Birthgiver of God: O Giver of light, glory to Thee.

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory... of the Apostle; Both now... of the Feast.*

*After the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 2:*
Having caught the nations, the glorious Apostle, and having taught the ends of the earth to worship Thee with the Father and the Spirit, O Christ God: for his sake establish Thy Church, and send down Thy blessing upon the faithful, O only Merciful One and Lover of mankind. *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*
Showing forth the human change that is to be at Thy second and dread coming with Thy glory, O Savior, Thou wast transfigured upon Mount Tabor; Elias and Moses conversed with Thee, and having called three of the disciples with Thee, who, beholding Thy glory, O Master, marveled at Thy radiance: O Thou Who didst then cause Thy light to shine upon them, enlighten our souls.

*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4:*
Christ the Sun of righteousness sent thee forth as a ray to enlighten the whole earth, O glorious Apostle Matthias; by thy divine prayers thou dost illumine, and with unwaning divine light thou dost enlighten all who in faith celebrate thy holy memory. *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, the same tone [Tone 4], Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Having ascended Mount Tabor with the disciples, and having shone forth in the Father’s glory: Moses and Elias stand before Thee; for the Law and the Prophets serve Thee as God; to Whom also the Father, confessing His natural Sonship, called Thee Son: Whom we hymn together with Thee and the Spirit.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^266]
Having cast down the pride of idols, desiring the Savior's passions, O Apostle Matthias, thou didst appear unto the world from heaven, pouring forth miracles for all; and thou wast a teacher unto the Gentiles, O most blessed one. Therefore we honor thy memory worthily, glorifying thee in hymns and faithfully magnifying thee, O most rich proclaimer of God: pray unto Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who with love honor thy holy memory. *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*
Upon Mount Tabor Thou wast transfigured, O God, in the midst of the most wise Elias and Moses, with James, and Simon, and John. And Peter being there present, said these things unto Thee: It is good for us to be here, to make three booths: one for Moses, and one for Elias, and one for Thee, Master Christ: O Thou Who didst then cause Thy light to shine upon them, enlighten our souls.

*Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi], the 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:*
*Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world [LXX Ps. 18:4].* *Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands [LXX Ps. 18:1].* *Let every breath... Gospel according to John, composite pericope 67 [Jn. 21:15–25].[^267] After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6: "Grace was poured out..." Written at "Lord, I have cried," at Glory...*

*Canon of the Feast, with the Heirmos to 6. The composition of Lord Cosmas. Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
The choirs of Israel, with undrenched feet, having traversed the Red Sea and the watery deep, seeing the riders and captains of the enemy submerged therein, sang with joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He hath been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Christ, speaking words of life unto His friends and teaching concerning the divine Kingdom, said: Know the Father in Me, as shining with unapproachable light, singing in joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He hath been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Ye shall be clothed with the power of the Gentiles, O friend-disciples, and marvelous shall ye be in their wealth, for ye shall be filled with glory, as shining brighter than the sun He appears unto those who sing in joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He hath been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Today Christ, having shone forth clearly upon Mount Tabor with divine radiance as He promised, laid bare His form unto the disciples; and being filled with the light-bearing divine radiance, they sang in joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He hath been glorified.

*Another Canon of the Holy Apostle, to 8.*[^268]
*Whose acrostic is: "With songs I hymn thee, O Matthias, friend of Christ." The composition of Theophanes. Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone hath been gloriously glorified.

*Troparion:*
As I now sing in hymns of thy light-bearing solemnity, O Matthias, pray that the radiance of salvation be given me from heaven.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear as a radiant sun of the Great Light, O most laudable one; for thou didst converse immediately, without mediation, with Him Who came to be with us, O Apostle.

*Troparion:*
Obeying the laws of the Master, O blessed one, with the net of thy tongue thou didst catch the ends of the earth out of the deep of ignorance.

*Theotokion:*
He shall save me, having received the Divine Word in goodness, Who willed to become incarnate from thy womb, O pure Virgin Mother.

*Katavasia of the Feast: The choirs of Israel...*
*Ode 3. Heirmos:*

The bow of the mighty hath waxen weak, and the weak have girded themselves with strength; therefore my heart is established in the Lord [1 Sam. / 1 Kgs. 2:4].

*Troparion:*
Having clothed Thyself with the whole Adam, O Christ, Thou didst change and enlighten the nature that of old had darkened, and by the changing of Thy form Thou didst make it godlike.

*Troparion:*
He Who of old in the wilderness guided Israel by a fiery pillar and a cloud, today upon Mount Tabor, Christ hath ineffably shone forth in light.

*Another Canon. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of those that flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit doth hymn Thee.

*Troparion:*
Being strengthened by the life-originating nod, O wise one, thou wast able to despoil the spoils of the strong man and subdue them by grace.

*Troparion:*
Serving the all-effective commands of the Teacher, thou didst save men from delusion, O most laudable one.

*Troparion:*
The all-effecting Spirit, O divinely-speaking one, chose thee to fill up the number twelve of the choir of the holy disciples.

*Theotokion:*
Having thee as help, O pure one, I fear not the onsets of the evil enemies; having thee as protectress, I conquer their ranks.

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 7:*
Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, and as far as they were able Thy disciples beheld Thy glory, O Christ God; that when they should see Thee crucified, they might understand Thy voluntary passion, and proclaim unto the world that Thou art truly the Father's Radiance.

*Ikos:*[^269]
Arise, ye slothful thoughts of my soul, that ever bow down to the earth; arise and be lifted up to the height of divine ascent. Let us run unto Peter and the sons of Zebedee, and together with them let us reach Mount Tabor, that with them we may behold the glory of our God, and hear the voice which they heard from on high, and proclaimed the Father's Radiance.

*Sessional Hymn of the Apostle, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
By the lightnings of thy divine words thou didst burn up the deceit of evil; thou didst enlighten the faithful to hymn the coming in the flesh of Him Who rules over all, O blessed one, and didst emulate His divine passion. Therefore, having come together, we celebrate thy holy memory with joy, and in unison we cry unto thee: O Apostle Matthias, entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who with love honor thy holy memory. *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, the same tone [Tone 8], Podoben: "That which was commanded" (Повелѣ́нное):*
The lightning hidden beneath the flesh of Thy essence and Thy divine splendor, Thou didst show forth upon the holy mountain, O Benefactor, shining unto the disciples who were with Thee; and having understood Thy glory to be unendurable, they cried aloud: Holy art Thou! For being unapproachable, Thou wast seen in the flesh by the world, O only Lover of mankind.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard of Thy glorious dispensation, O Christ God, that Thou wast born of the Virgin, that Thou mightest deliver from deception those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Prefiguring the Law in Sinai by writing, O Christ God, Thou didst appear borne in the cloud, and in fire, and darkness, and tempest: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
That Thou mightest confirm Thy glorious dispensation, O Christ God, as being before the ages, and the same Who didst make the clouds Thy chariot, Thou didst shine forth ineffably upon Tabor.

*Troparion:*
Conversing with Thee, they stood before Thee as servants, unto Thee Master Christ, unto whom Thou didst speak with the vapor of fire, and darkness, and a light breeze: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
They proclaimed the glory to come through Thy Cross, having come unto Tabor: Moses who of old foresaw Thee in the fire and the bush, and Elias who was taken up in the fiery chariot, O Christ.

*Another Canon. Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I understood Thy works, and I glorified Thy Divinity.

*Troparion:*
Proclaiming one nature of the Godhead, one Kingdom and Dominion, O most blessed one, thou didst catch the flocks of the Gentiles.

*Troparion:*
The Lord brought Thee unto the sea of life, O most wise one, like a horse troubling the bitter waters of polytheism.

*Troparion:*
By the divine word thou didst renew the withered hearts, O glorious one, teaching all piety and knowledge of the truth.

*Theotokion:*
O vessel of virginity, and dwelling-place of the uncontainable Nature, enlighten my soul, darkened by many passions.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Thou Who from light didst cleave the first-born light, that Thy works might hymn Thee in light, O Christ the Creator: in Thy light direct our paths.

*Troparion:*
Before Thy face the mountains gave way; for light, even under Thy feet, came with unwaning rays, O Christ the Sun, when Thou didst deign to take the form of earthly men.

*Troparion:*
This is the Savior, Moses and Elias cried out unto the disciples who were instructed upon the holy Mount Tabor: this is Christ, Whom of old we proclaimed as God.

*Troparion:*
The immutable Nature, mingled with the human, abundantly baring the light of immaterial Divinity unto the Apostles, shone forth ineffably.

*Troparion:*
When the disciples beheld Thee, the Everlasting Radiance, shining forth in the Father's glory, O Christ, they cried unto Thee: In Thy light direct our paths.

*Another Canon. Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Why hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O Never-setting Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my paths, I pray Thee.

*Troparion:*
As a fruitful vine the true Vine stretched thee out, yielding a cluster that pours forth saving wine; and drinking thereof, O blessed one, those held fast by unreason cast away the drunkenness of delusion.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear filling the blessed choir of the twelve disciples, from which the traitor cast himself out and gained bitter hanging, having lifted up his heel against Christ, O wonder-working Matthias, proclaimer of mysteries.

*Troparion:*
As divine salt thou wast cast into the world, O blessed one, Apostle Matthias, cleansing the corruption of delusion with sweet teachings, driving away sicknesses, and taking away the wounds of souls and bodies, O all-praised one.

*Theotokion:*
Adam stripped off the unseemly garments and ancient deadness through thy most pure childbirth, O Virgin, Bride of God, and was clothed with a holy and true garment, nowise defiled by passions.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
When I was afflicted, I cried unto the Lord, and the God of my salvation heard me.

*Troparion:*
Shining brighter than the sun, a most clear Light, the Savior shone forth on Tabor and enlightened us.

*Troparion:*
Having ascended Mount Tabor, Thou wast transfigured, O Christ, and having darkened all delusion, Thou didst enlighten us.

*Troparion:*
Knowing Thee to be God, the glorious Apostles, being amazed on Tabor, O Christ, bowed their knees.

*Another Canon. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the deep of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou hear me, O God of my salvation.

*Troparion:*
Thou becamest a god by adoption, being deified by divine union, and receiving primary rays from God, thou didst enlighten the faithful and cleanse the earth from the mist of idolatry.

*Troparion:*
In thee, O disciple, every prophesying of the sacred prophets is fulfilled; for having become an eye-witness of Him Whom of old they proclaimed, O minister Matthias, thou wast His imitator and Apostle.

*Theotokion:*
An intelligible dwelling, and untouched mercy-seat, a golden lampstand, and an animate table that bore the Bread of life, O Virgin, do we the faithful call thee.

*Kontakion [of the Apostle], Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*[^270]
Thy sound, going forth like a radiant sun into all the world, enlightens the Church of the Gentiles with grace, O wonder-bearing Apostle Matthias.

*Ikos:*
Grant me holy speech, O Lord, Who didst fashion the nature of water, and strengthen my heart, O Merciful One, Who didst establish the earth by Thy word; and enlighten my thought, O Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment: that I may strive to speak and sing, worthily to honor Thine Apostle Matthias, O Most Merciful One.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Abrahamic children of old in Babylon trampled down the furnace flame, crying aloud in hymns: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Shining with the light of unapproachable glory upon Mount Tabor, the Apostles cried unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Enjoying the radiance of the divine sound, and the dew-yielding cloud, O Christ, and Thy splendor, the Apostles sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
When Peter saw Thee shining forth in unapproachable light upon Mount Tabor, O Christ, he cried aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Being with the Author of life, Christ, when He sent forth the light of His form, the sons of Zebedee thundered: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Another Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto ages.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst dry up the torrent of delusion by the flood of thy divine wisdom, and with the torrent of delight thou didst give drink unto those who faithfully cry, O glorious one: Blessed art Thou, O God! *Twice.*

*Troparion:*
By the power of the Almighty God, the wiles of the enemy grew weak before the presence of thy journey, O Apostle, and the mountains and hills of godlessness were crushed.

*Theotokion:*
Behold, the Virgin whom the great Isaiah proclaimed by the Spirit conceived God in her womb and brings Him forth; unto Whom we sing: O Lord God, blessed art Thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
In Babylon the children, inflamed with divine zeal, manfully trampled upon the threat of the tyrant and the flame; and cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed, they sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Bearing all things by His nod, Christ ascended Mount Tabor with His most pure feet; whereon, shining with His face brighter than the sun’s rays, He showed forth the foremost of the Law and of grace unto those who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having appeared in unapproachable glory ineffably upon Mount Tabor, the uncontainable and unwaning Light, the Father's Radiance, illuminating creation, deified mankind who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Standing in sacred splendor upon Mount Tabor, Moses and Elias clearly beheld the Hypostasis of the divine imprint, and seeing Christ shine forth in the Father’s glory, they sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
The face of Moses was once glorified in appearance because of the divine vision; but Christ is clothed with light and glory as with a garment, for being Himself the Author of light, He enlightens those who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Beholding Christ enveloped by a luminous cloud upon Tabor, the disciples, falling face down upon the earth, being enlightened in mind, sang of Him together with the Father and the Spirit: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Another Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant furiously heat the furnace for the godly; but seeing them saved by a greater power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having become the mouth of God, thou didst snatch out of the throat of the murderer of men those whom he had cruelly swallowed and made food for his malice, O wise one; and by the bath of regeneration thou didst bring them unto the Lord, unceasingly singing: Ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Becoming a weapon of the word of God, O glorious one, thou didst utterly crush the sword of delusion and the weapons of malice, the sacrifices of idols, and by divine power didst cast down pillars and altars, and didst make those who sang of gods into temples of the Trinity: Ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear as a noetic heaven, declaring the ineffable divine glory of the Only-begotten, O all-wise Matthias, lightning of grace, catcher of the lost, lamp of divine radiance, initiate of ineffable mysteries: let us joyfully hymn him in unison.

*Theotokion:*
Beyond nature thou didst conceive, beyond word thou didst bear the Creator of human nature Who became man, yet was not separated from the Father, O pure Sovereign Lady; Whom all creation hymns: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Thy childbirth was shown forth incorrupt; God came forth from thy loins, and appeared flesh-bearing upon earth and lived among men: therefore, O Birthgiver of God, we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
Terrified by the new pouring-forth of light, the disciples, being suddenly illumined, looked one upon another; and being amazed, they bowed down unto the earth and worshipped Thee, the Master of all.

*Troparion:*
A divinely-sounding voice was sent forth from the cloud, certifying the miracle: for the Father of lights cried aloud unto the Apostles: This is My beloved Son; listen unto Him!

*Troparion:*
Having seen strange and most marvelous things, and having heard the Father’s voice upon Mount Tabor, the ministers of the Word cried out: This is the Image of the Archetype, our Savior!

*Troparion:*
O immutable Image of Him Who is, immovable, unalterable Seal, Son, Word, Wisdom, and Arm, Right Hand and Power of the Most High, we hymn Thee together with the Father and the Spirit.

*Another Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every ear was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God: that the Most High voluntarily descended even unto the flesh, becoming man from a virginal womb: therefore, O most pure Birthgiver of God, we the faithful magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
The Savior called thee His friend as one serving His commandments, O blessed Apostle, and an heir of His kingdom, a co-assessor in the dread day to come, O all-wise Matthias, fulfillment of the twelve disciples.

*Troparion:*
With the sail of the Cross, O blessed one, having crossed the surging sea of life, thou didst reach the haven of rest and the ultimate desire; rejoicing now, thou standest with the apostolic choir, entreating the Lover of mankind for us.

*Troparion:*
A truly golden-beaming lampstand, a luminary of the Spirit shining brightly did thy tongue appear, burning up heathen teachings, quenching strange fire, O wise one, and shining light upon those sleeping in the darkness of unreason.

*Theotokion:*
Thou alone among women didst abolish the curse of the first-fashioned, O Bride of God, by bearing in the flesh the Uncircumscribable One circumscribed; thou didst renew the bounds of nature, O undefiled one; and things separated before, by thy diligent intercession thou didst unite.

*Exapostilarion of the Apostle, Podoben: "In the spirit in the sanctuary" (Дх҃омъ во ст҃и́лищи):*[^271]
Having finished the course of thy fair feet, turning them around, O Apostle Matthias, thou didst ascend with joy unto the heavenly journey, and standing before the Trinity, in the Father thou seest the Son and the Divine Spirit: therefore in faith we celebrate thy most sacred and divine memory. *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast:*
O Word, immutable Light of the unbegotten Light the Father: in Thy manifest light today on Tabor have we seen light the Father, and light the Spirit, guiding all creation by light.

*At the Praises, Stichera on 6: of the Feast, 3; Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Е҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^272]
Upon Mount Tabor the glory of the Trinity was clearly known, when Thou wast transfigured, O my most good Savior.

*Sticheron:*
A luminous cloud clearly spread out in the Transfiguration, and astonished the foremost of the divine disciples.

*Sticheron:*
Elias with Moses stood before Thee, O Word, in the Transfiguration; and the Father bore witness, speaking from heaven.

*And of the Apostle, 3; Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^273]
With the rod of grace, from the deep of vanity thou didst draw up mankind, O most laudable one, obeying the nod of the Teacher, O Matthias, Who enlightened thine understanding in all things and showed thee forth, O most blessed one, as an apostle and honorable proclaimer of His unsearchable Divinity.

*Sticheron:*
The radiance of the Spirit came down upon thee in the appearance of fire, and made thee, O blessed one, a divine receptacle: quickly driving away the mist of godlessness and enlightening the world with the brightness of thy most wise words, O speaker of mysteries, adornment of apostles, blessed eye-witness of Christ.

*Sticheron:*
By the lightnings of preaching, having enlightened those sitting in darkness, O glorious one, thou didst show them to be sons of the Master and God through faith; Whose passion and death thou didst emulate, and didst become an heir of glory, as one wise, as one divinely-speaking, as a disciple of truth.

*Glory... Tone 2:*
Leaving behind earthly things, Thou didst follow Christ, and being sealed by the inspiration of the Holy Spirit, Thou wast sent by Him unto the lost nations, to turn men unto the light of the knowledge of God, O Apostle Matthias; and having finished the struggles of thy divine holiness and varied torments, thou didst commend thy soul unto Christ: entreat Him, O all-blessed one, to grant great mercy unto us.

*Both now... of the Feast, the same tone [Tone 2]:*
Moses and Elias saw upon Mount Tabor God incarnate of the Virgin Maiden, for the deliverance of mankind.

*Great Doxology.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 4, to 4; and of the Apostle, Ode 6, to 4. Prokeimenon of the Feast, and of the Saint, Tone 8: Their sound is gone out into all the earth, and their words unto the ends of the world [LXX Ps. 18:4].* *Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God, and the firmament proclaims the work of His hands [LXX Ps. 18:1].* *Epistle: Acts of the Apostles, composite pericope 2 [Acts 1:12–17, 21–26].[^274] Alleluia, Tone 1: The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord [LXX Ps. 88:6]. Gospel according to Luke, pericope 40 [Lk. 9:1–6].[^275] Communion Hymn of the Feast, and of the Saint: Their sound is gone out...*

*The same month, on the 10th day: Holy Martyr and Archdeacon Laurence.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́меніе):*[^276]
Today Thou hast shone forth luminous as the sun of brightness upon Mount Tabor, showing unto Thy friends that Thou, O Savior, art the Father's Word, Who in compassion didst bear the flesh of human nature, O Christ: therefore we glorify Thy dispensation of love for mankind, O all-powerful Jesus, Savior of our souls.

*Sticheron:*
Today the angels of the invisible hosts rejoice, celebrating with men, O Christ, the vision of Thy glorious shining upon Mount Tabor; whereon Thou didst cause Moses and Elias to stand, O Christ the Lover of mankind, with Peter, and James, and John, who hymn Thee: O all-powerful Jesus, Savior of our souls.

*Sticheron:*
Today let the earthly and heavenly order be moved to praise Christ our God, Who rules over the dead and the living; for having been transfigured in divine beauty, He placed beside Himself the leaders and preachers of the Law and of grace upon Mount Tabor, as He was well pleased, the Savior of our souls.

*Other Stichera of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́меніе):*

*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^277]
Having blamelessly served the Word, O venerable one, by material fire thou didst offer thyself unto the Lord as a sweet-smelling sacrifice; and thou wast accounted worthy of heavenly joy, ever praying for us who honor thee, O Laurence.

Having counted the commandments of the Lord as a treasure unto thyself, and submitting to His words, O divinely-blessed one, thou didst richly distribute unto the poor an unstolen wealth, O wise martyr, marvelously preparing for thyself the myrrh of martyrdom.

The beauty of martyrs, the glorious and marvelous warrior, fountain of miracles, helper of the oppressed, most luminous star, holy martyr Laurence: let him now be praised radiantly!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 4, Automelon (Самогла́сенъ):*[^278]
Before Thy Cross, O Lord, the mountain resembled heaven, and the cloud spread out as a tabernacle, when Thou wast transfigured, and borne witness to by the Father; there were Peter with James and John, as they were also to be with Thee at the time of Thy betrayal: that having seen Thy wonders, they might not be afraid of Thy sufferings; which grant us to venerate in peace, for the sake of Thy great mercy.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Е҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^279]
Inexpressibly the Lord showed the glory of Divinity on Mount Tabor now unto the disciples whom He willed.

*Verse: Thine are the heavens, and Thine is the earth [LXX Ps. 88:12].*

Come, let us all run with the disciples unto the height of virtues, that with them we also may be accounted worthy of grace.

*Verse: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name [LXX Ps. 88:13].*

We earthborn, let us cleanse our hearts, so that straightway the glory of the thrice-radiant grace may come unto us also.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 1, Automelon (Самогла́сенъ):*[^280]
Before Thy Cross, O Lord, taking the disciples up into a high mountain, Thou wast transfigured before them, illuminating them with a beam of power; desiring on the one hand through love for mankind, and on the other through authority, to show forth the brightness of the Resurrection: of which make us also worthy in peace, O God, for Thou art merciful and the Lover of mankind.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^281]
Thy martyr Laurence, O Lord, through his suffering received an incorruptible crown from Thee, our God. For having Thy strength, he cast down the torturers, and crushed the powerless boldness of demons. By his intercessions, save our souls.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 7:*[^282]
Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, O Christ God, having shown Thy glory unto Thy disciples as far as they could bear it. Let Thine everlasting light shine forth also upon us sinners, through the intercessions of the Birthgiver of God: O Giver of light, glory to Thee!

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^283]
Having been transfigured upon Mount Tabor, O Savior, Thou didst show Thy glory unto the foremost disciples, O Christ, and didst flash forth the immutable radiance of Divinity; a bright cloud brought together Elias and Moses, speaking with Thee. Therefore Peter also said: It is good for us to be here with Thee. O Thou Who didst then make Thy light shine forth unto them, enlighten our souls.

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, the same tone and podoben [Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled"] (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*
In Thine unapproachable glory, O Christ, being transfigured in divine light, Thou didst shine upon Thy divine disciples, John, and Peter, and James, and didst fill them with awe by divine grace. And they heard the Father's voice bearing witness to the beloved Son, and beheld the glory of Thy countenance, O Savior. O Word, Who desirest to save all men, enlighten our souls.

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Canon of the Feast, with the heirmos to 8, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^284]
Having passed through the water as on dry land, and having escaped from the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparion:*
Moses at the sea prophetically seeing of old the glory of the Lord in the cloud and pillar of fire, cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparion:*
Covered by the body as by a stone, beholding the Invisible One by deification, Moses the God-seer cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast seen by Moses upon the mountain of the Law and upon Tabor: of old in darkness, but now in the unapproachable light of Divinity.

*Another Canon, of the Saint, to 4; whose acrostic is: "I sing of the mighty and all-wise Laurence." Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^285]
O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, drown the mighty captains in the deep of dispassion, the three parts of my soul, I pray Thee: that unto Thee, as upon a timbrel, in the mortification of the body I may sing a triumphal song.

*Troparion:*
Worthily rejoicing in the flower-garden of delight as a warrior of Christ, and dancing in chorus with the angelic powers,[^286] pray that light-bearing radiance may be granted unto me who hymn thee, O blessed Laurence.

*Troparion:*
Having entered into the struggles of honorable martyrdom as a victorious sufferer, thou wast shown forth by firmness of soul, O Laurence, beauteously adorned with the crown of righteousness and the diadem of victory.

*Troparion:*
Having become a son of light and of the day, most gloriously from the west, as a visible sun thou didst shine forth upon us, illuminating the ends of the earth with a most bright radiance, O most glorious martyr Laurence.

*Theotokion:*
Having been delivered by thy precious childbearing, O Virgin, from the bonds of hades and corruption, and from the condemnation of the Law, O most pure one, with thanksgiving we cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace, saving door of goodness!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^287]
O Lord, Artificer of the vault of heaven and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
The overshadowing glory in the tabernacle of old, and to Thy servant Moses who beheld God, was an image of Thine ineffable Transfiguration, O Master, that shone forth upon Tabor.

*Troparion:*
There ascended with Thee, O only-begotten Word, the highest chiefs of the apostles, upon Mount Tabor; and Moses and Elias stood beside Thee as servants of God, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Being God the Word, He became completely earthly, uniting humanity to all Divinity in His hypostasis; Whom Moses and Elias beheld in two natures upon Mount Tabor.

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^288]
The bow of the mighty has grown weak, and the feeble have girded themselves with strength: therefore my heart is established in the Lord.

*Troparion:*
Having the victory of the Cross, valiantly didst thou array thyself against adversaries, and wast shown forth as a crown-bearer of victory, O marvel-worthy one.

*Troparion:*
Fenced about by the law of Christ, as unconquerable thou didst oppose the statutes of the ungodly[^289] by noble patience, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by the power of God, Thou didst destroy the weakness of polytheism, and didst make clear the eternal Divinity of Christ.

*Theotokion:*
Equal to the Father and the Spirit in nature, essence, and Divinity, the Word became like unto men, being incarnate of thee, O all-immaculate one.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^290]
Having kindled thy heart with divine fire, the fire of passions thou didst burn away utterly unto the end, O God-bearing martyr Laurence, confirmation of sufferers; and in thy sufferings thou didst cry out faithfully: Nothing shall separate me from the love of Christ!

*Ikos:*[^291]
Him who shone forth as a most radiant luminary in the world, having assembled, all we faithful, let us honor Laurence the passion-bearer with hymns, as an initiate of ineffable things: delivered by his prayers from grievous transgressions, having cleansed our hearts, let us glorify Christ Who glorifies him, who was steadfast in suffering, saying: Nothing shall separate me from the love of Christ!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "The Wisdom and Word" (Премꙋ́дрости):*[^292]
Having gathered heavenly wealth, and having brought it to the poor, thou didst dispense it; thou didst give thy bread unto the hungry, and having acquired thereby incorruptible life, thou didst shine forth in the confession of Christ, O glorious one. Therefore, having acted manly, and having lawfully suffered, thou didst receive from God the crown of thy labors, O passion-bearer Laurence. Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who with love honor thy holy memory.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, the same tone [Tone 8], Podoben: "That which was commanded mystically" (Повелѣ́нное та́инственно):*[^293]
The lightning concealed beneath the flesh of Thy nature, O Christ, and of Thy divine majesty, Thou didst show upon the holy mountain, O Benefactor, shining forth unto the disciples who were with Thee. And having perceived Thine unendurable glory, they cried out: Holy art Thou! For being unapproachable, Thou wast seen in the flesh by the world, O only Lover of mankind.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^294]
From Thy flesh beams of Divinity went forth unto the prophets and the apostles. Therefore their chiefs singing cried out: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
O Thou Who didst preserve the bush unconsumed when it touched the fire: unto Moses Thou didst show Thy God-radiant flesh, O Master, as he sang: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
The sensible sun was hidden by the radiance of Divinity, when upon Mount Tabor it saw Thee transfigured, O my Jesus: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
An immaterial fire, not consuming the substance of the body, was seen; as unto Moses and Elias, so unto the apostles Thou didst appear, O Master, One of the two, perfect in two natures.

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^295]
Sitting in glory upon the throne of Divinity on a swift cloud, Jesus the most divine came with incorruptible hand, and saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
A minister of the Word, and adorned by the Word, through love for the Word in the disposition of his soul he is slaughtered; and with the Word he now rightly reigns, filled with His joy and glory.

*Troparion:*
With the slumber of impiety piously thou didst not slumber, by divine vigilance; through thy martyric endurance thou didst shake off from thine eyes the sleep that is unto death, O holy martyr of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Against the adversaries' impious resistance, fenced about with the true weapons of piety, thou didst cast down their memory utterly unto the end, for the sake of faith and through love of virtue.

*Theotokion:*
Having put away all the defilement of the first food, we are nourished by the Bread of life from heaven, Who shone forth from the virgin earth; whom, as the mediatress of blessings, let us hymn.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^296]
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O Light never-setting, and an alien darkness has covered me, a wretch? But turn me back, and direct my ways unto the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee!

*Troparion:*
What majesty can an eloquent tongue not declare? For as Holding life and Ruling over death, Thou didst place upon Mount Tabor Moses and Elias, who bore witness to Thy Divinity.

*Troparion:*
Having fashioned man according to Thine image with invisible hands, O Christ, Thou didst show forth Thy primal beauty in the creation: not as in an image, but as Thyself being in essence God and man.

*Troparion:*
Being united without confusion, Thou didst show us a burning coal of Divinity, consuming sins and enlightening souls upon Mount Tabor; and Thou didst astonish Moses with Elias, and the foremost among the disciples.

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^297]
The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ; but we, rising early from the night, hymn Thee, O only-begotten Son, Radiance of the Father's glory of Divinity, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Gazing with unwavering vision upon divine beauty, O Laurence, thou didst despise all the fair things that are upon earth, and the grievous bodily pains, O marvel-worthy one.

*Troparion:*
Having recognized Christ, Who became a servant for our sake, as gifts from the Father, having become His minister, unto Him thou didst depart by blood, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
As a sacred oblation and a beauteous adornment thou didst offer thyself unto Christ, O blessed one;

thou didst make clear the bright tabernacle, where now dwelling, thou dost enjoy the radiance.

*Theotokion:*
Being co-natural and co-form with the Father, the only-begotten Son voluntarily became kin to men; the Most High was incarnate from thy womb, O Virgin Mother.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^298]
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee: for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparion:*
How great and fearful a vision was seen today! From heaven, indeed, the sensible sun shone forth, but from the earth the incomparable, intelligible Sun of righteousness upon Mount Tabor.

*Troparion:*
The shadow of the Law has passed away, having grown feeble, and Christ the Truth has manifestly come! cried Moses upon Tabor, having seen Thy Divinity.

*Troparion:*
The pillar of fire most clearly foreshowed Christ transfigured, and the cloud manifestly prefigured the grace of the Spirit that overshadowed upon Tabor.

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^299]
I am come into the depths of the sea, and the tempest of my many sins has submerged me: but as God, lead up my life from the abyss, O greatly Merciful One!

*Troparion:*
The common fire roasted thee, and prepared a sweet banquet for the King of all, Who loves our salvation, O most glorious Laurence.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst draw near entirely to the Light of the primal Trinity; as being Its minister, thou wast light-like, O passion-bearer, illuminating those who hymn thee.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by divine power, lying upon the fiery gridiron, thou didst endure, O martyr, through the love of Christ, inflaming thy soul with fire and spiritual dew.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast, O Virgin Mother of God, a spacious receptacle, an animate throne, a holy mountain, and an ark, a tabernacle fashioned by God, and a gold-radiant lampstand.

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 7:*[^300]
Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, and as far as they could bear it Thy disciples beheld Thy glory, O Christ God; that when they should see Thee crucified, they might understand Thy suffering to be voluntary, and might proclaim to the world that Thou art truly the Father's Radiance.

*Ikos:*
Arise, ye sluggish thoughts of my soul, that ever bow down to the earth! Be lifted up and raised unto the height of divine ascent! Let us hasten to Peter and the sons of Zebedee, and together with them let us reach Mount Tabor, that with them we may behold the glory of our God, and may hear the voice which they heard from on high, and proclaimed the Father's Radiance!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^301]
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Now the invisible things were seen by the apostles: Divinity in the flesh, shining forth upon Mount Tabor unto them who cry: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
The apostles were struck with fear, marveling at the majesty of the divine Kingdom upon Mount Tabor, crying out: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Now things unheard of were heard: for without a father the Son of the Virgin is gloriously testified to by the Father's voice, that He is God and man, the same unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast not Son of the Most High by adoption, but beloved Son by essence, existing beforehand, Thou hast drawn near unto us immutably: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^302]
O Thou Who didst save Thine Abrahamite children in the fire, and didst slay the Chaldeans whom justice rightly entrapped: O supremely-praised Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Forechosen to be an initiator of mysteries and to serve the Word, thou wast shown forth as a sacred vessel and oblation of the heavenly temple, singing unto Him Who gathered thee: O supremely-praised Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Fenced about by the law of life which is in Christ, O all-blessed one, thou didst not bow thy mind unto the lawgivers of death and corruption, singing, O Laurence: O supremely-praised Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
As one manifestly without suffering, though compassed about with the body, O all-blessed one, with noble resolve thou didst venture against all-devouring fire, crying aloud, O exceedingly rich one: O supremely-praised Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown to be the spiritual door of the Orient on high, Who appeared upon earth unto men from thee, O Bride of God, beyond word and understanding, God of the fathers, Who is blessed!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^303]
Sevenfold the Chaldean tyrant madly kindled the furnace for the godly ones; but having seen them saved by a greater power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having heard Thee testified to by the Father, O Master, and being unable to endure the shining of Thy countenance, stronger than the human visage, Thy disciples fell prostrate upon the earth, singing with fear: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou art the all-beauteous King of kings and Lord of lords everywhere, mighty, blessed, and dwelling in light unapproachable; at Whom the disciples marveled, and Moses and Elias cried aloud: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
As Ruling over heaven, and Holding dominion over the earth, and Having authority over the nether regions, there stood before Thee, O Christ: from the earth, the apostles; as from heaven, Elias the Thesbite; and Moses from the dead, singing in unison: Ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having left behind upon earth the griefs that engender despondency, O Lover of mankind, the chosen of the apostles followed Thee unto the divine way of life that translates from earth; therefore, having also worthily obtained Thy Theophany, they sang: Ye people, supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^304]
O all-powerful Deliverer of all, having descended into the midst of the flame, Thou didst bedew the godly ones, and didst teach them to sing: All ye works, bless and hymn the Lord!

*Troparion:*
With steadfast mind thou wast stronger than the unendurable flame, as though being burned in an alien body, O blessed one, singing with faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Emulating the three children, thou didst extinguish the coals of fire by the dew of divine grace, crying out and singing: All ye works, bless and hymn the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Christ having become thine illumination, girded thee with His strength, and brought thee up unto Himself as thou didst piously sing: All ye works, bless and hymn the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast a rod from the root of Jesse, and beyond nature didst bud forth Christ, my God and Lord, the flower of Divinity: let all works bless thy childbearing, O Virgin!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^305]
Every ear was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God: how the Most High voluntarily descended even unto the flesh, being made man from a virginal womb; therefore, we faithful magnify the most pure Birthgiver of God!

*Troparion:*
That Thou mightest clearly show Thine ineffable second coming, that Thou mightest appear as God the Most High, standing in the midst of the gods the apostles upon Tabor, Thou didst ineffably illumine Moses and Elias: therefore, all of us magnify Thee, O Christ!

we magnify Thee, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
Come, obey Me, ye peoples; ascending the holy and heavenly mountain, let us stand immaterially in the city of the living God, and mentally behold the immaterial Divinity of the Father and of the Spirit shining forth in the only-begotten Son!

*Troparion:*
Thou hast drawn me by love, O Savior, and transformed me by Thy divine longing; but burn up my sins with immaterial fire, and account me worthy to be filled with Thy delight, that leaping with joy in both, I may magnify Thy majesties, O Good One!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^306]
Eve indeed brought in the curse by the disease of disobedience; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, didst blossom forth blessing unto the world through the offshoot of thy womb: therefore, all of us magnify thee!

*Troparion:*
As a perfect slaughter and an acceptable censing thou didst offer thyself unto the Master, tried like gold in the furnace by the fire of suffering, and having become the adornment of the Church of the firstborn, O marvel-worthy one.

*Troparion:*
Deified by an unwavering gaze toward God, and by superior union, by the Word and by divine contemplation, with supreme light, O Laurence, thou wast radiantly enlightened: therefore, all of us call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
As a sun, O blessed one, thou didst shine forth from the west, a great and most glorious wonder, enlightening all the Church with thy flashings, O marvel-worthy one, and warming all by the heat of faith: therefore, all of us call thee blessed.

*Theotokion:*
The prophets, beholding the types of thy childbearing, O all-immaculate one, instructed therein from afar by the divine inspiration of the Spirit, loudly proclaimed them unto the world; at whose fulfillment we now marvel!

*Exapostilarion of the Feast:*[^307]
O immutable Light, Word of the unbegotten Light the Father: in Thy manifest light today on Tabor have we seen light the Father, and light the Spirit, guiding all creation by light.

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "The Angelic Powers" (А҆́ггелскїѧ):*[^308]
Having shone forth beyond understanding from the all-pure Virgin, O Word of God, clothed entirely in Adam, Thou didst enlighten all our nature that was darkened before, by the divine changing of Thy form, being transfigured upon Mount Tabor, O Master, before Thy precious Cross, O Christ our God!

*Verse: Thine are the heavens, and Thine is the earth [LXX Ps. 88:12].*

The mountain that once was dark and smoky is now honorable and holy: for He Who formerly guided Israel, as it is written, by a pillar of fire and a cloud, today has shone forth ineffably brighter than the sun, Christ, as God enlightening all things upon it!

*Verse: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name [LXX Ps. 88:13].*

Cephas beheld on Tabor two men serving Him as servants; It is good for us to be here, he cried: Let us make three tabernacles, if Thou willest, unto each: one for Thee, my Christ, and for Moses and Elias Thy servants; not knowing what he said, for he was held by dread.

*Glory... Both now... the same tone [Tone 6]:*[^309]
O pure, all-holy Birthgiver of God, deliver from afflictions those who trust in thee, and save from every harm those who pray unto thee night and day, now and ever, and deliver them from the future judgment, entreating thy Son, Who ineffably shone forth upon Tabor.

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 5, of both canons, with the heirmos to 6.*[^310]

*The same month, on the 11th day: Holy Martyr and Archdeacon Euplus.*[^311]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*[^312]
He Who spoke with Moses of old upon Mount Sinai in types, saying: I am God Who is; today, having been transfigured upon Mount Tabor, showed forth the archetype, flashing with rays: therefore, O Christ, I magnify Thy power!

Taking Thy disciples apart, Peter, James, and John, O Christ, voluntarily Thou didst lead them up into the mountain, showing a fearful wonder, and the unendurable, everlasting majesty of Thy divine coming from afar.

The apostles of old, trembling at Thine incomprehensible coming, O Christ, fell prostrate upon the earth, marveling at Thy divine power; for Thou didst shine forth brighter than the sun, O Good One, by Thine ineffable power!

*Other Stichera of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́меніе):*[^313]
Well didst thou traverse the abyss of suffering, having sailed safely, O exceedingly rich one exceedingly rich one, by the inspiration of the Spirit, and hast hastened unto the quiet haven of the Kingdom of God, wherein thou hast richly received the recompense of the ends of thy labors, and thou standest before the Master of creation with the angels, O blessed crown-bearer.

Clothed in a purple robe reddened with thy blood, O glorious one, and crowned with the wreath of victory by the life-originating hand, thou dwellest in the heavens, reigning together with the All-Compassionate One. Therefore, we call thee blessed, as a true martyr, a minister of the Word, and a confirmation of the faith.

Euplus the marvelous, having become a minister of eternal mysteries, led himself as an innocent sheep, being slain by the sword, an oblation of sweet fragrance and a divine lamb. Him therefore do we call blessed, sacredly celebrating his holy suffering today, ye lovers of the martyrs!

*Glory... Both now... the same tone [Tone 4]:*[^314]
The mountain that once was dark and smoky is now honorable and holy, whereon Thy feet stood, O Lord; for Thine awesome Transfiguration made manifest at the end of days the mystery hidden from before the ages unto Peter, John, and James. Being unable to endure such radiance of Thy countenance and the brightness of Thy garments, they fell face downwards upon the face of the earth; and held by dread, they marveled, beholding Moses and Elias speaking with Thee of the things that were about to happen unto Thee; and the voice from the Father bore witness, saying: This is My beloved Son, in Whom I am well pleased; give ear unto Him, Who also grants great mercy unto the world!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Е҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^315]
Willing to renew the nature which was in Adam, Christ now ascends Mount Tabor, unveiling His Divinity unto the disciples.

*Verse: Thine are the heavens, and Thine is the earth [LXX Ps. 88:12].*

The thrice-radiant light of Thy divine glory shone forth ineffably upon Mount Tabor, O Savior, illuminating all things.

*Verse: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name [LXX Ps. 88:13].*

Suddenly beholding the change of garments and the goodness of Thy body, O Word, the disciples fell prostrate and were dazed.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 1:*[^316]
Having been transfigured upon a high mountain, O Savior, having the foremost disciples, Thou didst shine forth most gloriously, showing that having shone with the height of virtues, they also shall be accounted worthy of divine glory. As Moses and Elias spoke with Christ, they showed that He rules over the living and the dead, and is God Who spoke of old through the Law and the Prophets; unto Whom also the Father's voice bore witness from a bright cloud, saying: Give ear unto Him, Who by the Cross has despoiled hades, and grants eternal life unto the dead!

*Troparion of the Saint, Tone 4:*[^317]
Thy martyr Euplus, O Lord, in his suffering received an imperishable crown from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, he cast down the torturers, and crushed the powerless audacities of the demons. By his prayers, save our souls!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 7:*[^318]
Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, O Christ God, showing Thy glory unto Thy disciples as far as they were able to bear it; let Thine everlasting Light shine forth also upon us sinners, through the prayers of the Birthgiver of God: O Giver of light, glory to Thee!

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^319]
Thou wast transfigured upon Mount Tabor, O God, in the midst of the all-wise Elias and Moses, together with James and Simon and John. And Peter being there, said these things unto Thee: It is good to make three tabernacles here: one for Moses, and one for Elias, and one for Thee, Christ the Master. O Thou Who didst then cause Thy light to shine upon them, enlighten our souls!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, the same tone [Tone 4], Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*[^320]
The most radiant feast of the Master is come: come, all ye, having pre-purified ourselves, let us mentally ascend Mount Tabor to behold Christ!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Canon of the Feast, with the heirmos to 8. The creation of Kyr Cosmas. Tone 4.*[^321]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^322]
The companies of Israel, having passed through the Red Sea and the watery deep with unwetted feet, and beholding the enemy commanders submerged therein, sang with rejoicing: Let us sing unto our God, for He has been glorified!

*Troparion:*
Christ, teaching His friends words of life and instructing them concerning the divine Kingdom, said: Recognize the Father in Me, as I shine with unapproachable light, singing in joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He has been glorified!

*Troparion:*
Ye shall be clothed with the power of the Gentiles, O friend-disciples, and ye shall be marvelous in their wealth, for ye shall be filled with glory; for I shall appear shining brighter than the sun, among those who sing in joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He has been glorified!

Today Christ upon Mount Tabor, having shone clearly with divine radiance as He had promised, unveiled His form unto the disciples; and filled with light-bearing divine brightness, they sang in joy: Let us sing unto our God, for He has been glorified!

*Another Canon, of the Saint, to 4. Its acrostic: "Piously I praise the glorious Euplus." The creation of Joseph. Tone 4.*[^323]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^324]
O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, drown, I pray Thee, the three parts of my soul in the depth of dispassion, the mighty commanders, that as on a timbrel, in the mortification of the body, I may sing unto Thee a triumphal song!

*Troparion:*
Having safely traversed the sea of life by the weapon of the Cross, thou didst enter into the haven of life; dwelling joyfully therein, O glorious one, calm the turbulent surging of my heart, that I may serenely praise thy memory.

*Troparion:*
As a bright star illuminating the faithful with virtuous teachings, thou didst shine forth, O wise one, upon the honorable firmament of the Church, O Euplus, and didst drive away demonic darkness by spiritual power.

*Troparion:*
Illumined by divine power, O wise one, with bold resolve thou didst preach God born of God, Who voluntarily endured crucifixion, and by death destroyed death.

*Troparion:*
Let every mouth be moved unto thy praise, for from the mouths of enemies by divine teachings, O God-bearer, thou didst snatch away all who obediently received thy words, O valiant passion-bearer of Christ!

*Theotokion:*
Having illuminated thy light-bearing womb by an awesome descent, O pure one, Christ shone forth as the sun unto those who are in the world, dispelling the dark mist of polytheism and enlightening the ends of the world.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^325]
The bow of the mighty has grown feeble, and the weak have girded themselves with power; therefore, my heart is established in the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Clothed in all of Adam, O Christ, changing the nature that had grown dark of old, Thou didst enlighten it, and by the change of Thy form Thou didst make it godlike.

*Troparion:*
He Who guided Israel of old in the wilderness with a pillar of fire and a cloud, today upon Mount Tabor, Christ has ineffably shone forth in light.

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^326]
As the barren church from the nations bore fruit, and the assembly that was rich in children grew feeble, let us cry unto our marvelous God: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst deliver thyself as a lamb, voluntarily desiring to be slain desiring for the sake of Christ, Who voluntarily became poor for thy sake, and was slain, O all-blessed Euplus!

*Troparion:*
An immaculate slaughter, an oblation of sweet fragrance, and a perfect offering, thou didst offer thyself unto God, joyfully crying aloud, O blessed one: Holy art Thou, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Established by evangelical laws, O godly-wise one, thou didst stand in the midst of lawless men, crying out openly: I have been found by them that sought me not, to refute their impiety!

*Theotokion:*
God, having dwelt in the midst of thee, in no wise shook the key of thy virginity, O pure one; Whom do thou earnestly entreat to establish all who hymn thee.

*Kontakion of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Ли́къ а҆́ггелскїй):*[^327]
Carrying about the laws of Christ in thy hand, thou didst stand crying unto the enemies in the contest: Unbidden have I come to struggle most valiantly! Therefore, gladly bowing thy neck, thou didst yield unto the beheading by the sword, having finished thy course.

*Sessional Hymn [of the Saint], Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*[^328]
Having safely sailed through the sea of life by the weapon of the Cross, thou didst enter into the quiet haven of the heavenly mansions; wherein dwelling, for those who today celebrate thy memory in supplication for thy struggles, pray unto the Creator that we may be delivered from every necessity and affliction.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^329]
Having been transfigured by the unapproachable glory of Thy divine light, O Christ, Thou didst illuminate Thy divine disciples, John and Peter and James; Thou didst astonish them by divine grace. And they heard the Father's voice bearing witness unto the beloved Son, and beheld the glory of Thy countenance, O Savior. O Word Who desirest to save all, enlighten our souls!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^330]
I heard of Thy glorious dispensation, O Christ God, how Thou wast born of the Virgin that Thou mightest deliver from deceit those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Prefiguring the Law in Sinai by writing, O Christ God, Thou wast seen borne in a cloud, in fire, in darkness, and in a tempest: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
That Thou mightest confirm Thy glorious dispensation, O Christ God, as being before the ages, and the same Who didst make a cloud Thy chariot, Thou didst ineffably shine forth upon Tabor.

*Troparion:*
Standing before Thee as servants, they spoke together with Thee, Christ the Master, unto whom Thou didst converse through the vapor of fire, and darkness, and a gentle breeze: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

Having arrived upon Tabor, Moses, who foresaw Thee of old in the fire and the burning bush, and Elias, who was taken up in a fiery chariot, proclaimed the glorious things that should come to pass because of Thy Cross, O Christ.

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^331]
Out of love for Thine image, O Compassionate One, Thou didst stand upon Thy Cross, and the nations melted away; for Thou, O Lover of mankind, art my strength and praise!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast truly shown forth as an animate temple of the All-Holy Trinity, and thou didst cast down idol temples, O Euplus, by the weapon of faith.

*Troparion:*
Enjoying divine beauty, O Euplus, thou didst disregard wounds, sicknesses, and every torment, O blessed one, and didst suffer rejoicing.

*Troparion:*
Having humbled thyself, O godly-wise one, through thy suffering thou didst humble the proud heart of Flaminius, and didst ascend unto Him Who exalted men.

*Troparion:*
Kindled with zeal for the ages, O martyr, thou didst truly wipe away every deceit, and didst zealously separate thyself for martyrdom.

*Theotokion:*
Thy rich people entreat the Maiden who bore the Mother, Christ our God, Who enriched all with divine understanding.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^332]
O Thou Who didst cut off the firstborn light from darkness, that works might hymn Thee in the light, O Christ the Creator: direct our paths in Thy light!

*Troparion:*
The mountains bowed before Thy face; for the Light also before Thy feet, immaterial rays, Christ the Sun came, as Thou wast pleased to assume an earthly form.

*Troparion:*
Lo, the Savior! cried Moses and Elias unto the disciples instructed upon the holy Mount Tabor: Christ, Whom we proclaimed of old as God Who is!

*Troparion:*
The immutable nature, having mingled with the human, ineffably shone forth, unveiling abundantly unto the apostles the light of immaterial Divinity.

*Troparion:*
Having beheld Thee shining forth as the everlasting Radiance in the Father's glory, O Christ, the disciples cried unto Thee: Direct our paths in Thy light!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^333]
The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ; but we, rising early from the night, hymn Thee, O only-begotten Son, Radiance of the Father's glory of Divinity, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Entirely illumined by divine nods, and strengthened with courage like a chosen lion, thou didst run unto the tribunal, hoping in God, conquering the impious!

*Troparion:*
The Deliverer grants thee untroubled life, for Whose sake, O glorious passion-bearer, having endured a bitter death, He numbers thee with the choirs of valiant martyrs.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst bow thy neck with zealous resolve, and being beheaded, thou didst draw near unto Christ more clearly and purely, O venerable one, enjoying deification.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast shown to be higher than the angels in truth, having borne beyond understanding the Creator of the angels, Who glorified the holy martyrs, for He numbered them, O Virgin.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^334]
When I was afflicted, I cried unto the Lord, and the God of my salvation heard me!

*Troparion:*
A light clearer than the sun's radiance, the Savior, having shone upon Tabor, has enlightened us.

*Troparion:*
Having ascended Mount Tabor, Thou wast transfigured, O Christ, and having darkened all deceit, Thou hast enlightened us.

*Troparion:*
Having recognized Thee as God, the glorious apostles marveled upon Tabor, O Christ, and bowed their knees.

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^335]
I am come into the depths of the sea, and the tempest of my many sins has submerged me; but as God, lead up my life from the abyss, O greatly Merciful One!

*Troparion:*
Streaming with blood, thou didst stand before the Founder of thy contest, O valiant, godly-wise Euplus; and from Him thou didst receive the crowns of victory, rejoicing.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stand manfully before the judgment seat, holding in thy hands the Master's laws, O Euplus; and manifestly and zealously didst refute the dispute of the lawless.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst not fear the multitude of torments, nor didst thou dread the sword: on the tribunal of the torturers thou didst piously preach the truth, and didst put deceit to death.

*Theotokion:*
O marvelous wonder! How dost thou give birth, having known no man? And how dost thou nourish with milk, O Virgin Mother, the Giver of milk and Nourisher of all creation?

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 7:*[^336]
Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, and as far as they were able Thy disciples beheld Thy glory, O Christ God; that when they should see Thee crucified, they might understand Thy suffering to be voluntary, and might proclaim to the world that Thou art truly the Father's Radiance.

*Ikos:*[^337]
Arise, ye sluggish thoughts of my soul, that ever bow down to the earth! Be lifted up and raised unto the height of divine ascent! Let us hasten let us hasten to Peter and the sons of Zebedee, and together with them let us reach Mount Tabor, that with them we may behold the glory of our God, and may hear the voice which they heard from on high, and proclaimed the Father's Radiance!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^338]
The Abrahamite children in Babylon of old trampled upon the flame of the furnace, crying aloud in hymns: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the light of unapproachable glory upon Mount Tabor, the apostles cried unto Christ: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Delighting in the radiance of the divine sound, in the dew-bearing cloud, O Christ, and in Thy brightness, the apostles sang: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
When Peter beheld Thee shining in unapproachable light upon Mount Tabor, O Christ, he cried out: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Being together with Christ, the Author of life, when He sent forth the light of His form, the sons of Zebedee thundered: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^339]
The three youths in Babylon, having turned the tyrant's command into foolishness, cried aloud in the midst of the flame: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thy blood dripping upon the earth extinguished the fire of deceit, O all-blessed one, and richly watered the Church of Christ, which piously hymns thee.

*Troparion:*
Having steadfast wisdom and an unshakable mind, thou didst shake deceit, and by truthful teachings didst refute those who championed falsehood, O all-wise Euplus.

*Troparion:*
The door of paradise was opened unto thee, O martyr of Christ, Euplus, O blessed one, beholding thee adorned with divine suffering and the wreath of victory.

*Troparion:*
Having purified thy soul with divine burning coal, O martyr, thou didst extinguish the coals of polytheism, crying: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
The Persian furnace prefigured thy divine womb, O all-immaculate one, saving the youths unconsumed, who cry aloud: O Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^340]
In Babylon, the children, inflamed with divine zeal, manfully trampled upon the tyrant and the threat of the flame; and cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed, they sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
He Who carries all things by His nod, ascended Mount Tabor with His most pure feet, Christ: whereon He shone with His face brighter than the rays of the sun, and showed forth the foremost of the Law and grace, who sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
In unapproachable glory, the uncontainable and never-setting Light, the Father's Radiance, appeared ineffably upon Mount Tabor, illuminating creation and deifying men, who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Standing in sacred dignity upon Mount Tabor, Moses and Elias, clearly beholding the Hypostasis of the divine character, Christ shining in the Father's glory, sang: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
The face of Moses was glorified of old because of the appearance in divine form; but Christ is clothed with light and glory as with a garment: for being the Author of light Himself, He illumines those who sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Encompassed by the light-bearing cloud, the disciples, beholding Christ upon Tabor and falling face downwards upon the earth, having their minds enlightened, sang together with the Father and the Spirit: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^341]
O all-powerful Deliverer of all, having descended into the midst of the flame, Thou didst bedew the godly ones, and didst teach them to sing: All ye works, bless and hymn the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thy relics are the cleansing of every sickness and the expulsion of cruel spirits, standing around which we cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
The assembly of the faithful rejoiced in thee, having acquired thee as an intercessor before Christ, a fervent petitioner, and a protector, O all-blessed deacon Euplus, companion of the holy angels.

*Troparion:*
Coming together, O men, draw forth healing from diverse diseases from the martyr's shrine, and cry aloud with faith: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Adorned beyond every angelic beauty, O pure and all-immaculate one, having given birth to the Creator of men and angels, unto Whom we sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^342]
Thy childbearing was shown forth imperishable: God came forth from thy womb, as He appeared flesh-bearing upon earth and lived among men; therefore, O Birthgiver of God, all of us magnify thee!

*Troparion:*
Terrified by the new downpouring of light, being suddenly illumined, the disciples looked one upon another; and marvelling, and bowing down to the earth, they worshiped Thee, the Master of all.

A divinely-sounding noise was sent forth from the cloud, certifying the wonder: for the Father of lights cried unto the apostles: This is My beloved Son; give ear unto Him!

*Troparion:*
Having seen new and most glorious things, having heard the Father's voice upon Tabor, the ministers of the Word, beholding the Image of the Archetype, cried: This is our Savior!

*Troparion:*
O immutable Image of Him Who is, Unmovable, indelible Seal, O Son, Word, Wisdom, and Arm, Right Hand, and Power of the Most High: Thee do we hymn together with the Father and the Spirit!

*Another [Canon of the Saint]. Heirmos:*[^343]
Eve indeed brought in the curse by the disease of disobedience; but thou, O Virgin Birthgiver of God, didst blossom forth blessing unto the world through the offshoot of thy womb: therefore, all of us magnify thee!

*Troparion:*
Having been accounted worthy truly to behold desirable things, O exceedingly rich one, thou didst acquire the desired longing, as having attained the very completion of desired things, the passing over of thy longing from hence, O deacon Euplus, worthy of marvel!

*Troparion:*
Holding the Cross as a scepter in thy hand, O blessed one, having dipped the purple robe in thy sacred blood, radiantly robed, thou standest now before Christ the King, O deacon Euplus, worthy of marvel!

*Troparion:*
Today the honorable metropolis of Catana,[^344] having acquired thee as its preeminent guardian, celebrates, performing thy divine suffering, together with all the eparchies and cities, which do thou preserve by thy supplications!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast manifestly an all-radiant sun, and hast passed over unto the divine brightness, O martyr, deified by divine communion, O blessed one; and now thou enlightenest those who hymn thy light-bearing suffering.

*Theotokion:*
By the rays of light that are in thee, O Bride of God, enlighten my soul; raise it up as it lies in the pit of destruction, shattering the enemies that ever afflict my heart and drag me down unto passions.

*Exapostilarion of the Feast:*[^345]
O immutable Light, Word of the unbegotten Light the Father: in Thy manifest light today on Tabor have we seen light the Father, and light the Spirit, guiding all creation by light.

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Е҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^346]
The Trinity of divine apostles rejoices, which saw the Trinity, One in essence, upon Mount Tabor.

*Verse: Thine are the heavens, and Thine is the earth [LXX Ps. 88:12].*

As unto thee, O Tabor, that once wast dark: for thou wast accounted worthy of light, not of the sensible sun, but of the brightness from on high.

*Verse: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name [LXX Ps. 88:13].*

Illumined by the light of Thy face, Thy disciples, O Savior, unable to endure the radiance, suddenly fell unto the earth.

*Glory... Both now... the same tone and podoben [Tone 2] (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
And the Father's voice was heard from heaven, crying: This is My beloved Son; give ear unto Him, all ye!

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 6 of both canons, with the heirmos to 6.*[^347]

*The same month, on the 12th day: Holy Martyrs Photios and Anicetus.*[^348]
*The service is sung together with them also of our venerable father Maximus the Confessor.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Martyrs, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́меніе):*[^349]
Ye feared not the fire, nor the cutting instrument, nor the fury of the fiercely raging one threatening madly, nor the assault of lions, nor boiling cauldrons, nor the severing of limbs, nor the threat of a bitter death, which rendered you immortal and gloriously dispatched you unto the immortal God, O valiant sufferers!

A blessed and all-honorable end, worthily desired, did ye receive, and eternal hopes, O valiant ones; ye pour forth unto us streams of wonders, taking away the scorching heat of the passions of souls and bodies of those who ever celebrate your prayerful feast with piety, O all-praised ones!

The glorious Photios and the godly-wise Anicetus, the all-valiant pair, adorned with fertile brightness and richly variegated with the beauty of wounds, rejoices ever with the angels; and unto those who celebrate their glorious and sacred suffering with faith, they grant enlightenment and mercy.

*Other Stichera of the Venerable One [Maximus], the same tone [Tone 4], Podoben: "As noble among the martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^350]

Him Who through tender mercy became man, even as He was well pleased to be understood in two wills and operations, O venerable one, thou didst proclaim, O Maximus, stopping the impure, unbridled mouths of those who glorify Him as of one will and one operation, who through the devil's temptation have spread malice.

With the sinews of thy teachings, O father Maximus, thou didst strangle Pyrrhus the foolish, the false one;[^351] and thou didst endure persecutions and afflictions, O ever-memorable one, being grievously beaten and having thy tongue cut out, and that divine hand ever stretched out unto God, with which writing thou didst accomplish exalted words.

As the reed of a swift-writing scribe dipped in the Spirit, thy holy tongue became, O blessed one, fair-writing by grace upon the tablets of our hearts the law of divine virtues and the certainty of teachings: and the Incarnation unto men in two essences, and Him Who willed to appear in one hypostasis.

*Glory, Tone 8:*  
We honor thee, O our father Maximus, as an instructor of the multitude of monks; for by thy path we have learned truly to walk rightly. Blessed art thou who didst labor for Christ and didst denounce the enemy's power; converser with angels, companion of the venerable and righteous: with them pray thou unto the Lord that our souls may find mercy.

*Now and ever, the same tone [Tone 8]:*  
The bright cloud of the Transfiguration received the darkness of the Law, wherein Moses and Elijah having been, and having been accounted worthy of transcendent glory, said unto God: "Thou art our God, the King of the ages."

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
Let us draw near with zeal unto Mount Tabor, to behold the glory of Christ, the King of all, our Redeemer.

*Verse: Thine are the heavens, and Thine is the earth.*[^352]  
Thou didst appear with Moses and Elijah, O my Savior, and the chosen of the disciples, upon Mount Tabor, having shone brighter than the sun.

*Verse: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name.*[^353]  
They heard the Father's voice proclaiming Thy glory, O my Savior; and the chosen of the disciples fell prone upon the earth.

*Glory, Tone 6:*  
O venerable father, into all the earth went forth the report of thy right deeds; therefore in the heavens thou hast found the reward of thy labors; thou didst destroy the demon hosts, and hast attained unto the angelic ranks, whose blameless life thou didst emulate. Having boldness toward Christ God, entreat peace for our souls.

*Now and ever, Tone 2:*  
Uncovering a little beam of Thy Godhead, O Savior, unto those who ascended with Thee onto the mountain, Thou didst make them lovers of Thy transcendent glory. Therefore they cried out in awe: "It is good for us to be here!" With them we also praise Thee, Christ the Savior, Transfigured unto the ages.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*  
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received crowns of incorruption from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the tormentors and broke the powerless insolence of demons. By their supplications, save our souls.

*Glory, of the Venerable One, Tone 8:*  
Instructor of Orthodoxy, teacher of piety and purity, light of the universe, divinely-inspired ornament of monks, all-wise Maximus: by thy teachings thou hast enlightened all, O spiritual lyre; entreat Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*Now and ever, of the Feast, Tone 7:*  
Thou wast transfigured on the mountain, O Christ God, having shown Thy glory unto Thy disciples as far as they were able to bear it. Let Thy light ever-existing shine also upon us sinners, through the prayers of the Birthgiver of God: O Giver of light, glory to Thee.

*At Matins, after the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
