Having ascended with the disciples onto the mountain, and having shone forth in the Father's glory, Moses and Elijah stand before Thee; for the Law and the Prophets serve Thee as God. Unto Whom also the Begetter, professing natural Sonship, called Thee Son: Whom we hymn with Thee and the Spirit.

*Glory, and now and ever, the same.*

*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, the same tone [Tone 4].*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ):*  
On Mount Tabor Thou wast transfigured, O Jesus, and a bright cloud stretching out like a tabernacle covered the apostles with glory. Therefore also they fell prone upon the earth, unable to bear the sight of the radiance of the inaccessible glory of Thy countenance, O beginningless Savior, Christ God. Thou Who didst then cause Thy light to shine upon them, enlighten our souls.

*Glory, and now and ever, the same.*

*Canon of the Feast, with the heirmos to 6, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Having passed through the water as if dry land, and having escaped the evil of Egypt,[^354] the Israelite cried aloud: "Let us sing unto our Redeemer and God."

Moses on the sea prophetically, having seen of old in the cloud and the pillar of fire the glory of the Lord, cried aloud: "Let us sing unto our Redeemer and God."

Covered with the body as with a rock, beholding through deification the invisible One, Moses the beholder of God cried aloud: "Let us sing unto our Redeemer and God."

Thou wast seen by Moses upon the mountain of the Law and upon Tabor: of old in darkness, but now in the unapproachable light of the Godhead.

*Another Canon of the Martyrs, to 4, whose acrostic is: "I praise the fair pair of martyrs. Joseph." Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^355]  
Having traversed of old the Red Sea's deep with unwetted feet, Israel, by Moses' hands stretched out in the form of a cross, vanquished the power of Amalek in the wilderness.[^356]

*Troparion:*  
Having endured bodily pains, O all-praised ones, with the most steadfast mind of faith ye have passed over unto the painless life, rejoicing, ever praying for us.

*Troparion:*  
Having shielded thyself with the divine weapons of faith for the struggle against bodiless foes, O Anicetus, thou didst steadfastly array thyself; and their armies thou didst destroy by divine power.

*Troparion:*  
The Ontian babbling, O glorious one, and the inexpert Hellenic mind, being enlightened by divine understanding, thou didst put to shame; and thyself, O Anicetus, thou didst wholly give over unto suffering.[^357]

*Theotokion:*  
Pure one, both before giving birth and after giving birth thou hast truly appeared as God's Mother; for beyond word thou didst give birth unto God, Who crowned the holy martyrs who suffered.

*Another Canon of the Venerable One, to 4, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Thou Who by Thy sovereign right hand didst deliver Israel from bondage, O Lord: as Thou didst save him, now save us.

*Troparion:*  
Having acquired the treasure of highest wisdom in thy heart, O venerable one, because of it thou hast enriched the world with teachings.

*Troparion:*  
Living upon earth, thou didst show forth an angelic life, and wast a temple of God, O preacher of Christ, venerable one; therefore thou didst dedicate thyself unto Him.

*Troparion:*  
Thinking the same thing one toward another, in one faith in Thee, O Christ, the venerable ones proclaimed Thee with tongueless mouths like divinely-sounding trumpets.[^358]

*Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, pure Birthgiver of God, who in the latter times didst give birth in the flesh unto the pre-existing Word of God;

Rejoice, wall for us all, and protection of those who honor thee.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
O Lord, Framer of the vault of heaven and Builder of the Church, do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desire, Confirmation of the faithful, sole Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*  
The overshadowing glory in the tabernacle of old, which conversed with Moses Thy servant, was an image of Thy Transfiguration, O Master, which shone forth ineffably on Tabor.

*Troparion:*  
The highest of the apostolic choir ascended with Thee onto Mount Tabor, O only-begotten Word; and Moses and Elijah stood before Thee as servants of God, O sole Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*  
Being God the Word, Thou becamest wholly earthly, having united humanity unto all the Godhead in Thy hypostasis: which Moses and Elijah beheld in two essences upon Mount Tabor.

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: "Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and confirmation."

*Troparion:*  
Having vanquished with Christ the hostile ranks, thou didst receive the crown of victory, O martyr and passion-bearer Anicetus.

*Troparion:*  
Neglecting the flesh as corruptible, Christ's martyrs endured cruel tortures with a steadfast mind.

*Troparion:*  
Filled with living waters, O wise martyrs, ye have stayed the turbid streams of delusion by the shedding of your blood.

*Theotokion:*  
The Lord Who dwells in the heavens took up His abode within thy womb, and hath driven away all delusion from us, O Birthgiver of God.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable One], Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
There is none holy like the Lord, and none righteous like our God, Whom all creation hymns: there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord.[^359]

*Troparion:*  
Drops drip from thy honorable hand, and teachings flow from thy chaste tongue, crying aloud clearly after its excision: Christ in two natures!

*Troparion:*  
Thy hand ceased not crying out by its writings, and the tongue of thy teachings trumpets thy faith, O thrice-blessed speaker of God; for God moved both to speak good things.

*Troparion:*  
In three bodies indeed, yet in one soul, ye wonderfully opposed with one mind, O marvelous fathers, those who evilly take away Christ's passions and double operation.[^360]

*Theotokion:*  
Women indeed give birth, O pure Virgin Mother, but they remain no longer virgins as thou dost; for neither did any of them give birth unto God, but thou alone, O pure Birthgiver of God.

*Kontakion, Tone 6.*  
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Fulfilling the dispensation" (Є҆́же ѡ҆ на́съ):*

The thrice-radiant Light having taken up His abode within thy soul showed thee forth as a chosen vessel, O all-blessed one, manifesting divine things unto the ends of the earth, expounding hard-to-understand conceptions, O blessed one; and clearly preaching to all people the transcendent, beginningless Trinity, O Maximus.

*Ikos:*  
Thou didst show thyself an imitator of the Savior's sufferings, and having Him in thy soul, O all-blessed one, thou didst set ascents in thy heart, O most rich one. Therefore He bestowed grace upon thee from heaven: for thou didst manly and wisely oppose the tyrants, preaching the beginningless, divine, and consubstantial Trinity, and exposing the ill-glorifying and God-fighting ones. Immeasurable afflictions didst thou endure, O all-praised one, the cutting off of thy God-proclaiming tongue, O venerable one, and likewise of thy hand; yet thou didst not cease to speak with boldness and to establish the faithful by thy divine teachings, clearly preaching unto all people the transcendent, beginningless Trinity.

*Sessional Hymn of the Martyrs, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*  
Having cast down the enemy's haughtiness by the endurance of cruel tortures, O passion-bearers, ye now dwell in the heavens rejoicing, O glorious Photios and blessed Anicetus. Therefore ye are blessed unto ages of ages, praying for us unto Christ, who celebrate your memory.

*Glory, of the Venerable One, Tone 3.*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*  
Holding fast divine teaching, thou wast a pillar of Orthodoxy and a bulwark of the faith, O divinely-wise one. In two essences and two wills thou didst proclaim Christ our God, O father Maximus: Him do thou earnestly entreat to grant us great mercy.

*Now and ever, of the Feast, the same tone [Tone 3].*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "The beauty of virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*  
The radiance of Thy divine glory, as far as they were able to bear it, Thou didst show forth unto Thy disciples, O Word of God, having been transfigured upon Mount Tabor. Whereby we also have been enlightened who hymn Thee, O sole immutable and immortal Lord; for in faith we cry unto Thee: "Glory, O Christ, unto Thy kingdom!"

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
From Thy flesh rays of divinity went forth unto prophets and apostles; therefore the leaders singing cried aloud: "Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*  
Thou Who didst preserve unharmed the bush that touched the fire, didst show forth Thy divinely-radiant flesh unto Moses, O Master, as he sang: "Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*  
The sensible sun was hidden by the beam of the Godhead, when upon Mount Tabor it beheld Thee transfigured, O my Jesus: "Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*  
A non-material fire not burning bodily matter was seen: even as Thou didst appear unto Moses, and the apostles, and Elijah, as one of two, in two perfect natures.

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
The Church, beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of righteousness, stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: "Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"[^361]

*Troparion:*  
Kindled with divine love, ye passed zealously through the flame of fire, remaining unconsumed through spiritual dew, O unconquerable martyrs, divine intercessors for our souls.

*Troparion:*  
Having denied the body, being scraped in diverse manners, O sufferers: with all their mind they looked unto God Who granted them endurance for the sake of faith.

*Troparion:*  
Strong as pillars of piety, ye threw down the enemy's walls, and became citizens of the divine city, O martyrs, crying aloud: "Glory to Thy power, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*  
Ye slumbered not with the slumber of impiety, but by divine wakefulness, O martyrs, ye guided those who ever slept in death unto the light of divine knowledge by grace.

*Theotokion:*  
As fair thou didst give birth unto the Fair One, O pure Virgin Mother, Who showed forth the martyrs as fair, who suffered steadfastly and destroyed godlessness.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable One], Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
From the overshadowing mountain, O Word, the prophet divinely foresaw Thee about to become incarnate of the only Birthgiver of God, and with fear he glorified Thy power.[^362]

*Troparion:*  
Likening thyself unto Moses, O divinely-wise one, thou didst intelligently receive the tablets of teaching; for having entered into the darkness of visions, thou wast enriched with the light of understanding.

*Troparion:*  
Emulating Abraham, O divinely-wise one, thou didst bring another Isaac unto the Lord: having sacrificed thine own heart, O father, in the fire of conscience.

*Troparion:*  
Preaching the one Christ, O fathers: ye confess the one Son in two essences, willing according to essence, and operating both.

*Theotokion:*  
Thou didst give birth in time unto the timeless Son, and didst remain incorrupt after giving birth, O pure one, for our sake incarnate in thy womb, O Mother of God, ever-Virgin.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy presence, O never-setting Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, wretched one? But turn me back, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my ways, I pray Thee.[^363]

The eloquent tongue cannot utter Thy majesty; for holding life and ruling over death, Thou didst present upon Mount Tabor Moses and Elijah, who bore witness to Thy Godhead.

Having created man after Thine image with invisible hands, O Christ, Thou didst show forth Thy archetypal beauty in the creation: not as in an image, but as Thyself being in essence, Thou wast God and man.

Having flared up without confusion, Thou wast shown forth unto us as a burning coal of the Godhead, consuming sins and enlightening souls upon Mount Tabor: Thou didst strike with wonder Moses and Elijah, and the chief of the disciples.

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Thou, my Lord, camest as a light into the world: a holy light turning those who hymn Thee in faith from gloomy ignorance.[^364]

*Troparion:*  
Thy body put to death by wounds without number, O Anicetus, became a venerable mediator of incorruptible life unto thee, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*  
Thou wast food for the burning fire, O Photios, partaker of the divine radiance, and true child of the never-setting Day.

*Troparion:*  
They utterly cast off the corruption of fleeting things, having desired the fair things that abide, with one soul, O glorious martyrs.

*Theotokion:*  
Who can tell thy mystery, O Lady Birthgiver of God? For beyond mind and word thou didst ineffably give birth unto God.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable One], Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Rising early from the night we cry aloud unto Thee: O Lord, save us; for Thou art our God, and beside Thee we know none other.[^365]

*Troparion:*  
The sword of thy boldness, O father, was not cut off; exile, O venerable one, quenched not thy faith.

*Troparion:*  
Rome indeed proclaims thy zeal, O God-bearer, and the Church trumpets thy words, O venerable one.[^366]

*Troparion:*  
Having proclaimed the Savior before princes, ye became martyrs by choice, O divinely-wise ones.

*Theotokion:*  
We hymn thee as the fountain of immortality, O Birthgiver of God, for thou didst give birth unto the ever-flowing Life for the world.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and bring me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee: for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou hear me, O God of my salvation.[^367]

*Troparion:*  
How great and fearful a vision was seen today! From heaven indeed the sensible sun, but from the earth the incomparable noetic Sun of righteousness shone forth upon Mount Tabor.

"The failing legal shadow hath passed away, and Christ the Truth hath manifestly arrived!" cried Moses on Tabor, having seen Thy Godhead.

The pillar of fire and the cloud clearly prefigured most manifestly Christ transfigured and the grace of the Spirit that overshadowed on Tabor.

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy side.[^368]

*Troparion:*  
Thou wast mercilessly lifted up upon the tree and scraped, casting off the thickness of mortality, being clothed in a divine garment from heaven, O martyr Anicetus.

*Troparion:*  
Having torn asunder the bonds of delusion, O holy ones, when ye were bound fast: by endurance and patience, with divine cords ye strangled the prince of darkness.

*Troparion:*  
Ye appeared as great beacons, enlightening all creation with the rays of healings and the beams of divine exploits, O most noble sufferers of Christ.

*Theotokion:*  
Apart from the laws of nature, O all-immaculate one, thou didst give birth unto the true Lawgiver: Him do thou entreat to have compassion upon my soul, overcome by the law of sin, and to save me.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable One], Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Out of the belly of Hades Thou didst hear my voice, the cry of my supplication, and didst deliver my life from corruption, O many-merciful One.[^369]

*Troparion:*  
Jesus, Who spoke within thee together with those who suffered with thee, made thy confession marvelous, O thrice-blessed one.

*Troparion:*  
Fearing neither the tortures of princes, nor the sword, nor the fire that threatened thee, O venerable one, thou wast strong through faith.

*Troparion:*  
The all-wicked enemy shook not thy pillar of Orthodoxy, but was vanquished and perished.

*Troparion:*  
By thy woven sling, the arrogance of Arius is mystically shot at with the arrows of the mysteries of Christ.

*Theotokion:*  
Beyond mind and word, O Virgin, nursing from thy breasts the Nourisher of all, thou knewest not maternal laws.

*Kontakion, Tone 7, Automelon (Самогла́сенъ):*  
Thou wast transfigured upon the mountain, and as far as they were able to bear it, Thy disciples beheld Thy glory, O Christ God: that when they should see Thee crucified, they might understand Thy suffering to be voluntary, and might proclaim unto the world that Thou art truly the Father's Radiance.

*Ikos:*  
Arise, ye sluggards, who are ever bowed down to the earth! Thoughts of my soul, take flight and ascend unto the height of divine ascent! Let us hasten unto Peter and the sons of Zebedee, and together with them reach Mount Tabor, that with them we may behold the glory of our God, and hear the voice which they heard from on high, and they proclaimed the Father's Radiance.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The Hebrew children in the furnace boldly trampled upon the flame, and turned the fire into dew, crying aloud: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!"[^370]

*Troparion:*  
Now the invisible things were seen by the apostles: the Godhead shining forth in the flesh upon Mount Tabor, as they cried aloud: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!"

*Troparion:*  
Stricken with awe, the apostles were amazed at the majesty of the divine Kingdom upon Mount Tabor, crying aloud: "Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!"

*Troparion:*  
Now things unheard-of were heard: for the Fatherless Son of the Virgin is gloriously witnessed by the Father's voice as God and man, the same unto the ages.

*Troparion:*  
Not by adoption wast Thou of the Most High, but by essence the beloved Son, being beforehand; Thou hast drawn near unto us without change: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages!

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
In the Persian furnace, the children of Abraham, burning with the love of piety rather than with flame, cried aloud: "Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!"[^371]

*Troparion:*  
Having accomplished a strange struggle, ye truly appeared strange unto fleshly mindsets, O glorious ones, fellow citizens with angels and co-heirs with Christ, O great and victorious martyrs!

*Troparion:*  
By endurance and waiting ye vanquished the godless plots of all enemies; and having become radiant through sufferings, ye have passed over unto the never-setting radiance, O most glorious ones.

*Troparion:*  
Having become a temple of the all-divine Trinity, ye manly threw down the pillars and temples of the godless, O all-praised ones; and unto the heavenly temple ye have passed over, rejoicing.

*Theotokion:*  
As more spacious than the heavens, O pure one, thou didst receive the Word, Whom nothing can contain, beyond understanding: Who leads those who walk the narrow path, O all-immaculate one, unto the expanse of divine life.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable One], Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
In the beginning Thou didst establish the earth, and by the Word didst confirm the heavens: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers![^372]

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst adorn the garment of natural dignity with thy blood, O divinely-wise one, moistening the tongue and speaking, giving through these a manifestation unto the end of words.

The grave held not your tongues, nor did a distant land hide your words; as David writes in the hymns: truly into all the earth have they been proclaimed.[^373]

Like gold tried in the fire, having been tested in those temptations, ye three made your faith in Christ most pure to increase, O honorable fathers.[^374]

*Theotokion:*  
The mysterious image of the Virgin Thou didst prefigure on Sinai in the bush that burned without being consumed: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers![^375]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant madly heat the furnace for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud to the Creator and Redeemer: "Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!"[^376]

*Troparion:*  
Having heard Thee witnessed to by the Father, O Master, and unable to endure the beholding of the radiance of Thy countenance, exceeding mortal sight, Thy disciples fell prone upon the earth, singing in fear: "Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!"

*Troparion:*  
Thou art a most fair King of those who reign, and Lord ruling everywhere, mighty, blessed, and dwelling in light unapproachable; at Whom the disciples, Moses, and Elijah, being amazed, cried aloud: "Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!"

*Troparion:*  
As unto One Who rules heaven, and masters earth, and hath dominion over the nether regions, O Christ, there stood before Thee: the apostles indeed from earth, Elijah the Tishbite as from heaven, and Moses from the dead, singing in unison: "Ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!"

*Troparion:*  
The chosen of the apostles left on earth the sorrows that bring forth despondency, O Lover of mankind; for they followed Thee unto the translation from earth to the divine life. Therefore having also worthily attained unto Thy Theophany, they sang: "Ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!"

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Daniel, stretching out his hands, stopped the gaping mouths of lions in the pit; and the children, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of fire, crying aloud: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"[^377]

*Troparion:*  
Showing a steadfast mind, O wise ones, ye feared not the rushing of wild beasts, nor were ye terrified by the boiling of cauldrons, nor the severing of limbs nor the threats of the torturers, crying aloud: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion:*  
Ye heal the afflictions of the faithful, having truly prefigured the blessed Passion by your sufferings; and ye remove infirmities, and drive away evil spirits, crying aloud: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Troparion:*  
The most noble pair of sufferers confessed the uncreated Trinity, and truly destroyed the regiment of dark-glorious adversaries, and joined the noetic myriads, singing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Theotokion:*  
O holy Virgin, who didst bear the all-holy God Who by grace crowned His holy martyrs, save us who are embraced [by perils], as we fervently sing: "Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!"

*Another Canon [of the Venerable One], Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Him Who was glorified upon the holy mountain, and in the bush through fire revealed unto Moses the mystery of the Ever-Virgin: hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^378]

*Troparion:*  
Having received into thy heart the ray of divine teaching, O divinely-wise one, thou wast enriched with the enlightenment of understanding, and didst deposit the treasure of wisdom into thy soul, O father.

*Troparion:*  
Having set forth teachings, O divinely-blessed one, thou didst drive away heresies from the midst; and having emulated the sufferings of Christ, thou becamest a pure sacrifice and acceptable unto God by thy free choice.

*Troparion:*  
Having preserved your life in purity, and adorned your deeds with faith, ye were counted worthy of heavenly glory, O honorable fathers. Therefore ye hymn Christ unto the ages.

*Theotokion:*  
Rejoice, O portal, and animate cloud! Rejoice, O mountain, and bush, and lampstand! Rejoice, O temple, and throne, and paradise! Rejoice, O blessed one, who didst bear the Creator of all!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Every ear was terrified at the ineffable condescension of God: how the Most High of His own will came down even unto the flesh, having become man from a virginal womb. Therefore the pre-immaculate Birthgiver of God do we the faithful magnify.[^379]

*Troparion:*  
That Thou mightest clearly show forth Thine ineffable second coming, that Thou mightest appear as the Most High God, standing in the midst of gods, the apostles, upon Tabor: Thou didst ineffably illuminate Moses and Elijah. Therefore, O Christ, we all magnify Thee.

*Troparion:*  
"Come, submit yourselves unto Me, O ye people!" Having ascended the holy and super-heavenly mountain, let us immaterially stand in the city of the living God, and noetically behold the immaterial Godhead of the Father and of the Spirit shining forth in the only-begotten Son.

Thou hast drawn me with love, O Savior, and transformed me by Thy divine desire; but burn away my sins with immaterial fire, and vouchsafe me to be filled with Thy nourishment, that rejoicing in both, O Good One, I may magnify Thy majesty.

*Another Canon [of the Martyrs], Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
A cornerstone was cut without hands from the unquarried mountain, which is thee, O Virgin: Christ, Who joined together the parted natures. Therefore, rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Birthgiver of God.[^380]

*Troparion:*  
Behold, the bright and light-bearing day, full of grace, hath shone forth: of the divine Photios and Anicetus, enlightening all who celebrate therein, through their radiant sufferings.

*Troparion:*  
Ye appeared as burning coals, consuming all the substance of malice; ye appeared as pure lambs, voluntarily slaughtered, and brought unto the Lamb Who takes away sins.

*Troparion:*  
Celebrating today your wounds, O passion-bearers, your sufferings and sicknesses, and your patience surpassing mind, and your exploits, and your most glorious end, we all rejoice.

*Troparion:*  
There have blossomed forth in the valleys of martyrs' graves, as roses, the most noble sufferers; and they perfume the hearts of the pious with the Spirit, driving away the foul stench of delusion.

*Theotokion:*  
Enlighten us, O Virgin who didst bear the unapproachable Light! Fill us with gladness and joy, and divine understanding, who with pure heart divinely-wisely magnify thee.

*Another Canon [of the Venerable One], Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
The Birth of the Ever-Virgin, which was revealed of old unto the Lawgiver upon the mountain in fire and the bush, for the salvation of us the faithful: with unceasing hymns we magnify.[^381]

*Troparion:*  
Through dispassion and contemplation, O thrice-blessed one, thou didst instruct us with words of eternal life. An animate pillar of Orthodoxy unto the world hast thou left thy confession.

*Troparion:*  
Emulating Phinehas, O God-bearer, as with another spear, with the sword of the word thou cuttest down the newly-planted faith, cutting off those who blaspheme Christ.

*Troparion:*  
Truly were ye glorified, O God-bearing fathers, by wounds and scourgings, persecutions and perils; having suffered in bonds, and prisons, and irons, and in exiles.

*Theotokion:*  
The bush burning with fire and not consumed did Moses of old foresee, O Virgin Mother of God, prefiguring thy God-receiving womb, which received the unconsumed Fire.

*Exapostilarion of the Saint.*  
*Podoben: "O Light immutable" (Свѣ́те неизмѣ́нный):*  
Thou didst show thyself a trumpet of wisdom by thy melodious tongue, terrifying the regiments of adversaries, O Maximus, light of monastics. Therefore, being nourished by all thy words, we know the reason of things.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast:*  
O Word, immutable Light of the unbegotten Light, the Father: in Thy revealed light today upon Tabor we have seen the light of the Father, and the light of the Spirit, guiding all creation by light.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast, Tone 2.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
Thou didst shine forth, O Christ, whiter than snow, laying bare unto the disciples the essence of Thine unapproachable glory.

*Verse: Thine are the heavens, and Thine is the earth [LXX Ps. 88:12].*  
Desiring to show forth Thy glory unto the disciples, with Moses and Elijah upon Mount Tabor: when they beheld Thee, O Savior, they were terrified.

*Verse: Tabor and Hermon shall rejoice in Thy Name [LXX Ps. 88:13].*  
Today Christ upon Mount Tabor hath renewed the darkened nature of Adam, and having enlightened it, made it divine.

*Glory... Both now... the same tone and podoben [Tone 2] (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Tabor, the mountain of God, rejoices in Thy name, O Savior, having proclaimed Thee the beloved Son unto the Father.

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast: Ode 7 and Ode 8,[^382] from both canons, with the heirmos on 6.*

*The same month, on the 13th day: Our venerable father Maximus the Confessor.*  
*His service is sung on the 12th day with the martyrs, as was written before. And on the 13th day is the Apodosis of the feast of the Lord's Transfiguration: and all the service of the feast is sung.*  
*In the evening, the usual Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast on 6, sung on the feast itself: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. If it be Saturday: Glory... of the Feast: Both now... Dogmatikon of the current tone. At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Troparion of the Feast. Litany, and Dismissal. At Compline, after the Trisagion, the Kontakion of the Feast. Midnight Office we sing according to custom, with the troparia* with the daily troparia. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Feast, thrice. After the Kathismata, Sessional Hymns of the Feast. Canon of the Feast with the heirmos on 8; the second without heirmoi on 6. Katavasia: "Having traced the Cross" (*Крестъ начерта́въ*), both choirs together. After the 3rd Ode, two Sessional Hymns. After the 6th Ode, the Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast. At the 9th Ode, we sing not "More honorable," but we sing the refrain of the Feast. Exapostilarion of the Feast, twice.

At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast on 4: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Feast, litanies, instruction [catechesis], and dismissal; and the customary procession, and the First Hour in the narthex. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes: the 9th Ode of both canons, with the heirmos on 8. Troparion of the Feast: Glory... Both now... Kontakion of the Feast. Prokeimenon, Alleluia, and Communion verse of the Feast. Epistle and Gospel of the day only.

*But if the Apodosis of the feast of Christ's Transfiguration happen on a Sunday:*  
*At Little Vespers, Resurrection Stichera. At the Aposticha, the Resurrection Sticheron, and the Aposticha of the Feast with their own refrains of Great Vespers: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. Resurrection Troparion: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. At Great Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Resurrection Stichera, 3; and of Anatolius, 1; and of the Feast, 6, sung on the feast itself: Glory... of the Feast: Both now... Dogmatikon of the 4th Tone. At the Litiya, Stichera, and Glory... Both now... of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Resurrection Stichera: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. After the Trisagion, the Troparion "O Birthgiver of God and Virgin," twice; and of the Feast, 1; and the blessing of the loaves, and reading. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," Resurrection Troparion, twice: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. After the Kathismata, Resurrection Sessional Hymns with their Theotokia; and the reading in the Gospel, the Sunday order; and the rest according to custom. Resurrection Canon with the heirmos on 4; and of the Birthgiver of God on 2; and both canons of the Feast on 8. Katavasia: "Having traced the Cross" (Крестъ начерта́въ), both choirs together. After the 3rd Ode, Kontakion of the Feast, and Ikos; and the Sessional Hymn of the Feast, twice. After the 6th Ode, Resurrection Kontakion and Ikos. At the 9th Ode, we sing "More honorable." Resurrection Exapostilarion: Glory... Both now... of the Feast. At the Praises, Resurrection Stichera, 4; and of the Feast, 4, with the Doxastikon: with the festal refrains: Glory... Gospel Sticheron: Both now... "Most blessed art thou": Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, the Resurrection Troparion only, litanies, instruction, and dismissal, and the First Hour in the narthex. At the Hours, Resurrection Troparion: Glory... of the Feast: Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. Kontakion of the Feast. Likewise on the other hours, saying the Resurrection Kontakion and that of the Feast alternately. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the tone on 6, and of the Feast, Tone 4, Ode 9 on 4. Resurrection Troparion and of the Feast: Glory... Resurrection Kontakion: Both now... of the Feast. Prokeimenon, Alleluia, and Communion verse of the day and of the Feast. Epistle and Gospel of the day.*

*The same month, on the 14th day: Forefeast of the Dormition of the Most Holy Birthgiver of God; and the Holy Prophet Micah.*  
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Forefeast, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*  
With cymbals let us sound aloud, in hymns let us shout, celebrating the forefeast of the departing festival; and brightly let us cry the funeral song: for the Mother of God, the all-golden Ark, prepares now to pass from earth unto the highest, being translated again unto living divine brightness.

O choir of the apostles, gather yourselves most gloriously from the ends today: for the animate city of the King of all departs gloriously from earth unto better things, to hold festival with the King, her Son. Unto whose divine burial, of one mind with the hosts on high, sing ye a departing hymn.

O assembly of the sacred, kings and princes with virgins' ranks, now anticipate: and let all people assemble, sending up the funeral song together. For the Sovereign Lady of all will tomorrow deliver her soul into the hands of her Son, being translated unto an eternal dwelling.

*Other Stichera of the Holy Prophet, the same tone [Tone 4].*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*  
Thou didst proclaim a conspicuous and high mountain, a twofold preaching, to appear upon the top of the mountains, O blessed one, manifesting the transcendent knowledge of God: unto which the nations, having gathered in faith, fervently flee, and are taught the paths of the Lord, and are saved with eternal salvation.[^383]

"A Ruler shall go forth and shall shepherd His people, going forth from Bethlehem," thou didst foretell, O marvelous among prophets, Micah, with divinely-inspired tongue: Whose goings forth are truly from the days of eternity. And now seeing thy prophecy fulfilled, we divinely-wisely glorify Him Who spoke through thee.

Standing before the throne of God, O all-honorable prophet, and accounted worthy of joy, and beholding glory, and enjoying divine delight, and filled with spiritual gladness and joy: look down upon those who now celebrate thy memory in faith, delivering them from perils by thine unceasing prayers.

*Glory... Both now... the same tone [Tone 4]:*  
Thy most honorable falling asleep, O most holy and pure Virgin: the multitude of angels in heaven and the human race upon earth do bless, for thou wast the Mother of Christ God, the Creator of all: praying Him for us, cease thou not, we pray thee, who after God have placed our hope on thee, O all-hymned and unwedded Birthgiver of God.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
O marvelous novelty! O strange sign! How the life-bearing Maiden submits unto mortality, and is now covered by a tomb!

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest: Thou, and the ark of Thy sanctuary [LXX Ps. 131:8].*[^384]  
Let every nature of the earthborn dance: for behold, the Virgin, daughter of Adam, is translated unto heaven.

*Verse: The Lord hath sworn truth unto David, and He will not turn away from it [LXX Ps. 131:11].*[^385]  
Conceive in mind the Virgin's bier, with the apostolic choir adorning it around, and singing the departing hymn.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 2, by Anatolius:*  
She who is higher than the heavens, and more glorious than the cherubim, and more honorable than all creation, who through her exceeding purity became the receptacle of the everlasting Essence: today into the Son's hands she delivers her all-holy soul; and with her all things are filled with joy, and unto us He grants great mercy.

*Troparion, Tone 4:*  
O ye people, dance beforehand, clapping your hands in faith, and gather together in love today, rejoicing and brightly shouting aloud, all in gladness: for the Mother of God is about to pass gloriously from earthly things unto the highest, whom we ever glorify as the Birthgiver of God with hymns.

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ):*  
She who was shown forth upon earth wider than the heavens, who gave birth in the flesh unto the Creator of all, is now translated from earth into the heavens themselves. The souls of the righteous, beholding thee, and the angelic choirs beholding thee, as unto a Queen worthily ever offer praise unto thee. Therefore, praying, cease thou not on behalf of those who hymn thee.

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5.*  
*Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*  
The multitudes of angels glorify thee, and the human race sings unto thee forefeast hymns: for thou hast passed over from earth unto Him Who was born of thee, O pure Birthgiver of God. And do thou pray earnestly to deliver from perils those who in faith perform thy translation, O Virgin.

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Canon of the Forefeast with the heirmos on 8; and of the Prophet on 4.*  
*Canon of the Forefeast, Tone 5. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
The horse and the rider into the Red Sea, He Who shatters warfare with a high arm, Christ shook off, but saved Israel as it sang a song of victory.[^386]

*Troparion:*  
Let us the faithful draw near unto the tomb that received the Virgin, striving to receive divine sweet fragrance: for herein has been poured out the fragrance breathing from the vessel of myrrh, perfuming our souls.

*Troparion:*  
Sion was taken from the mountain, animate Sion, and came to rest in the town of Gethsemane, as animate, having received the immortal God, to rest in incorruptible bosoms.

*Troparion:*  
Sounding the trumpets, O apostolic mouths of God-bearing men, cry aloud mightily: "Ye powers, lift up the gates; receive the Queen into higher realms!"

*Troparion:*  
How does mortality submit unto her who manifestly gave birth unto the everlasting Life, the all-immaculate one beyond nature? But having cast this off, the Master translated thee unto the heavenly reception.

*Another Canon of the Prophet, Tone 8. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Having passed through the water as if dry land, and having escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: "Let us sing unto our Redeemer and God!"[^387]

*Troparion:*  
Truly as a pure mirror of God, and being of divine things, thou standest before the Creator, O blessed one: entreat Him to save from perils those who in faith sing unto thee.

*Troparion:*  
Being filled with spiritual understanding and wisdom from God, O Micah, Thou wast taught things to come, as a pure mind, exceedingly.

*Troparion:*  
Piously preaching prophecy, thou didst utter a voice, O blessed one: that He should appear from heaven upon the earth, whose manifestation unto us is nourishment.

*Theotokion:*  
Delivering those previously held captive, He Who was known before the ages was manifested upon earth in the last days by the assumption of flesh, O Mother of God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
Thou Who didst establish the earth upon nothing by Thy command, and didst suspend its weight hanging without support: upon the unshakable rock of Thy commandments, O Christ, establish Thy Church, O sole Good One and Lover of mankind.[^388]

*Troparion:*  
Funeral hymns let us now send up unto the pure Mother of the Creator; and with the bodiless hosts let us surround the life-bearing bier, rejoicing, as a camp around a divine throne.

*Troparion:*  
Seeing lying and stretched out, closing her eyes, the divine and ever-flowing life-giving Spring: come, with faithful hearts shedding tears, let us touch her incorruptible body.

*Troparion:*  
O divine and noble dwelling of God, truly the seat and animate city of the King of all: grant forgiveness of all our transgressions through thy memory, and save from perils.

*Another Canon [of the Prophet], Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
Thou art the confirmation of those that flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.[^389]

*Troparion:*  
Thy tongue became the pen of the divine Spirit, whereby thou didst manifest unto us the fulfillment of things to come.

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst show forth the Pre-eternal One become flesh in Bethlehem, the Ruler of all, both Savior and Guide.

*Troparion:*  
An initiate revealing things beyond mind hast thou been unto us, O glorious prophet, illumined by prophetic enlightenments.

*Theotokion:*  
A most manifest mountain wast thou, O all-pure one, upon which the Lord ascended and appeared, unto which we now flee.

*Sessional Hymn of the Prophet, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*  
Being enlightened in the pure Spirit, O wise one, thou didst clearly foretell the knowledge of things to come, making clear what is hard to understand, O most rich Micah: for a Ruler and Leader shall come forth from Bethlehem, Christ Who will shepherd His people, even as thou didst prophesy.

*Glory... Both now... of the Forefeast, Tone 4. Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*  
Sing beforehand, ye virgins and maidens! Praise, ye mothers! And sing, all ye, the departing hymn, celebrating beforehand the falling asleep of the Virgin and Mother of the Lord in divinely-fair songs: for she is translated gloriously unto heavenly realms.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
Perceiving Thy divine emptying, Habakkuk cried aloud unto Thee with trembling, O Christ: "For the salvation of Thy people, to save Thine anointed, art Thou come!"[^390]

*Troparion:*  
Heavenly ranks rejoice and prepare to receive the pure Mother of God, Queen piously sovereign over all creation.

*Troparion:*  
Let the spirits of the air give way! Let the prince of this world himself withdraw! Let him fall down put to shame, seeing the Mother of God borne on high!

*Troparion:*  
No longer figuratively in riddles is the Ark of the Covenant borne by priests; but truly by holy apostles the Mother of God is borne.

*Troparion:*  
Having fallen asleep into the divine resting place, thou didst rest in the living treasures that pass not away, O dove of Solomon, good and fair, and all-honorable.

*Another Canon [of the Prophet], Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I have understood Thy works, and glorified Thy Godhead.[^391]

*Troparion:*  
Illumined with the rays of divine inspiration from on high, thou didst proclaim things to come as though seeing present things, O praiseworthy one.

*Troparion:*  
Out of Sion went forth the law, as thou didst prophesy, O divinely-blessed one, and ran through the ends of the earth, preaching the word of the Lord.

*Troparion:*  
The Lord of all was shown forth working in the house of Jacob, and in the glory of His name, as a Shepherd, He will feed His people, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*  
The offshoot of David, the Mother of God, was entrusted unto the Creator: for the flesh that became His of her, she ineffably brought forth.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, unto Thee I rise early, and unto Thee I cry: Enlighten my darkened soul, O Christ, as Thou alone art tenderhearted.[^392]

*Troparion:*  
He Who was on Mount Sinai breathed upon the pure, divine dwelling of the All-Holy Spirit, and enlightened all creation beyond nature.

*Troparion:*  
Thou wast the dwelling of this Godhead: therefore the Godhead translated thee, O Mother of God, wholly from the earth, O Birthgiver of God, unto the heavenly passage.

*Troparion:*  
Jesus, the Lord of transcendent divine glory, born from thy womb, O tender-hearted one, Himself translated thee from earth, O Mother of God, with soul and body.

*Another Canon [of the Prophet], Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
Rising early from the night we cry aloud unto Thee: O Lord, save us; for Thou art our God, and beside Thee we know none other.[^393]

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst love to walk after the Lord, being prepared in His ways, O all-wise one.

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst perceive that which was to come: the salvation unto all the Gentiles from the Judeans, O marvelous one.

*Troparion:*  
Beholding grace, thou didst proclaim it, O divinely-revealed one, unto which the flocks of the Gentiles flee, and are saved.

*Theotokion:*  
A dwelling of the Lord wast thou, O Mother of God, out of whom He came forth, delivering the world from delusion.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Calm the sea of passions raging with soul-corrupting tempest, O Master Christ, and bring me up from corruption, as Thou art tenderhearted.[^394]

*Troparion:*  
With angels let us run, and with archangels, principalities, powers, thrones, and dominions, unto Sion, to behold a great wonder!

*Troparion:*  
How does the Mother of Life submit unto death and corruption, having escaped all corruption? But truly, O pure Virgin, thy mysteries are beyond nature.

*Troparion:*  
As many as are citizens of Sion, light lamps, and let us come to meet her who is clothed with light in Gethsemane, and escort her with hymns.

*Another Canon [of the Prophet], Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
Grant me a robe of light, Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, O many-merciful Christ our God![^395]

*Troparion:*  
Obeying the laws of God, Thou didst rebuke all transgressors, keeping the law, O most glorious one.

*Troparion:*  
O divine man and honorable instrument of the Spirit, by thy supplications preserve the faithful from perils.

*Theotokion:*  
Thou, O Virgin, didst make the house of Jacob bright, having manifested Him Who shone forth from thy holy womb.

*Kontakion, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*  
In thy glorious memory the universe, adorned with the immaterial Spirit, noetically cries unto thee with gladness: Rejoice, O Virgin, boast of Christians!

*Ikos:*  
He Who on the clouds brightly gathered from all the ends His divine apostles unto thy body, O pure one, showed thee forth unto all as the Birthgiver of God and an awe-inspiring, pre-adorned bridal chamber; and having become God and thy Son, taking thy soul upon His pre-immaculate hand, He placed it in paradise: for there is the tree of life, whereof mortal men having eaten found immortality through thee. Therefore we also cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Virgin, boast of Christians!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The supremely exalted Lord of the fathers quenched the flame, and bedewed the children as they sang in unison: "Blessed art Thou, O God!"[^396]

*Troparion:*  
Ye priestly assemblies, congregations of people, and all ye maidens, singing in choir, hymn the divine translation of the Virgin!

*Troparion:*  
Ye best ones of the Law and of grace, praise singing the falling asleep of the Mother of God, chanting in unison: "Blessed art Thou, O God!"

*Troparion:*  
From Sion the all-pure one is translated today, for the Holy of holy of holies, the holy and great mountain, unto whom we cry: Rejoice, O all-immaculate one!

*Another Canon [of the Prophet], Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
The Children that came down from Judea in Babylon once trampled the flame of the furnace by their faith in the Trinity, chanting: "O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!"[^397]

*Troparion:*  
Thou wast shown forth necessary before the face of all, and most valiant; thou didst rebuke the harshness of rulers and didst instruct them, crying aloud, O praiseworthy one: "O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!"

*Troparion:*  
Shining with the rays of a divine life, thou wast shown forth as a most pure prophet of the divine epiphany, chanting, O blessed one: "O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!"

*Theotokion:*  
Co-working with Him Who begat Him, the Word, equal in power and co-everlasting, in the womb of the Virgin by the good pleasure of the Father is formed as man, the blessed God of our fathers.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
Unto Thee, the Creator of all, the Children in the furnace, having woven a universal choir, sang: "All ye works, praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!"[^398]

*Troparion:*  
O Virgin among women, offer supplication unto Christ the King of all on behalf of those who with love perform thy memory, O Savior, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*  
As luminous and hallowed by the beauty of the Godhead is thy face, O all-immaculate one, shining with the glory of divine virginity, supremely adorned unto all ages.

*Troparion:*  
I cannot speak, I am amazed and tremble, and am utterly overwhelmed before thy exceeding great marvels; but beholding love, O Virgin, reward thou unceasingly with divine gifts.

*Another Canon [of the Prophet], Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
When the musical instruments sounded in harmony and innumerable peoples were worshipping the image in Dura, the three children, refusing to submit, sang unto the Lord and glorified Him unto all ages.[^399]

*Troparion:*  
He Who is from the beginning of the age, the Word of God, appeared, delivering those that are of Adam, even as Micah foretold: "Praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!"

*Troparion:*  
Spiritually taught, thou didst cry aloud: The Master delights in mercy; for as merciful, He took oath concerning the iniquities of those who in faith sing and supremely exalt Him unto the ages.

*Troparion:*  
As a bright lightning flash wast thou manifested, having received the divine ray; and having become as a mirror, O Micah, thou didst cry aloud: "Praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!"

*Theotokion:*  
Having strong consolation in Him Who was born of thee, O Virgin, we hold fast in apostolic manner the honorable confession, chanting: "Praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!"

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Rejoice, O Isaiah! The Virgin had in womb, and brought forth a Son, Emmanuel, God and man; the Orient is His name: Whom magnifying, we call the Virgin blessed.[^400]

*Troparion:*  
Fairly, fairly art thou adorned, O Bride, Mother of God, O Virgin: for from thee Adam's lapse was righted, and the all-destructive defeat of Eve. Therefore we all magnify thee unceasingly with hymns.

*Troparion:*  
God renders glory worthily unto thee as thy Master, O Virgin; but what shall we, the humble, offer unto thee? Yet do thou accept our earnest zeal alone, and preserve those who with love honor thee as the Birthgiver of God.

*Troparion:*  
Even as the Most High came down and passed through thy womb, O Virgin, He visited us from thee, O Bride of God; as is fitting unto Him, honor from the heavenly bridal chambers thy singers through thy memory.

*Troparion:*  
Grant victory unto our King,[^401] who earnestly holds thy honorable burial as an enduring confirmation; and subjugating enemies, grant peace unto the Church that honors thy memory, O Mother of God.

*Another Canon [of the Prophet], Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
Truly we confess thee to be the Birthgiver of God, saved by thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.[^402]

*Troparion:*  
Before the incorruptible and purest Mind, shining with the rays of piety, praying for the world, thou standest ever.

*Troparion:*  
Thy light-bearing memory has arrived, O prophet, filled with prophetic gifts, and pouring forth streams of repentance.

*Troparion:*  
Having seen the Savior come into the world according to thy prophecy, deified beyond mind, thou delightest in gladness, O blessed one.

*Theotokion:*  
As thou art higher and purer than all, cleanse the defilements of my soul, for thou didst conceive the all-good God.

*Exapostilarion of the Forefeast.*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Thou Who hast adorned heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*  
Angels and shepherds sang of thy birth at first; and now the angelic ranks, and the apostolic choir, and every tongue, O Virgin, sing of thy falling asleep.

*Glory... Both now...*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите):*  
Celebrating the most radiant memory of thy honorable falling asleep, we hymn it with love, O Birthgiver of God. And do thou, as a most pure Mother, departing in thy glory unto thy Son and Lord, pray for the Christians who in faith hymn thee.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "On the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*  
Following the voice of divine Gabriel, we cry unto thee, O pure one: "Rejoice!" For thou art most holy, and the Mother of the Lord;

having passed over unto Him, remember those who hymn thee.

*Verse:* Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy sanctuary.[^403]

The immeasurable Wisdom of God created for Himself beyond mind a temple, O Birthgiver of God, out of thee by the Holy Spirit; and now He has translated thee, O all-hymned one, unto immaterial splendors.

*Verse:* The Lord hath sworn truth unto David, and He will not turn away from it.[^404]

We thy servants now come unto thee, the Mother of the God of all, praying to be delivered from all perils. But O Parent of God, reigning with thy Son, preserve the Christian race.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 3, by Germanus:*  
Come, all ye ends of the earth, let us praise the honorable translation of the Mother of God; for into the hands of her Son she committed her immaculate soul. Therefore by her holy falling asleep the world is quickened; in psalms and hymns and spiritual songs, with the bodiless hosts and the apostles it celebrates radiantly.

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes: Ode 3 and Ode 6. After the Entrance, the troparion: first of the temple of Christ, and of the forefeast, and of the temple of the saint. Glory... the kontakion of the temple of the saint; Both now... of the forefeast.*

*Take heed:*  
*If the forefeast of the Falling Asleep of the Birthgiver of God falls on a Sunday: we chant the entire service of the forefeast with the Resurrectional and the prophetic service, at Vespers, at Matins, and at the Liturgy, even as was indicated for the forefeast of the Transfiguration of Christ. Only at Matins, the Katavasia: "Adorned with divine glory" (Преукраше́нная). After the Entrance we say the Resurrectional troparion, and of the forefeast, and of the temple of the saint (male or female). Then the Resurrectional kontakion; Glory... of the saint (male or female); Both now... of the forefeast.*

*On the 15th Day of the Same Month: The Dormition of our Most Holy Glorious Lady, the Birthgiver of God and Ever-Virgin Mary.*  
*At Small Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 4, Tone 1.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*  
At the immortal falling asleep of the Mother of God, the angelic assembly rejoices; and she, departing, settles into the eternal mansions, and passing over unto heavenly gladness, into divine joy and everlasting delight.

Desiring the higher life, thou didst leave this one behind, O Parent of God, maiden and flower of virginity, who didst give birth unto Christ, the Life of all. Piously the apostolic choir stands before thy honorable burial, O Bride of God.

By thy departing hymns and funeral songs the air was sanctified, by thy ascension, O Mother of God the Lover of mankind, an awesome wonder, O unwedded Birthgiver of God! Therefore we the faithful faithfully worship thee, O unwedded Birthgiver of God.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*  
Come, let us celebrate the universal falling asleep of the all-immaculate Birthgiver of God; for today the angels solemnly celebrate the honorable translation of the Mother of God, and invite us earthly ones unto gladness, to cry aloud with unceasing voice: Rejoice, thou who hast been translated from earth and hast removed unto the heavenly mansions! Rejoice, thou who didst gather into one the choir of disciples upon a light cloud! Rejoice, our hope and salvation: for the Christian race unceasingly calls thee blessed!

*Prokeimenon of the day.*  
*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2.*  
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*  
The choir of the disciples and divine apostles gathered together to bury the God-receiving body of the only Mother of God.

*Verse:* Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy sanctuary.[^405]

Thou Who ineffably didst shine forth from her, in manner befitting a Son receive with Thy hands the all-holy spirit of the Birthgiver of God, O Master.

*Verse:* The Lord hath sworn truth unto David, and He will not turn away from it.

When thou wast translated unto Him Who was born of thee, the apostles came together upon the clouds to bury thy body, O Virgin.

*Glory... Both now...*  
Be ye lifted up, O heavenly gates, beholding the Door of the Most High going with glory unto her Son and God.

*Troparion, Tone 1:*  
In birth-giving thou didst preserve virginity; in falling asleep thou didst not forsake the world, O Birthgiver of God: thou didst pass over unto life, being the Mother of Life, and by thy supplications thou deliverest our souls from death.

*Little Litany, and the Dismissal.*  
*At Great Vespers, we chant: "Blessed is the man", the 1st antiphon.*  
*On "Lord, I have cried," we set 8 verses, and chant stichera, Tone 1:*  
O marvelous wonder! The Source of Life is laid in a tomb, and the tomb becomes a ladder unto heaven. Be glad, O Gethsemane, holy dwelling of the Birthgiver of God! Let us the faithful cry aloud, having Gabriel as leader of the rank: "Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee, Who through thee grants great mercy unto the world!" *Thrice.*

Marvelous are thy mysteries, O Birthgiver of God: thou wast shown forth as the throne of the Most High, O Sovereign Lady, and from earth unto heaven hast thou been translated today! Thy glory is divinely-splendid, shining with Godlike wonders. Ye virgins, together with the Queen-Mother, be ye lifted up on high! "Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee, Who through thee grants great mercy unto the world!" *Thrice.*

Thy falling asleep is glorified by principalities and thrones, authorities and dominions, powers and cherubim, and the awesome seraphim; the earthborn rejoice, adorning themselves in thy divine glory. Kings fall down, together with the archangels and angels, chanting: "Rejoice, thou full of grace, the Lord is with thee, Who through thee grants great mercy unto the world!" *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... the same Tone [Tone 1]:*  
By the sovereign nod of God, the God-bearing apostles from everywhere, borne on high upon clouds, having arrived at thy most pure and life-originating body, lovingly kissed it. And the supreme heavenly hosts, having come with their Master, escorting the God-receiving and most pure body, were seized with awe; and they went beautifully before, invisibly crying aloud unto the supreme hierarchies:

"Behold, the Queen of all, the Maiden of God is come! Lift up the gates, and receive her above the world, the Mother of the Everlasting Light! For through her the universal salvation of mankind has come to pass, upon whom we are unable to look, and unto whom it is impossible to render worthy honor; for her surpassing excellence transcends all mind!" Therefore, O most pure Birthgiver of God, living ever with the Life-bearing King and Thine Offspring: pray unceasingly to preserve and save from every assault of the adversary thy new people: for we have acquired thy protection, radiantly calling thee blessed unto the ages!

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day. Three Readings.*  
*Reading from Genesis (Chapter 28).*[^406]  
Jacob went forth from the well of the oath, and went toward Haran. And he found a place, and slept there, for the sun had set; and he took of the stones of that place, and put them for his pillow, and slept in that place. And he dreamed, and behold, a ladder was set up on the earth, whose top reached unto heaven, and the angels of God were ascending and descending on it. And the Lord leaned upon it, and said: "I am the God of Abraham thy father, and the God of Isaac; fear not: the land whereon thou sleepest, unto thee will I give it, and unto thy seed. And thy seed shall be as the sand of the earth, and shall spread abroad to the sea, and to the southwest, and to the north, and to the east; and in thee shall all the tribes of the earth be blessed, and in thy seed. And behold, I am with thee, keeping thee in all the way whithersoever thou shalt go; and I will bring thee back into this land; for I will not leave thee until I have done all that I have spoken unto thee." And Jacob awoke from his sleep, and said: "Surely the Lord is in this place, and I knew it not." And he was afraid, and said: "How awesome is this place! This is none other than the house of God, and this is the gate of heaven."

*Reading from the Prophecy of Ezekiel (Chapters 43 and 44).*[^407]  
Thus says the Lord: It shall come to pass from the eighth day and onward, that the priests shall make upon the altar your burnt offerings and your peace offerings, and I will accept you, says Adonai the Lord. And He brought me back by the way of the outer holy gate looking toward the east, and it was shut. And the Lord said unto me: "This gate shall be shut, and it shall not be opened, and no man shall pass through it; for the Lord God of Israel shall enter in by it, and it shall be shut. For the Prince, he shall sit in it to eat bread; he shall enter by the way of the porch of the gate, and by the way and by the way of the porch thereof he shall go forth. And He brought me by the way of the holy gate which looks toward the north, before the temple; and I looked, and behold, the temple was full of the glory of the Lord.

*Reading from Proverbs (Chapter 9).*[^408]  
Wisdom hath built her house, and supported it with seven pillars. She hath slaughtered her sacrifices; she hath mingled her wine in her cup, and prepared her table. She hath sent forth her servants, calling with high proclamation to the cup, saying: "Whoso is foolish, let him turn aside unto me; and to them that want understanding she said: 'Come, eat of my bread, and drink of the wine which I have mingled for you. Forsake foolishness, and ye shall live; and seek understanding, that ye may live, and set right understanding in knowledge.' He that instructs evil men shall get unto himself dishonor; and he that reproves an ungodly man shall defame himself: for reproofs to the ungodly are bruises to him. Reprove not evil men, lest they hate thee: reprove a wise man, and he will love thee. Give occasion to a wise man, and he will be wiser; instruct a righteous man, and he will receive it further. The beginning of wisdom is the fear of the Lord, and the counsel of the saints is understanding: for to know the law is the sign of a good mind. For in this manner thou shalt live long, and years of life shall be added unto thee."

*At the Litiya, Stichera Idiomela, Tone 1:*  
It was fitting that the eyewitnesses and ministers of the Word should also behold the falling asleep of His Mother according to the flesh, the final mystery concerning her: so that they might not only behold the Savior's ascension from earth, but might also bear witness unto the translation of her who gave Him birth. Therefore, gathered from everywhere by divine power, they arrived at Sion, and escorted her on her way to heaven, who is higher than the cherubim: whom we also with them worship, as one who prays for our souls.

*Tone 2, by Anatolius:*  
She who is higher than the heavens, and more glorious than the cherubim, and more honorable than all creation, who through her exceeding purity became the receptacle of the everlasting Essence: today into the Son's hands she commits her all-holy soul; and with her all things are filled with joy, and unto us He grants great mercy.

*By John:*  
The all-immaculate Bride, and Mother of the Father's good pleasure, who was foreordained for God as a dwelling for Himself of unconfused union, today commits her most pure soul unto the Creator and God: whom the hosts of the bodiless receive in manner befitting God, and she passes over unto life, being the Mother of Life: candlestick of the Unapproachable Light, salvation of the faithful, and hope of our souls.

*Tone 3, by Germanus:*  
Come, all ye ends of the earth, let us praise the honorable translation of the Mother of God; for into the hands of her Son she committed her immaculate soul. Therefore by her holy falling asleep the world is quickened; in psalms and hymns and spiritual songs, with the bodiless hosts and the apostles it celebrates radiantly.

*Glory... Tone 5, by Theophanes:*  
Come, ye assembly of feast lovers! Come, let us form a choir! Come, let us crown the Church with songs at the resting of the Ark of God: for today heaven opens its bosom, receiving her who gave birth unto Him Whom nothing can contain; and the earth, yielding up the Source of Life, is adorned with divine splendor. Angels form a choir with the apostles, gazing in awe upon her who is translated from life unto life, who gave birth unto the Author of life. Let us all worship her, praying: Forget not, O Sovereign Lady, thy kindred ties unto those who faithfully celebrate thine all-holy falling asleep!

*Both now... the same Tone [Tone 5]:*  
Sing, ye people, sing unto the Mother of our God! For today she commits her all-radiant soul into the most pure hands of Him Who was incarnate of her without seed: Whom she also prays unceasingly to grant peace unto the universe, and great mercy.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera Idiomela, Tone 2:*  
Come, ye people, let us hymn the most holy, pure Virgin, out of whom the Father's Word came forth ineffably, incarnate, crying out and saying: "Blessed art thou among women! Blessed is the womb that contained Christ!" Having committed thy soul into His holy hands, pray, O most pure one, that our souls may be saved.

*Verse:* Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy sanctuary.[^409]

Today, ye people, let us sing the Davidic song unto Christ God: "Virgins shall be brought unto the King after her, and her fellows shall be brought in gladness and rejoicing."[^410] For she who is of David's seed, through whom we have been deified, is gloriously and beyond word transferred into the hands of her Son and Master. Hymning her as the Mother of God, we cry aloud and say: "Save us who confess thee to be the Birthgiver of God from every distress, and deliver our souls from afflictions!"

*Verse:* The Lord hath sworn truth unto David, and He will not turn away from it.[^411]

Thine all-honorable falling asleep, O most holy, pure Virgin, the multitude of angels in heaven and the human race upon earth do call blessed, for thou wast the Mother of Christ God, the Creator of all: praying unto Him, cease thou not on our behalf, we pray thee, who after God have placed our hope in thee, O all-hymned and unwedded Birthgiver of God!

*Glory... Both now... the same Tone [Tone 2]:*[^412]  
When thou didst depart, O Birthgiver of God and Virgin, unto Him Who was ineffably born of thee, James the Brother of the Lord and first Hierarch was present, and Peter the most honorable chief, the head of theologians, and the whole divine apostolic choir, singing in distinct theological praise the divine and awesome mystery of Christ God's dispensation; and having buried thy life-originating and God-receiving body, they rejoiced, O all-hymned one. And the supreme, most holy, and most elder angelic hosts, marvelling at the wonder, leaning toward one another said: "Lift up your gates, and receive her who gave birth unto the Maker of heaven and earth! And with glorifications let us hymn the honorable and holy body that contained the Lord unseen by us!" Therefore we also, celebrating thy memory, cry aloud unto thee, O all-hymned one: Exalt the horn of Christians, and save our souls!

*At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion, Tone 1:*  
In birth-giving thou didst preserve virginity, in falling asleep thou didst not forsake the world, O Birthgiver of God: thou didst pass over unto life, being the Mother of Life, and by thy supplications thou deliverest our souls from death. *Thrice.*  
*Written at Small Vespers.*

*And a Reading.*

*At Matins, on "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Feast, thrice.*  
*After the 1st Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 1.*  
*Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*  
The all-honorable choir of the all-wise apostles gathered wondrously to bury gloriously thy most pure body, O all-hymned one: with whom also the multitude of angels sang, honorably praising thy translation, which we celebrate in faith.

*Glory... Both now... the same.*  
*And a Reading.*

*After the 2nd Stichology, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Beholding the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*  
In thy birth-giving was conception without seed, in thy falling asleep was incorruptible mortification: wonder upon wonder doubly concurred, O Birthgiver of God! For how can an unwedded one be a nursing mother, remaining pure? And how can the Mother of God, bearing mortality, breathe forth fragrance of myrrh? Therefore with the angel we cry unto thee: Rejoice, thou full of grace!

*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*  
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ):*  
Cry aloud, O David! What is this feast which thou didst hymn of old in the Book of Psalms? As a Daughter, Maiden of God, and Virgin, Christ, Who was born of her without seed, hath transferred her unto those mansions; and for this reason mothers and daughters and brides of Christ rejoice, crying aloud:

"Rejoice, thou who hast been translated unto the kingdom on high!"

*Glory... Both now... the same.*  
*The Graduals [Anabathmoi]: the 1st antiphon of the 4th Tone.*  
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: I will remember thy name in every generation and generation.*[^413]  
*Verse: Give ear, O daughter, and see, and incline thine ear.*[^414]  
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*  
*Gospel according to Luke, pericope 4.*[^415]  
*After Psalm 50, Sticheron, Tone 6, by Byzantios:*  
When the translation of thy most pure body was being prepared, then the apostles, standing around the bier, gazed upon thee with trembling; and some, gazing upon the body, were seized with awe. But Peter with tears cried aloud unto thee: "O Virgin, I see thee clearly stretched out, who art the life of all, and I marvel: in whom the delight of future life made its abode! But O most pure one, pray earnestly unto thy Son and thy God, that thy flock may be saved unharmed!"

*Two Canons: of Lord Cosmas, and of Lord John. The heirmoi of both choirs, twice; and the troparia on 12. Katavasia: each choir sings its own heirmos. The First Canon, whose acrostic is: "Let the divinely-wise celebrate." Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
Adorned with divine glory, thy sacred and glorious memory, O Virgin, hath gathered all the faithful unto gladness, as Miriam led the way with choirs and timbrels, singing unto Thine Only-begotten: "Gloriously hath He been glorified!"[^416]

*Troparion:*  
Let the immaterial ranks escort thy heaven-like, divine body unto Sion! And suddenly the multitude of apostles, coming together from the ends of the earth, O Birthgiver of God, straightway stood before thee: with whom, O pure Virgin, we glorify thy honorable memory.

*Troparion:*  
Thou didst bear away the prizes of victory over nature, O pure one, having given birth unto God; yet imitating thy Creator and Son, submitting beyond nature unto natural laws: therefore having died, thou dost rise with thy Son, living unto eternity.

*Another Canon, of John Damascene, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
I will open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I will utter a word unto the Queen-Mother; and I will be seen radiantly keeping festival, and singing with gladness of her falling asleep.[^417]

*Troparion:*  
Ye maiden virgins, with Miriam the prophetess cry aloud now the departing song: for the Virgin and sole Mother of God is translated unto the heavenly reception!

*Troparion:*  
Worthily as an animate heaven the heavenly, most pure, and divine habitations received thee: and stood and stood radiantly adorned, as an all-immaculate bride before the King and God.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*  
O Christ, Wisdom and Power of God, Who createst and upholdest all things: establish Thine unswerving, immovable Church; for Thou alone art holy, resting in the saints.[^418]

*Troparion:*  
Beholding thee as a mortal woman, yet beyond nature also the Mother of God, O all-immaculate one, the glorious apostles touched thee with their hands with trembling, shining with glory, as they beheld a God-receiving habitation.

*Troparion:*  
Judgment overtook the hands of the insolent one, inflicting severance; for God preserved the honor unto the animate Ark by the glory of the Godhead, wherein the Word became flesh.[^419]

*Another Heirmos:*  
O Mother of God, living and bountiful wellspring: establish those who hymn thee, who have gathered as a spiritual choir, in thy divine memory, granting them crowns of glory.[^420]

*Troparion:*  
Having been brought forth from mortal loins, O pure one, conforming unto nature, thou didst accept departure; but having given birth unto the true Life, thou wast translated unto the divine and hypostatic Life.

*Troparion:*  
The choir of theologians from the ends of the earth, and from on high the multitudes of angels, came unto Sion by the almighty nod, worthily ministering at thy burial, O Sovereign Lady.

*Ypakoi, Tone 8:*  
All generations call thee blessed, O Virgin Mother of God; for in thee Christ our God, Whom nothing can contain, was well pleased to be contained.[^421] Blessed are we also, having thee as our protectress; for day and night thou prayest for us, and the scepters of the kingdom are established by thy prayers. Therefore singing we cry aloud unto thee: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace, the Lord is with thee![^422]

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*  
The sayings and riddles of the prophets manifested Thine incarnation from the Virgin, O Christ: the radiance of Thy brightness shall go forth for the light of the nations, and the abyss shall shout unto Thee with gladness: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^423]

*Troparion:*  
Behold, ye peoples, and marvel: for the holy and manifest Mountain of God is taken up on high into the heavenly habitations; the earthly heaven is settled into the heavenly and incorruptible dwelling!

*Troparion:*  
Thy death, O pure one, became a passing unto ever-existing and better life: translating thee, O pure one, from temporal life unto that which is truly divine and never-ending, to behold in gladness thy Son and Lord.

*Troparion:*  
The heavenly gates were lifted up, and the angels sang; and Christ received the vessel of His Mother's virginity:

the cherubim made way for thee with gladness, and the seraphim glorify thee, rejoicing.

*Another Heirmos:*  
The Prophet Habakkuk, perceiving the unfathomable divine counsel of Thine incarnation from the Virgin, O Thou Most High, cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord![^424]

*Troparion:*  
It was a marvel to behold the animate heaven of the King of all passing beyond the earthly bounds: how marvelous are Thy works! Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*  
If her incomprehensible Fruit, by Whom the heavens were made, willingly submitted unto burial as a dead man: how should she who bore Him unwedded refuse burial?

*Troparion:*  
At thy translation, O Mother of God, the angelic hosts covered thy most spacious and God-receiving body with sacred wings, with trembling and joy.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*  
We will tell of the divine and ineffable beauty of Thy virtues, O Christ: for shining forth from ever-existing glory as the co-eternal and hypostatic Radiance, having become incarnate from the virginal womb, Thou hast shone forth as a sun unto those in darkness and the shadow of death.[^425]

*Troparion:*  
Borne as upon a cloud, O Virgin, the apostolic choir was gathered from the ends of the earth unto Sion, to minister unto thee, the light cloud, from whom the Most High God, the Sun of righteousness, shone forth unto those in darkness and the shadow of death.[^426]

*Troparion:*  
With harmonious voices, the God-pleasing tongues of the divinely eloquent men cried aloud as trumpets unto the Mother of God, proclaiming by the Spirit the departing song: Rejoice, incorruptible wellspring of God's life-giving and saving incarnation for all!

*Another Heirmos:*  
All creation was amazed at thy venerable falling asleep: for thou, O unwedded Virgin, didst pass from earth unto the eternal mansions and unto endless life, bestowing salvation upon all who hymn thee.[^427]

*Troparion:*  
Let the trumpets of the theologians sound today; and let the eloquent human tongue now praise; let the air resound, shining with immeasurable light; and let the angels sing of the falling asleep of the most pure Virgin!

*Troparion:*  
It was fitting for the chosen vessel to be wholly astounded by singing thy funeral hymn, O Virgin, wholly consecrated unto God, acceptable unto all, and truly shown to be such, O all-hymned Mother of God.[^428]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*  
The deep-sea inner fire of the sea-monster was a prefiguration of Thy three-day burial, whereof the Prophet Jonah was shown forth; for saved as he had been cast forth, unharmed he cried aloud: I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord![^429]

*Troparion:*  
God, the King of all, grants unto thee things that are beyond nature: for even as He preserved thee a Virgin in childbearing, so in the tomb He preserved thy body incorruptible, and glorified it together by thy divine translation, granting thee honor as a Son unto His Mother.

*Troparion:*  
Truly, as a radiant lampstand of immaterial Light, a golden censer of the divine Ember, He settled thee into the Holy of Holies: the urn and the rod, the God-inscribed tablet, the holy ark, the table of the Word of life, O Virgin, even Thine Offspring.[^430]

*Another Heirmos:*  
Celebrating this divine and all-honorable feast of the Mother of God, come, O ye divinely wise, let us clap our hands and glorify God Who was born of her.[^431]

*Troparion:*  
From thee Life shone forth without breaking the keys of virginity; how then did thy most pure and life-originating body become a partaker of the trial of death?

*Troparion:*  
Having been the temple of Life, thou hast obtained ever-existing life: for by death thou hast passed over unto life, having given birth unto the hypostatic Life.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*  
The tomb and mortification could not hold the Mother of God, who is unsleeping in prayers and an unwavering hope in intercessions; for He Who dwelt in her ever-virginal womb translated her unto life, as the Mother of Life.[^432]

*Ikos:*  
Encompass my thoughts, O my Christ, for I dare to hymn the wall of the world, Thy pure Mother: strengthen me upon the pillar of words, and defend me in difficult thoughts; for Thou fulfillest the supplications of those who cry aloud and ask in faith. Do Thou therefore grant me a fitting tongue and an unashamed mind: for every gift of illumination is sent down from Thee, O Giver of light, Who didst dwell in her ever-virginal womb.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*  
Divine longing, contending against shameless fury and fire, bedewed the fire and laughed to scorn the fury, sounding in response with the divinely inspired rational three-stringed harp of the holy youths against the musical instruments in the midst of the flame: Supremely praised and blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers and our God![^433]

*Troparion:*
