Moses broke in anger the God-wrought tablets written by the divine Spirit; but the Master of this one, preserving unharmed her who gave Him birth, hath now settled her within the heavenly mansions, having preserved her. Leaping with her, we cry aloud unto Christ: Supremely praised and blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers and our God!

*Troparion:*  
With cymbals of pure lips, and musically with a radiant heart, with the sweet-sounding trumpet of sublime contemplation, on the appointed and chosen day of the translation of the pure Virgin, clapping with active hands, we cry aloud: Supremely praised and blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers and our God![^434]

*Troparion:*  
O ye divinely wise people, gather together: for the habitation of God's glory is translated from Sion unto the heavenly dwelling, where the voice of those keeping festival is pure, the voice of ineffable rejoicing, and of those who cry aloud in gladness unto Christ: Supremely praised and blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers and our God![^435]

*Another Heirmos:*  
The divinely wise served not the creature rather than the Creator, but manfully trampling upon the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Supremely hymned and blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers![^436]

*Troparion:*  
Young men and maidens, honoring the memory of the Virgin and Mother of God, elders and princes, kings and judges, sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers![^437]

*Troparion:*  
Let the heavenly mountains sound with the spiritual trumpet, let the hills rejoice, and let the divine apostles leap for joy: the Queen is translated unto the Son, reigning with Him!

*Troparion:*  
The all-sacred translation of Thy divine and incorruptible Mother hath gathered the ranks of the transcendent powers on high, to make merry with those on earth who sing unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*  
The almighty Angel of God showed unto the children a flame that bedewed the pious, but consumed the ungodly; and He made the Mother of God a life-originating spring, pouring out the decay of death and life unto those who sing: We the redeemed praise the only Creator, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^438]

*Troparion:*  
With words the whole multitude of theologians in Sion followed the sacred divine ark, crying aloud: Whither goest thou now, O tabernacle of the living God? Cease not to look down upon those who sing in faith: We the redeemed praise the only Creator, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*  
As thou didst lift up thy hands, departing, O all-immaculate one, the hands wherewith thou didst bear God in the flesh, with boldness as a Mother thou didst say unto Him Who was born of thee: Preserve unto the ages those whom Thou hast given me, who cry aloud unto Thee: We the redeemed praise the only Creator, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Another Heirmos:*  
The childbearing of the Mother of God saved the pious children in the furnace, then prefigured, but now brought into act, stirring the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^439]

*Troparion:*  
Thy memory, O pure Virgin, principalities and authorities and powers, angels, archangels, thrones, dominions, cherubim, and the awesome seraphim glorify; and we, the human race, sing and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*  
He Who marvelously dwelt within thy pure womb, becoming incarnate, O Mother of God, having received thy most sacred spirit, gave it rest with Himself as a debtor Son; therefore we sing of thee, O Virgin, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*  
O wonders beyond understanding of the Ever-Virgin and Mother of God! For having taken up abode in the tomb, she showed it forth as paradise, standing before which today, rejoicing we sing: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*At Ode 9, we do not sing the "More honorable"; but we sing the festal refrains: All generations call thee blessed, the only Mother of God. Or: The angels, seeing the falling asleep of the all-pure one, were amazed, how the Virgin ascends from earth unto heaven.*  
*Then the Heirmos: The bounds of nature are overcome:*  
*And the second choir sings the same refrain and heirmos.*  
*And for each troparion they sing the same refrain once.*  
*Ode 9. Heirmos:*  
The bounds of nature are overcome in thee, O pure Virgin: for childbearing remains virginal, and death is betrothed unto life; virgin after childbearing, and alive after death, thou ever savest thine inheritance, O Mother of God.[^440]

*Troparion:*  
The angelic powers were amazed in Sion, beholding their Master carrying a woman's soul in His hands; for unto her who bore Him in all-purity, He cried aloud in a manner befitting a Son: Come, O pure one, be glorified with thy Son and God!

*Troparion:*  
The apostolic choir wrapped thy God-receiving body, gazing with fear and proclaiming with a loud voice: As thou ascendest unto the heavenly mansions unto thy Son, thou ever savest thine inheritance, O Mother of God!

*Then the first choir sings the refrain of the second canon: Magnify, O my soul, the venerable translation of the Mother of God from earth unto heaven. And the Heirmos: Let every earthborn mortal:*  
*And the second choir sings the same refrain and heirmos.*  
*And for each troparion they sing the same refrain once.*  
*Another Heirmos:*

Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy in spirit, being enlightened; and let the nature of the immaterial minds celebrate, honoring the sacred translation of the Mother of God, and let it cry aloud: Rejoice, all-blessed Mother of God, pure Ever-Virgin![^441]

*Troparion:*  
Come ye unto Sion, upon the divine and fertile mountain of the living God; let us rejoice, beholding the Mother of God: for Christ hath translated her as His Mother unto the far better and divine tabernacle, into the Holy of Holies.[^442]

*Troparion:*  
Come, ye faithful, let us draw near unto the tomb of the Mother of God, and let us embrace it with hearts and lips, touching it purely with eyes and face, and drawing forth bountiful gifts of healings from the ever-flowing spring!

*Troparion:*  
Receive from us the departing song, O Mother of the living God, and overshadow us with thy light-bearing and divine grace: granting victories unto the king,[^443] peace unto Christ-loving people, remission unto those who sing, and salvation unto our souls!

*Then, both choirs having come together, they sing the refrain and heirmos of the first canon: The bounds of nature:*  
*And the refrain and heirmos of the second canon: Let every earthborn mortal:*  
*And a prostration. Exapostilarion.*  
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Thou Who hast adorned heaven with stars" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*  
Ye apostles, having gathered here from the ends of the earth in the village of Gethsemane, bury my body; and do Thou, my Son and God, receive my spirit! *Thrice.*[^444]

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4: Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant among martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*  
At thy glorious falling asleep, the heavens rejoice, and the angelic hosts are glad; and all the earth makes merry, proclaiming a funeral hymn unto thee, the Mother of Him Who rules over all, O unwedded, all-holy Virgin, who hast delivered the human race from the ancestral condemnation!

From the ends of the earth the chiefest of the apostles assembled together by divine command to bury thee; and beholding thee taken up from the earth unto the height, with joy they cried aloud unto thee the voice of Gabriel: Rejoice, chariot of the whole Godhead; rejoice, thou who alone hast united earthly things with things on high through thy childbearing!

Having given birth unto Life, thou hast passed over by thy venerable falling asleep unto immortal life, being escorted by angels, principalities, and powers, by apostles and prophets, and by all creation; while thy Son received with incorruptible hands thine immaculate soul, O Virgin Mother, Bride of God!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*  
At thine immortal falling asleep, O Mother of God, Mother of Life, clouds caught up the apostles through the air; and being dispersed throughout the world, they were brought together in one company before thy most holy body: which having also buried honorably, they cried aloud unto thee, singing Gabriel's greeting: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace, Virgin Mother unwedded, the Lord is with thee![^445] With them do thou pray unto Him as thy Son and our God, that our souls may be saved!

*The Great Doxology. And the Dismissal.*  
*And oil is given from the lamp of the most holy Mother of God. And the First Hour. And the final dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy. The Beatitudes: of the 1st canon, Ode 3, on 4; and of the 2nd canon, Ode 6, on 4. After the Entrance, the Troparion of the feast; Glory... Both now... the Kontakion. Prokeimenon, the Canticle of the Mother of God, Tone 3: My soul magnifies the Lord, and my spirit hath rejoiced in God my Savior.*[^446]  
*Verse: For He hath regarded the lowliness of His handmaiden; for behold, from henceforth all generations shall call me blessed.*[^447]  
*Epistle to the Philippians, pericope 240.*[^448]  
*Alleluia, Tone 2: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest.*[^449]  
*Verse: The Lord hath sworn truth unto David, and will not turn from it.*[^450]  
*Gospel according to Luke, pericope 54.*[^451]  
*Instead of "It is truly meet," the heirmos is chanted: The bounds of nature are overcome; and this unto the Apodosis.*  
*Communion Verse: I will take the cup of salvation, and call upon the name of the Lord.*[^452]  
*At the refectory, consolation for the brethren. If this feast falls on a Wednesday or Friday: we dispense only for fish and wine. But if on a Monday: laymen dispense for meat, cheese, and eggs; but monastics only for fish and wine. This feast is celebrated for nine days.*

*It should be known if the feast of the falling asleep of the most holy Mother of God happens on Sunday:*[^453]  
*On Saturday, at Small Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera of the Tone, 4; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha, one Resurrectional Sticheron; and of the feast, the Aposticha of Great Vespers, with the refrains of the feast; Glory... Both now... of the feast. Resurrectional Troparion; Glory... Both now... of the feast. Little Litany, and Dismissal.*  
*At Great Vespers. After the customary Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried," Resurrectional Stichera, 3; and one Anatolian [Eastern]; and of the feast, 6. Glory... Both now... of the feast. Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings of the feast. At the Litiya, Stichera, and Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha, Resurrectional Stichera; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the blessing of loaves, the Troparion of the feast, thrice; and a reading from the Epistles.*

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Resurrectional Troparion, twice; Glory... Both now... of the feast. After the customary Kathismata, Resurrectional Sessional Hymns with their Theotokia. And a reading in the Commentary Gospel or the appointed reading. Then the Polyeleos; and the Troparia: "The Assembly of Angels"; and all the Sessional Hymns of the feast, once each; and a reading of the feast. The Songs of Ascents [Anabathmoi] of the Tone. Prokeimenon, and Gospel of the feast; then, "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," Psalm 50, and the veneration of the Gospel, according to custom. Thereafter, Glory... Through the intercessions of the Mother of God; Both now... the same. Sticheron of the feast. Resurrectional Canon with the heirmos on 4; and of the Mother of God on 2; and of the feast, both canons on 8. Katavasia: the heirmoi of the feast, of both canons. After Ode 3, Resurrectional Kontakion, and Ypakoi of the feast, once. After Ode 6, Kontakion of the feast, and Ikos; and we read the Prologue. At Ode 9, we do not sing the refrains, but the "More honorable." Resurrectional Exapostilarion; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Praises, Resurrectional Stichera, 4; and of the feast, 4, with the refrains of the feast; Glory... of the feast; Both now... "Thou art most blessed." And the Great Doxology. Trisagion. Resurrectional Troparion only. Litanies, and Dismissal. Oil is also given from the lamp of the feast. Then the Gospel Sticheron, and the Catechesis, and the customary departure. And the First Hour in the narthex: at the First Hour, the Resurrectional Troparion; Glory... of the feast; Both now... the Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," the Resurrectional Kontakion, and Dismissal. Likewise also at the other Hours: Resurrectional Troparion; Glory... of the feast; Both now... the Theotokion of the Hours. The Kontakion of the feast and the Resurrectional Kontakion we say alternately.*

*At the Liturgy, the Resurrectional Beatitudes of the Tone on 6; and of the feast, Ode 3, on 4. After the Entrance, Resurrectional Troparion, and of the feast; Glory... Resurrectional Kontakion; Both now... of the feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Verse of the day, and of the feast.*

*(Engraving: Woodcut printer's device of the Basilian Order featuring the sacred monogram IHS surmounted by a Cross and three nails, an inflamed bleeding heart encircled by the crown of thorns, framed within floral ornamentation and surmounted by a ducal crown)*[^454] *On the 16th day of the same month.*[^455]
*The translation from Edessa unto the imperial city of Constantinople of the Icon Not-Made-With-Hands of our Lord Jesus Christ, that is, the Holy Keramion and Cloth [Mandylion]; and the commemoration of the Holy Martyr Diomedes.*[^456]  
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 6: of the Dormition, 3; and of the Image, 3. Stichera of the Dormition, Tone 2, Podoben: "With what crowns of praise" (Кі́ими похва́льными):*  
With what unworthy lips shall we call the Mother of God blessed? More honorable than creation, and being more holy than the cherubim and all the angels, the immovable royal throne, the house wherein the Most High took up His abode, the salvation of the world, God's sanctification: who lavishly bestows great mercy upon the faithful in her divine memory.[^457]

What awesome hymns did all the apostles of the Word offer thee then, O Virgin, standing round about thy bier, and crying aloud in awe: The palace of the King departs, the ark of sanctification is lifted up! Be ye lifted up, O gates, that the Gate of God may enter into joy, unceasingly asking great mercy for the world![^458]

What spiritual songs shall we now offer thee, O pious one? For by thine immortal falling asleep thou hast illumined the whole world, and hast passed over unto the transcendent realms, to contemplate the beauty of the Almighty, and to rejoice with Him as a Mother, while the angelic ranks escort thee, O pure one, and the souls of the righteous; with whom do thou ask peace and great mercy for us!

*Other Stichera of the Image, in the same Tone and Podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*  
With what eyes shall we who are earthborn look upon Thine image, shining with divine light, upon which the angelic armies cannot easily gaze? For it departs today from the land of the unbelieving, and comes unto the ruling city and unto the pious people by divine nod: at whose arrival emperors appear, falling down before it with fear and faith, O Christ![^459]

With what hands shall we who are earthly touch Thine image, O Word, being defiled with sins, of our sinless God, inaccessible unto the defiled? The cherubim cover their eyes in trembling, the seraphim cannot bear to look upon Thy glory, and creation serves Thee with fear: condemn us not therefore, who unworthily faithfully kiss Thy likeness with trembling, O Christ!

Again the divine day of the Master's feast hath arrived; for behold, He Who sits on high hath now manifestly visited us, for the sake of His venerable icon: He Who is invisible to the cherubim on high is seen in likeness wherein He became like unto us, delineated before our eyes by His most pure countenance, ineffably according unto His likeness; venerating which with faith and love, we are sanctified.

*Glory... Tone 8:*  
O Master and Lover of mankind, great is the abyss of Thy dispensation; for working benefaction unto generations of generations, having imprinted the form of Thy most pure face, Thou didst send it unto the faithful Abgar,[^460] who longed to see Thee, Who in Thy Godhead art invisible to the cherubim: that beholding Thine image, Who for our sake wast incarnate and didst suffer willingly, we might be kindled unto Thy love, which Thou hast poured out upon us for the sake of Thy great mercy!

*Both now... the same Tone:*  
Today the divine choirs mystically stand round about the bier of the Virgin and Mother, and the souls of the righteous, surrounding the Queen, glorify her: some indeed offer virginity as a gift instead of myrrh; and others offer immaterial hymnody with virtue. For it is fitting that the Mother of God, as Queen, should be escorted by royal, brightly shining virtues; with whom let us also, having brought a pure life, go forth unto the burial of the true Mother of our God, harmoniously calling her blessed with chants and spiritual songs!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Dormition, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ знаме́нїе):*  
No fiery chariot translated thee from earthly things, as Elijah the righteous; but the Sun of righteousness Himself, receiving thy most holy soul into His hands as immaculate, gave it rest in Himself; and having translated thee most gloriously, O pure one, He honored thee unto joy beyond understanding.[^461]

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy holiness.*[^462]  
Come, O ye feast-lovers, today let us radiantly celebrate the holy translation of the Mother of God; for in truth into the hands of Jesus our God, Who shone forth from the incorruptible Father before all ages, and in the last times from her womb, she rejoicing gave up her divine and most pure soul, and she prays that we may be saved.

*Verse: The Lord hath sworn truth unto David, and will not turn from it.*[^463]  
O surpassing wonder! For she who bore the uncontainable King in her womb is laid in a tomb and the angelic hosts with the apostles bury with fear that God-receiving and honorable body; and having exalted it, Jesus her Son and the Savior of our souls carried it up into the heavens.

*Glory... Tone 6:*  
Lord, Thou wast incarnate as Thou didst desire; Thou wast pleased to assume our poverty, and didst show forth the riches of Thy compassion, whereby Thou hast deified me, who am dust. We glorify Thee, O Lover of mankind, beholding the image of the dispensation of Thy countenance: through this, O Savior, grant unto Thy servants unhindered entrance into Eden, overlooking all our transgressions.

*Both now... Tone 8:*  
*By Leontios the Despot:*[^464]  
Jesus, Thy Son, O Mother of God and our God, confirmed His two natures: for as man He dies, and as God He rises; and He was well-pleased that thou also, O Mother of God, shouldest die according to the law of nature, lest unto the unbelieving the dispensation should seem to be an apparition.[^465] For thou hast been taken up unto the heavenly realm, O heavenly Bride, taken from the earth as from a bridal chamber of thy dwelling: the air was sanctified by thy departure, as the earth was enlightened by thy childbearing; the apostles send thee forth, and the angels receive thee. Therefore, having buried thy most pure body and sung the funeral song, they gazed in awe and said with fear: "This change is of the right hand of the Most High,[^466] for He is in the midst of thee, and thou shalt not be moved."[^467] But O all-hymned Mother, depart not from watching over us: for we are thy people, and the sheep of thy pasture,[^468] and we call upon thy name, asking through thee for salvation and great mercy!

*Troparion, Tone 2:*[^469]  
We venerate Thine immaculate icon, O Good One, asking forgiveness of our transgressions, O Christ God; for of Thine own will Thou wast pleased to ascend the Cross in the flesh, that Thou mightest deliver them whom Thou hadst created from the bondage of the enemy. Therefore thankfully we cry aloud unto Thee: Thou hast filled all things with joy, O our Savior, Who camest to save the world!

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 1:*  
In giving birth thou didst preserve thy virginity, in falling asleep thou didst not forsake the world, O Mother of God. Thou hast passed over unto life, being the Mother of Life; and by thy prayers thou deliverest our souls from death.

*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Image, twice:*  
*Glory... Both now... of the Feast of the Mother of God.*  
*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*  
Thy grace, O Savior, hath been poured out upon us, when Thou Who art uncircumscribed wast seen, and the darkness of deceit was quenched: therefore in the light of Thy countenance direct our steps, that walking in Thy commandments, we may be accounted worthy to see Thee, O Light unapproachable. *(Twice) Glory... Both now... Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ):*
Beholding in truth thy translation, O all-holy Lady, pure, blessed, all-hymned Mother of God, the angelic hosts, having the will of Him Who was born of thee, and the choir of the disciples having gathered together, with joy carried thy precious body into paradise, chanting unto Christ the Giver of life, Who is unto the ages.

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5, Podoben: "The Word co-unoriginate" (Собезнача́лное сло́во):*  
The Co-unoriginate Son with the Father and the Pre-eternal, invisible in essence and intangible: having come under time through unutterable mercy, by the delineation of Thy flesh, O Master, Thou hast left us a sweet image, for the salvation of our souls. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ):*  
Into the hands of Him Who for our sake was incarnate of thee having surrendered thy soul, as unto thy Creator and God, thou hast passed over unto incorruptible life. Therefore honorably we call thee blessed, the only pure and undefiled one, and all confessing thee truly to be the Mother of God, we cry aloud: Pray unto Christ, unto Whom thou hast passed over, that He save our souls!

*Canon of the Feast of the Mother of God, with the Heirmos on 6; and of the Image, on 6.*  
*Canon of the Mother of God, by Lord Cosmas, Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^470]  
Adorned with divine glory, thy sacred and glorious memory, O Virgin, hath gathered all the faithful unto gladness, while Miriam leads the dance with timbrels and choirs,[^471] chanting unto Thine Only-begotten: For gloriously hath He been glorified!

*Troparia:*  
Let the immaterial ranks escort thy divine body into Zion; and the multitude of the apostles having suddenly gathered from the ends of the earth, O Mother of God, stood before thee straightway: with whom, O pure Virgin, we glorify thy precious memory.

The prizes of victory over nature hast thou received, O pure one, having given birth unto God; yet imitating thy Creator and Son, beyond nature thou dost submit unto the laws of nature: therefore having died, thou dost rise with thy Son, abiding unto eternity.

*Canon of the Image, whose acrostic is: "I venerate the depiction of Thy printed sight, O Savior." A composition of Germanus the Patriarch, Tone 6.*[^472]  
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^473]  
Traversing the deep on foot as if on dry land, Israel, seeing Pharaoh the pursuer drowned, cried aloud: Let us chant a hymn of victory unto God!

*Troparia:*  
To be depicted bodily like unto us, Who before wast bodiless, by the Father's good pleasure, Thou didst not reject the form, and didst grant unto us a divine image.

Immutable Nature, the most clear Boundary of the Father, having come into human flesh, didst leave on earth Thine own properties unto us, when Thou didst ascend into the heavens.

Thy heritage shaken by the deceit of adversaries Thou hast justified, O Christ, having confirmed it by Thy precious passions and by the delineation of Thy countenance.

It was fitting that unto the city that reigns over all cities, the city of heaven and earth, should be brought the delineation of the icon, which He Himself having become incarnate sovereignly dipped, by the cooperation of the emperor and the Christ-lovers.[^474]

*Theotokion:*  
Having borrowed flesh beyond nature from thy pure womb, He Who grants unto all men to exist, O all-immaculate one, was seen, not having departed from where He was before.

*Katavasia of the Feast:*  
*Adorned with divine glory...*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^475]  
O Christ, the Wisdom and Power of God, Who createst and upholdest all things: establish the Church immovable and unshaken; for Thou alone art holy, resting in the saints.

*Troparia:*  
Having seen thee as a mortal woman, yet beyond nature the Mother of God, O all-immaculate one, the glorious apostles with awe touched thee with their hands, shining with glory, as they beheld a God-receiving tabernacle.

The judgment of God overtook the insolent hands of the arrogant one, inflicting severing upon them: God having preserved honor unto the living ark, through the glory of the Godhead, wherein the Word became flesh.[^476]

*Another [Canon of the Image]. Heirmos:*[^477]  
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and hast established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparia:*  
With the garment of virtues Thou hast clothed the precious Church, O Savior, having moved the emperor's heart to seek protection for Thy likeness.[^478]

Wished to strip away good things and in time to diminish them, the father of envy shamefully delivered these into the hands of the defiled; but he was put to shame, he who plotted all things.

David once rejoiced brightly, dancing before the holy ark; and now, having received the scepters to govern kingdoms, he rejoices yet more in the divine image.[^479]

Unto Thee Who didst create man according to Thine image at first: wherein Thou didst take form straightway out of compassion. Therefore, having received Thee, the truly immutable Likeness, with the angels, O Christ, we glorify Thee.

*Theotokion:*  
Desiring to cut off from the root every disobedience that sprang from the forefather, O Virgin, Thy Son from thy womb, O pure one, takes all that is human.

*Kontakion of the Feast of the Mother of God, Tone 2:*[^480]  
In prayers the unceasing Mother of God, and in intercessions our unchanging hope, the tomb and mortality held not: for as the Mother of Life, He Who dwelt in the ever-virgin womb translated her unto life.

*Ikos:*[^481]  
Surround my thoughts with a wall, O my Christ, for I dare to sing the rampart of the world, thy pure Mother: strengthen me upon the pillar of words, and defend me in difficult thoughts; for Thou fulfillest the supplications of them that cry aloud and ask in faith. Grant me therefore a fluent tongue and an unashamed mind: for every gift of illumination is sent down from Thee, O Giver of light,[^482] Who didst dwell in the ever-virgin womb.

*Sessional Hymn of the Image, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й сп҃се):*  
The King of Edessa, having recognized Thee, the King of all, Who brought forth multitudes of miracles by Thy word without scepters or armies, prayed to receive Thy divine-human likeness unto himself; and when he beheld Thee in the image, he cried aloud: Thou art God and Lord! *(Twice)*[^483]

*Glory... Both now... the same Tone, Podoben: "When the stone had been sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*  
The choir of the divine apostles gathered to bury thee, the most pure Mother of God, all-immaculate one; with whom also the angelic ranks sang of thy translation, crying aloud with surpassing praise. With them we also, rejoicing with love, O pure one, bring praise unto thee, the faithful with voices of song, and cry aloud: Rejoice, O intercessor of them that ever honor thee!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^484]  
The sayings and dark riddles of the prophets foreshowed Thine incarnation from the Virgin, O Christ: the radiance of Thy flashing brightness shall go forth for a light unto the nations, and the abyss shall shout unto Thee in gladness: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparia:*  
See, O ye peoples, and marvel: for the holy and manifest mountain of God is taken up on high into the heavenly mansions; the earthly heaven is settled into the heavenly and incorruptible dwelling![^485]

...unto everlasting and better life thy death became a passing, O pure one: translating thee from the temporal unto the divine truly and unpassing, O pure one, in gladness to behold thy Son and Lord.

The heavenly gates were lifted up, and the angels sang; and Christ received the vessel of His Mother's virginity: the cherubim yield unto thee with gladness, and the seraphim glorify thee, rejoicing![^486]

*Another [Canon of the Image]. Heirmos:*[^487]  
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church chants in a manner befitting God, crying aloud from a pure mind, feasting in the Lord!

*Troparia:*  
Sweet indeed is the sun, illuminating the eyes; but sweeter still is the sight of Thy likeness, O Christ: for the former illuminates the senses, but the latter illumines the spiritual senses.

The strength of the Ishmaelites hath failed: for the bold-minded emperor placed in his own land all the protection of the weapon of the Cross on the right hand.[^488]

All ye princes and judges of the earth, bless Christ today together with the high priests and hierarchs, and all the people, who have been accounted worthy of the sight of Him![^489]

*Theotokion:*  
That thy city may be preserved unharmed from barbarian invasion, O all-immaculate one, we pray, we pray: for behold how many snares the plotters devise against it!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^490]  
The divine and unutterable beauty of Thy virtues, O Christ, will I declare: for having shone forth from the ever-existing glory as the co-eternal and hypostatic effulgence,[^491] from a virginal womb, incarnate unto them that were in darkness and the shadow of death, Thou hast shone forth as the Sun!

*Troparia:*  
As upon a cloud, O Virgin, the apostolic choir borne along gathered unto Zion from the ends of the earth to minister unto thee, the light cloud: from whom the Most High God, unto them that were in darkness and the shadow, shone forth as the Sun of righteousness![^492]

At the tomb the God-receiving tongues of the theologically-speaking men most eloquently cried aloud unto the Mother of God, intoning by the Spirit the departure hymn: Rejoice, O incorruptible fountain of God's life-originating and saving incarnation for all!

*Another [Canon of the Image]. Heirmos:*[^493]  
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illuminate in love the souls of them that rise early unto Thee, I pray: that they may know Thee, O Word of God, the true God, Who recallest us from the gloom of sins!

*Troparia:*  
A small city of old prepared a reception for Thee, O Christ: whose infirmity the coming of Thaddaeus transformed, along with the writing of Thy hand and the divine imprint of Thy face.[^494]

...the gifts of Thy graces, O Christ, were multiplied: for those things which Edessa of old harbored and boasted in, the New Rome having now received, rejoices![^495]

The eyes of the Lord are upon the righteous rulers, and His ears attend unto the prayer of them that think piously, who honor His self-depicted likeness in a manner befitting God.[^496]

*Theotokion:*  
Stay the onslaughts of the enemies, O pure Virgin, destroy their counsels, help thine inheritance: for thou seest how we have been afflicted by malice.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^497]  
The sea-born internal fire of the whale from the deep was a prefiguration of Thy three-day burial, whereof Jonah the prophet appeared as a type: for saved as he was sent forth, unharmed he cried aloud: I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord![^498]

*Troparia:*  
The King of all, God, gives unto thee that which is above nature: for as He preserved thee a Virgin in childbearing, so in the tomb He kept thy body incorrupt, and glorified thee together by a divine translation, granting honor unto thee as a Son unto a Mother.

In truth, as a radiant lampstand of the immaterial Light, a golden censer of the divine coal, into the Holy of Holies He settled thee: the jar and the rod, the God-inscribed tablet, the holy ark, the table of the Word of life, O Virgin, thy Childbearing![^499]

*Another [Canon of the Image]. Heirmos:*[^500]  
Jonah was seized, yet not held fast in the whale's belly: for bearing the image of Thee Who didst suffer and gavest Thyself unto burial, as from a bridal chamber he came forth from the monster, and cried out unto the watch: Ye that keep vanities and lies have forsaken this mercy![^501]

*Troparia:*  
Fairer in beauty art Thou, O Savior, than all the sons of men; for though Thou hadst neither form nor comeliness at the time of Thy Passion, yet Thou didst illuminate all that exist, and didst manifest the appearance of Thy countenance: the depiction of whose likeness upon an aged cloth was granted unto us as a treasure![^502]

From the assaults inflicted by the multitude of the Hagarenes, as the ark from the aliens unto the new Israel, now unto this city Thy depiction of Thy face was given, O Christ, and the glory which it acquired: for neither was it fitting for falsehood that holy things should be cast before dogs![^503]

Unto him that knew not of Thy heavenly Kingdom, unto which, O Good One, Thou hast raised us up from the corruptible and low-lying things, nor how, being unapproachable unto the immaterial cherubim, Thou didst descend even unto hades: Thou didst send Thine apostle unto Abgar, O Christ, instructing him in the mystery.[^504]

...deprived of the strength of Thy hand, O Savior, am I now left; yet Thou hast turned away Thy wounds from me, by the prayers of her that bore Thee unwedded, O Word: leave me not to sink in the storm of sin, and to toss fiercely upon the waves, O only Deliverer!

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "In prayers" (Въ моли́твахъ):*[^505]  
Knowing the uncircumscribed Word of the Father, and Thine unpainted and God-inscribed, victorious icon, of Thine unutterable and divine dispensation toward men, and of Thine unfeigned incarnation: we honor and embrace it with veneration!

*Ikos:*[^506]  
Confirming unto men the awesome mystery of His becoming man, the Lord inscribed of His own accord His divine-human image upon the towel; and having exalted the Prototype from the Mount of Olives, He seated it with the Father upon the throne, to be worshipped by the bodiless angels; and unto us He granted to venerate the likeness of the Prototype: which embracing with soul and heart, we honor and kiss it with love!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^507]  
Opposing shameless fury and fire, divine desire bedewed the fire, and laughed fury to scorn, by the God-inspired rational three-stringed harp of the holy youths, counter-sounding unto the musical instruments in the midst of the flame: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers and our God, Who art supremely glorified!

*Troparia:*  
Moses in anger broke the God-made tablets, written by the divine Spirit; but the Master of this Law, having preserved her that bore Him unharmed into heavenly mansions, now hath settled her within: with whom rejoicing, we cry aloud unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers and our God, Who art supremely glorified![^508]

With cymbals with venerable lips, and musically with a bright heart, with a sweet-sounding trumpet, with high contemplation, clapping with active hands on the chosen and distinguished day of the translation of the pure Virgin, we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers and our God, Who art supremely glorified![^509]

Gather together, O God-wise people: for the dwelling of God's glory is translated from Zion unto the heavenly habitation, where is the pure voice of them that feast, the voice of unutterable rejoicing, and of them that cry aloud in gladness unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers and our God, Who art supremely glorified!

*Another [Canon of the Image]. Heirmos:*[^510]  
The youths in Babylon feared not the fiery flame, but cast into the midst of the flame, being bedewed they sang: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*  
By a word, O Word of God, Thou didst drive away every disease while walking upon the earth; but ascending unto the Father's throne, by Thy depiction Thou healest our infirmity.

Having created all things by Thy Word, and having taken form in that which was alien, Thou didst leave unto us the characteristics of Thy countenance, which receiving, we rejoice in gladness.

Seeking with all his soul the imprint of Thy properties, the faithful king, as he sought, so also did he receive, O Lord, obtaining in return the fulfillment of his divine desire.

Let us prepare our eyes for the vision of God, and let us strive worthily to behold the all-venerable icon of Christ, for our salvation.

*Theotokion:*  
The condemnation of ancient childbearing was loosed by thy childbearing: for having found thee alone more spacious than the heavens, O all-immaculate one, God took up His abode in thee.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^511]  
The all-powerful Angel of God showed unto the youths a flame that bedewed the holy ones, but consumed the ungodly; and He made the Mother of God a life-originating spring, pouring out the decay of death and pouring out life unto them that sing: Delivered, let us chant unto the only Creator, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*  
The entire multitude of theologians in Zion followed with words the sacred divine ark: Whither goest thou now, O tabernacle of the living God? Cease not looking down upon them that chant with faith: Delivered, let us chant unto the only Creator, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^512]

Lifting up thy hands, O all-immaculate one, as thou didst depart, the hands wherewith thou didst bear God in the flesh, with boldness as a Mother thou didst say unto Him Who was born of thee: Them whom Thou hast given me, preserve unto all ages, who cry aloud unto Thee: Delivered, let us chant unto the only Creator, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^513]

*Another [Canon of the Image]. Heirmos:*[^514]  
Enduring danger in Babylon for the ancestral laws, the blessed youths spat upon the mad command of the ruler; and united together, not burned by the fire, they sang a worthy hymn unto the Sovereign: Bless the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*  
Hoping to receive Thee, the Lord, face to face, all the most chosen people of the city make a marvelous meeting for Thee, the divine choir of the hierarchs leading the way, borne upon whose shoulders, and placed in Thine inheritance, O Compassionate One, Thou didst rest in the temple of Thy Mother.[^515]

*Troparia:*  
The mouths of innocent children sang praises of old unto Thee as Thou didst go unto the prophet-slaying city; but the lawless gathering of the Jewish assembly armed a murderous hand raging against Thee.[^516] But now the law-abiding city, all together running before Thee, seeing Thee as the Creator, rejoices, receiving the image of Thy countenance!

More than Moses the God-beholder, who received the God-inscribed tablets, hast thou been magnified, O venerable governor, having received the precious letter of the Word of God, which blesses Thee who didst believe in piety, who didst not at all see His miraculous deeds![^517]

*Theotokion:*  
He that willed to bear flesh, the Angel of Great Counsel of the Father, was fully accomplished in thy womb, O all-immaculate one: and He depicted thee below as Mother, O pure one, and renewed corrupted human nature. Whom let us all bless as Creator, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^518]  
The bounds of nature are overcome in thee, O pure Virgin: for childbearing remains virginal, and death is betrothed unto life; virgin after childbearing, and alive after death, thou savest ever thine inheritance, O Mother of God.

*Troparia:*  
The angelic hosts marveled in Zion, beholding their Master holding a woman's soul in His hands; for unto her that bore Him most purely, He proclaimed in a manner befitting a Son: Come, O pure one, be glorified with thy Son and God![^519]

The apostolic choir prepared thy God-receiving body, gazing in fear, and proclaiming with a great voice: Ascending into heavenly mansions unto thy Son, thou savest ever thine inheritance, O Mother of God!

*Another [Canon of the Image]. Heirmos:*[^520]  
Come, O ye faithful, with elevated minds let us enjoy the Master's hospitality and the immortal table in the elevated place, having learned of the ascended Word from the Word: Whom we magnify!

*Troparia:*  
An unreasoning populace spat upon Thee, O Savior, and instead of benefaction, insolently offered unseemly things unto Thee;[^521] but we who are far distant have been accounted worthy of Thy holy gifts.

Help against enemies hath He granted unto the pious and faithful rulers: that having acquired the depiction of Thy flesh as an unconquerable rampart, O Master, through these they may see Thy salvation!

O immutable Image of the Father, Effulgence of the glory of the everlasting Light, Seal of the Most High, O Word, Power, and Wisdom:

...unto them that theologize Thee, be Thou a gracious Transformer!

*Theotokion:*  
The Word possessing flesh was seen upon earth, twofold in nature from thee, O all-immaculate one, by the creation of the Godhead, united in one hypostasis, without confusion of mixture: Whom we glorify![^522]

*Exapostilarion of the Image, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите):*[^523]  
O Christ, the unwaning Light, illumine us with the countenance of Thy face, and make us worthy of the brightness of the saints, when Thou shalt sit to render unto each according to his works, by the prayers of the Mother of God who bore Thee. *(Twice)*

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*[^524]  
O how great things the Mighty One hath done for thee![^525] For thou wast born of a barren woman, yet thou didst give birth being a virgin, and didst remain a virgin again; and thou abidest after death, having become pure from death: and not forsaking the world, thou prayest for the world.

*At the Praises, Stichera of the Image, on 4, Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*[^526]  
Being God in the form of God, as the effulgence of glory and the express image of the Father,[^527] through boundless compassion, by divine will Thou wast seen as an icon, having borne the form of a servant. The likeness thereof He alone, as He Himself knows, distinctly formed in a God-originating manner in the likeness of His own face: by whose arrival He united the multitude of angels with men, and enlightened the visible and invisible world. *(Twice)*

He Who of old by the beholding of His back parts glorified the face of Moses, and thereby sent the God-inscribed Law unto the unbelieving people of Israel:[^528] behold, He hath come as out of Egypt, having arrived from the land of aliens into the Christ-named city of the new people, by the divine depiction of His awesome Archetypal Image: which we, being accounted worthy to behold face to face, glorify!

He Who reigns over all creation, having accepted voluntary poverty, enriches with Godhead them that have recognized His Lordship: therefore also having sent God-inscribed letters unto Abgar,[^529] who asked for salvation and health, from the likeness of Thy divine countenance: whose arrival joyfully celebrating now, let us rejoice in faith, drawing forth forgiveness!

*Glory... the same Tone:*[^530]  
Let the clouds drop down sweetness, and let the mountains leap for joy in gladness, at the most glorious wonders of Christ our God! For yesterday on Mount Tabor, the light of His Godhead shone forth, confirming the supreme disciples, and Moses and Elijah with glory He placed alongside, witnessing unto Him, ruling over the Law, and holding sway over both the living and the dead.[^531] Today in the midst of the inhabited earth, the radiant depiction of God, confirming all things, shines forth, that He is our God Who hath enriched us: unto Whom we cry aloud: Glory to Thy love for mankind, O Christ!

*Both now... Tone 2:*[^532]  
It was fitting for the eyewitnesses and ministers of the Word to behold also the Dormition in the flesh of His Mother, the final mystery concerning her: that they might not only behold the Savior's ascension from the earth, but might also bear witness unto the translation of her that bore Him. Therefore, having gathered from every quarter by divine power, they arrived at Zion, and escorted her as she went unto heaven, who is higher than the Cherubim: whom we also with them worship, as she prays for our souls.

*Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, the Troparion of the Image; Glory... Both now... of the Feast of the Mother of God.*[^533]

*At the Liturgy. The Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 1, on 4; and of the Image, Ode 6, on 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4:* Sing unto the Lord a new song, for the Lord hath done marvelous things.[^534] *Verse:* All the ends of the earth have seen the salvation of our God. *The Epistle to the Colossians, composite pericope 250; or to the Corinthians, pericope 173. Then the Epistle of the day. Alleluia, Tone 4:* Lord, in the light of Thy countenance shall we walk, and in Thy name shall we rejoice unto ages.[^535] *The Gospel of Luke, pericope 48 from the half. Communion Hymn:* Lord, in the light of Thy countenance shall we walk, and in Thy name shall we rejoice unto ages.

*The Service of the Holy Diomedes is chanted at Compline.*[^536]

*Canon of the Holy Diomedes, Tone 4.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^537]  
I will open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I will utter a word unto the Queen and Mother; and I shall be seen brightly keeping festival, and rejoicing I will sing her wonders.

*Troparia:*  
Illumined by the tri-sunned Light, dispel the gloom of mine ignorance, O blessed one: that I may sing of thy light-bearing memory, and the rectitude of thy life.

*Troparia:*  
Having rendered thy mind inaccessible to the passions, thou wast manifested, O glorious one, as a dwelling-place of the Spirit, and didst pour forth an ocean of healings, drowning all the defilement of diseases.

Desiring endless life, thou didst hasten to drink the cup of death, O all-praised one, which also thou didst obtain, even as He Who called thee knows, unto an unfading crown, O invincible martyr!

*Theotokion:*  
O ensouled city of the King, all-immaculate one, who hast shown forth a strange childbearing and a divine dormition: show thy city unharmed from every earthquake and ruin.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^538]  
Establish thy singers, O Mother of God, thou living and plentiful fount, who have gathered together a choir spiritually: in thy divine glory, account them worthy of crowns of glory.

*Troparia:*  
By ineffable and divine surgery, purifying the passions of men, thou hast been given from God unto the faithful as an excellent physician, and a fervent intercessor for them that honor thee, O most rich one!

Unto the path of Thy testimonies I run, and imitating Thy voluntary Passion, the martyr cried aloud, as he was dragged unto slaughter, unto Thee, the only God of ours!

The glorious martyr Diomedes, having summoned us mystically unto a table today, sets forth his sufferings as immaterial food: come, ye faithful, let us delight therein!

*Theotokion:*  
This city of thine, O pure one, from every earthquake and ruin deliver, and preserve in peace and concord, O Bride of God, for upon thee, O full of God's grace, hath it cast its hope.

*Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*[^539]  
Filled with divine gifts, thou wast manifested as a physician of souls and bodies, and having been fittingly enriched with the martyrs' fair renown: rejoicing, thou didst pass over unto the ever-living mansions. Therefore, celebrating thy holy memory, we cry aloud: Remember us that honor thee, O God-wise and most rich one!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^540]  
The unsearchable divine counsel of Thine incarnation from a Virgin, the prophet having perceived, cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparia:*  
Bearing in his heart the word of divine understanding as a river, he flooded the streams of deception, and submerged idol-might unto the end, the glorious martyr Diomedes!

He shone forth as the sun upon earth with the radiance of virtues, and of wonders with the richness of wonders, and illumined the assemblies of the Orthodox faithful, the all-wise martyr Diomedes.

Thy temple hath appeared as a free clinic unto all that have recourse in faith, O most blessed one, and a firm rampart unto the imperial city, fencing off the invasions of alien foes.

*Theotokion:*  
Strange was thy childbearing, as also thy venerable dormition was shown forth, O all-immaculate, blessed one: which thy city honors, escaping the injuries of aliens through thy fervent intercession.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^541]  
All things were amazed at thy divine glory: for thou, O Virgin who knewest not wedlock, didst hold in thy womb Him Who is God over all, and gavest birth unto an eternal Son, granting peace unto all that sing praises unto thee.

*Troparia:*  
Thou wast manifested as a bloodless martyr worthy of wonder, a crowned victor without sufferings, an excellent conqueror without wounds, casting down the ranks of enemies by thy struggles, O blessed Diomedes!

An unquenchable light shone forth unto thee who art righteous, with which, as its spouse, O God-wise one, gladness followed thee, as one having an upright soul, following all the commandments of the Savior.

The tormentors were indeed deprived of bodily light who cut off thy head after thy decease; but the eyes of the soul are radiantly opened, as they stand before Christ in faith, O marvelous Diomedes![^542]

*Theotokion:*  
Having thee as a rampart, O Queen and Lady, the imperial city overcomes the beast-like assaults of the barbarians, is delivered from grievous perils, and grows and is confirmed, O all-immaculate Mother of God!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^543]  
Celebrating this divine and all-venerable feast of the Mother of God, come, ye God-wise, let us clap our hands, and glorify God Who was born of her!

*Troparia:*  
By the course of thy struggles, thou didst bury the bodiless foe, and by the gushing forth of thy wonders, thou washest away the manifold defilements of the passions, O right-worthy of wonder!

Moved by divine breaths, guided by the nod from on high, thou hast rightly reached the calm haven, praying for us, O all-praised martyr!

The pastures of the passions are destroyed by thine ineffable surgery, O glorious one, and the demonic host is driven away by thy divine intercession, O most blessed martyr!

*Theotokion:*  
In thee, O Virgin, we boast, through thee we are delivered from enemies, our hope by our hope in thee, we shall not fear the assault of alien foes, who sing praises unto thee.

*Kontakion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the things on high" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):*[^544]  
A true and invincible passion-bearer, a sacred initiate of piety and grace thou wast manifested, O glorious Diomedes; having conquered the tormentor, and become a trophy-bearer, thou wast glorified with victorious crowns!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^545]  
The God-wise youths served not the creature rather than the Creator; but manfully trampling upon the threatening fire, they rejoiced, chanting: Blessed art Thou, O supremely praised Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*  
When thy head was severed, light shone forth unto them that cut it off; beholding which as though alive, the tormentor was put to shame, slain in his mind; yet he did not understand to cry aloud, O all-glorious one: Blessed art Thou, O God!

Thou didst strip off the deformed garments of mortification, O glorious one, and by grace didst clothe thyself in the fair robe of immortality. Therefore, rejoicing, thou criest aloud unto thy Master: Blessed art Thou, O God!

With the fire of endurance thou didst consume the material deceit of the enemies; and as a star called by God hast thou appeared, O martyr, flashing rays of healings upon them that cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*  
O Queen of the world, who gavest birth unto Christ the King, deliver the reigning city from corruption, earthquake, and the assault of foreign nations, O Lady, as it sings: Blessed is the Fruit of thy womb!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^546]  
The pious youths in the furnace the offspring of the Mother of God saved; then indeed as a type, but now as fulfillment, He raises up all the inhabited earth to chant unto Thee: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*  
Thy venerable feet, O passion-bearer, were directed toward the paths of martyrdom; and now, rejoicing, they walk in the land of the living, O all-wise one, in joyful delight. Therefore, having gathered together, we honor thee, chanting praises unto Christ unto the ages!

A crown of incorruption is woven for thee, who didst contend steadfastly and didst conquer the evil-originating dragon, O martyr worthy of praise; and the choir of sufferers received thee, as thou criest: Praise the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Thou sendest forth rays of wonders, O martyr, clothed with divine power: thou allayest sicknesses, loosest diseases, and drivest away evil spirits, crying aloud: Praise the Lord, all ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*  
Bowed down by many sinful assaults, unto thine immovable protection we flee: confirm thy servants, strengthen them against the passions, and deliver thy flock from earthquake, famine, and the invasion of foreign nations, O Virgin, for upon thee hath it cast its hope.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^547]  
Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, being enlightened by the Spirit; and let the nature of the bodiless minds keep festival, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let it cry aloud: Rejoice, O all-blessed Mother of God, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Troparia:*  
Thou hast found the recompense of thy labors, the nourishment prepared for thee on high. Dwelling there, adorned with martyr-beauties, thou delightest in the comeliness of thy Creator: ask for them that honor thee, O martyr, cleansing and deliverance from evils!

A pillar of virtues and a confirmation of the faith, an immovable rampart and an unyielding wall hast thou appeared, O martyr, unto the Church of Christ; and a river of healings, gushing forth streams of free remedies unto them that celebrate thy memory with love, O all-glorious one!

Thou didst drive away idol-godlessness from the earth, thou didst consume the gloom, and didst pass over unto the unwaning dawn of the light-originating life. The manifestation thereof thou didst first acquire, O glorious one, illumined by fiery radiances and filled with everlasting joy.

*Theotokion:*  
O City of God, into which He Himself entered, truly unshakable, deliver thy flock from earthquake and ruin, O Virgin, from barbarian captivity, and the fire and sword of enemies, from all affliction, O all-immaculate one, through thy fervent intercession unto Christ!

*Stichera of the Holy Diomedes, Tone 1, Podoben: "As noble among the martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*[^548]  
To heal passions, to drive away sicknesses, to expel evil spirits by the operation of the Holy Spirit wast thou accounted worthy, O most blessed one, having manfully destroyed the pagan rites by the resistance of thy suffering, O crowned martyr, fellow-citizen of angels, co-dweller with the passion-bearers!

Thou didst fight the good fight, and didst finish the course, and didst keep the faith, O glorious one,[^549] and didst depart unto God, having received from Him the gift of wonders and the unfading crown of victory, as an unconquered martyr, as an heir of the Kingdom on high, praying for the world.

*Glory...:*[^550]  
In the path of thy martyrdom exulting as in riches, Diomedes, by Thy will, O Lord, committed his spirit into Thy hands, O Christ; he was beheaded, and after his blessed decease became a guide a true guide unto the godless who were blinded, and who marvelously received sight through faith.

*Both now...: Instead of a Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion, we chant: It is truly meet...*

* * *

*It should be known: If the Afterfeast of the Dormition of the Mother of God and of the Icon Not-Made-by-Hands occur on a Sunday, outside the temple [of the dedicatee]:*[^551]

*On Saturday at Small Vespers, the Sunday stichera, and of the Mother of God according to custom. At Great Vespers, after the Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried," Sunday stichera, 3; and of the feast, 3; and of the Image, 4. Glory... of the Image; Both now... Dogmatic Theotokion of the Tone. Entrance. At the Litiya, the stichera of the feast, the aposticha of Vespers of that day. Glory... of the Image; Both now... of the feast, Tone 5: "Chant, ye peoples." At the Aposticha, Sunday stichera. Glory... of the Image; Both now... of the feast. At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion of the Feast, twice; and of the Image, once. If there is no Vigil, after "Now lettest Thou depart," the Sunday troparion; Glory... of the Image; Both now... of the feast. At Compline, after "It is truly meet," the Kontakion of the Image; Glory... Both now... of the feast. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Sunday troparion, twice; Glory... of the Image; Both now... of the feast. And the rest we chant as on Sunday up to the Canon. The Sunday Canon with the Heirmos on 4; and of the feast on 4; and of the Image on 6. Katavasia of the feast: "Adorned with divine glory..."*[^552] *After Ode 3, the Kontakion and Ikos of the feast; and the Sessional Hymn of the Image; Glory... Both now... of the feast. After Ode 6, the Kontakion and Ikos of the Image. At Ode 9, we chant "More honorable." Sunday Exapostilarion; Glory... of the Image; Both now... of the feast. At the Praises, Sunday stichera, 4; and of the Image, 4, with its verses. Glory... the Evangelical sticheron; Both now... "Supremely blessed art thou." Great Doxology. Then the Sunday Troparion, litanies, and dismissal, and catechesis, and departure into the narthex, and the First Hour. At the Hours, we say the Sunday Troparion; Glory... of the Image; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," the Sunday Kontakion. At the Third Hour, the Sunday Troparion; Glory... of the feast; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," the Kontakion of the Image. Likewise we say also at the remaining Hours, the Sunday troparion; but of the Image and the feast we alternate the troparion and kontakion. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone on 4; and of the feast, Ode 1, on 4; and of the Image, Ode 6, on 4. After the Entrance, the Sunday troparion, and of the Image, and of the feast. The Sunday Kontakion; Glory... of the Image; Both now... of the feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, Gospel, and Communion Hymn: of Sunday, and of the Image.*

*If the Afterfeast of the Dormition of the Mother of God occur on a Saturday:*[^553]  
*We say at "Lord, I have cried," Glory... of the feast; Both now... the first Theotokion of the current Tone. At Matins, the Canon of the feast on 6, and of the saint on 6.*

*If the Afterfeast of the Dormition of the Most Holy Mother of God occur on a Sunday, outside the Feast of the Icon Not-Made-by-Hands:*[^554]  
*On Saturday at Small Vespers, the Sunday stichera, and of the Mother of God, according to custom. At Great Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," Sunday stichera, 3; and an Anatolian [sticheron], 1; and of the feast, of that day in order, 3; and of the Menaion of the saint, 3. Glory... of the feast, or of the saint; Both now... the first Theotokion of the Tone. Entrance; Prokeimenon of the day; and the litanies. At the Litiya, stichera of the feast, the aposticha of Vespers of that day. Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Aposticha, Sunday stichera. Glory... of the saint. But if not, Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Blessing of the Loaves, Troparion: "O Mother of God and Virgin, rejoice," twice; and of the feast, once; and reading from the Apostolic Epistles. At Matins, the Sunday Troparion, twice; Glory... of the saint. But if not, Glory... Both now... of the feast. After the Kathismata, the Sunday Sessional Hymns, with their Theotokia; and reading from the Explanatory Gospel. Then the Blameless [Kathisma 17]; and the Troparia, "The assembly of the angels"; and the Hypakoe of the Tone, and reading. The Anabathmoi, and Prokeimenon of the Tone. The Sunday Gospel. "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ," and Psalm 50, and the rest as on Sunday. The Sunday Canon with the Heirmos on 4; and of the Mother of God on 2; and of the feast on 4; and of the saint on 4. Katavasia of the feast. After Ode 3, the Kontakion and Ikos of the feast, or of the saint; and the Sessional Hymn: Glory... Both now... of the feast. After Ode 6, the Sunday Kontakion and Ikos. At Ode 9, we chant "More honorable." Sunday Exapostilarion; Glory... of the saint. But if not: Glory... Both now... of the feast. At the Praises, Sunday stichera, 4; and of the feast, 4: the aposticha of that day, with the Doxastikon, with the verses of the feast. Glory... the Evangelical sticheron; Both now... "Supremely blessed art thou." After the Great Doxology, the Sunday Troparion only; and the litanies; and the dismissal; and the catechesis; and departure into the narthex; and the First Hour. At the First Hour, the Sunday Troparion; Glory... of the feast; Both now... Theotokion of the Hours. After "Our Father," the Kontakion of the feast; and the final dismissal. At the Third Hour, the Sunday Troparion; Glory... of the saint. But if not: Glory... of the feast. After the Trisagion, the Kontakion of the feast and the Sunday [kontakion], alternating as we say them. At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone on 6; and of the feast, from the canon, the appointed ode, on 4. After the Entrance, the Sunday troparion, and of the feast; and of the temple of the saint, and of the saint if there be one. Then the Sunday kontakion, and of the temple of the saint [male] or of the saint [female]. Glory... of the saint, if there be one; but if not: Glory... of the temple of the saint* of the saint; Both now... of the feast. Prokeimenon, Sunday of the Tone, and of the feast; the Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel of the day. Communion Hymn: "Praise the Lord from the heavens," and of the feast. If the saint have an Epistle and Gospel: then we chant the Prokeimenon, Sunday of the Tone, and of the saint; the Epistle and Gospel of the day, and of the saint. Communion Hymn: "Praise the Lord from the heavens," and of the saint.

*In the same month, on the 17th day: The Holy Martyr Myron.*[^555]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "Being called from on high" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*[^556]  
Thou who didst conceive Life, O Mother of God, diest by the law of nature, and passest from earth unto the heavenly realms, at the nod of Him Who was incarnate of thy most pure and divine blood. Therefore from the ends of the earth all the God-proclaiming apostles stood by thee to bury thee, singing unto thee: O ensouled throne of the King of all, and venerable ark of sanctity, rejoice, thou who alone gavest birth unto the Savior of our souls!

Receptors of the purest Light, luminaries who dispelled the night of polytheism, the Savior's apostles, when they learned by the Spirit that thou, O blessed one, wast translated from worldly things unto transcendent joy, crossed upon clouds unto the divine cloud from which the unwaning Light shone forth, and escorting thee with departing hymns unto the life-bearing tomb, O Mother of God, our hope!

Let creation celebrate prayerfully: the Queen of all hath passed unto the intelligible Kingdom, to reign together with Him Who reigns over all creation. For whose sake the kingdom of hades was destroyed, and we were raised up from the earth, and were accounted worthy to dwell with angels: by whose dormition all the intelligible nature was gathered together, patriarchs, prophets, and apostles with the martyrs!

*Stichera of the Saint, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^557]  
When the godless kindling consumed every land, O most blessed one, then, ignited by the warmth of the Spirit, thou didst proclaim the Word Who was clad in flesh from the Maiden Virgin of God for goodness' sake. Therefore thou didst endure the torment of fire, the cruel afflictions, being strengthened by the power of grace.

When the evil-named foe sought by flatteries to steal thy mind, then, adorned with courage, thou didst steadfastly resist: and didst endure torments which lead thee unto painless rest, O praiseworthy martyr Myron, unto the heavenly Kingdom and everlasting delight.

When the enemy beat thee with raw ox-sinews, exhausting thy sacred flesh with ceaseless scrapings, O martyr, thou didst look unto Christ the combat-master, stretching forth His hand of divine power unto thee. Therefore, having finished the course, thou didst obtain great honors, O Myron, most noble sufferer!

*Glory... Both now... the same Tone [Tone 2], Idiomelon:*[^558]  
She who is higher than the heavens, and more glorious than the Cherubim, and more honorable than all creation: who for her exceeding purity became the dwelling-place of the Everlasting Essence, today commits her all-holy soul into the hands of her Son: and with her all things are filled with joy, and she grants great mercy unto us!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera [of the Feast], Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^559]  
With hymns escorting thy venerable and God-receiving body, the divine apostles cried aloud: Whither now art thou departing, O Lady?

*Verse:* Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy holiness.[^560]

Come, ye earthborn, let us form a choir, crying the departing chant at the translation of the Mother of God today!

*Verse:* The Lord hath sworn in truth unto David, and He will not turn aside from it.[^561]

The earth indeed was blessed by thy burial, O Virgin; and the air was sanctified by thy strange ascension, having died by the law of nature.

*Glory... Both now... the same Tone [Tone 2], Idiomelon:*[^562]  
The all-immaculate Bride, and Mother of the Father's good pleasure, who was pre-ordained for God into His own dwelling-place of unconfused union, today commits her most pure soul unto the Creator and God: which the hosts of the bodiless receive in a manner befitting God, and she is translated unto life, being the Mother of Life: the lamp of the unapproachable Light, the salvation of the faithful, and the hope of our souls!

*Troparion of the Feast, Tone 4 [Tone 1]:*[^563]  
In childbearing thou didst preserve virginity, in thy dormition thou didst not forsake the world, O Mother of God: thou wast translated unto life, being the Mother of Life, and by thy prayers thou deliverest our souls from death.

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "At the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́тва):*[^564]  
Into the heavens Into the heavens thy noetic soul, into paradise thy pure body, having been translated from corruption, O all-immaculate one, into a radiant place: therefore the Lord shall reward the lawless, for they wrought deceit against thy venerable body. Therefore with the apostles we cry aloud: Rejoice, O full of grace!

*Glory... Both now... the same [Sessional Hymn].*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^565]  
Into the hands of Him Who became man for our sake from thee, having committed thy soul as unto thy Creator and God, thou hast been translated unto incorruptible life. Therefore we honorably bless thee, confessing thee alone as pure and undefiled, and truly the Mother of God, crying aloud: Pray to Christ, unto Whom thou hast been translated, to save our souls!

*Glory... Both now... the same [Sessional Hymn].*

*Canon of the Feast with the Heirmos on 8, the work of Kyr John of Damascus, Tone 4.*[^566]

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*  
I will open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I will utter a word unto the Queen and Mother; and I shall be seen brightly keeping festival, and rejoicing I will sing her dormition.

*Troparia:*  
Ye maiden handmaidens, with Miriam the prophetess, shout now a departing song:[^567] for the Virgin and only Mother of God is translated unto the heavenly reception!

Worthily, as an ensouled heaven, the heavenly, all-pure, divine habitations received thee, and thou standest brightly adorned as an all-immaculate Bride unto the King and God!

*Canon of the Saint on 4, whose Acrostic is:* I sing, O Myron, thy myrrh-breathing grace. Joseph.[^568]

*Tone 2, Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^569]  
Come, ye peoples, let us chant a hymn unto Christ God, Who divided the sea, and guided the people whom He led forth from the bondage of Egypt, for He is glorified!

*Troparia:*  
Forming a choir in the memory of the sufferer Myron, let us sing praise unto God today, unto Him Who strengthened him to destroy the dominion of the adversary!

Thou didst offer thyself as a gift unto the Benefactor, a sacred oblation, a good slaughter, and a fragrant sacrifice, having suffered lawfully, O much-suffering martyr!

As wise, as a pure temple of Him Who shone forth from the Virgin, O glorious one: thou didst drive away the soul-destroying wolf who rushed to trouble the divine flock.

*Theotokion:*  
Alone thou didst give birth unto God without knowing wedlock, Whom the passion-bearing martyrs confessing, became likened unto us, and abolished deceit, O pure Lady!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^570]  
Establish thy singers, O Mother of God, thou living and plentiful fount, who have gathered together a choir *(Fol. 94r)*
...spiritually establish the choir gathered together for thee, and in thy divine memory vouchsafe them crowns of glory.

*Troparion:*
Having sprung forth from mortal loins, being like unto nature, O pure one, she dissolved her departure; and having given birth unto the true Life, she passed over unto divine and hypostatic Life.

*Troparion:*
The choir of theologians from the ends [of the earth], and from on high the multitudes of angels, came unto Zion by the all-powerful nod [of God], duly serving at thy burial, O Sovereign Lady.

*Another Canon. Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^571]
Having established me upon the rock of faith, Thou hast enlarged my mouth against mine enemies, for my spirit rejoiced in singing: There is none holy as our God, and there is none righteous more than Thee, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Having elevated thy true desire unto the Lord Who was lifted up upon the Cross, thou wast lifted up, O most glorious one, scraped upon the tree, fervently crying aloud: For there is none righteous more than Thee, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
With the sharp arrows of thy words thou didst pierce the tormentor through the heart, O valiant warrior of Christ the King, all-lauded one; and singing thou didst say: There is none righteous more than Thee, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Having shone forth as the dawn, as the day, thou didst dispel the darkness of ignorance, and didst illumine those who ever sing, O much-suffering Myron: Thou art our God, and there is none righteous more than Thee, O Lord.

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto God the Healer, O Virgin, thou didst most gloriously heal all creation which was ailing with godlessness. Therefore, giving thanks, we cry aloud unto thee: There is none all-pure more than thee, O Sovereign Lady.

*Kontakion of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Who wast lifted up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Having loved Christ from Thy youth, O most glorious one, and keeping His divine commandments, thou didst hasten wholly intact unto Him, O all-venerable Myron; and fervently praying with the angels, ask remission of sins for all who honor Thy memory.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy tomb, O Savior" (Гро́бъ тво́й Сп҃се):*
Thou hast shown thyself as a fire-breathing fragrance; by thy suffering thou dost namesake-like sweeten our hearts, O adornment of martyrs and embellishment of the faithful. Therefore, as we celebrate thy most holy memory today, we are all cleansed who honor thee with love.

*Glory... Both now... Tone and Podoben the same (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
The divine choir of the all-wise apostles was marvelously gathered together to bury gloriously thy most pure body, O all-hymned Mother of God. With them also the multitude of angels *(Fol. 94v)*
...choring, honorably praising thy dormition, which we celebrate with faith.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^572]
Perceiving the unsearchable divine counsel of Thine incarnation from the Virgin, O Thou Most High, the Prophet Habakkuk cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
It was a marvel to behold the animate heaven of the King of all passing beyond the earthly realms: For marvelous are Thy works; glory to Thy power, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Though her incomprehensible Fruit, through Whom the heavens came to be, willingly accepted burial as mortal: how should she who bore Him without knowing wedlock refuse burial?

*Troparion:*
At thy departure, O Mother of God, the angelic hosts covered thy most spacious and God-receiving body with sacred wings in trembling and joy.

*Another Canon. Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^573]
I sing to Thee, for by hearing, O Lord, I heard and was dismayed, for Thou comest unto me, seeking me who had gone astray. Therefore I glorify Thy great condescension toward me, O Thou Who art plenteous in mercy.

*Troparion:*
Confessing the Savior, God, and Lord Who shone forth from the Virgin in the midst of the judgment hall, O glorious one, thou didst humble the insolence of the tormentors by thy patience, and didst destroy the multiplicity of gods.

*Troparion:*
In no wise terrified when scraped and beaten, O valiant-minded one, thou didst astonish the angels who beheld thy patience, whereby having destroyed the bodiless foes, thou didst become a victorious martyr.

*Troparion:*
Filled from on high with divine dew, thou didst pass with valiant mind through the fiery furnace, seen therein, O wise Myron, rejoicing with the angels and in no wise scorched by the flame.

*Theotokion:*
Great and fearful is the mystery of thy childbearing; it astonishes every mind, O pure one: for God in His goodness was well-pleased to become incarnate from thee for the salvation and restoration of the world.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^574]
All things were amazed at thy venerable dormition; for thou, O Virgin who knewest not wedlock, didst pass from earth unto the eternal mansions and unto endless life, bestowing salvation upon all who hymn thee.

*Troparion:*
Let the trumpets of the theologians sound forth today, and let the eloquent tongue of man praise now; let the air resound, shining with immeasurable light; let the angels sing of the dormition of the all-pure Virgin.

*Troparion:*
A chosen vessel was meet to be wholly enraptured by hymnody at thy departure, O Virgin, wholly consecrated *(Fol. 95r)*
...unto God, acceptable unto all, and truly shown to be such, O all-hymned Mother of God.

*Another Canon. Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^575]
O Light of those lying in darkness, Salvation of the despairing, O Christ my Savior: unto Thee I rise early, O King of peace; illumine me with Thy radiance, for other than Thee I know no God.

*Troparion:*
Thy radiant and light-bearing memory, extended throughout all the world by the power of the Holy Spirit, with the illumination of piety illumines those who honor it and proclaim thy valiant deeds.

*Troparion:*
Youthfully didst thou endure bitter torments as one bodiless, O valiant martyr; for when flayed of thy skin, thou didst endure as if another were suffering, O glorious one, manifestly strengthened by an invisible power.

*Troparion:*
Thy sufferings dropped fragrant myrrh, O martyr, driving away all the stench of godlessness, and ever sweetening the holy Church of Christ, which faithfully and in love manifestly calls thee blessed.

*Theotokion:*
Pray Him Who was incarnate from thee by an ineffable word for our sake, O all-pure and blessed one, that we who ever confess thee to be the Mother of God may be delivered from all visible and invisible enemies, O all-immaculate one.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^576]
Celebrating this divine and all-venerable feast of the Mother of God, come, O divinely wise, let us clap our hands, glorifying God Who was born of her.

*Troparion:*
Life shone forth from thee, without breaking the seals of virginity; how then was thy most pure and life-originating body made partaker of the trial of death?

*Troparion:*
Having been the temple of Life, thou didst obtain everlasting life; for through death thou didst pass over into life, having given birth unto hypostatic Life.

*Another Canon. Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^577]
Unto the Lord out of the whale Jonah cried aloud: Praying, lead me up out of the depths of hades; that as unto the Deliverer, with the voice of praise and the spirit of truth, I may sacrifice unto Thee.

*Troparion:*
By the sprinkling of thy sacred blood thou didst drown the demonic regiments, O valiant warrior; and bearing a crown thou didst ascend unto God, having become an excellent victor.

*Troparion:*
Beholding thy face illumined with divine brightness, O passion-bearer, the tormentors were astonished; yet not knowing God, they received darkness, being dispatched unto eternal torment.

*Troparion:*
By the command of the cruel tormentor, thou didst endure the stripping off of thy skin beyond nature, O praiseworthy one, having shown thy pure *(Fol. 95v)*
...thy pure desire and continual vision, O all-blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
The laws of nature are renewed in thee, O Virgin; for beyond nature thou didst most gloriously give birth unto God Emmanuel, incomprehensible by nature, for our rebirth who believe.

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 2:*
The tomb and mortality could not hold the Mother of God, who is unsleeping in her supplications and an unfailing hope in intercessions; for as the Mother of Life, He Who dwelt in her ever-virginal womb translated her unto life.

*Ikos:*
Hedge round my thoughts, O my Christ, for I dare to hymn the wall of the world, Thy pure Mother; establish me upon the pillar of words, and protect me in heavy thoughts, for Thou fulfillest the petitions of those who cry aloud and ask in faith. Grant me, therefore, an apt tongue and a blameless mind, for every bestowal of illumination is sent down from Thee, O Giver of light, Who didst dwell in her ever-virginal womb.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^578]
The divinely wise served not the creation rather than the Creator, but valiantly trampling underfoot the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord and God of our fathers, praised above all.

*Troparion:*
Ye young men and maidens, honoring the memory of the Virgin and Mother of God, ye elders and rulers, kings and judges, sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord and God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Let the heavenly mountains sound with the spiritual trumpet, let the hills rejoice, and let the divine apostles dance: the Queen is translated unto her Son, reigning with Him.

*Troparion:*
