The all-sacred translation of Thy divine and incorrupt Mother has gathered together the transcendent ranks of the supernal powers, to rejoice with those on earth, who sing unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, O God.

*Another Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^579]
When the golden image was served on the plain of Dura, Thy three youths disregarded the godless decree; cast into the midst of the fire, being bedewed, they sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Thy mind brightly inflamed with divine desire, O wise one, rejoiced and was glad as thou didst stand in the midst of the flame, even as the three youths; with whom thou didst faithfully cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Beholding thee standing in the flame, O wise one, in the midst of angels, shining with ineffable form, the onlookers were astonished; and they turned unto singing, crying aloud *(Fol. 96r)*
...crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Standing with a pure mind, thou singest with the noetic angels, rejoicing in the midst of the furnace, praising the all-good Lord, Who curbed the flame and saved thee as thou didst sing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast been revealed, O all-pure Virgin, as the receptacle of God, a fair palace, and the divine throne upon which He sat and prepared a heavenly session for all. Therefore we cry aloud: Blessed is she who bore God in the flesh.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^580]
The childbearing of the Mother of God saved the pious youths in the furnace: then prefigured, but now fulfilled in act, it moves the whole world to sing unto Thee: Sing unto the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Thy memory, O pure Virgin, the principalities, powers, and virtues, angels, archangels, thrones, dominions, cherubim, and fearful seraphim glorify; and we, the human race, sing and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
He Who strangely made His abode in thy pure womb, becoming incarnate, O Mother of God, received this all-holy spirit of thine and gave it rest with Himself, as a Son Who was a debtor. Therefore we sing unto thee, O Virgin, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
O the marvels of the Ever-Virgin and Mother of God, surpassing the mind! For having made her dwelling in the tomb, she showed it forth as paradise; standing before which today, rejoicing we sing: Sing unto the Lord, ye works, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Another Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^581]
In the furnace of the youths Thou didst of old prefigure Thy Mother, O Lord; and this image delivered from the fire those who entered unburned. Her we sing, who through Thee was revealed unto the ends [of the earth] today, and we exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Beholding thee invincible, O valiant-minded one, when he cut leather strips even unto the very bones, the senseless tormentor added to tear thee with claws, having been incurably wounded by intolerable wounds.

*Troparion:*
A divine voice from heaven was brought unto thee, O martyr Myron, in the hearing of all the people, calling thee unto those ineffable resting places, O glorious one, and unto the fair choir-stations of the angels.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stand like Daniel in the midst of beasts, which were abashed at thy blessed passion and the immeasurable greatness of thy struggles, O all-blessed one, and which were kept alive by thy voice of intercession.

*Theotokion:*
(Continues onto next leaf) *(Fol. 96v)*
Having shot me with an arrow, the enemy wounded my soul utterly, and I suffer incurably. O all-immaculate one who didst ineffably bear Christ the Savior, heal me and save me, thou hope of the hopeless.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^582]
Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, being illumined in spirit; and let the nature of the bodiless minds celebrate, honoring the sacred translation of the Mother of God, and cry aloud: Rejoice, O all-blessed Mother of God, pure Ever-Virgin.

*Troparion:*
Come unto Zion, unto the divine and fertile mountain of the living God, let us rejoice, beholding the Mother of God; for Christ has translated her as a holy mother into the Holy of Holies, unto an exceedingly better and divine tabernacle.

*Troparion:*
Come, ye faithful, let us draw near to the tomb of the Mother of God, and embrace it with our hearts and lips, touching it purely with our eyes and face, and drawing forth the unstinting gifts of healings from the ever-flowing fountain.

*Troparion:*
Receive from us a departing hymn, O Mother of the living God, and overshadow us with thy light-bearing and divine grace: granting victories unto the King,[^583] peace unto the Christ-loving people, forgiveness unto those who sing, and salvation unto our souls.

*Another Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^584]
O God the Word of God, Who by ineffable wisdom camest to renew Adam who had grievously fallen into corruption through eating, and Who for our sake wast ineffably incarnate of the holy Virgin: Thee, O faithful, with one accord we magnify in hymns.

*Troparion:*
That thou mightest valiantly be vouchsafed to behold future glory and ineffable beauty, Christ the Contest-Arbiter, thou dost bow thy neck and receive decapitation by the sword, and dost fill the divine regiments of martyrs with joy.

*Troparion:*
Even as sweet-breathing myrrh, O praiseworthy Myron, sweetening the hearts of the faithful, thy praiseworthy memory has arrived for those who yearn for it; wherein, through thy prayers, fill us who celebrate with divine sweet fragrance.

*Troparion:*
Achaia boasts in thy family and sufferings, O valiant sufferer of the Lord; but yet more is Cyzicus adorned, possessing thy much-suffering body as true riches, a fountain of healings, and a purgation of diseases.

*Troparion:*
The chosen among the highest of all the saints, the mother of cities, noetic Zion, the fair church of the firstborn, as it is written, has thee as a martyr who prays for us *(Fol. 97r)*
...the summit of the martyrs, has thee as an intercessor for us.

*Theotokion:*
We all cry aloud unto thee with the voice of the angels, O Virgin, palace and throne of God: Rejoice, through whom we have been vouchsafed the heavenly kingdom, we who of old were cast away through corruption, and foolishly lost our primal beauty.

*Exapostilarion of the Feast, Podoben: "As the disciples were looking on" (Оу҆ченикѡ́мъ зрѧ́щымъ):*
Having passed over from earth unto the heavens, O pure one, forsake not thine inheritance; strengthen the scepters of the kingdom,[^585] subdue barbarian nations, and pour forth peace unto the ends of the world. *Glory... Both now... the same.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "On the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Following the words of divine Gabriel, we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O pure one! For having passed over unto this, O all-holy Mother of the Lord, remember those who hymn thee.

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou, and the ark of Thy holiness.*[^586]

The immeasurable wisdom of God built for Itself a temple beyond understanding from thee through the Holy Spirit, O Mother of God; and now has He translated thee unto the immaterial mansions, O all-hymned one.

*Verse: The Lord has sworn in truth unto David, and He will not turn away from it.*[^587]

As a servant I now draw near unto thee, O Mother of the God of all, praying to be delivered from all distresses. But O Birthgiver of God, reigning with thy Son, preserve the Christian race.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*
Come, all ye ends of the earth, let us praise the venerable translation of the Mother of God; for into the hands of her Son she placed her spotless soul. Therefore through her holy dormition the world was brought to life; in psalms and hymns and spiritual songs, with the bodiless hosts and the apostles it brightly keeps festival.

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 3 of both canons, with the heirmos on 6.*

*In the same month, on the 18th day: The Holy Martyrs Florus and Laurus.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "With what crowns of praise" (Кі́ими похва́льными):*
With what unworthy lips shall we bless the Mother of God? Who is more honorable than creation, and more holy than the cherubim and all the angels; the immovable royal throne; the house wherein the Most High dwelt; the salvation of the world; the sanctification of God; who bestows upon the faithful *(Fol. 97v)*
...in her divine memory richly great mercy.

What fearful hymns were those which they then offered unto thee, O Virgin, who stood round about thy bier: all the apostles of the Word, crying aloud in dread: The royal palace departs; the ark of sanctification is lifted up. Lift up your gates, that the gate of God may enter into joy, unceasingly asking great mercy for the world.

What spiritual hymns shall we now offer unto thee, O all-holy one? For by thine immortal dormition thou hast sanctified the whole world, and hast passed over unto transcendent realms, to understand the beauty of the Almighty, and to rejoice with Him as His Mother; while the angelic ranks and the souls of the righteous escort thee, O pure one. With them ask peace and great mercy for us.

*Other Stichera of the Saints, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
As all-perfect stones cut by martyrdom, and uncut by punishments, O holy ones, ye were built into the church of the Lord, and cast down to the earth the idol temples and altars. Them therefore we call blessed, as dwellers of paradise, as offerings of the heavenly temple.

Draw forth grace and receive illumination, joy and mercy, ye who have touched the relics of the holy martyrs Florus and Laurus: from whom abundantly flows the divine operation of healings. And calling them blessed as servants of the Lord, let us fervently cry aloud: O our God, glory to Thee.

Shut up in a pit and covered beneath the earth by command of the tormentor, by divine victories and the revelation of the Holy Spirit ye were manifested unto us as stars, flashing forth signs and wonders and gifts of healings, O twin-brother passion-bearers, co-dwellers with the angels.

*Glory... Tone 2:*
Holy zealots, twin brothers of Christ, Florus and Laurus, be honored with worthy praises; for having valiantly struggled in the confession toward God, and having patiently suffered, ye obtained crowns of victory. Therefore with the angels on high ye dance in choir, fervently praying the Holy Trinity to bring peace to the world and save our souls.

*Both now... Tone 4:*
Come, ye people, let us sing to the all-holy pure Virgin, from whom ineffably came forth the incarnate Word of the Father, crying and saying: Blessed art thou among women, blessed is the womb that contained Christ! Having commended her soul into His holy hands *(Fol. 98r)*
...pray, O all-pure one, that our souls may be saved.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Sing, all ye earthly choirs: for the Virgin Mother has most gloriously passed over from earth unto the heavenly realms.

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou, and the ark of Thy holiness.*[^588]

The choir of the disciples gathered most gloriously from the ends of the world, O Maiden, to bury thy divine and incorrupt body.

*Verse: The Lord has sworn in truth unto David, and He will not turn away from it.*[^589]

Lift up your gates, ye heavenly gates, beholding the gate of the Most High, the only Mother of God, going unto the highest.

*Glory... Both now... Tone the same:*
Today, ye people, let us sing a virginal hymn unto Christ God: Virgins shall be brought unto the King after her, it says, and her companions shall be brought in gladness and rejoicing.[^590] For she of the seed of David, through whom we were deified, is gloriously and beyond speech translated into the hands of her Son and Master. Hymning her as the Mother of God, we cry aloud and say: Save us who confess thee to be the Mother of God from every distress, and deliver our souls from misfortunes.

*Troparion of the Saints, Tone 4:*
Let us, the faithful, praise worthily the most adorned, divinely-wise, and most radiant pair, all-blessed Florus and all-venerable Laurus, who fervently and clearly preached the uncreated Trinity unto all. Therefore, having suffered unto blood and being crowned with most radiant wreaths, pray Christ God that He may save our souls.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 1:*
In giving birth thou didst preserve thy virginity, in thy dormition thou didst not forsake the world, O Mother of God. Thou hast passed over unto life, being the Mother of Life; and by thy prayers thou deliverest our souls from death.

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
In thy glorious memory, the universe, adorned by the immaterial Spirit, noetically with rejoicing cries unto thee: Rejoice, O Virgin, boast of Christians. *Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 6, Podoben: "The doors of compassion" (Млⷭ҇рдїѧ двє́ри):*
Praising thy translation, O pure one, all we faithful hymn thee; for thou didst pass over from earth, from life unto life, and being in the heavens thou prayest God that the human race may be delivered from misfortunes. *Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Canon of the Feast, with the heirmos on 8.*
*[By] Master Cosmas. Tone 1.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^591]

*(Fol. 98v)*
Adorned with divine glory, thy sacred and glorious memory, O Virgin, has gathered all the faithful unto gladness, while Miriam leads the dance with choirs and timbrels, as they sing unto Thine Only-begotten: For gloriously has He been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Let the immaterial ranks escort thy divine, immaterial body unto Zion; and the multitude of apostles, suddenly gathered together from the ends [of the earth], O Mother of God, straightway stood before thee: with whom, O pure Virgin, we glorify thy venerable memory.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst win the prizes of victory over nature, O pure one, having given birth unto God; yet, resembling thy Creator and Son, beyond nature thou didst submit to the laws of nature. Therefore having died, thou risest with thy Son, reigning eternally.

*Another Canon of the Saints, on 4.*
*Whose acrostic is: I sing the fair pair of martyrs.*[^592]
*A work of Joseph. Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^593]
The wonderworking rod of Moses of old submerged the chariot-driver Pharaoh, striking crosswise and dividing the sea; and it saved the fugitive Israel who went on foot, singing a hymn unto God.

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the radiant flashes of the Spirit, dispel the dark clouds of my soul, that rejoicing I may hymn your light-bearing and venerable memory, O holy ones, most valiant martyrs of Christ.

*Troparion:*
Having built yourselves upon the rock of blameless faith by divine virtues, O blessed ones, ye showed yourselves to be a most excellent and fair temple, wherein the Father, the Son, and the Holy Spirit dwelt.

*Troparion:*
Cutting off the thorns of deceit from the root, O renowned ones, by the sharp word of divine faith they renewed hearts that had become childish through deception, and showed them forth fruitful, blossoming into blameless faith.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst gild the virgins' wombs, of the unwatered root, having become man and suffered the passions, the Planter of all; Whom desiring, the divine martyrs faithfully suffered, O all-pure Virgin, all-hymned one.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^594]
O Christ, the creating and sustaining Wisdom and Power of God of all, establish the Church unyielding and immovable; for Thou alone art holy, Who restest in the saints.

*Troparion:*
Beholding thee a mortal woman, yet beyond nature also the Mother of God, O all-immaculate one, the glorious apostles touched thee with trembling hands as thou didst flash with glory *(Fol. 99r)*
...beholding thee as an acceptable habitation of God.

*Troparion:*
Judgment overtook the insolent hands of the rash one, bringing severing upon them:[^595] God preserving the honor of the ensouled ark by the glory of the Godhead, wherein the Word became flesh.

*Another Canon. Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^596]
O Lord, Maker of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, Thou summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Fulfilling the divine law, O praiseworthy martyrs, ye richly gave bread unto the poor, and guide them unto divine piety, having become mediators of salvation for them.

*Troparion:*
The pair of light-bearing sufferers having shone forth, with rays of wonders drove away the darkness of polytheistic deception; and all that were deceived ye guide unto the radiance of the divine faith.

*Troparion:*
The people that were childish, O pious martyrs, by divine teachings ye guide unto the path of divine understanding; and the demonic shrines, O blessed ones, by prayer ye cast down.

*Theotokion:*
O unwedded Virgin, for whose sake the Word shone forth unto us, Who is understood to be above every beginning: save thy flock, and every city and country, from every distress, O thou who art full of divine grace.

*Kontakion of the Saints, Tone 8, Podoben: "As firstfruits of nature" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ нача́тки):*
As martyrs of piety and divinely wise sufferers of Christ, the inhabited earth most gloriously honors Florus and Laurus today: that we may obtain grace and mercy through their prayers, and be delivered from perils and misfortunes, and from wrath and affliction on the day of judgment.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Ye showed yourselves to be spiritual builders: by faith ye build up the souls of the pious by divine grace upon the rock of faith, and having made yourselves a sacred temple of the honorable Trinity, ye suffered steadfastly. Therefore ye also pour forth healings unto those who have recourse in faith unto you, and drive away passions, O twin-brother passion-bearers. Pray Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who honor your holy memory with love.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast:*
Having conceived God without seed, and having given birth in the flesh without corruption, thou hast been clothed in the new garment of the incorruption of the Spirit: for as the Mother of Life and Queen of all, thou hast passed over, O Virgin, unto immaterial life. Therefore worthily *(Fol. 99v)*
...worthily hast thou truly appeared as a cloud pouring forth the waters of our life, O all-immaculate Mother of God. Pray Thy Son and God to grant remission of sins unto those who celebrate thy holy dormition with love.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^597]
The sayings and riddles of the prophets manifested Thy becoming incarnate from the Virgin, O Christ: the radiance of Thy lightning shall go forth for a light of the nations, and the abyss shall shout aloud unto Thee with gladness: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Behold, ye peoples, and marvel: for the holy mountain, clearly manifested unto God, is taken up higher into the heavenly habitations; the earthly heaven settling into the heavenly and incorruptible dwelling.

*Troparion:*
A passing over unto everlasting and better life was thy death, O pure one: translating thee, O pure one, from temporal life unto life truly divine and unpassing, in gladness to behold thy Son and Lord.

*Troparion:*
The heavenly gates were lifted up, and the angels sang; and Christ received the vessel of His Mother's virginity: the cherubim lifted thee with gladness, and the seraphim glorify thee, rejoicing.

*Another Canon. Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^598]
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art also my power; Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who didst not leave the bosom of the Father, and didst visit our poverty. Therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Grappling youthfully with the enemy, O valiant ones, ye cast him down steadfastly with the weapon of the Cross; and those crushed by sin ye made into living temples of the Lord, who piously sing: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Strengthened by Christ, the stone cut without hands, as it is written, from the mountain, the Virgin Maiden of God, O all-glorious holy ones:[^599] ye wrought the crushing of the idols, and were taken up unto the manifest mountain of martyrdom, and were clearly likened unto the angels.

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing, the venerable pair of martyrs, Florus and Laurus, announced unto the godless the one radiance of the tri-sunned Godhead; and they zealously drank the cup of martyrdom, singing unto Christ: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Submitting unto the law of God, they bowed the lawless to honor Him alone by the opening of the eyes, having illumined their noetic sight, O divine martyrs, two luminaries who have illumined all the ends [of the earth].

*Theotokion:*
As pure among the generations, the Creator of all and God chose thee, O Ever-Virgin Mother of God; and in thy womb *(Fol. 100r)*
...thy womb an undefiled palace the only King of the ages made, and from thee He appeared unto men.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^600]
The divine and ineffable beauty of Thy virtues, O Christ, we shall confess: for from the everlasting glory, having shone forth as the co-everlasting and hypostatic radiance from the virginal womb, becoming incarnate, Thou didst shine forth as the sun upon those who were in darkness and the shadow [of death].

*Troparion:*
As upon a cloud, O Virgin, the apostolic choir borne unto Zion from the ends [of the earth] gathered together to minister unto thee, the light cloud: from whom the Most High God shone forth as the righteous Sun upon those who were in darkness and the shadow [of death].

*Troparion:*
The God-receiving tongues of the theological men, like trumpets, most melodiously cried aloud unto the Mother of God, sounding forth a departing hymn in the Spirit: Rejoice, O incorruptible fountain of God's life-originating and saving incarnation for all.

*Another Canon. Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^601]
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy countenance, O unwaning Light, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my paths, I pray.

*Troparion:*
The divine pair of martyrs, twin brothers, gave no division: for believing with one hope in the undivided Holy Trinity, those who were held by the improper division of deception they united by grace.

*Troparion:*
By the faith of the truth, and by the chisel of divine understanding, piously hewing out petrified thoughts, O sufferers, ye made most glorious temples unto the Trinity by divine faith, through the power of the Spirit.

*Troparion:*
Ye appeared as root-cutters of all malice, O glorious martyrs, and planters of all piety, both by words and deeds: having caused to sprout the blossom of Jesus from the divine root, ye planted it in impenetrable hearts.

*Theotokion:*
Before all ages, O Virgin, the hidden great mystery, which was not made known unto previous generations, O all-immaculate one, through thee was revealed: the Father's eternal Word, becoming incarnate and becoming man.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^602]
The sea-born inner fire of the whale in the deep was a prefiguration of Thy three-day burial, whereof the Prophet Jonah was shown forth; for saved as he was sent forth, unharmed he cried aloud: I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
The King of all and God gives unto thee the things that are beyond nature: for even as He preserved thee a Virgin in childbearing, so in the tomb He kept thy body *(Fol. 100v)*
...kept it incorrupt, and glorified thee with a divine translation, bestowing honor upon thee as a Son unto His Mother.

*Troparion:*
Truly as a radiant lampstand of the immaterial Light, a golden censer of the divine coal, into the Holy of Holies He settled thee: the jar and the rod, the God-inscribed tablet, the holy ark, the table of the Word of Life, O Virgin, even thy childbearing.

*Another Canon. Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^603]
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray; for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation.

*Troparion:*
Conquering the lawless by unyielding endurance, lawfully, O sufferers, ye brought a praiseworthy end unto Christ, having not been covered by the glamour of impiety.

*Troparion:*
Manifested as weapons of the mighty Spirit, O blessed ones, ye conquered the bodiless foes, and piously depicted the blessed passion of Him Who was pierced in the side.

*Troparion:*
As two most excellent breasts, O glorious ones, ye appeared unto the Church of Christ, not pouring forth milk, but pouring forth an unstinting and saving gift of healings.

*Theotokion:*
Heal my soul, which is sickened with sin, O all-immaculate one who didst give birth unto the Physician of souls and bodies; Whom confessing, the divine martyrs, brothers and passion-bearers, were shown forth.

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 2:*[^604]
The Mother of God, unslumbering in intercessions and an immovable hope in mediation, the tomb and mortification did not hold: for as the Mother of Life, He Who dwelt in her ever-virginal womb translated her unto life.

*Ikos:*
Enclose my thoughts, O my Christ, for I dare to hymn the rampart of the world, Thy pure Mother: confirm me upon the pillar of words, and protect me in heavy thoughts, for Thou fulfillest the supplications of those who cry aloud and ask in faith. Do Thou therefore grant me an acceptable tongue and a thought unashamed: for every giving of radiance is sent down from Thee, O Light-Giver, Who didst make Thine abode in the ever-virginal womb.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^605]
Opposing itself unto shameless fury and fire, divine longing bedewed the fire, and laughed at the fury, with the divinely inspired rational three-stringed lute of the venerable ones, sounding against musical instruments in the midst of the flame: O supremely praised God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou.

*(Fol. 101r)*
...and our God, blessed art Thou.

*Troparion:*
Moses in anger broke the God-fashioned tablets written by the divine Spirit:[^606] but his Master, having preserved unharmed her who bore Him in the heavenly mansions, has now settled her within. Leaping for joy with her, we cry aloud unto Christ: O supremely praised God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou.

*Troparion:*
With cymbals of pure lips, musically with a radiant heart, with a melodious trumpet, with high thought, on the eminent, chosen day of the Virgin and pure one's translation, clapping active hands, we cry aloud: O supremely praised God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou.

*Troparion:*
Gather together, ye divinely wise people: for the dwelling-place of God's glory is translated from Zion unto the heavenly habitation, where there is the pure voice of those who feast, the voice of ineffable rejoicing, and of those who in gladness cry aloud unto Christ: O supremely praised God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou.

*Another Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^607]
At God's condescension the fire was abashed in Babylon of old; therefore the youths in the furnace, dancing with a joyful foot as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Having destroyed the wooden and stone gods of the godless, the renowned Florus and Laurus raised up for themselves singing pillars of divine knowledge: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

*Troparion:*
Being filled with the fire of the divine Spirit, O martyr sufferers, when they unjustly wished to cast the venerable men into the furnace, by the showers of your divine prayers, O glorious ones, ye preserved them unharmed.

*Troparion:*
Healing unto the sick, deliverance unto the wronged, salvation unto all in perils Florus and Laurus ever bestow, as martyrs crying aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers.

*Theotokion:*
As a rose of the valley in life did Christ choose thee, O Maiden; in thee He dwelt and passed through in the flesh, making us fragrant with the breaths of divine knowledge, O all-immaculate one, who ever confess thee to be the Mother of God.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^608]
The all-powerful angel of God showed forth unto the youths a flame bedewing the venerable, but burning up the ungodly; and He made the Mother of God a life-originating fountain, pouring out corruption upon death, and life unto those who sing: We the redeemed sing unto the only Creator, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
The whole multitude of theologians followed the divine words, unto the sacred ark in Zion *(Fol. 101v)*
...in Zion: Whither art thou departing now, O tabernacle of the living God? crying aloud: Cease not to look upon those who sing in faith: We the redeemed sing unto the only Creator, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Lifting up departing hands, O all-immaculate one, hands wherewith thou didst bear God in the flesh, with boldness as a Mother thou didst say unto Him Who was born of thee: Those whom Thou gavest unto me, preserve unto the ages, as they cry aloud unto Thee: We the redeemed sing unto the only Creator, and exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Another Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^609]
Sevenfold the furnace the Chaldean tormentor furiously kindled for the godly; but beholding them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Nourishing souls with the living word, O ever-memorable ones, ye appeared as saving food unto those wasting with hunger, as provision for the poor, and for orphans, providing sufficiently for all, and singing unto Christ: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Having had a sanctified life and end, hidden for a long time, O wise ones, ye were brought forth from the earth, flashing rays of most glorious wonders more than the sunshine upon us who sing in faith: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Two luminaries ye appeared upon the noetic firmament, sacredly adorning the Church, and brightly illumining all creation ever with wonderworkings, O passion-bearers, crying aloud: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
Holier art thou than the angels, O most holy Virgin, having given birth unto the Holy of Holies, Who for compassion's sake unchangeably became man even as we are, to save those who unceasingly cry aloud unto Him: Ye youths, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, exalt Him above all unto all ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^610]
The laws of nature are overcome in thee, O pure Virgin: for childbearing remains virginal, and death betroths life; after childbearing a virgin, and after death alive, thou savest thine inheritance ever, O Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
The angelic hosts marveled in Zion, beholding their Master carrying a woman's soul in His hands; for unto her that bore Him all-immaculately, in a manner befitting a Son He proclaimed: Come, O pure one, be glorified with thy Son and God.

*(Fol. 102r)*
...the apostolic choir escorted thy God-receiving body, beholding with fear, and with a great voice proclaiming: As thou ascendest unto the heavenly habitations unto thy Son, thou savest thine inheritance ever, O Mother of God.

*Another Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^611]
Heaven was amazed at this, and the ends of the earth were astonished, that God appeared in the flesh unto men, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens. Therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee as the Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
Behold, David of old cried aloud: What is so good and fair and saving, but that divine brethren should clearly dwell together in the Spirit,[^612] who struggled clearly and obtained victory over the devil: whom worthily we call blessed.

*Troparion:*
Fair eyes of the honorable Church ye appeared by grace; and eyes of old blinded by grievous darkness, both before your death, O holy ones, and after your sacred end, ye opened unto the glory of the Benefactor of all, O Florus and Laurus right worthy of praise.

*Troparion:*
By manifest and fearful signs, O holy ones, Christ reveals your tomb and shrine, as He foretold of old; where your patient bodies lay, sacredly sending forth rays of healings richly, and praiseworthy sweet fragrance.

*Troparion:*
Divine and truly light-bearing pair, Laurus and Florus, gloriously triumphant martyrs: standing ever in the heavens before the all-creating Trinity, ask deliverance from grievous sins for those who celebrate your divine memory upon earth in faith.

*Theotokion:*
Fearful is thy childbearing, O pure Mother: for Him at Whom the heavenly ranks tremble, the Word of God Who became man upon earth, beyond nature thou didst bear, remaining a Virgin as before. Therefore confessing thee to be the Mother of God, both with tongue and heart, we magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion of the Feast, Podoben: "Having adorned the heavens with stars" (Не́бо звѣзда́ми):*
Ye apostles, who have gathered here from the ends [of the earth] in the village of Gethsemane, bury my body; and Thou, my Son and God, receive my spirit. *Glory... Both now... the same.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
The choir of disciples gathers to bury the Mother of God, having come from the ends [of the earth] by an all-powerful nod.

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou, and the ark of Thy holiness.*[^613]

The Bride of God, the Virgin Queen, the glory of the elect, the praise of virgins, is translated unto her Son.

*Verse: The Lord has sworn in truth unto David, and He will not turn away from it.*

*(Fol. 102v)*
...Thy body was not a partaker of corruption, and was given over to burial by the law of nature, yet remains incorrupt.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 2:*
Thy most venerable dormition, O most holy pure Virgin, the multitude of angels in heaven and the human race upon earth call blessed, for thou wast the Mother of Christ God, the Creator of all: cease not to pray Him for us, we pray thee, who after God have placed our hope in thee, O all-hymned and unwedded Mother of God.

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 4 of both canons, with the heirmos on 6. Prokeimenon of the Feast, and of the Saints: Unto the saints that are in His earth, the Lord has made marvelous all His desires in them.*[^614] *Verse: I beheld the Lord ever before me.*[^615] *The Epistle to the Ephesians, pericope 233.*[^616] *The Gospel of Luke, pericope 106.*[^617] *Communion Hymn: Rejoice.*

*In the same month, on the 19th day: The Holy Martyr Andrew the General, and with him two thousand five hundred and ninety-three.*[^618]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, Tone 4, Podoben: "As noble among the martyrs" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Thy holy, all-immaculate, and honorable translation was not unto separation for those who love thee, O Virgin, but an inseparable union: for noetically with hymns unto those who truly glorify thee as the Mother of God, thou art manifested, granting thy grace, and showing that thou hast been given as an intercessor unto all men.

Having become the ark of sanctification of Him Who made His abode in thee, O pure Mother of God, thou art translated from earth unto unfading rest: therefore also illumined with His brightness. Therefore from on high thou lookest upon those who sing unto thee with love, and who preach the divine majesty of thy wonders.

As a most radiant bridal chamber, thy Son and Lord, having received thee into His habitations, O all-immaculate one, as an ark of sanctification showed thee forth unto the bodiless hosts and the dwellings of the saints with ineffable glory: delivering from corruption and perils those who sing thy majesty with love, O pure one.

*(Fol. 103r)*
*Other Stichera of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "O all-praised martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи мч҃ницы):*
Showing forth an image of manly courage, in the assembly thou didst dare to surrender thyself unto the tribunal of suffering, even as grace went before this, O most rich passion-bearer Andrew; and ever unto the faithful thou pourest forth waters of healings, having received grace from heaven.

Showing thyself true to the name of courage,[^619] acting valiantly like a man, grappling with the enemy, thou didst destroy him like another Pharaoh: with streams of thy blood having submerged all his army, O right marvelous one. Therefore pray that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls.

A numerous army thou didst lead unto God the Giver of good things, O glorious one, which struggled and by death found immortal glory with thee, O most glorious one. With them pray that peace and great mercy may be granted unto our souls.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 4:*
When thou didst depart, O Virgin Mother of God, unto Him Who was ineffably born of thee, James the Brother of the Lord and first Hierarch was present, and Peter the most honorable leader, chief of theologians, and the whole divine apostolic choir, chanting hymns with clear theology concerning the divine and fearful mystery of Christ God's dispensation; and having buried thy life-originating and God-receiving body, they rejoiced, O all-hymned one. And higher, the most holy and elder angelic hosts, marveling at the wonder, stooping down said to one another: Lift up your gates, and receive her who bore the Maker of heaven and earth! And with doxology let us praise the honorable and holy body that contained the Lord Who is invisible unto us. Therefore we also, celebrating thy memory, cry aloud unto thee, O all-hymned one: Exalt the horn of Christians, and save our souls.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 4, Podoben: "As of the celestial ranks" (Небє́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
At the immortal dormition of the Mother of God, the angelic assembly rejoices; and as she departs, she settles into eternal mansions, and passes unto heavenly gladness, unto divine joy and everlasting delight.

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou, and the ark of Thy holiness.*[^620]

Desiring the life on high, thou didst leave this [present life], O Mother of God, Maiden, blossom of virginity, who didst bear Christ the Life of all. Reverently the apostolic assembly stood before thine honorable burial, O Bride of God.

*Verse: The Lord has sworn in truth unto David, and He will not turn away from it.*

*(Fol. 103v)*
...By thy departing and funeral hymns the air was sanctified, by thine ascent, O Mother of God the Lover of mankind: a fearful wonder, O unwedded Mother of God. Therefore in faith we, the faithful, worship thee, O unwedded Mother of God.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 2:*
Come, ye assembly of feast-lovers, come and let us form a choir; come, let us crown the Church with hymns by the resting of the ark of God: for today heaven spreads out its bosom, receiving her who bore Him Whom none can contain; and the earth, giving back the fountain of life, is adorned with majesty. The angels form a choir with the apostles, looking with dread upon her who is translated from life unto life, who bore the Prince of Life. Let us all worship her, praying: Forget not thy kinship and connection, O Lady, of those who celebrate thy most holy dormition in faith.

*Troparion of the Saint, Tone 5:*
Having left the glory of earthly rank, thou didst inherit the heavenly kingdom; with drops of blood as with most marvelous stones thou didst adorn incorruptible crowns, and didst lead an assembly of passion-bearers unto Christ. With the angelic choirs in the unwaning light, thou hast found Christ, the unsetting Sun, O holy Andrew the General. Whom pray, together with those who suffered with thee ever, that He may save our souls.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 1:*
In giving birth thou didst preserve thy virginity, in thy dormition thou didst not forsake the world, O Mother of God. Thou hast passed over unto life, being the Mother of Life; and by thy prayers thou deliverest our souls from death.

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "At the beauty of thy virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дв҃ства):*
Unto the heavens thy noetic soul, and into paradise thy pure body was translated, from corruption into a radiant place, O all-immaculate one; therefore the Lord repaid the lawless, for they wrought deceit against thine honorable body. Therefore having come together, we honor thy translation, O Virgin. *Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
The earth indeed was blessed by thy burial, and the air was sanctified by thine ascent, O all-hymned Mary. And the angels opened the heavenly gates unto thee, where now standing with thy Son, thou prayest for the world, O unwedded Mother. *Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Canon of the Feast, with the heirmos on 8. The work of John of Damascus. Tone 4.*

*(Fol. 104r)*
*Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^621]
I will open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I will utter a word unto the Queen Mother; and I will be seen celebrating radiantly, and rejoicing I will sing her dormition.

*Troparion:*
Ye virgin maidens, with Miriam the prophetess,[^622] cry aloud now an exodus-song:[^623] for the Virgin and only Mother of God is translated unto the heavenly reception.

*Troparion:*
Worthily, as an ensouled heaven, did the celestial, most pure and divine tabernacles receive thee; and thou didst stand radiantly adorned, as an all-immaculate bride, before the King and God.

*And of the Saint, on 4. Whose acrostic is: With songs I praise thee, Andrew the General. The work of Joseph. The same Tone:*[^624]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^625]
Having traversed on foot with dryshod steps the deep of the Red Sea, ancient Israel conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness by the cross-shaped hands of Moses.

*Troparion:*
As I eagerly desire to sing thy sacred festival, enlighten my soul with the radiant rays of the Spirit, and drive away the mist of ignorance, O glorious martyr.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast richly enriched with the splendors of tortures, O blessed one; with manly wisdom and divine power thou didst trample the deceiver, and didst become a victor, O martyr Andrew of great renown.[^626]

*Troparion:*
Having put to death earthly thoughts, O much-suffering martyr, thou didst manfully follow the Word Who put Himself to death for thy sake; and by death thou didst pass over unto life that perishes not.

*Theotokion:*
He Who for His exceedingly great goodness was truly clothed in flesh from thee, O all-immaculate one, for the salvation of all men, God together and man, is known twofold in nature.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^627]
O Mother of God, living and bountiful fountain: establish those who form a spiritual choir unto thee, who sing thy praise; in thy divine memory vouchsafe them crowns of glory.

*Troparion:*
Having been produced from mortal loins, conforming unto nature, O pure one, she accomplished her departure; yet having given birth unto the true Life, she was translated unto the divine and hypostatic Life.

*Troparion:*
The choir of theologians from the ends of the earth, and from on high the multitudes of angels, came unto Zion by the almighty nod of God, worthily ministering unto thy burial, O Lady.

*Another Canon. Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^628]
Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and confirmation.

*Troparion:*
By the light of the Cross navigating the deep of temptations, the enemies *(Fol. 104v)*
...in the streams of thy blood thou didst submerge.

*Troparion:*
Higher than the stumbling-blocks of the evil one and his craftiness, having become an excellent victor, O martyr, thou hast been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Adorned with a crown of majesty, O martyr of Christ, ever with the chosen martyrs thou standest in joy before Him.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth unto the Lord of all, Who delivers men from the tyranny of the enemy, O all-immaculate Virgin Lady.

*Kontakion of the Saint, Tone 2:*
Standing before the Lord in prayers, as a star running before the sun, thou didst behold the desired treasure of the kingdom, being filled with ineffable joy; and unto the immortal King throughout endless ages, Who is praised unceasingly by angels, thou singest, O Andrew the General: with them pray unceasingly for us all.

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thy tomb" (Гро́бъ тво́й):*
Strengthening thy soul with courage, O martyr, thou didst destroy the strong cruelty of the enemy, and rejoicing thou didst suffer, and didst please God. Therefore we celebrate all thy most holy memory with gladness of heart, O Andrew most rich.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 4 [Tone 1], Podoben: "While the stone was sealed" (Ка́мени запеча́танꙋ):*[^629]
The choir of divine apostles was gathered together, to bury thee reverently, O all-immaculate Mother of God; and with them the angelic ranks sang thy translation, crying aloud with highest praise. With them we also, leaping with joy in love, O pure one, bring praise unto thee, the faithful with voices of song, and we cry aloud: Rejoice, O protectress of those who ever honor thee.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^630]
Perceiving the unsearchable divine counsel of Thine incarnation from the Virgin, O Thou Most High, the prophet Habakkuk cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
It was a wonder to behold the ensouled heaven of the King of all passing through the depths below the earth; how marvelous are Thy works: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
If her incomprehensible Fruit, by Whom the heavens were made, willingly accepted burial as mortal: how shall she who gave birth unwedded refuse burial?

*Troparion:*
At thy translation, O Mother of God, the angelic hosts with fear and joy covered thy most spacious and God-receiving body with sacred wings.

*Another Canon. Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^631]
Beholding Thee, the Sun of righteousness, lifted up upon the Cross, the Church stood in her order, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power *(Fol. 105r)*
...O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having acquired thee as a strong wall, O all-wise one, the Church shows herself unshaken by the adversary assaults of the enemy, crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Having strengthened the regiment of martyrs with divine teachings, as a general, when the body was about to undergo corruption, O glorious one, thou didst by no means submit unto sparing it.

*Troparion:*
Armed with courage as with a weapon, and as with a two-edged sword with the honorable Cross, O all-blessed Andrew, thou didst destroy the enemies as a most valiant general.

*Troparion:*
We praise thee, the champion of the faith, as an unconquered sufferer and partaker of celestial splendor, and a fountain pouring forth waters of miracles unto those in need.

*Theotokion:*
We praise thee, the boast of martyrs and salvation of the faithful, all the generations of mankind, O all-immaculate one: for thou didst bear God, remaining an incorrupt Virgin.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^632]
All creation was amazed at thine honorable dormition: for thou, O Virgin who hast not known wedlock, hast been translated from earth unto the eternal mansions and unto endless life, granting salvation unto all who sing thy praise.

*Troparion:*
Let the trumpets of the theologians sound today; and let the eloquent tongue of men now give praise; let the air resound, shining with immeasurable light; and let the angels sing the dormition of the most pure Virgin!

*Troparion:*
It was fitting that the chosen vessel,[^633] wholly astounded by the departure-song unto thee, O Virgin, wholly consecrated unto God, should be acceptable unto all as God-pleasing, being truly shown forth as such, O Mother of God, all-hymned one.

*Another Canon. Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^634]
Thou, my Lord, hast come into the world as a light, a holy Light turning from the dark gloom of ignorance those who sing Thy praise in faith.

*Troparion:*
Having been named for true courage,[^635] thou didst drive away thine enemies, and utterly destroy them, O all-blessed Andrew.

*Troparion:*
Thou ever pourest forth waters of healings unto the faithful, O most praised one, having enriched them with saving spiritual grace.

*Troparion:*
For the praise of thy miracles, and the radiance of the divine grace of thine honorable martyrdom, has truly gone forth through all the earth, O divinely inspired one.

*Theotokion:*
The weapons of the enemy have now utterly failed,[^636] since thou, O Mother of God, didst give birth unto Him Who was pierced with a spear and renewed the world.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^637]
Divine *(Fol. 105v)*
...this divine and all-honorable festival of the Mother of God, come, ye divinely wise, let us clap our hands and glorify God Who was born of her.

*Troparion:*
Life dawned forth from thee, without destroying the keys of virginity; how then did thy most pure and life-originating body become a partaker of the trial of death?

*Troparion:*
Having been the temple of Life, thou didst obtain everlasting life: for by death thou hast passed over unto life, having given birth unto the hypostatic Life.

*Another Canon. Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^638]
I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that for mercy's sake flowed from Thy ribs.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst blunt the stings of the evil one by thy sufferings, and didst depart rejoicing unto painless glory, O martyr Andrew, general of the honorable regiments of martyrs.

*Troparion:*
By a little blood thou didst exchange for the brightness of eternal glory, and joy that never fades away, and heavenly crowns, and unwaning radiance.

*Troparion:*
Having gathered a numerous army, O martyr, thou didst number it among the angelic ranks, O blessed one, having suffered with thee; joyfully ye stand before the Master of all.

*Theotokion:*
Ineffably thou didst bear the One co-unoriginate with the Father, Who was made like unto men beyond mind and word, O Parent of God: Whom pray, that thy servants may be delivered from tribulations.

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 2:*[^639]
The tomb and mortality could not hold the Mother of God, unslumbering in intercessions and an unfailing hope in mediations: for as the Mother of Life, He Who dwelt in her ever-virginal womb translated her unto life.

*Ikos:*
Surround my thoughts with a rampart, O my Christ, for I dare to sing the wall of the world, Thy pure Mother; strengthen me upon the pillar of words, and defend me in difficult thoughts: for Thou fulfillest the supplications of those who cry aloud and ask in faith. Grant me therefore a profitable tongue and an unashamed mind: for every gift of radiance is sent down from Thee, O Giver of light, Who didst dwell in the virginal womb.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^640]
The divinely wise served not creation more than the Creator, but manfully trampling upon the threatening fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Ye youths and virgins, and honoring the memory of the Virgin and Mother of God *(Fol. 106r)*
...ye elders and princes, kings and judges, sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord and God of our fathers![^641]

*Troparion:*
Let the heavenly mountains blow the spiritual trumpet; let the hills rejoice, and let the divine apostles leap for joy: the Queen is translated unto the Son, reigning together with Him.[^642]

*Troparion:*
The all-sacred translation of Thy divine and incorrupt Mother gathered together the ranks of the transcendent celestial hosts on high, to make merry with those on earth singing unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Another Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^643]
In the Persian furnace the Abrahamite youths, burned more with love of piety than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
By streams of blood thou didst cause a spring to gush forth, which truly heals the pain of all kinds of diseases for those who draw near unto thee with unhesitating faith, O divinely inspired martyr.[^644]

*Troparion:*
Thou didst bring an assembly of saints unto the all-holy Word by blood, O wise martyr, and didst inherit life that grows not old; with them pray Christ the Lover of mankind for us.

*Troparion:*
With divine zeal having dyed for thyself a purple robe with thy blood, O martyr, and clothed in it, thou now reignest with Him Who reigns over all, adorned with a victor's crown.

*Theotokion:*
Having manifestly deified men beyond nature by thy divine giving of birth, O Virgin, in that thou didst ineffably give flesh unto God: blessed art thou among women, O all-immaculate Lady.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^645]
The Offspring of the Mother of God saved the pious youths in the furnace: then prefigured, but now in reality, It stirs up the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Praise the Lord, ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thy memory, O pure Virgin, the principalities and authorities and powers, the angels, archangels, thrones, dominions, cherubim, and the fearful seraphim glorify; and we, the race of men, sing and supremely exalt it unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
He Who wondrously dwelt in thy pure womb, becoming incarnate, O Mother of God, having received this thy most sacred spirit, gave it rest with Himself as a Son Who was a debtor; therefore we sing unto thee, O Virgin, and supremely exalt thee unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
O wonders beyond understanding of the Ever-Virgin and Mother of God! For settling into the tomb, she showed forth paradise; standing before which today, rejoicing we sing: Praise the Lord, ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*(Fol. 106v)*
*Another Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^646]
Stretching out his hands, Daniel stopped the gaping mouths of lions in the pit; and girded with virtue, the youths, lovers of piety, quenched the power of fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst restrain the onsets of wild lions, O glorious one, by thy steadfast struggles; by divine power thou didst bring low the insolence of godless tormentors, and crying aloud as a victor, thou didst ascend unto heaven: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast vouchsafed to behold the splendor of the saints, and to live in joy, O martyr, having contended valiantly, and having finished thy contest by the severing of the sword; and now thou criest aloud with gladness: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Fleeing unto the divine temple, let us receive the light of the divine martyr; and ever touching the reliquary of his relics with rejoicing, having received sanctification that imparts grace, let us cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
O holy Virgin, who holily didst bear the most holy God, Whom all the holy martyrs confessed at the judgment-seat: send down sanctification and enlightenment unto us who cry aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^647]
Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, enlightened by the Spirit; and let the nature of immaterial minds celebrate, honoring the sacred translation of the Mother of God, and let them cry aloud: Rejoice, O all-blessed Mother of God, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Troparion:*
Come into Zion, unto the divine and fertile mountain of the living God, let us rejoice beholding the Mother of God; for Christ has translated her into the Holy of Holies as His Mother, unto an exceedingly better and divine tabernacle.[^648]

*Troparion:*
Come, ye faithful, let us draw near unto the tomb of God's Mother, and let us embrace it with hearts and lips, purely touching it with eyes and forehead, drawing bountiful gifts of healings from the ever-flowing fountain.

*Troparion:*
Receive from us an exodus-song, O Mother of the living God, and overshadow us with thy light-bearing and divine grace: granting victories unto the king,[^649] peace unto the Christ-loving people, remission unto those who sing, and salvation unto our souls.

*Another Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^650]
A cornerstone was cut out without hands from thee, the unhewn mountain, O Virgin, even Christ, Who joined together the sundered natures; therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
The light-bearing memory of the passion-bearer has shone forth *(Fol. 107r)*
...light-bearingly, driving away demonic darkness and enlightening all the faithful. We celebrate this radiantly, worthily magnifying him.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear as a great sun in the midst of those who suffered with thee, O Andrew, guiding them by thy words unto the unwaning radiance of heavenly splendor; with them in faith we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Thee, who by the splendor of miracles dost enlighten all creation; thee, who by unshaken martyrdom didst become a pillar of the Church and confirmation of the faithful, O martyr Andrew, we magnify.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst blossom in the midst of the paradise of sufferers as a rose, mystically bearing fragrance; and thou didst make glad the fullness of the faithful, and didst drive away the stench of deception, O all-wise one, by divine grace.

*Theotokion:*
Holding Christ upon thine arms, Who by His nod upholds all: pray Him, O Lady, to deliver me from the hands of the stranger, as with Orthodox faith I unceasingly magnify thee.

*Exapostilarion of the Feast, Tone 3, Podoben: "O Heaven with stars" (Не́бо звѣзда́ми):*[^651]
Ye apostles, having gathered together here from the ends [of the earth] in the village of Gethsemane, bury my body; and Thou, my Son and God, receive my spirit. *Glory... Both now... the same.*

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^652]
The assembly of disciples and divine apostles was gathered together to bury the God-receiving body of the only Mother of God.

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou, and the ark of Thy holiness.*[^653]

Thou Who didst ineffably shine forth from her, as befits a Son, receive the all-holy spirit of the Mother of God into Thy hands, O Master.

*Verse: The Lord has sworn in truth unto David, and He will not turn away from it.*[^654]

When thou didst pass over unto Him Who was born of thee, O Virgin, the apostles came together upon the clouds to bury thy body.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 2:*
Sing, ye peoples, sing unto the Mother of our God: for today she commits her all-radiant soul into the most pure hands of Him Who became incarnate of her without seed; Whom she also prays unceasingly to grant peace unto the universe and great mercy.

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 5 of both Canons, with the heirmos on 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Unto the saints that are in His earth, the Lord has been marvelous in all His will in them.*[^655]
*Verse: I foresaw the Lord always before me, for He is at my right hand.*[^656]
*Epistle to the Ephesians, pericope 233 [Eph. 6:10–17].*[^657]
*Alleluia, Tone 4: The righteous cried aloud, and the Lord heard them.*[^658]
*Verse: Many are the tribulations of the righteous.*[^659]
*Gospel according to Luke, pericope 106 [Luke 21:12–19].*[^660]
*Communion Hymn: Rejoice in the Lord, O ye righteous; praise is becoming unto the upright.*[^661]

*(Fol. 107v)*
*The same month, on the 20th day. The Holy Prophet Samuel:*[^662]
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, Tone 6, Podoben: "Having placed all" (Всю̀ ѿло́жше):*[^663]
Unto everlasting life she who bore Life has passed over, granting mercy unto the faithful, the all-immaculate Mother of God today; and all creation now leaps and rejoices; the angelic gatherings now accompany her as Queen, rejoicing with her, performing festal chanting in hymns; and her only Son and King of all has settled her with Himself, as ruling over all.

Borne upon clouds, the divinely eloquent apostles from the whole earth arrived in Zion, taken up to behold the glorious and truly holy dormition of the Mother of the Lord; and to offer light-bearing words, and to perform the marvelous burial of her who bore the Lord; who has truly become a refuge and mighty protection unto all the faithful on earth, divine and invincible.

Radiantly we celebrate in the divine dormition of her who is higher than all creation, offering all these glorious and radiant, truly divine gifts, which are becoming: faith, and hope, and unmeasured love, purity, sanctification, and righteousness, wherein she delights: that she may also vouchsafe us to be partakers of them, and make us worthy of the kingdom on high.

*Other Stichera of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^664]
An acceptable gift from maternal hands, she who bore thee, O God-bearer, having inherited thee as the fruit worthy of prayer, O Samuel, gave thee back unto God the Benefactor as she had promised; therefore the grace of the Spirit rested in thee, O glorious one, having reared thee in guilelessness and adorned thee with simplicity.[^665]

Invested with sacred anointing as a priest, shown forth as a prophet, thou lookest upon things to come; and by divine command having anointed kings, thou declarest future events, judging righteously the people of Israel who continually sinned and departed from God, O right marvelous Samuel, God-bearer most rich.[^666]

Now not in riddles, nor in shadows as before, but face to face thou beholdest Him Whom *(Fol. 108r)*
...thou didst desire. Having put off earthly gloom and heaviness, and traversing the heavens rejoicing, O all-honorable prophet, peer of prophets, companion of the righteous, and partaker with angels!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*
At thine immortal dormition, O Mother of God, Mother of Life, clouds caught up the apostles through the air, and dispersed throughout the world, they stood before thy most holy body in a single choir; and having buried it reverently, singing they cried aloud unto thee Gabriel's voice: Rejoice, thou who art full of grace, Virgin unwedded Mother, the Lord is with thee! With them pray Him as thy Son and our God, that our souls may be saved.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 6, Podoben: "On the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*[^667]
Following the words of the divine Gabriel, we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O pure one! As thou hast departed, being the Mother of the Lord, having passed over unto Him, remember those who sing thy praise.

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou, and the ark of Thy holiness.*[^668]

The unmeasured Wisdom of God built Himself a temple beyond understanding from thee by the Holy Spirit, O Mother of God; and now He has translated thee unto immaterial mansions, O all-hymned one.

*Verse: The Lord has sworn in truth unto David, and He will not turn away from it.*[^669]

As a servant I now come unto thee, the Mother of the God of all, praying to be delivered from all perils. But O Parent of God, reigning with thy Son, preserve the Christian race!

*Glory... Both now... the same Tone:*
*Idiomelon:*
When the translation of thy most pure body was being prepared, then the apostles standing around the bier looked upon thee with trembling; and some indeed beholding the body were seized with amazement. But Peter cried aloud unto thee with tears: O Virgin, I clearly see thee stretched out, the simple one, the Life of all, and I marvel: in whom the delight of the life to come dwelt! But O most pure one, pray fervently thy Son and God, that thy flock may be preserved unharmed.

*Troparion of the Saint, Tone 2:*
Celebrating the memory of Thy prophet Samuel, O Lord, therefore we pray Thee: save our souls.

*Glory... Both now... Troparion of the Feast, Tone 1:*
In giving birth thou didst preserve thy virginity, in thy dormition thou didst not forsake the world, O Mother of God. Thou hast passed over unto life, being the Mother of Life; and by thy prayers thou deliverest our souls from death.

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn (Fol. 108v)*
...Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):[^670]
Shown forth upon earth as more spacious than the heavens, having given birth in the flesh unto the Creator of all, now thou hast been translated from earth into the highest heavens. The souls of the righteous beholding thee, and the angelic ranks looking upon thee, ever worthily offer praise unto thee as Queen. Therefore cease not to pray for those who sing thy praise. *Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 6, Podoben: "Co-unoriginate Word" (Собезнача́льное Сло́во):*[^671]
The multitudes of angels glorify thee, and the race of men faithfully sings thy praise, in that thou hast been translated from earth unto Him Who was born of thee, O pure Mother of God. And now we fervently pray thee, that those who celebrate thy translation in faith, O Virgin, may be delivered from tribulations. *Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Canon of the Feast, with the heirmos on 8, Tone 1. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^672]
Adorned with divine glory, thy sacred and glorious memory, O Virgin, has gathered all the faithful unto gladness, Miriam leading the way with dances and timbrels, singing unto Thine Only-begotten: For gloriously has He been glorified!

*Troparion:*
Let the immaterial ranks escort thy divine body, made immaterial unto Zion; and the multitude of the apostles, having suddenly assembled from the ends of the earth, O Mother of God, straightway stood before thee; with them, O pure Virgin, we glorify thine honorable memory.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst bear away the prizes of victory over nature, O pure one, having given birth unto God; yet imitating thy Creator and Son, beyond nature thou submittest unto natural laws; therefore having died, thou dost rise again with thy Son, living eternally.

*Another Canon of the Saint, on 4. Whose acrostic is: I sing the glory of the seer Samuel. The work of Joseph. Tone 4:*[^673]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^674]
O Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, drown the tripartite soul in the depth of dispassion, I pray Thee, [as Thou didst drown] the mighty captains,[^675] that unto Thee as upon a timbrel, in the mortification of the body, I may sing a song of victory.

*Troparion:*
Vouchsafe my barren soul to blossom with the good fruit of virtues through thy prayers, O wise one, offspring of a barren mother, that with sacred songs I may praise thy light-bearing festival, O Samuel.

*Troparion:*
He Who of old opened the womb of Sarah manifestly fulfills the prayer of Hannah, and now *(Fol. 109r)*
...and now hath made the barren woman fruitful by grace, being well pleased that thou, O Samuel, shouldest shine forth from her like a star.

*Troparion:*
Having holily dyed thy sacred vestment, O most blessed one, thou didst enlighten thy soul with the mystical rays of the Spirit, being taught every mystery, O divinely wise one, having served God the Almighty as an angel.

*Theotokion:*
He Who is with the Father as the eternal Son, having become incarnate in the latter times, truly became thy Son, O pure one; and through faith He hath adopted unto God all who were enslaved to the cruel adversary.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^676]
O Wisdom and Power of God, Creator and Preserver of all, establish the Church unyielding and immovable, O Christ; for Thou alone art holy, Who restest in the saints.

*Troparion:*
Seeing thee as a mortal woman, yet beyond nature the Mother of God, O all-immaculate one, the glorious Apostles touched thee with trembling hands as thou didst shine forth with glory, beholding thee as a tabernacle accepted of God.

*Troparion:*
Judgment overtook by severing the insolent hands of the audacious man,[^677] God having preserved honor for the living Ark by the glory of the Godhead, in whom the Word was made flesh.

*Another Canon. Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^678]
We boast not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in riches, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Cleaving unto God the Most High, O glorious one, Thou wast beloved of Him, being adorned with divine wisdom and beautified with chastity.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst show forth an angelic life on earth, O Samuel; for thou manifestly hadst the angels as companions, who taught thee things beyond understanding, O blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired an enlightened spiritual eye, thou didst truly see distant things as present through grace, being revealed as a prophet of the Almighty.

*Theotokion:*
Jesus Christ came forth from thee incarnate, and deified us by the assumption of flesh, O all-immaculate one; therefore we honor thee as His Mother.

*Kontakion of the Prophet, Tone 8:*[^679]
As a most precious gift, thou wast dedicated unto God before conception, and from infancy thou didst serve Him as an angel, O all-blessed one, and wast counted worthy to foretell future things. Therefore we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Samuel, prophet of God, great high priest!

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃е́ственныѧ вѣ́ры):*
The Law's *(Fol. 109v)*
...The Law's judgments having nurtured thee, thou didst show thyself to be a venerable chrism; having served as priest like Aaron, and having enlightened thy heart by the Spirit, thou didst foresee distant things as present, O venerable prophet. Entreat Christ God to grant our souls peace and great mercy.

*Glory... Both now... the same Tone [Tone 3], Podoben: "Awed by the beauty" (Красотѣ̀ дв҃ства):*[^680]
In thy birthgiving was a seedless conception, and in thy dormition an incorrupt mortality: a twofold wonder met together in a wonder, O Mother of God. For how canst thou be unwedded, yet be a pure nourisher of a babe? And how can the Mother of God be a corpse exuding myrrh? Therefore with the angel we cry unto thee: Rejoice, O full of grace!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^681]
The sayings and riddles of the prophets foreshowed Thine incarnation from the Virgin, O Christ; the radiance of Thy brightness shall go forth for a light to the nations, and the abyss shall cry aloud unto Thee with rejoicing: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Behold, O ye peoples, and marvel: for the holy and manifest mountain of God is exalted on high into the heavenly mansions, the earthly heaven being settled in the heavenly and incorruptible dwelling.

*Troparion:*
Thy death became a passage unto everlasting and better life, O pure one, transferring thee from the temporal unto the truly divine and unending life, in rejoicing to behold thy Son and Lord.

*Troparion:*
The heavenly portals were lifted up, and the angels sang, and Christ received the vessel of His Mother's virginity; the Cherubim took thee up with joy, and the Seraphim glorify thee, rejoicing.

*Another Canon. Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^682]
Sitting in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the most divine came on a light cloud, and with His incorruptible palm saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
Holily adorned with an excellent life, the saving precepts of God's Law were entrusted unto thee, to declare the transgressions unto Israel, who ever provoked the Merciful One to anger.

*Troparion:*
Eli the priest was condemned because of his iniquitous sons, and by a righteous judgment was separated from the divine ministry;[^683] but Samuel the priest, as an obedient servant of Him Who called, was brought in with a guileless soul.

*Troparion:*
Standing excellently before the ordinances of the Law, like Aaron of old, thou didst serve the King of all, and didst offer the legal sacrifices, which prefigured Christ's saving slaughter.

*(Fol. 110r)*
For thy foolish people, O Samuel, who did not abide in the divine commands of the King of all, thou didst anoint a king by God's behest as they had asked, cutting off their foolish strength with a horn.[^684]

*Theotokion:*
Without emptying the Father's bosom, He was well pleased to recline in thy bosom as a newborn infant, for the restoration of our souls; unto Whom we cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^685]
We will declare the divine and ineffable beauty of Thy virtues, O Christ; for having shone forth as the co-eternal and hypostatic brightness from the everlasting glory, and having become incarnate from the virginal womb, Thou hast shone forth as the Sun upon those in darkness and the shadow.

*Troparion:*
As upon a cloud, O Virgin, the choir of the Apostles, borne from the ends of the earth to Zion, gathered together to minister unto thee, the light cloud, from whom the Most High God, the righteous Sun, shone forth upon those in darkness and the shadow.

*Troparion:*
The God-receiving tongues of the divinely eloquent men, sounding louder than trumpets, cried unto the Mother of God, proclaiming by the Spirit the funeral hymn: Rejoice, O incorruptible fountain of God's life-originating and saving incarnation for all.

*Another Canon. Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^686]
The ungodly shall not behold Thy glory, O Christ; but waking early at dawn out of the night, we hymn Thee, the Only-begotten, the brightness of the Father's glory of the Godhead, O Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Made wise by the Divine Spirit, thou wast a servant of God from infancy; with the oil of anointing, O glorious one, by grace thou didst anoint the king by divine behests.

*Troparion:*
When Saul persisted in disobedience, thou didst foretell his disorderly downfall, O blessed Samuel, and the divine will and the manifest departure of the Divine Spirit.[^687]

*Troparion:*
Ever living entwined with guilelessness, he manifestly reproved evil perversity, setting it right as a divine priest and as a most true prophet.

*Theotokion:*
Having been with us through mercy, the Savior became incarnate beyond understanding from thy sacred blood, O all-immaculate one, and is known as God and Man, the Lover of mankind.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^688]
The inner fire of the sea-monster born of the watery deep was a prefiguration of Thy three-day burial, whereof the Prophet Jonah was shown forth; for saved and unhurt as he had been sent forth, he cried: I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise *(Fol. 110v)*
...of praise, O Lord![^688]

*Troparion:*
The King and God of all gives thee things above nature; for even as He preserved thee a Virgin in birthgiving, so also He kept thy body incorrupt in the tomb, and glorified thee with a divine translation, bestowing honor upon thee as a Son upon His Mother.

*Troparion:*
As a radiant candlestick of immaterial light, a golden censer of the divine Coal, into the Holy of Holies He settled thee, even thy birthgiving, O Virgin: the jar and the rod, the God-inscribed table, the holy ark, the table of the bread of life.

*Another Canon. Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^689]
Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah cried aloud, praying in the sea-monster: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of hosts!

*Troparion:*
Thy word, divinely deified by the grace of the Word, declared distant things as being near, O Prophet Samuel, vessel of the Divine Spirit.

*Troparion:*
Exalted by lofty contemplations and deeds, as a prophet and divine priest of the Almighty, by the legal ministries thou cleansest the people, O right marvelous one.

*Troparion:*
Saul was rejected, having become a transgressor; and God commands thee, O divinely wise Samuel, to anoint with oil gentle David in his stead.[^690]

*Theotokion:*
Eve was delivered from pangs, O all-immaculate one; for without pain thou didst give birth unto Christ our God, Who manifestly healed the passions and sicknesses of all.

*Kontakion, Tone 2:*[^691]
The tomb and mortality could not hold the Mother of God, unslumbering in intercessions and an unfailing hope in mediations: for as the Mother of Life, He Who dwelt in her ever-virginal womb translated her unto life.

*Ikos:*[^692]
Surround my thoughts with a rampart, O my Christ, for I dare to sing the wall of the world, Thy pure Mother; strengthen me upon the pillar of words, and defend me in difficult thoughts: for Thou fulfillest the supplications of those who cry aloud and ask in faith. Grant me therefore a profitable tongue and an unashamed mind: for every gift of radiance is sent down from Thee, O Giver of light, Who didst dwell in the ever-virginal womb.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^693]
Divine love, contending against shameless wrath and fire, dewed the flame and laughed at wrath, with the divinely inspired rational three-stringed lute of the holy ones singing against musical *(Fol. 111r)*
...instruments in the midst of the flame: Blessed art Thou, O exceedingly glorified God of our fathers and our God!

*Troparion:*
Moses in anger shattered the God-fashioned tablets written by the Divine Spirit; but the Master of this [Law], having preserved unhurt in heavenly mansions her who gave Him birth, now hath settled her within. Leaping with her, we cry aloud unto Christ: Blessed art Thou, O exceedingly glorified God of our fathers and our God![^694]

*Troparion:*
With pure lips upon cymbals, musically with a radiant heart, with a sweet-sounding trumpet, with a lofty mind, on the notable and chosen day of the translation of the pure Virgin, clapping with active hands, we cry aloud: Blessed art Thou, O exceedingly glorified God of our fathers and our God!

*Troparion:*
Assemble together, ye divinely wise people: for the dwelling-place of God's glory is translated from Zion unto the heavenly habitation, where is the pure voice of those who feast, the voice of ineffable gladness, and of those who cry aloud unto Christ in joy: Blessed art Thou, O exceedingly glorified God of our fathers and our God!

*Another Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^695]
The three youths in Babylon, having accounted the tyrant's command as folly, cried aloud in the midst of the flame: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
She who gave thee birth brings so great a gift unto the King of all, O blessed one, having accomplished a sacred prayer, and singing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Within the tabernacle, serving thee in the temple and the order of Aaron unto the Master of all, divine grace enlightened thee, O glorious Samuel.

*Troparion:*
Judging the people of Israel with the divine word, O blessed one, ever declaring unto them the ordinance of God, and turning away every harm of enemies by fervent prayer.

*Troparion:*
Having emptied thy mind of all material things, O glorious one, thou didst show thyself to be a mirror of the Spirit, crying unceasingly: Blessed art Thou, O Lord, the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast revealed as having unburnt given birth unto the Fire of the Godhead in truth, O most pure one; therefore burn up my material passions, O Birthgiver of God, as one full of prayer and loving God.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^696]
The almighty angel of God showed unto the youths a flame that dewed the holy and burned the ungodly, and made the Mother of God a life-originating fountain, pouring out corruption unto death and life unto those who sing: We the redeemed sing unto the only Creator and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
The whole multitude of theologians in Zion followed the divine, sacred ark with words *(Fol. 111v)*
...Whither goest thou now, O tabernacle of the living God? Cease not to look upon those who sing in faith: We the redeemed sing unto the only Creator and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
