Even as having lifted up thy hands, O all-immaculate one, thou departest, those hands wherewith thou didst bear God in the flesh, with boldness as a Mother thou didst say unto Him Who was born of thee: Those whom Thou hast given me, preserve unto ages, who cry aloud unto Thee: We the redeemed sing unto the only Creator and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Another Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^697]
O almighty Redeemer of all, having descended into the midst of the flame, Thou didst dew those who lived piously, and didst teach them to sing: All ye works, bless and sing the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Adorned with Aaron's vesture, thou didst enter into the Holy of Holies, cleansing with sacrifices, O most blessed one, which foreannounced the saving slaughter of the Lamb.

*Troparion:*
Having received the radiance of the Spirit with a pure mind, O Samuel, like a divine prophet thou proclaimest distant things as present; therefore in faith we praise thee.

*Troparion:*
David was chosen among the sons of Jesse, even as thou didst reveal, having anointed him with holy oil, O Samuel, and didst cry aloud: All ye works of the Lord, bless the Lord![^698]

*Troparion:*
Celebrating thy solemnity, O Samuel, companion of the venerable, by thy prayers may we obtain the kingdom on high, crying aloud: All ye works of the Lord, bless the Lord!

*Theotokion:*
Vouchsafe me the mercies of God by thy prayers, O Lady who lovest good, and deliver me from Gehenna and that outer darkness, that in faith and love I may honor thee.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^699]
The ordinances of nature are overcome in thee, O pure Virgin: for birthgiving remains virginal, and death is betrothed unto life; virgin after birthgiving, and alive after death, thou ever savest thine inheritance, O Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
The angelic hosts marveled, beholding in Zion their Master carrying a woman's soul in His hands; for unto her who bore Him in all purity, in a manner befitting a Son, He cried aloud: Come, O pure one, be glorified with the Son and God!

*Troparion:*
The choir of the Apostles wrapped thy God-receiving body, looking upon it with fear, and crying aloud with a great voice: Ascending into the heavenly mansions unto thy Son, thou ever savest thine inheritance, O Mother of God.

*Another Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^700]
Eve indeed brought in the curse through the sickness of disobedience; but thou, O Virgin Mother of God, through the bud of thy childbearing *(Fol. 112r)*
...through the bud of thy childbearing thou hast blossomed forth a blessing unto the world; therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparion:*
Standing as a servant before the face of our Master and God, O blessed one, adorned with an immaculate life, thou didst serve Him, receiving divine rays and prophesying most clearly.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear from thy mother's womb as a vessel of the Holy Spirit, O glorious one; and shining forth with sacred vesture, thou didst please God with meekness of heart, O most rich one, and with beauty of mind.

*Troparion:*
Today thy memory has shone forth upon us like the sun, O God-bearer, with rays of bountiful gifts enlightening the souls of those who honor thee, and driving away the mist of cruel things; therefore we all call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast taken up unto light-bestowing mansions, and didst stand brighter than the sun, beholding what the prophets, apostles, and all the righteous behold, being deified by communion, O divinely inspired one; therefore we all call thee blessed.

*Theotokion:*
Knowing no man's corruption, thou didst receive in thy womb the incorrupt Word, Who delivers us from many sins which corrupted us, through the suffering of His incorrupt flesh, O only incorrupt and all-immaculate one.

*Exapostilarion, Tone 3, Podoben: "O Heaven with stars" (Не́бо звѣзда́ми):*[^701]
Let Samuel be sung, the gift given unto God the Most High before conception by his all-glorious mother, who anointed kings as priest and prophet.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast:*[^702]
Ye Apostles, assembled here from the ends of the earth in the village of Gethsemane, bury my body; and Thou, my Son and God, receive my spirit.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^703]
O pure one, above natural are thy mysteries; for as the Mother of God, O Birthgiver of God, thou hast now radiantly passed over unto Him.

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou, and the ark of Thy holiness.*[^704]
The tomb of the all-pure Mother of God is a ladder unto heaven, leading up those who sing this divine dormition.

*Verse: The Lord has sworn in truth unto David, and He will not turn away from it.*[^705]
When thou wast translated unto Him Who was born of thee, the Apostles came together upon the clouds to bury thy body.

*Glory... Both now... the same Tone and Podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
All ye earthborn, let us run eagerly together with the bodiless hosts, to bury together the Mother of the Master.

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 6 of both Canons, with the heirmos on 6.*

*(Fol. 112v)*
*The same month, on the 21st day. The Holy Apostle Thaddaeus, and the Holy Martyr Bassa:*[^706]
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Apostle, Tone 8, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*[^707]
O blessed Apostle Thaddaeus, drawing near unto the supreme Light, thou wast truly a second light by divine communion; and having destroyed the night of polytheism, thou didst bring souls unto the Almighty. Therefore rejoicing, we honor thy radiant, notable, and light-bearing memory, glorifying Christ.

O blessed Thaddaeus, who received God, after Christ's divine and light-bearing Resurrection and most holy Ascension into heaven, thou didst entrust the divine preaching unto the city of Edessa; having arrived therein, by words and miracles thou didst establish Prince Abgar of that place, and with him all the people, unto the truth.[^708]

O blessed Thaddaeus, divinely wise, granting sight unto the blind by thy divine touch, walking unto the lame, health unto the paralyzed, restoration unto the bedridden, and saving understanding unto the unlearned; for thou wast filled with the gifts of the all-working Spirit, O most rich one; therefore we hymn thee.

*Other Stichera of the Saint, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*[^709]
Thou didst endure the suffering of many tortures, O all-praised one, approaching valiantly; whence thou hast passed over unto painless rest, unwaning radiance, bright joy, and eternal divine delight. Therefore we call thee blessed, and celebrate today thy holy feast, O passion-bearer acceptable unto God.[^710]

After many painful and diverse tortures, having been cast into the sea, God, the Savior of all, saved thee, who didst trample the power of the stranger by divine power, O much-suffering Bassa. And thence thou didst enlighten the minds of the pious, having accomplished signs beyond mind and thought.

As a sweet-singing bird, having sounded forth in the vine of beautiful suffering, thou didst summon thy beautiful nestlings, having escaped with them the snaring of deception, O divinely wise, right marvelous Bassa; with whom also thou didst take up thy dwelling *(Fol. 113r)*
...in the celestial beauties, O most blessed one, praying unceasingly for all.

*Glory... Both now... Tone 6:*
Come, let us celebrate the universal dormition of the all-immaculate Mother of God: for today the angels make festival over the honorable translation of the Mother of God, and summon us earthborn mortals unto rejoicing, to cry with unceasing voice: Rejoice, thou who hast been translated from earth and removed unto heavenly mansions! Rejoice, thou who by a light cloud didst gather into one the choir of disciples! Rejoice, our hope and salvation; for the Christian race unceasingly calls thee blessed.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "When from the Tree" (Є҆гда̀ ѿ дре́ва):*[^711]
Come, all ye light-bearers, with sacred hymns and cymbals let us honor the much-hymned dormition of the Mother of God: for she is translated from earth, and by the immaterial hosts she is settled and takes up her dwelling in glory, beholding the divine beauty, and pouring forth grace unto all who faithfully honor her memory.

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou, and the ark of Thy holiness.*[^712]
The sacred apostolic choir of the divine ones, dew-dropped by clouds, was gathered from the ends of the earth, O Lady, to bury thy honorable, God-receiving, and incorrupt body. Therefore also the hosts above the world, invisibly surrounding the bier, sang unto thee in hymns: Rejoice, O new ark of sanctification!

*Verse: The Lord has sworn in truth unto David, and He will not turn away from it.*[^713]
All thy mysteries are incomprehensible, all truly ineffable and fearful: for thou wast the Parent of God; thou didst adorn the heavens, thou didst enlighten the world, thou didst fill all things with fragrance by thy divine memory. Therefore the nations call thee blessed; therefore they glorify thee: Rejoice, O new ark of sanctification!

*Glory... Both now... Tone 8:*[^714]
Today the virgin choirs mystically stand around the bier of the Virgin Mother, and the souls of the righteous surrounding the Queen glorify her. Some indeed offer virginity as a gift instead of myrrh; and others offer immaterial singing with virtue. For it is becoming for the Mother of God, as Queen, to be soul-fashioned with royal virtues shining radiantly. Together with them, having brought a pure life, let us go forth unto the burial of the Mother of our God, harmoniously calling her blessed with songs and spiritual hymns.

*Troparion of the Apostle, Tone 3:*[^715]
O holy Apostle Thaddaeus, entreat the merciful God to grant our souls remission of transgressions *(Fol. 113v)*
...remission of transgressions.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast, Tone 1:*[^716]
In giving birth thou didst preserve virginity, in thy dormition thou didst not forsake the world, O Mother of God: thou hast passed over unto life, being the Mother of Life, and by thy prayers thou deliverest our souls from death.

*At Matins, after the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*[^717]
By the command of her Creator, Who was born of thee, a cloud gathered the Apostles to behold thy translation. Therefore having buried with glory and great joy, hymning the most pure body of thy blessedness, O Mother of Christ God.
*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, the same Tone [Tone 4], Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):*[^718]
The apostolic choir, dispersed throughout the earth, gathered in Zion to escort from earth the Mother of God unto the Most High, Whom she bore. The super-mundane heavenly hosts ran before, and together made chorus, crying spiritually: Rejoice, ye heavens, having received the Mother of God, Who exercises dominion over things visible and invisible!
*Glory... Both now... the same.*

*Canon of the Feast, with the heirmos on 6: The work of Lord John [of Damascus], Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^719]
I will open my mouth, and it shall be filled with the Spirit, and I will utter a word unto the Queen Mother; and I will be seen celebrating radiantly, and rejoicing I will sing her dormition.

*Troparion:*
Ye virgin maidens, with Miriam the prophetess, cry aloud now an exodus-song: for the Virgin and only Mother of God is translated unto the heavenly reception.

*Troparion:*
Worthily, as an ensouled heaven, did the celestial, most pure and divine tabernacles receive thee; and thou didst stand radiantly adorned, as an all-immaculate bride, before the King and God.

*Canon of the Apostle, on 4. Whose acrostic is: I sing of Thaddaeus the wise speaker of God. The work of Joseph. Tone 8:*[^720]
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^721]
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone has gloriously been glorified.

*Troparion:*
Standing before the Master's throne, O all-wise Thaddaeus, grant me enlightenment to sing thy light-bearing festival.

*Troparion:*
The ageless Light, the Pre-eternal One, appeared among us in time, and left thee, O blessed one, as a ray enlightening the world.

*Troparion:*
Having girded thee with power, He Who is invincible in strength strengthened thee to destroy all the might of the deceiver.

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast revealed by all creations as the tabernacle of Him Who is ineffably *(Fol. 114r)*
...born ineffably, O all-hymned Virgin Mother of God.

*Canon of the Holy Martyr Bassa, to 4. Whose acrostic is: I offer thy struggles, O venerable Bassa.*[^722] *The composition of Joseph. Tone the same [Tone 8].*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^723]
Having traversed the water as if it were dry land, and having escaped the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Deliverer and God.

*Troparion:*
Unto me who praise with joy thy all-glorious memory, pray, O blessed one, that illumination from heaven be given me, and forgiveness of soul-corrupting sins.

*Troparion:*
Thy soul, fortified with the fear of the Lord, feared not the God-abhorred decrees, nor diverse stripes and perils; therefore thou didst take courage valiantly.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst patiently endure manifold torments with thy three sons, O passion-bearer; with them thou didst weave incorruptible crowns, glorifying the Trinity.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst conceive the Word of the Father without maternal pangs, O all-holy one; and Him ineffably, beyond word, thou didst bring forth for our salvation.

*Ode 3 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^724]
O Mother of God, living and bountiful fountain, establish those who sing thy praises, who have spiritually gathered a choir for themselves, and in thy divine memory vouchsafe unto them crowns of glory.

*Troparion:*
Proceeding from mortal loins, like unto us by nature, O pure one, thou didst accomplish thy departure; and having given birth unto the true Life, thou didst pass over unto the divine and hypostatic Life.

*Troparion:*
The choir of theologians from the ends of the earth, and the multitudes of angels from on high, came to Zion by the almighty nod, worthily ministering unto thy burial, O Sovereign Lady.

*Another Canon [of the Apostle]. Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^725]
Thou art the establishment of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the Light of the darkened, and my spirit hymns Thee.

*Troparion:*
Illumining the people with the beam of divine grace, O glorious one, thou wast revealed as a pure light unto those who were in the darkness of grievous evils.

*Troparion:*
Having come unto King Abgar, O wise one, by God-working behests thou didst bring him healings and deliverance.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst make men temples for the all-effecting Spirit, O wise one, having pulled down the ruinous temples of the earthborn.

*Theotokion:*
The prefigurations of the Law and the prophetic voices, O Sovereign Lady, divinely and blessedly declared thy awesome childbearing.

*Another Canon [of the Saint]. Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^726]
O Lord, Builder of the vault of heaven, and Founder of the Church, do Thou establish me *(Fol. 114v)*
...establish me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O Thou Who alone art the Lover of mankind.

*Troparion:*
Kindled with faith and fervent desire of the exceeding love of Christ, O all-lauded one, thou didst extinguish the fire by the presence of an angel, and didst immediately consume the senseless people.

*Troparion:*
Blessed now is the generation of the upright that shone forth from thy womb with the brightness of sufferings, O most glorious Bassa, and dispelled the darkness of the adversaries' iniquities by firmness of mind.

*Troparion:*
O mother's surpassing of the law of transient nature! For by a union beyond nature, she endured to see unjustly die in diverse manners those whom she had nurtured with faith and milk.

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto the one Hypostatic Life, O all-immaculate Virgin, thou didst save me who was slain by the eating of disobedience; therefore, as is meet, we now call thee blessed, who art ever-blessed.

*Kontakion of the Apostle, Tone 4:*[^727]
As a most radiant star the Church acquired thee, O Apostle Thaddaeus, being ever illumined by thy wonders; save those who honor thy memory in faith.

*Sessional Hymn of the Apostle, Tone 8, Podoben: "Guiding unto wisdom" (Премудрости):*[^728]
Having drawn nigh unto the noetic Sun, thou didst receive mystical rays from Him; and thou wast like a most radiant star, enlightening the fullness of all the earth, and dispelling the mist of error, O blessed one. Therefore we celebrate thy light-bearing memory, faithfully praising thee, and with one accord we cry unto thee, O Apostle Thaddaeus: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who lovingly honor thy holy memory.

*Glory... Sessional Hymn of the Saint, Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb" (Гробъ твой):*[^729]
Having quenched the fire of torments with the dew of the Spirit, O venerable one, thou didst pass over unto the divine and immaterial Light; and after thy blessed demise, thou dost pour forth drops of healings, quelling the inflammation of passions by spiritual power.

*Both now... of the Feast, Tone and Podoben the same [Tone 1, Podoben: "While the soldiers were guarding Thy tomb"] (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*[^730]
The honorable choir of the all-wise Apostles gathered wondrously to bury gloriously thy most pure body, O all-hymned Mother of God; with whom the multitude of angels also sang, honoring in praise thy venerable dormition, which we celebrate in faith.

*Ode 4 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^731]
Perceiving the unsearchable divine counsel of Thine incarnation from the Virgin, O Thou Most High, the Prophet Habakkuk cried aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
A wonder it was to behold the heaven of the King of all *(Fol. 115r)*
...the animate heaven of the King of all, passing beyond the earthly realms: How marvelous are Thy works! Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
If Her incomprehensible Fruit, by Whom the heavens were made, willingly accepted burial as mortal, how shall She refuse burial, who bore Him without knowing wedlock?

*Troparion:*
In thy dormition, O Mother of God, the angelic hosts covered thy body, which was more spacious than the heavens and received God, with sacred wings in trembling and joy.

*Another Canon [of the Apostle]. Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^732]
I have heard the mystery of Thy dispensation, O Lord, I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Godhead.

*Troparion:*
Having an exalted mind, thou didst receive divine understanding and spiritual gifts, O disciple Thaddaeus of the King of all.

*Troparion:*
Having been instructed, O blessed one, in the divine mysteries, O most rich one, thou didst enlighten men with ineffable words to honor the consubstantial Trinity.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst inscribe the divine Law upon hearts, O blessed one, having first blotted out the inscription of ignorance and the graven images of impiety.

*Theotokion:*
As having given birth to the Creator, thou wast revealed as Queen of all creation; therefore we glorify thee, O Mother of God, only Ever-Virgin.

*Another Canon [of the Saint]. Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^733]
*I have heard the mystery of Thy dispensation, O Lord, I have understood Thy works, and I have glorified Thy Godhead.*

*Troparion:*
Having an undivided mind, thou didst set thyself apart for the struggle against the deceitful adversary, and didst drown him in the depth of thy blood.

*Troparion:*
Having received heavy torments, condemned to an unjust death, thou wast calmed and saved, O passion-bearer Bassa, by divine behest.

*Troparion:*
An unshakable kingdom, with thy praiseworthy sons, O glorious one, thou hast inherited: and truly the heavenly bridal chamber and the never-setting light.

*Theotokion:*
Habakkuk of old foresaw thee in spirit as the unhewn mountain: God appeared from thee, O Virgin, and saved us.

*Ode 5 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^734]
All things were amazed at thy venerable dormition; for thou, O Virgin unwedded, didst pass from earth unto the eternal mansions and unto endless life, granting salvation unto all who praise thee.

*Troparion:*
Let the trumpets of the theologians sound today; and let the eloquent tongue of man now give praise; let the air resound, shining with immeasurable light; let the angels hymn the dormition of the all-pure Virgin!

*Troparion:*
It was fitting that the chosen vessel, wholly astonished at the singing unto thee *(Fol. 115v)*
...at thy departure, O Virgin: he was wholly consecrated unto God, accepted of God by all, and truly revealed as such, O all-hymned Mother of God.

*Another Canon [of the Apostle]. Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^735]
Thou Who out of the night of ignorance didst enlighten the ends of the earth with the knowledge of God: enlighten me with the dawn of Thy love for mankind, O Lord.

*Troparion:*
Bearing again the divine likeness, O blessed one, thou didst come unto the local prince Abgar as a divine physician.

*Troparion:*
The consecrated Thomas, after the divine Ascension of the Word, sent thee as a preacher, O all-wise Thaddaeus, unto those in need.

*Troparion:*
Seeing diseases driven away and incurable ailments of the heart healed, Abgar was filled with divine illumination through thy mediation.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth unto the incarnate Word, bearing a twofold operation, O unwedded Maiden, having remained an undefiled Virgin.

*Another Canon [of the Saint]. Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^736]
Why hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O Light never-setting? And strange darkness hath covered me, the wretched one: but convert me, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my ways, I pray Thee.

*Troparion:*
Possessed with thirst for Christ's love, O glorious, divinely blessed Agapius, thou didst utterly hate malice, and didst valiantly endure the pains of torments and bodily slaying by a spear.

*Troparion:*
With thy bowels opened, scraped with claws, O martyr Theognis, thou didst know the God of all more abundantly, purely, and perfectly; unto Whom thou didst ascend with glory, shining with the brightness of sufferings.

*Troparion:*
With unhesitating faith, O all-wise Pistus, having entered into the judgment seat of the infidels, O blessed one, thou didst astonish the hearts of the senseless, when thy bones were crushed and thy whole body was broken limb from limb.

*Theotokion:*
Thou hast now set right the fall of the foremother Eve, having given birth unto the Deliverer of all, the Savior and Creator and Lord, O Mother of God, only blessed among women; therefore we the faithful glorify thee.

*Ode 6 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^737]
Celebrating this divine and all-venerable feast of the Mother of God, come, O divinely wise, let us clap our hands, glorifying God Who was born of her.

*Troparion:*
Life shone forth from thee, without violating the keys of virginity: how then was thy most pure and life-originating body made partaker of the trial of death?

*Troparion:*
Having been the temple of Life, thou didst attain unto everlasting life *(Fol. 116r)*
...for through death thou didst pass over unto life, having given birth unto the Hypostatic Life.

*Another Canon [of the Apostle]. Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^738]
Thou didst settle Jonah alone in the whale, O Lord; do Thou save me also, who am bound in the nets of the enemy, as Thou didst save him from corruption.

*Troparion:*
The illumination of the Holy Spirit, dwelling in Thy heart, made thee a true luminary for the world, O Thaddaeus, dispelling the darkness of deceit.

*Troparion:*
Bearing in the flesh the saving wounds of Christ as an adornment, O Thaddaeus, thou didst deliver men from all deformity, O wise one, and wast taken up unto most fair joy.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst show forth unto us the morning Sun of righteousness, O glorious one; and having been enlightened thereby, we earthborn have become sons of the noetic Light, O Thaddaeus.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bring forth for us a new Babe, consubstantial with the Father, Who leads corrupt human nature back unto its first beauty, O pure one.

*Another Canon [of the Saint]. Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^739]
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depths of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst utterly destroy the exaltations of the abominable gods, having anchored thy soul in the ineffable love of God; and thou wast a co-dweller with the angels, O passion-bearer Bassa.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst accomplish great struggles for Christ, O martyr, and didst receive great recompenses from Him: immortal good fame, and the divine bridal chamber, and food that never passes away.

*Troparion:*
As of old Jonah from the deep of the sea, O Savior, God, Who is marvelous in thee and ever glorified in the counsels of the saints, saved thee by His all-effecting power, O all-praised one.

*Theotokion:*
The depths of sin and the billows of despair tempest-toss my mind: take pity, O Sovereign Lady, and stretch forth thy hand unto me and save me, who didst give birth to the Savior.

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 2:*[^740]
Neither the tomb nor death could hold the Mother of God, who is unsleeping in prayers and an unchanging hope in intercessions; for as the Mother of Life, He Who dwelt in her ever-virginal womb translated her unto life.

*Ikos:*
Fortify my thoughts, O my Christ, for I dare to hymn the rampart of the world, Thy pure Mother; establish me upon the rock of Thy words, and defend me in heavy thoughts: for Thou fulfillest the petitions of those who call and ask in faith. Do Thou therefore grant me *(Fol. 116v)*
...an acceptable tongue and a blameless thought: for every giving of radiance is sent down from Thee, O Giver of light, Who didst dwell in the ever-virginal womb.

*Ode 7 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^741]
The divinely wise served not creation rather than the Creator; but valiantly trampling down the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, the all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Ye young men and virgins, honoring the memory of the Virgin and Mother of God, ye elders and rulers, kings and judges, sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Let the heavenly mountains sound with the spiritual trumpet; let the hills rejoice, and let the divine Apostles dance: the Queen passes over unto the Son, to reign with Him!

*Troparion:*
The all-sacred dormition of Thine incorruptible and divine Mother hath gathered together the ranks of the transcendent powers on high, to make merry with those on earth who sing unto Thee: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Another Canon [of the Apostle]. Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^742]
The godly Children in Babylon bowed not down to the golden image, but bedewed in the midst of the fiery furnace, they sang a hymn, saying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers, Who art supremely exalted!

*Troparion:*
Working wonders by the invocation of Christ Who was manifested in the flesh, thou didst draw peoples and cities, O all-praised one, unto the certain faith, crying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stop the corruptions of godlessness by the divine salt of thy sweet words, and didst heal hearts wounded by the serpent's sores, O blessed Apostle, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast taken up unto the height of vision, O Thaddaeus, being filled with the Divine Spirit; and thou wast divinely enriched with the saving word, and didst teach the people to cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Beset with the slips of my conduct and by unbecoming deeds, I flee unto thee: help me, O Sovereign Lady, granting patterns of repentance unto my humble soul, that I may glorify thee.

*Another Canon [of the Saint]. Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^743]
The Children having arrived from Judea in Babylon of old, trampled down the flame of the furnace by their faith in the Trinity, singing: O God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Having passed through the flame of fire unburnt by divine power, O martyr, by the fire of thy blood *(Fol. 117r)*
...crying, thou didst consume: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
He Who bound the rushings of the lions in the pit, and saved His Daniel, showed thee, O passion-bearer, unharmed by wild beasts, as thou didst sing unto Thee, O Master: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Illumined with the bright beams of the Cross, O glorious Bassa, thou didst pass through the darkness of deceit unharmed, and didst go forth unto the Light, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Burst asunder the manifoldly woven bonds of my sins, O Bride of God, who beyond word didst give birth unto God Who takes away the sin of the world, O blessed, pure, and ever-all-glorified one.

*Ode 8 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^744]
The childbearing of the Mother of God saved the godly Children in the furnace; then prefigured, but now in reality, it stirs up the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Sing unto the Lord, ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thy memory, O pure Virgin, the Principalities and Authorities and Powers, Angels, Archangels, Thrones, Dominions, Cherubim, and awesome Seraphim glorify; and we, the human race, sing and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
He Who strangely dwelt in thy pure womb, becoming incarnate, O Mother of God, having received this thy all-sacred spirit, gave it rest with Himself as a Son who is a debtor; therefore we sing unto thee, O Virgin, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
O wonders of the Ever-Virgin and Mother of God, surpassing understanding! For having settled in the tomb, she showed it to be Paradise, standing before which today, rejoicing we sing: Sing unto the Lord, ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Another Canon [of the Apostle]. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^745]
Having become victors over the tyrant and the flame by Thy grace, cleaving earnestly unto Thy commandments, the Children cried: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast revealed as a heaven, declaring the glory of God, O Thaddaeus, and wast the enlightenment of the nations, leading them unto divine faith as they fervently cry: Sing unto the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
Granting strength unto the weak, and sight unto the blind, and good walking unto the lame, O blessed one, by spiritual power: thou wast a luminary of the city of Edessa, which ever faithfully praises thee, O Thaddaeus.

*Troparion:*
He who by the wealth of wonders and by divine nod transformed an unbelieving nation unto the faith, and saved those who fled unto him from idolatrous deceit, Thaddaeus is glorified in faith according to God.

*Theotokion:*
The shadow of the Law *(Fol. 117v)*
...The shadow of the Law hath ceased, O Sovereign Lady, since thou didst give birth unto the Lawgiver, Who enlightens the universe with grace; Whom ever entreat speedily to have compassion upon me, who am vanquished by the law of sin.

*Another Canon [of the Saint]. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^746]
Sevenfold the furnace the Chaldean tyrant fiercely kindled for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing praise; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
He Who clothed Himself with our weakness manifestly strengthened thy weak nature, and made thee, O martyr, stronger than fire, water, wild beasts, and instruments of torture, as thou didst faithfully cry: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Youthfully acting valiantly, they manly conquered the opposition of the adversaries, the venerable youths: divine Theognis, sacred Agapius, together with Pistus, having shown themselves so in deed and in word; and having received crowns of victory from God, they supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Like a fruitful olive tree, like a bountiful vineyard directed by three branches, with them cultivating clusters of confession, pouring forth the wine of testimony, O Bassa, making glad the hearts of those who piously cry: Ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto the ages!

*Troparion:*
With the bodiless ranks and the armies of martyrs, standing before the throne of God, O all-praised ones, and filled with glory, grace, and illumination, dispel the gloom of the sins of us who celebrate in faith your light-bearing and divine, gift-filled, excellent memory.

*Theotokion:*
He Who by a nod made the heavens, and the earth by His will, the Word of the Father, having made all things, O all-pure one, and created man: from thee He manifestly assumed flesh unto Himself, to restore our corrupt nature, and to crush the deceit of the serpent, O Virgin, He the only Merciful One.

*Ode 9 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^747]
Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, enlightened by the Spirit; and let the nature of immaterial minds keep festival, honoring the sacred dormition of the Mother of God, and crying: Rejoice, O all-blessed Mother of God, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Troparion:*
Come unto Zion, upon the divine and fertile mountain of the living God, let us rejoice, beholding the Mother of God; for Christ hath translated her as a Mother into the Holy of Holies, unto a far better and divine tabernacle.

*Troparion:*
Come, ye faithful *(Fol. 118r)*
...Come, ye faithful, let us approach the tomb of the Mother of God, and let us embrace it with hearts and lips, touching it purely with eyes and face, and drawing bountiful gifts of healings from the ever-flowing fountain.

*Troparion:*
Receive from us a funeral hymn, O Mother of the living God, and overshadow us with thy light-bearing and divine grace: granting victories unto the King,[^748] peace unto Christ-loving people, remission unto those who sing, and salvation unto our souls.

*Another Canon [of the Apostle]. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^749]
Truly we confess thee to be the Mother of God, saved through thee, O pure Virgin, magnifying thee with the bodiless choirs.

*Troparion:*
To behold the eternal divine recompenses and clearly to draw nigh unto the summit of desires, O blessed Thaddaeus, exult and dance!

*Troparion:*
Like a cedar, like a great cypress, O glorious Thaddaeus, thou didst lift thyself up by virtues, making fragrant the hearts of those who praise thee.

*Troparion:*
Thou hast been joined, O blessed one, unto the Apostles and martyrs and the bodiless choirs: make merry, and with them entreat to save those who honor thee.

*Troparion:*
Thy memory, with the rich radiance of gifts, enlightening the minds of the faithful, compels all to praise thee, O Thaddaeus.

*Theotokion:*
O blessed Virgin, beatify my soul, afflicted with sins, who didst give birth unto the all-good Word.

*Another Canon [of the Saint]. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^750]
The heaven was amazed at this, and the ends of the earth were struck with wonder, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the ranks of angels and men magnify thee, O Mother of God.

*Troparion:*
Desiring to behold what earthly eye truly hath not seen, thou didst valiantly endure bodily pains, the crushing of limbs, O all-praised one, the rush of wild beasts, the burning of fire, the surge of the sea, being made radiant with immaterial illumination, O all-blessed one.

*Troparion:*
Truly as a light-bearing and comely, God-bearing moon didst thou shine forth from the divine choir; for as in brightly shining stars the grace of God was brought forth with those who came from thy womb; and now ye make radiant the universe with immaterial illumination, O blessed ones.

*Troparion:*
Having established your minds upon the rock of endurance, O blessed ones, the ascents of the assaults of manifold trials: ye were truly revealed as unswerving, and having won the victory according to Christ, with Him *(Fol. 118v)*
...ye were counted worthy ever to reign rejoicing.

*Troparion:*
Truly as a pure dove, and a turtledove who loved God, a swallow flying with divine fledglings, Bassa settled in the heavens, at the divine and noetic dominion, having fled the winter and the snare of the demons: whom we lovingly call blessed.

*Theotokion:*
Having given birth unto the Lover of mankind and Deliverer, O marvelous and loving Sovereign Lady, grant me deliverance from sins through thy prayers; and beatify my all-honored soul, afflicted by thoughts, and show it free from deadly passions, O all-immaculate one.

*Exapostilarion of the Apostle, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены услышите):*[^751]
Having been anointed by the Spirit as a luminary, O all-wise Thaddaeus, wherein thou didst preach the divine dispensation of the Word, and having enlightened the local ruler Abgar and all those with him, thou didst show them forth as servants of the most radiant Trinity, O God-proclaiming Apostle.

*Glory... Both now... of the Feast:*
O thy mysteries, O pure Birthgiver of God! For thou, O Virgin, wast revealed as the awesome throne of the Most High, surpassing the Cherubim, whereon sat the Creator of creation, having appeared; unto Whom also having now passed over, protect and preserve the world.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (Доме евфраѳовъ):*[^752]
Remember fervently * those who pray unto thee, * O Virgin Mother of God, * and who lovingly honor * thy sacred dormition.

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy sanctuary [LXX Ps. 131:8].*[^753]
A ladder unto heaven * is the tomb * of the all-pure Mother of God, * leading up those who sing * of her divine dormition.

*Verse: The Lord hath sworn truth unto David, and He will not turn from it [LXX Ps. 131:11].*[^754]
The heavenly Kingdom, * the glory of the elect, * receives thee as the Bride of God, * O Virgin, * going unto thy Son.

*Glory... Both now... Tone and Podoben the same [Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha"] (До́ме Є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
All ye earthborn choirs, * sing aloud: * for the Virgin Mother * hath passed over most gloriously * from earth unto heaven.

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 7 of both canons, with the heirmos, to 6.*

*(Fol. 121r)*

*In the same month, on the 22nd day: The Holy Martyr Agathonicus and those with him.*
*On this day is also chanted the service of the Holy Luppus.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of Saint Agathonicus, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Далъ еси знамение):*[^755]

Seeking first grace * and beauty beyond understanding, O Agathonicus, * rejoicing thou didst go forth * unto the noble struggles set before thee. * And wrestling with the enemy, * thou didst manly dash him to the ground, * and didst weave for thyself a radiant crown of victory, * entreating the Lover of mankind * for those who sing of thee.

Adorned with wisdom, * thou didst pour forth life-flowing speech, * converting the foolish, reproving the tormentor, * calming the storm of polytheism, * and didst confirm the faithful * to abide in God's grace. * Therefore, having endured cruel burnings, * thou didst ascend crown-bearing unto the Kingdom on high.

By the drops of blood * of thy patient suffering, * thou didst present thyself unto God as a sweet-smelling sacrifice: * a pure slaughter, an acceptable gift, * a sacred oblation, * and an entirely blameless sanctification. * Therefore by thy prayers, * preserve the plenitude of the Church in peace, * O suffering-bearer Agathonicus.

*Other Stichera of the Holy Luppus, Tone and Podoben the same [Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign"] (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*

The blessed Luppus * melted the heavy ice of godlessness * by the warmth of the divine Spirit; * and having been beheaded with the sword, * he pours forth streams of healings, * and bedews with grace souls that have languished: * let us praise him as our excellent intercessor * and fervent advocate, * O ye lovers of the martyrs.

Thou didst consign the Hellenic sanctuaries * to the depth of the waters, * and didst astonish the godless who beheld the wonders * wrought by thy faith, O all-blessed one. * Standing in their midst, * thou didst receive the divine washing from on high, * God magnifying thee * as an ever-martyr and a noble adamant, * O much-suffering passion-bearer.

When the enemies attempted to cut thee down, * they cut down one another; * and shooting arrows, they wounded one another, * O victor Luppus; * and thinking to saw thee asunder, they applied the wound to a tree, * being darkened, O blessed one; for the Lord *(Fol. 121v)*

...for the Lord preserved thee, * for Whose sake thou didst choose to suffer, * O intercessor for our souls, * converser with the angels.

*Glory... Tone 1. By Anatolius:*
Truly namesake art thou named of good victory,[^756] * O much-suffering Agathonicus: * for wounded with divine desire, * having rejected the deceit of idols and the tormentor, * thou didst pass over most gloriously unto endless life. * Therefore in thy glorious memory, * having boldness toward God, * entreat that our souls may be saved.

*Both now... Tone the same [Tone 1]:*
It was fitting for the eyewitnesses and ministers of the Word * to behold also the dormition in the flesh of His Mother, * the final mystery concerning her: * that they might not only see the Savior's ascent from earth, * but might also bear witness to the translation of her who gave Him birth. * Therefore, gathered from everywhere by divine power, * they reached Zion, * and sent her who is higher than the Cherubim on her way to heaven; * whom we also worship with them, * as one who intercedes for our souls.

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast, Tone 2, Podoben: "O marvelous wonder" (О преславнаго чудесе):*[^757]

O marvelous wonder! * She who without knowing man gave birth to God in the flesh, * and remained a Virgin, * passes over from life: * and departing from temporal life, * she is translated unto that which grows not old. * Therefore Christ's disciples and Apostles run together upon the clouds today * unto her burial.

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy sanctuary [LXX Ps. 131:8].*[^758]

Today let us all exchange into a radiant celebration: * angels praising the venerable translation * of the Mother of our God; * for having surrendered her holy and light-bearing soul * into the hands of her Son, * she is united with Him in everlasting life, * spiritually ever entreating diligently for us.

*Verse: The Lord hath sworn truth unto David, and He will not turn from it [LXX Ps. 131:11].*[^759]

Come, let us mentally stand * before venerable Gethsemane, * and the holy house of the glorious body * of the Mother of our God. * With the Apostles let us praise, * and with the angels let us sing hymns; * for there is one joy today * for those on earth * and in heaven, in her dormition.

*Glory... Tone the same [Tone 2], Idiomelon:*
Having received a name synonymous with good treasures,[^760] * thou didst inscribe thyself as a temple, O martyr Agathonicus; * lawfully contending in torments for the King of all, * thou didst overthrow the power of the cruel enemy; * having taken the prize of victory, * crown-bearing thou standest before God on high, * Whom cease not to entreat *(Fol. 122r)*

...cease not to entreat for those who honor thee, O adornment of martyrs!

*Both now... of the Feast, Tone the same [Tone 2]:*
Confirming His two natures, Jesus, Thy Son, O Mother of God, and our God: as man He dies, and as God He rises. And thee, O Mother of God, by the law of nature He was well-pleased should die, lest the dispensation seem an illusion to unbelievers. For thou hast passed over unto the heavenly realm, O unwedded Bride, as taken from earth from the bridal chamber of thy dwelling: Apostles send thee forth by the ascent of thy departure through the air, even as the earth was enlightened by thy childbearing, and angels receive thee. Therefore, having buried thy most pure body and having sung the funeral hymn, they looked on with awe and said with fear: This is the change of the right hand of the Most High,[^761] for He is in the midst of thee, and thou shalt not be moved.[^762] But, O all-hymned Mother, depart not from watching over us: for we are thy people, and the sheep of thy pasture,[^763] and we call upon thy name, asking through thee for salvation and great mercy.

*Troparion [of the Martyrs], Tone 4:*[^764]
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received crowns of incorruption from Thee, our God; for having Thy strength, they cast down the tormentors, and crushed the powerless insolence of the demons. By their prayers, save our souls.

*Glory... Both now... Troparion of the Feast, Tone 1:*[^765]
In childbearing thou didst preserve virginity, and in thy dormition thou didst not forsake the world, O Mother of God. Thou hast passed over unto life, being the Mother of Life; and by thy prayers thou deliverest our souls from death.

*At Matins, after the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Удивися Иосиф):*[^766]
Shown forth on earth more spacious than the heavens, as having given birth in the flesh unto the Creator of all: now from earth thou hast been translated into the very heavens. And the souls of the righteous beholding thee, and the angelic choirs looking upon thee, as Queen worthily ever offer praise unto thee. Therefore cease not to entreat for those who sing of thee.
*Glory... Both now... Tone and Podoben the same (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже).*

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone the same [Tone 4], Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Скоро предвари):*[^767]
We celebrate thy dormition, O all-pure one, wherein Christ our God, Who was incarnate of thee, came with ineffable glory to receive thy spirit. Therefore, even though thou hast been translated, yet cease not, O Mother of God, by thy prayers to visit us who sing of thee.
*Glory... Both now... Tone and Podoben the same (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже).*

*Canon of the Feast, with the Heirmos to 6; by Lord Cosmas, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*

*(Fol. 122v)*

*Ode 1 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^768]
Adorned with divine glory, thy sacred and glorious memory, O Virgin, hath gathered all the faithful unto gladness, Miriam leading with dances and timbrels, singing unto Thine Only-begotten: for gloriously hath He been glorified!

*Troparion:*
Let the immaterial ranks escort thy divine body unto Zion; and the multitude of the Apostles having suddenly run together from the ends [of the earth], O Mother of God, stood before thee straightway: with whom, O pure Virgin, we glorify thy venerable memory.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst bear away the prizes of victory over nature, O pure one, having given birth to God. Yet, conforming to thy Creator and Son, beyond nature thou didst submit to natural laws: therefore, having died, thou dost arise, reigning with thy Son unto eternity.

*And two Canons of the Saints, to 8.*
*Canon of the Holy Martyr Agathonicus, the composition of Joseph, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^769]
Let us all sing a song of victory unto God, Who hath wrought marvelous wonders with a high arm and saved Israel: for He hath been glorified!

*Troparion:*
Beatify my soul, O holy one, afflicted by passions, having been good, an imitator of the good God; and grant me utterance to glorify thy venerable memory.

*Troparion:*
Known unto Him Who knows all things, thou wast a martyr, O God-inspired one, and didst preach His incarnation before the nations, not fearing bodily death.

*Troparion:*
By thy word thou didst raise up those who had fallen unto others,[^770] and didst show them forth as partakers of the resurrection and of eternal radiance, O exceedingly wealthy martyr.

*Theotokion:*
Surpassing wonders is the great wonder, O pure one, of the vision of thy childbirth: for thou givest birth unto Christ Who became in the likeness of flesh, without alteration and confusion.

*Canon of the Holy Luppus, the composition of Joseph, Tone 4. Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^771]
Thou Who wast born of the Virgin, drown, I pray, in the depth of dispassion the three parts of my soul, like mighty captains; that unto Thee, as upon a timbrel, in the mortification of the body, I may sing a song of victory!

*Troparion:*
Unto him who sings of thy bright and light-bearing memory, O warrior of Christ, noble suffering-bearer, illumine my soul with unwaning radiances, being ever illumined as thou standest before the divine Light.

*Troparion:*
Having manly entered into the martyr struggles, thou didst confess God Who appeared on earth, Who all-wisely deified human nature; and thou didst destroy the Hellenic *(Fol. 123r)*

...and thou didst destroy the Hellenic gods by spiritual power.

*Troparion:*
Having filled thy heart with the water of life,[^772] like a divine river, O all-wise one, thou didst dry up the streams of deceit; and by faith thou didst water the minds of men, sprouting forth heavenly fruit.

*Theotokion:*
As morning didst thou comely dawn from the east,[^773] O unwedded Sovereign Lady, Jesus the illumination of all and God, Who destroyed the night of polytheism and with unwaning radiances enlightens the world.

*Ode 3 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^774]
O creative and sustaining Wisdom and Power of God, Who upholdest all things: establish the Church immovable and unshaken, O Christ; for Thou alone art holy, resting in the saints!

*Troparion:*
Beholding thee, O all-immaculate one, as a mortal woman, yet beyond nature also the Mother of God, the glorious Apostles touched thee with their hands with fear, shining with glory, beholding thee as a God-receiving tabernacle.

*Troparion:*
A judgment by cutting off overtook the insulting hands of the insolent one,[^775] God having preserved honor unto the living Ark by the glory of the Godhead, wherein the Word became flesh.

*Another Canon [of St. Agathonicus]. Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^776]
Let my heart be established in Thy will, O Christ God, Who didst establish the second heaven above the waters, and didst found the earth upon the waters, O All-powerful One!

*Troparion:*
The vain-minded one hoped deceitfully to steal thee away by flatteries; but his empty reasoning was exposed, thou being established upon the rock of true faith, O martyr.

*Troparion:*
Having sailed over the abyss of wounds and bodily pains by the governance of Christ, O martyr Agathonicus, thou didst hasten rejoicing unto noetic havens.

*Troparion:*
"Let the cuttings and lacerations of my body come upon me today—swords and frying pans and fire, and wild beasts and death—I will not fear!" thou didst cry, O suffering-bearer Agathonicus.

*Theotokion:*
He Who is uncircumscribed by nature was fashioned of thee, having assumed our circumscription in the flesh, O Sovereign Lady, recognized in two natures and wills.[^777]

*Another Canon [of St. Luppus]. Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^778]
"As the barren one from the gentiles hath borne the Church, and the assembly that was rich in children hath grown weak, unto our marvelous God let us cry: Holy art Thou, O Lord!"

*Troparion:*
Established by the unconquerable power of the Spirit, thou didst manly go up unto the struggles, O martyr, and didst abolish the arrogance of deceit, O all-blessed Luppus.

*Troparion:*
Understanding thee to be a witness of Christ's sufferings, the fierce persecutor hoped by flatteries thy mind *(Fol. 123v)*

...hoped by flatteries to seduce thy mind, yet was the all-wicked one put to shame.

*Troparion:*
Like a lute struck by the all-effective Spirit, thou didst joyfully sound forth before tormentors the song of divine understanding, sweetening those who run to thee in faith.

*Theotokion:*
God dwelt in the midst of thee as He alone knows; He did not shake the key of virginity, O pure one, and He established all upon the immovable rock of faith.

*Kontakion of the Martyr [Agathonicus], Tone 1, Podoben: "The angelic choir" (Лик ангельский):*[^779]
Having acquired a good calling, O God-wise one, thou didst turn away from the faith of wicked men, not fearing torments, O glorious Agathonicus. Therefore thou hast become an heir of good things, and hast received worthy crowns with those who suffered with thee.

*Ikos:*
Entreat the Good One and Lover of mankind, O all-wise Agathonicus, to alleviate the affliction of my heart, and to grant me speech worthily to sing of thy struggles which thou didst suffer for the faith of Christ our God. How dost thou look everywhere as a shepherd, O martyr, and having driven away the wolves, didst guide thy flock unto the true land, crying with boldness: "We are accounted as sheep for the slaughter,[^780] therefore let us die, that we may receive incorruptible crowns!"

*Sessional Hymn [of St. Agathonicus], Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премудрости):*[^781]
Drowning the noetic Pharaoh with his warriors in the streams of thy blood, O glorious one, thou didst pass over unto the land where the meek and all the martyrs dwell, having received the crown of incorruption, O worthy of wonder! Therefore thou pourest forth healings unto the faithful, and calmest passions, and drivest away wicked spirits, O suffering-bearer Agathonicus. Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who lovingly honor thy holy memory.

*Glory... of Saint Luppus, Tone 1, Podoben: "Thy tomb" (Гроб твой):*[^782]
Enlightened by suffering like gold in a furnace, thou didst shine forth the dawn of healings, O wise one, and didst drive away the darkness of the demons by grace. Therefore we all celebrate thy most holy memory, O God-blessed Luppus, praise of martyrs!

*Both now... of the Feast, Tone the same [Tone 1], Podoben: "While the stone was sealed" (Камени запечатану):*[^783]
The choir of divine Apostles gathered together to bury thee most venerably, O all-honored Mother of God; and with them the angelic ranks sang of thy translation, crying out most praiseworthily. With them we also the faithful, skipping with love, O pure one, offer praise unto thee with singing voices, and we cry: Rejoice, O protectress of those who ever honor thee!

*Ode 4 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*

*(Fol. 124r)*

The speeches and dark sayings of the prophets clearly proclaimed Thine incarnation of the Virgin, O Christ: the radiance of Thy lightning shall go forth as a light unto the nations, and the deep shall cry unto Thee with gladness: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind![^784]

*Troparion:*
Behold, ye peoples, and wonder: for the holy mountain, manifestly belonging to God, is taken up higher into the heavenly mansions; the earthly heaven is dwelling in the heavenly and incorruptible tabernacle!

*Troparion:*
Thy death was a passing over unto a life everlasting and better, O pure one: translating thee from temporal life unto that which is truly divine and never-passing, in gladness to behold thy Son and Lord, O pure one!

*Troparion:*
The heavenly gates were lifted up, and the angels sang; and Christ received the vessel of His Mother's virginity: the Cherubim lifted thee up with gladness, and the Seraphim glorify thee rejoicing!

*Another Canon [of St. Agathonicus]. Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^785]
Foreseeing in spirit the incarnation of the Word, O prophet Habakkuk, thou didst preach, crying: When the years draw nigh, Thou shalt be known; when the time is come, Thou shalt be shown forth! Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst go away into the judgment hall of the tormentors, O wise one, brightly arming thyself with the weapon of divine faith; and wrestling with the enemy, having mightily conquered him, thou didst subdue him under thy feet, O martyr and suffering-bearer Agathonicus.

*Troparion:*
By the wisdom of thy words and the divine demonstrations of sacred deeds, thou didst frighten the enemies who hoped to conquer the patient endurance of thy mind and to draw thee unto deceit, O martyr of Christ, Agathonicus.

*Troparion:*
Having burnt thy mind as a whole burnt offering with the fire of divine love, O glorious one, thou didst not fear the fire of the adversary; but having kindled it like a coal, thou didst burn up the brushwood of godlessness, O sacred martyr Agathonicus.

*Theotokion:*
He Who rides upon the back of the Cherubim, O pure one, having been incarnate as man of thee in God-befitting manner: in thine embrace He sits as an infant and becomes a babe, saving the first-formed who was anciently foolish of mind.

*Another Canon [of St. Luppus]. Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^786]
He Who sits in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, Jesus the most divine, hath come upon a light cloud, with an incorruptible palm, and hath saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
Having surpassed human weaknesses and earthly bounds, O blessed one, thou didst endure wounds when beaten with rods, bearing the saving rod of power, the Cross of our God, O most glorious Luppus *(Fol. 124v)*

...bearing the saving rod of power, the Cross of our God, O most glorious Luppus.

*Troparion:*
Hoping like a mountain in the Lord,[^787] thou didst remain unshaken by divine help, O passion-bearer Luppus. Therefore thou didst endure the crushing of thy limbs, lifted up unto heaven by divine desire.

*Troparion:*
Being full of faith and wisdom and grace, in the midst of lawless men thou didst confess the Word of God; and thou didst not heed the shooting arrows, which were made powerless by the power of Christ to draw nigh unto thee.

*Theotokion:*
Upon thee like rain the Abyss of Wisdom, Jesus, came down, having found thee alone pure, O Virgin Mother of God; and He drowned the other streams of ungodliness by divine grace.

*Ode 5 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^788]
The divine and ineffable beauty of Thy virtues, O Christ, I will declare: for having shone forth from everlasting glory as co-eternal and hypostatic radiance, incarnate from a virginal womb, Thou hast shone forth as the Sun upon those in darkness and shadow!

*Troparion:*
As upon a cloud, O Virgin, the Apostolic choir carried unto Zion from the ends [of the earth] gathered to minister unto thee, the light cloud, from whom the Most High God, the righteous Sun, shone forth upon those in darkness and shadow.

*Troparion:*
More acceptable unto God than trumpets, the tongues of the God-proclaiming men most melodiously cried unto the Mother of God, sounding forth a funeral hymn in the Spirit: Rejoice, O incorruptible fountain of God's life-originating and saving incarnation for all!

*Another Canon [of St. Agathonicus]. Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^789]
A radiant light, everlasting, hath shone forth unto us who rise early in the morning concerning the judgments of Thy commandments, O Master, Lover of mankind, Christ our God!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst oppose the laws of the corrupter with saving laws, lawfully as a martyr, O glorious one, of the Lawgiver and Master of all, Christ our God.

*Troparion:*
Having become a bright and most glorious martyr, Agathonicus is now called blessed, brightly rejoicing ever with the heavenly powers.

*Troparion:*
Rather than to live, thou didst choose to die, O glorious one, so that thou mightest receive the life to come and acquire that which is eternal. Therefore thou didst bow thy neck unto the cutting sword.

*Theotokion:*
Foreseeing thy wonders by the divine Spirit, Isaiah cried aloud: For behold, in the womb thou shalt conceive the Uncontainable One, incarnate, O pure one![^790]

*Another Canon [of St. Luppus]. Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^791]
The ungodly shall not see Thy glory, O Christ; but we, rising early from the night, hymn Thee, O Only-begotten, Radiance of the Father's glory *(Fol. 125r)*

...radiance of the Godhead, rising early from the night, we hymn Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Even when the enemies threatened to cut off thy limbs, they rather cut down themselves, O wise one, by divine nods, while they beheld thee preserved uninjured and unwounded.

*Troparion:*
Having fixed the eye of thy soul upon the unshakable rock, O glorious one, thou wast in no wise shaken by the waves of bitter torments, O martyr, being strengthened by divine power.

*Troparion:*
By the sprinklings of thy blood thou didst quench the idolatrous burning, O suffering-bearer, and by the fire of thy sufferings thou didst burn up all deceit, being kindled by the Holy Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
With a pure mind let us honor the pure Beauty of Jacob,[^792] adorned with divine deeds, and let us piously sing of her as the Mother of our God.

*Ode 6 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^793]
The internal fire of the sea monster born of the deep was a prefiguration of Thy three-day burial, whereof the Prophet Jonah was shown forth; for saved as he was previously sent forth, unharmed he cried: I will sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of thanksgiving, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
Things beyond nature the King of all, God, grants unto thee: for as He preserved thee a Virgin in childbearing, so in the tomb He kept thy body incorrupt; and He glorified thee together by divine translation, bestowing honor upon thee as a Son unto a Mother.

*Troparion:*
Truly as a bright candlestick of the immaterial Light, a golden censer of the divine Coal, into the Holy of Holies He settled thee, O Virgin: the jar, and the rod, the God-inscribed tablet, the holy ark, the table of the Word of life, which thy childbirth is![^794]

*Another Canon [of St. Agathonicus]. Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^795]
Imitating the Prophet Jonah, I cry: Deliver my life from corruption, O Good One, and save me, O Savior of the world, who cry: Glory to Thee!

*Troparion:*
Alone, thou didst conquer a myriad-fold multitude of noetic enemies, O wise one, by the weakness of the flesh, and thou hast been united with myriads of sacred ministers.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst manly walk the path of martyrdom and didst narrow the paths of wickedness, O suffering-bearer, and didst reach the spacious heavenly dwellings.

*Troparion:*
Having been shown forth like gold in the furnace of cruel sufferings, O wise Agathonicus of Christ, Thou wast revealed as an image laid up in the divine treasures.

*Theotokion:*
Thou remainest a Virgin as before, even having given birth unto Christ, nourishing with milk Him Who gives food unto all, O pure one, most gloriously: a marvelous wonder and incomprehensible!

*(Fol. 125v)*

...incomprehensible!

*Another Canon [of St. Luppus]. Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^796]
Prefiguring the three-day burial, the Prophet Jonah cried, praying in the sea-monster: Deliver me from corruption, O Jesus, King of powers!

*Troparion:*
Firmly thou didst endure bodily torments, thou didst torment the godless reasonings of the lawless, and grievously wounded the multitudes of demons, O worthy of wonder!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst shake off like dust the temples of the godless;[^797] and thou didst raise up thyself as an ensouled pillar of piety and an image of manly courage, O God-inspired one.

*Troparion:*
The glorious passion-bearer rejoiced, nourished with torments, foreseeing in the Spirit crowns and eternal future glory for the martyrs.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst give birth unto Him Whom the Father begat before the ages, most without beginning; and with thy breasts thou didst nourish the Nourisher: a wonder beyond understanding, and a mystery beyond word, O pure one!

*Kontakion of the Feast, Tone 2:*[^798]
Neither the tomb nor death held fast the Mother of God, unsleeping in prayers and an unceasing hope in intercessions; for as the Mother of Life, He Who dwelt in her ever-virginal womb translated her unto life.

*Ikos:*
Surround my thoughts, O my Christ, for I dare to praise the rampart of the world, Thy pure Mother; establish me upon the pillar of words, and protect me in heavy thoughts: for Thou fulfillest the petitions of those who cry and ask in faith. Grant me then a fitting tongue and unashamed thoughts; for every bestowing of radiance is sent down from Thee, the Light-Giver, Who didst dwell in the ever-virginal womb!

*Ode 7 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^799]
Divine desire opposing shameless wrath and fire, bedewed the fire and laughed at wrath, resounding against the musical instruments in the midst of the flame with the God-inspired rational three-stringed lute of the holy ones: O exceedingly glorified God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Moses in anger shattered the God-fashioned tablets written by the divine Spirit; but their Master, having preserved unharmed in the heavenly dwellings her who gave Him birth, hath now settled her within. With her, skipping, let us cry unto Christ: O exceedingly glorified God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
On cymbals with pure lips, and musically with a radiant heart, with a sweet-sounding trumpet and lofty thought, on the notable and chosen day of the translation of the pure Virgin *(Fol. 124r)*

...translation, clapping our hands in active deeds, let us cry aloud: O exceedingly glorified God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Assemble, O God-wise people: for the dwelling-place of God's glory is translated from Zion unto the heavenly habitation, where there is the pure voice of those who keep festival,[^800] the voice of ineffable rejoicing, and of those who in gladness cry unto Christ: O exceedingly glorified God of our fathers and our God, blessed art Thou!

*Another Canon [of St. Agathonicus]. Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^801]
When Thy youths were in the furnace, O Savior, the fire touched them not, neither did it distress them; then the three, as with one mouth, sang and blessed, saying: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst become a partaker of heavenly glory, having passed by all things that are upon earth, and from the soul having loved the Master of heaven and earth, O glorious one; therefore we faithfully honor thy holy memory.

*Troparion:*
Following the passion of Christ, Who poured forth dispassion unto all the faithful, thou didst endure reproach and afflictions, O martyr, and an unjust death, when thy head was severed by the sword with joy.

*Troparion:*
In no wise didst thou render honor unto dead pagan temples, marveling at the immortal God, Who for our sake appeared dead and put to death the power of the deceitful one; therefore thou didst obtain the true life, O wise martyr.

*Theotokion:*
God became man from thee, O pure one, deifying humanity without confusion,[^802] and making them partakers of the divine nature[^803] who bless thee, O pure one, truly blessed among women!

*Another Canon [of St. Luppus]. Ode 7. Heirmos:*[^804]
Thou Who didst save Thy Abrahamic youths in the fire and didst slay the Chaldeans whom justice justly ensnared: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Enlightened by the beauties of beneficent sufferings, thou standest now rejoicing before Him Who for our sake endured the Passion upon the Cross, O glorious one, being illumined by divine outpourings of light.

*Troparion:*
From heaven water was manifestly poured out upon thee most gloriously,[^805] enlightening and strengthening thy soul, commanding thee to cry: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stand in the midst of enemies like an immovable pillar, with an unshaken mind, against the snares of manifold torments, O glorious passion-bearer, magnanimously destroying deceit.

*Theotokion:*
The only One Who lives in the heavens chose thee alone, the Beauty of Jacob;[^806] and having settled in thy midst, O pure one, He in no wise shook *(Fol. 124v)*

...the keys of thy virginity, O blessed one!

*Ode 8 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^807]
The almighty Angel of God showed forth unto the youths a flame that bedewed the holy ones, but burned up the ungodly; and He made the Mother of God a life-originating spring, gushing forth the destruction of death and life unto those who sing: We the redeemed hymn the only Creator, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
The whole multitude of theologians in Zion followed with words after the sacred divine Ark, crying aloud: Whither goest thou now, O tabernacle of the living God? Cease not looking down upon those who in faith sing: We the redeemed hymn the only Creator, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Even as thou didst lift up thy hands when departing, O all-immaculate one—hands wherewith thou didst bear God in the flesh—with boldness as a mother thou didst say unto the One born of thee: Keep unto the ages those whom Thou hast given me, who cry unto Thee: We the redeemed hymn the only Creator, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Another Canon [of St. Agathonicus]. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^808]
Him Whom the Angels and all the hosts dread as Creator and Lord, hymn, ye priests, glorify, ye youths, bless, ye peoples, and highly exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Dragged from place to place, O passion-bearer, thou didst sanctify the earth with thy footsteps; and having bowed thy neck unto the cutting sword, thou wast parted from the flesh and united unto God.

*Troparion:*
All scourgings and bodily torments thou didst despise, being consumed with divine desire; and having finished thy good fight,[^809] thou wast joyfully joined unto the hosts on high.

*Troparion:*
The power of the bodiless enemy was cast down: by bodily weakness the martyrs of Christ conquered him, being strengthened by the supreme power of the Master.

*Theotokion:*
As tabernacle and table, and divine ark, as jar containing the manna of life, and holy mountain, we name thee, O Virgin Mary, ever-blessed!

*Another Canon [of St. Luppus]. Ode 8. Heirmos:*[^810]
O almighty Redeemer of all, having descended, Thou didst bedew those who acted piously in the midst of the flame, and didst teach them to sing: Bless the Lord, all ye works, hymn the Lord!

*Troparion:*
After many torments, O blessed one, thou didst bow thy neck unto God; for being beheaded with the sword, thou didst cut off the heads of the lawless enemies by faith, as into terror.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst give thyself blamelessly unto voluntary slaughter as a lamb, imitating the Lamb of God Who takes away the sins of the world, neither crying out nor lifting up His voice.[^811]

*(Fol. 125r)*

...Thy shrine hath become a healing of all manner of diseases and an unexpected physician of souls; for it hath been enriched by God with the grace of healings, O all-praised Luppus!

*Theotokion:*
All creation blesses thy childbirth, O Virgin, which hath crowned us with blessings and taken away the curse, O all-blessed and only most-glorified one, who hast bestowed grace upon our race!

*Ode 9 [of the Feast]. Heirmos:*[^812]
The laws of nature are overcome in thee, O pure Virgin: for childbearing remains virginal, and death is betrothed unto life; virgin after childbirth, and alive after death, thou savest ever thine inheritance, O Mother of God!

*Troparion:*
The angelic powers marveled in Zion, beholding their Master carrying a woman's soul in His hands; for unto her who bore Him most purely He proclaimed in a manner befitting a Son: Come, O pure one, be glorified together with Thy Son and God!

*Troparion:*
The apostolic choir shrouded thy God-receiving body, looking on with fear and speaking with a great voice: As thou goest up into the heavenly mansions unto thy Son, thou savest ever thine inheritance, O Mother of God!

*Another Canon [of St. Agathonicus]. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^813]
The light-bearing cloud, whereon the Master of all descended from heaven like rain upon the fleece, and was incarnate for our sake, becoming man without beginning: we all magnify as the pure Mother of our God!

*Troparion:*
In the holy choirs of the sacred Apostles, and in the courts of the passion-bearers, and in the flocks of the righteous, and numbered with the Prophets, O wise Agathonicus: rejoicing with them, as a crown-bearer, thou standest now brightly before the Trinity.

*Troparion:*
How marvelous is thy zeal, how firm is thy faith, how good is thy hope toward God, O martyr, how radiant is thy life, how filled with grace was thy death: it made thee a partaker of immortal glory, O passion-bearer!

*Troparion:*
Thee, the lamb of Christ, the sacred slaughter, the beauty of the passion-bearers, the fountain of wonders, the fair adornment of the Church, O God-wise one, the confirmation of the faithful, we worthily praise, O wise Agathonicus!

*Troparion:*
Thy sacred memory, sanctifying the world, hath now come unto us, O all-glorious martyr, in which we pray to obtain thy protection, honoring thee with love, O Agathonicus, as a servant of the good Word.

*Theotokion:*
Having a sin-loving disposition, an uncorrected life, a sinful soul, and a defiled heart, prodigal that I am, I fall down before thee, O Mother of God: help me, and grant me amendment *(Fol. 124v)*

...grant me, before the cutting stroke of death overtaks me!

*Another Canon [of St. Luppus]. Ode 9. Heirmos:*[^814]
Eve indeed, through the disease of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Mother of God, through the budding of the fruit of the womb, didst blossom forth blessing unto the world: therefore we all magnify thee!

*Troparion:*
By divine power thou wast able, O most glorious one, while in the flesh to trample upon the ancient serpent, and thou wast crowned with a crown of victory; therefore, rejoicing with the martyrs, thou dwellest in the heavens.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear as a most radiant star, heralding the Sun unto those sitting in the darkness of destruction, O martyr; and illumined thereby, they were shown forth by faith to be partakers of the never-setting Day, O worthy of glory!

*Troparion:*
Today thy memory hath shone forth unto us brighter than the sun, enlightening all the earth, driving away the clouds from souls and taking away the gloom of passions, which we prayerfully celebrate.

*Troparion:*
The heavenly Zion, the city of the elect, bears thy spirit rejoicing; the Church of the firstborn is filled with awe; and we the faithful honor thee, asking to be saved through thy prayers, O God-wise and all-blessed Luppus!

*Theotokion:*
With the beams of the Light that is in thee, O Bride of God, enlighten my soul; raise it up as it lies in the pit of destruction, casting down the enemies that ever afflict my heart and urge me unto passions!

*Exapostilarion:*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "In the spirit in the sanctuary" (Дꙋ́хомъ во ст҃и́лищи):*[^815]
As namesake of good things and of victory together,[^816] entreat the good and merciful God, O martyr Agathonicus, that I, and those who faithfully hymn thy bright and praiseworthy memory, may be partakers of eternal good things!

*Glory, and now, of the Feast, Podoben [the same]:*[^817]
Assemble, O Apostles, to bury the body of the only Bride of God and Mother of the Lord! Rejoice now, all the town of Gethsemane, and sing burial hymns unto her who hath flown up unto the heavens and dances with the angels in unending glory!

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Feast, Tone 2, Podoben: "O house of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*[^818]
The divine Apostles, escorting thy precious and God-receiving body, cried aloud: Whither goest thou now, O Mother of God?

*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest: Thou, and the ark of Thy holiness.*[^819]
Come, ye earthborn, let us form a choir, crying aloud funeral hymns today at the translation of the Mother of God!

*Verse: The Lord hath sworn in truth unto David, and He will not turn from it.*[^820]

*(Fol. 126r)*

*Sticheron:*
The earth indeed was blessed by thy burial, O Virgin, and the air was sanctified by thy marvelous ascension, dying as thou didst by the law of nature!

*Glory, and now, Podoben the same (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
All ye earthborn, let us eagerly hasten together with the bodiless hosts to bury her who bore the Creator of creation!

*At the Hours we say the Kontakion of the Feast.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Feast, Ode 8 of both Canons with the Heirmos, to 6.*

*In the same month, on the 23rd day.*
*The Holy Martyr Luppus.*[^821]
*On this day the Feast of the Dormition of the Most Holy Mother of God is taken leave of.*
*And the entire service of the Feast is sung.*

*At Vespers, the customary Kathisma. At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Feast, to 6; Glory, and now, of the Feast. If on Friday evening: Glory of the Feast; and now, the Dogmatikon of the current Tone. Prokeimenon of the day, without Entrance or Old Testament readings. And immediately: Vouchsafe, O Lord; the litany: Let us complete our evening prayer. At the Aposticha, Stichera, and Glory, and now, of the Feast. After the Our Father, the Troparion of the Feast, and the dismissal. At Compline, the Kontakion of the Feast. At the Midnight Office we sing as customary with the Kathisma and the customary troparia, and not those of the Feast; and we say the prayer: Remember, O Lord. At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Troparion of the Feast, twice; Glory, and now, the same. After the Kathismata, the Sessional Hymns of the Feast; and reading, and Psalm 50. Canons of the Feast: the first with the Heirmos to 8; and the second with the Heirmos to 6. And we sing the Heirmoi of both Canons twice. Katavasia: "Adorned with divine glory..."[^822] After Ode 3, the Hypakoe of the Feast. After Ode 6, the Kontakion and Ikos of the Feast. At Ode 9 we do not sing "More honorable," but we sing the Refrains of the Feast, just as on the Feast itself. Exapostilarion of the Feast, twice. At the Praises, Stichera of the Feast, to 4; Glory, and now, of the Feast. Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, the Troparion of the Feast, the augmented litany, and the dismissal; and the procession into the narthex; and the First Hour. At the Hours, the Troparion and Kontakion of the Feast.*

*(Fol. 126v)*

*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of both Canons, Ode 4, to 8. After the Entrance, the Troparion of the Feast; Glory, and now, the Kontakion of the Feast. Prokeimenon, Alleluia, and Communion verse of the Feast. Epistle and Gospel of the day, and of the Feast.*

*It should be known if the Apodosis of the Dormition of the Mother of God falls on Sunday:*[^823]
*On Saturday at Small Vespers, the Resurrection Stichera according to custom. At the Aposticha, one Resurrection Sticheron, and the Aposticha of the Feast, with its own refrains, from Great Vespers; Glory, and now, of the Feast; the Resurrection Troparion; Glory, and now, of the Feast.*
*At Great Vespers, after the customary Kathisma, at "Lord, I have cried," Resurrection Stichera, 3, and one Anatolian; and 6 of the Feast, sung on the Feast itself; Glory of the Feast; and now, the first Theotokion of the Tone. At the Litiya, Stichera, and Glory, and now, of the Feast. At the Aposticha, Resurrection Stichera; Glory, and now, of the Feast. At the blessing of the loaves, the Troparion: "O Theotokos and Virgin," twice; and of the Feast, once; and a reading from the apostolic Epistles.*
*At Matins, at "God is the Lord," the Resurrection Troparion, twice; Glory, and now, of the Feast. After the Kathismata, the Resurrection Sessional Hymns with their Theotokia, and a reading from the Commentary Gospel. And the Polyeleos [Neporochny], and the Hypakoe of the Tone; and a reading. The Hymns of Ascents, and the Prokeimenon of the Tone; "Let every breath praise the Lord"; the Resurrection Gospel; "Having beheld the Resurrection of Christ"; Psalm 50; the Resurrection Sticheron; and the rest according to custom. Canon of the Resurrection with the Heirmos, to 4; and of the Mother of God, to 2; and of the Feast, both Canons, to 8; Katavasia: "Adorned with divine glory..."
