After Ode 3, the Kontakion of the Feast and Ikos; the Hypakoe of the Feast. After Ode 6, the Resurrection Kontakion and Ikos, and the Prologue. At Ode 9 we sing "More honorable." Resurrection Exapostilarion; Glory, and now, of the Feast. At the Praises, Resurrection Stichera, to 4, and of the Feast, to 4, with the Doxastikon, with the refrains of the Feast; Glory, the Holy Gospel Sticheron; and now, "Most blessed art Thou." Great Doxology; after the Trisagion, the Resurrection Troparion only; litanies, and dismissal; and the Catechesis of St. Theodore the Studite; and the customary procession into the narthex; and the First Hour; and the final dismissal.*
*At the Hours, the Resurrection Troparion; Glory of the Feast; and now, the Theotokion of the Hours. After the Our Father, the Kontakion of the Feast and the Resurrection Kontakion, saying them alternately.*
*At the Liturgy, the Beatitudes of the Tone, to 6; and of the Feast, Ode 4, to 4. After the Entrance, the Resurrection Troparion, and of the Feast; Glory, the Resurrection Kontakion; and now, of the Feast. Prokeimenon, Epistle, Alleluia, and Gospel of the day, and of the Feast. Communion verse: "Praise the Lord from the heavens," and of the Feast.*

*(Fol. 127r)*

*In the same month, on the 24th day.*
*The Holy Hieromartyr Eutyches, disciple of the Holy Apostle John the Theologian:*[^824]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ!):*[^825]
O blessed martyr Eutyches, having been sacredly taught the word by the honored disciple, the blessed preacher,[^826] from him thou didst learn all sacred teaching, and didst appear as a light, illuminating every mind with grace by thy sacred theologies; therefore we honor thy holy memory!

Thou didst cast down the stronghold of deceit by the firm struggle of endurance, O exceedingly rich one, by bonds and wounds and a glorious death; therefore thou didst obtain immortality, dwelling with the choirs of the martyrs and the angelic ranks, partaking of better things in a manner befitting God, well deified and completely!

Shut up in prison, thou didst receive the Bread of life from heaven, O glorious one; and cast into the flame, thou didst remain unconsumed; cruelly scraped, thou didst endure wounds; by prayers thou didst tame the onrushes of wild beasts; thy head was severed by the sword, and thou wast borne up unto heaven by thy blood as by a divine chariot!

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Save me, O all-pure Lady, who didst ineffably give birth unto the Savior Christ: for thee alone have I acquired as intercessor, an impregnable rampart, a protection, and rejoicing, and the divine consolation of my soul; do thou therefore deliver me from the unsleeping worm and the eternal fire,[^827] O Mother of Christ God!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
What is this visible sight which is seen with mine eyes, O Master Who holdest all creation? Being lifted up upon the Tree, dost Thou die, Who givest life unto all? weeping, the Mother of God said, when she beheld Him lifted up upon the Cross Who had ineffably shone forth from her, God and man!

*At the Aposticha, of the Octoechos.*
*Troparion, Tone 4:*[^828]
Having become a sharer in customs and successor upon the thrones of the Apostles, thou didst find action as an ascent unto contemplation, O God-inspired one; therefore, rightly dividing the word of truth *(Fol. 127v)*

...and having suffered for the faith even unto blood, O hieromartyr Eutyches, entreat Christ God to save our souls!

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos, and of the Saint to 4. Composition of Joseph. Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*[^829]
The wonderworking rod of Moses, striking crosswise and dividing the sea, once submerged the chariot-driving Pharaoh; but it saved Israel, the fleeing pedestrian, as they sang a song unto God!

*Troparion:*
Shown forth as a companion of the Apostles and martyrs, and ever filled with divine radiance: show forth those who celebrate thy honored feast today to be partakers of the Light, O martyr Eutyches, worthy of glory!

*Troparion:*
Having piously become a disciple of the Beloved Disciple, O blessed one, and closely imitating his ways, thou didst love the Lord, having fulfilled His divine laws; and thou wast made conformable unto His sufferings,[^830] O Eutyches!

*Troparion:*
Piously obeying the divine law, thou didst mortify the flesh through fasting labors; having departed from the world and dwelling in the mountains, O glorious martyr, purifying thy soul, thou wast known as a beholder of God.

*Theotokion:*
Upon thee, O Virgin, have I set the hope of my salvation: wash me clean from all defilement of sin, and make me pure unto thy Son, excellently pleasing Him and doing His all-holy will!

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*[^831]
Thou Who in the beginning didst establish the heavens with understanding and founded the earth upon the waters: establish me, O Christ, upon the rock of Thy commandments, for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Finding Paul shining with the light of all-wise preaching like the sun, thou didst walk with him,[^832] enlightening hearts like a great star, O blessed and God-wise one, through the Spirit!

*Troparion:*
The foundation of theologians, the crown of the disciples, John, the true friend of the Savior, being divinely translated from the earth, delivered the flock of God unto thee to shepherd, O blessed one!

*Troparion:*
Fair and radiant and filled with the divine Spirit, thou didst stand before him that judged unjustly, rebuking his ungodliness, O exceedingly rich one, and confessing the divine incarnation of Christ!

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, thou who alone didst ineffably give birth unto Joy! Rejoice, chariot and cloud of the Word! Rejoice, Mother of God, Virgin who hast not known wedlock! Rejoice, glory of the righteous! Rejoice, crowning of the martyrs!

*(Fol. 128r)*

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*[^833]
Having shone forth with the radiance of divine knowledge, thou didst destroy the darkness of polytheism, having finished thy course as a martyr; and thou pourest forth the gifts of healings, and cleansest the diseases of those who come unto thee, O glorious martyr: entreat Christ God to grant our souls great mercy!

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Thou wast the divine tabernacle of the Word, O only all-pure Virgin Mother, who in purity didst surpass the angels: me, who beyond all have become dust, defiled with bodily transgressions, cleanse with the divine waters of thy prayers, bestowing great mercy, O pure one!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled ewe-lamb of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding hanging upon the Cross Him Who had budded forth from her without travails, maternally lamenting as was meet, cried aloud: Woe is me, O my Child! How sufferest Thou willingly, desiring to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^834]
Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who didst not leave the bosom of the Father and didst visit our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Having departed unto the Lord with all thy mind, thou didst feel nothing when beaten and cruelly torn on every side; and like a fragrant censer, O worthy of glory, thou didst fill those standing by with divine fragrance by the operation of the Holy Spirit, O God-wise one!

*Troparion:*
Preserved by God, O God-wise martyr, and sitting bound in prison, thou didst visibly receive heavenly bread with joy, God the only Arbitrator of the contest strengthening thee, O God-bearing Eutyches, whose divine Passion thou didst emulate!

*Troparion:*
Having opened thy honored mouth in the Spirit, standing before the tribunal of the persecutors, O all-wise one, thou didst explain the true ancestral faith unto them, and didst refute the idolatrous deceit and the reasonings of the godless persecutors, O blessed one!

*Troparion:*
After many other bodily wounds, being kindled with divine love, O blessed one, thou didst joyfully pass through the ignited flame of fire threatened by the torturer, and wast in no wise scorched; for divine dew from heaven bedewed thee, O Eutyches!

*Theotokion:*
Among women thou alone remainedst a Virgin both before childbearing and after childbearing; for it was God Who renews the laws of nature beyond word, Who from thee ineffably *(Fol. 128v)*

...is ineffably incarnate, Who was on high before the ages and was born upon earth in the latter years, O all-immaculate one!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^835]
Therefore hast Thou cast me off from Thy face, O never-setting Light, and an alien darkness hath covered me, wretch that I am? But convert me, and unto the light of Thy commandments direct my ways, I pray Thee!

*Troparion:*
Having become rich in the divine word, thou didst give unto all in need, O martyr; and having become a priest, thou didst shepherd the flock of Christ, comforting, teaching, and guiding unto salvation, O much-suffering passion-bearer Eutyches!

*Troparion:*
Having shaken off soul-destroying deceit by the power of the divine Spirit, thou didst draw near unto Christ, and unto Him didst bring multitudes of those saved through faith, O martyr, by thine honored divine proclamations, O Eutyches, adornment of the sufferers!

*Troparion:*
How beautiful are thy feet, O all-blessed one,[^836] which walked upon the path of the divine testimonies, and stepped well therein, and reached the vast expanse of the heavenly Kingdom, O glorious martyr!

*Theotokion:*
O Virgin Maiden Mother, who didst bear the Incomprehensible One in the flesh: glory of angels, and wound of demons, O Bride of God! Vouchsafe divine healing unto my heart wounded with sins!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*[^837]
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities, and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee; for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation!

*Troparion:*
Having received in Ephesus the sentence of death for Christ, thou didst set out as a prisoner from Ephesus, O wise one, and in thine honored homeland, adorned with sufferings, thou didst fall asleep.[^838]

*Troparion:*
Being a fair and all-pure temple of God, by grace thou didst cast down the defiled idolatrous temples, and hast been taken up unto the heavenly temple, O Eutyches, to stand before God!

*Troparion:*
A divine Angel strengthened thee as thou didst walk on the way, and manifestly foretold unto thee what was about to come to pass, O exceedingly rich Eutyches, who wast equal to the angels and outside the flesh in the Spirit!

*Theotokion:*
The perversions of my mind and the passionate, cruel stirrings of my soul, do thou correct, O Virgin; and keep me whole from those that ever unmercifully assail me!

*Kontakion, Tone 3, Podoben: "Today the Virgin" (Дв҃а дне́сь):*[^839]
Having become an enthroned fellow of the Apostles and the beauty of hierarchs, O Eutyches, thou wast glorified as a martyr; thou didst blossom forth like the sun, enlightening all, and didst dispel the deep night of godlessness!

*(Fol. 129r [marked 124])*

...therefore we honor thee as truly a divine hieromystic of Christ.

*Ikos:*
As a priest of God, as a heavenly minister, as a shepherd of shepherds, as an unconquered sufferer: dwelling now with the choirs on high, O hieromystic, send down light unto me and breathe into me a word, to praise thy divine sufferings, and thy persecutions and struggles, eminent and courageous, the crushing of thy bones, the severing of thy limbs, for the sake of which thou wast numbered among the angels, O blessed one, as truly a divine hieromystic of Christ.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The fire was put to shame in Babylon of old at the condescension of God; therefore the children in the furnace, dancing with joyful foot as in a flowery meadow, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
By the nod of God the fire was extinguished, which was kindled for thy destruction, O God-wise one; for thunder was heard from heaven, and snowy drops fell, covering those who knew not Christ.

*Troparion:*
Being fiercely scraped, thou didst pour forth the myrrh of sweet fragrance, O martyr, unto the fragrance of Him Who poured Himself out in His passions; for having run unto the Word and Creator, unto Him thou didst cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Thy life was blameless, thy suffering most marvelous, O wise one, and saving unto all who approach the holy shrine of thy relics, which pours forth healings and delivers from the defilements of many passions.

*Theotokion:*
Understanding from afar the unutterable depth of thy mystery, O Virgin, the venerable prophets in manifold ways made plain thy childbearing unto all, crying: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold the Chaldean tormentor furiously kindled the furnace for the godly; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Stretching out thy hands, O glorious one, unto Christ our God, contending with lions in the midst of the tribunal, thou didst remain unharmed, even as the Prophet Daniel of old, having an angel ever keeping thee, and singing unceasingly: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
A beast,[^840] seeing thee condemned by the lawless—a wild beast prepared for thy destruction—clearly spoke by almighty bidding, and proclaimed the majesty of Christ the Savior, astounding those who cried *(Fol. 129v)*

...unto them: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Having passed through the great contest of martyrdom by the power and grace of God, rejoicing Thou didst obtain the crowns of victory, and wast numbered among the ranks of all the apostles and martyrs, with whom Thou dost cry unceasingly: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate one, who alone didst reveal and give birth unto Life, enliven my soul which is slain by the biting of the serpent, and make me worthy to do the will of Him Who was born of thee for our sake, O Virgin, as I cry: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Heaven was dismayed at this, and the ends of the earth were amazed, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee, O Theotokos!

*Troparion:*
Thou pourest forth diverse healings and soothest grievous diseases, having truly received grace from Christ: having fought the good fight and conquered the enemies, O God-wise passion-bearer, fellow-citizen of the angels and crown of the martyrs!

*Troparion:*
Fair didst thou appear in thy struggles, and into fair bridal chambers didst thou enter to dwell, having been beheaded with the sword on thy holy head, which Jesus crowned with a beauteous crown of victory, O luminary of the Church, foundation and praise of those who piously call thee blessed!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst crush the dominion of the evil one, O most lauded passion-bearer Eutyches, and didst set up a trophy of victory over him with youthful courage; and now thou art a pre-eminent victor in the heavens, standing brightly before the Trinitarian throne with great boldness.

*Troparion:*
Thy divine and radiant memory hath shone forth upon all more brightly than the sun, enlightening with bright beams those who ever faithfully and piously celebrate it, O Eutyches; wherein we beseech thee: as thou hast boldness, entreat salvation for us!

*Theotokion:*
We proclaim unto thee Gabriel's greeting: Rejoice, O Maiden, chosen earth! Rejoice, O golden table! Rejoice, refuge of men! Rejoice, praise of martyrs![^841] Rejoice, joy of angels, O holy Theotokos, bulwark of those who with pure faith call thee blessed!

*Exapostilarion, Tone 2, Podoben: "Ye women, hear" (Жены оуслышите):*
Having become a successor upon the thrones and a partaker of the ways of the sacred Apostles, O God-wise one: send down [grace] from heaven unto those who celebrate thy holy suffering in faith, as a disciple of the Word *(Fol. 130r [marked 125])*

...O hieromartyr Eutyches!

*Theotokion:*
Look down, O pure one, upon thine inheritance, and preserve it by thine unconquerable protection: direct and establish the sceptres of kingdoms, subdue the heathen nations, and pour out peace unto the ends of the world!

*In the same month, on the 25th day.*[^842]
*The Return of the Relics of the Holy Apostle Bartholomew; and the Memory of the Holy Apostle Titus.*

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Holy Apostle Bartholomew, 3, Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ́кѡ до́бля):*
Let us diligently call blessed the glorious Bartholomew: an unsetting star of the most radiant Sun, an ensouled heaven declaring the saving glory of God, a divine preacher, a luminary of the nations, a river pouring forth a stream of understanding, and watering the hearts of the faithful!

Thy goings appeared in the sea, O Apostle, being revealed beyond human understanding: for being cast in with thy shrine, thou didst hasten toward the west, from the east, with glorious martyrs[^843] following thee on both sides and rendering honor unto thee, by the commands of the Lord of all, O Bartholomew most excellent!

By marvelous ascents of the waters thou didst sanctify them, and didst reach the island of Lipari, pouring forth myrrh, O glorious one, and healing incurable passions; and thou didst become salvation and a refuge, an intercessor and deliverer unto those who dwell there, before the King and Savior of all, O Bartholomew, Apostle!

*Other Stichera of the Holy Titus, 3, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast given a sign" (Да́лъ есѝ зна́меніе):*
Preaching the one tri-hypostatic Godhead, thou didst stir up the polytheistic sea of the nations; and unto calm harbors, O blessed one, thou didst lead up those engulfed by the deep of godlessness; therefore thou didst receive the heavenly reward, entreating the Lover of mankind for those who hymn thee.

Having illumined thy mind with divine radiances, like a ray thou didst journey with the sun, illuminating the darkened together with Paul, O divine Titus; and with him [thou didst deliver] the whole earth from the deep night *(Fol. 130v)*

...night thou didst deliver [them]; therefore we call thee blessed as a God-pleasing hierarch, as a divine apostle, as a fervent intercessor.

Hastening unto Crete, and returning into it as unto an unshakable foundation whereon the right faith was established, building up the faithful by his building, by a certain divine power, the blessed Titus, disciple and foremost enthroned hierarch, became a companion unto Paul, and the consolation of the faithful.

*Glory, Tone 2:*
Having left earthly things, thou didst follow Christ, and having been sealed by the inspiration of the Holy Spirit, thou wast sent by Him unto the lost nations, to turn men unto the light of the knowledge of God, O Apostle Bartholomew; and having finished the contests of thy divine passions and diverse torments, thou didst commit thy soul unto Christ: entreat Him, O all-blessed one, to grant our souls great mercy!

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding Thee nailed unto the Tree of the Cross, O Jesus, she that knew not wedlock weeping said: O sweet Child, why hast Thou left me alone who gave birth unto Thee, O unsearchable Light of the unoriginate Father? Make haste and be glorified, that they may receive divine glory who glorify Thy divine passions!

*Troparion [of the Apostles], Tone 3:*
O holy Apostles, entreat the merciful God, that He grant remission of transgressions unto our souls!

*At Matins, one Canon of the Octoechos, and of the Saints two, to 8. Canon of the Holy Apostle Bartholomew, the acrostic whereof is: "I honor with songs my good protector."[^844] Composition of Joseph. Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
I shall open my mouth, and it will be filled with the Spirit, and I shall utter a word unto the Queen Mother; and I shall be seen radiantly keeping festival, and rejoicing I shall sing her wonders!

*Troparion:*
The Rock of life showed thee forth as an honorable stone, whereon He built His Church, O Bartholomew, Apostle of the Lord; therefore we honor thee and call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast sacredly taught great things beyond mind and understanding, having found the hypostatic Wisdom; thou didst cut short the wisdom of the Hellenes, making wise the ends of the earth with divine teachings.

*Troparion:*
Though a buried corpse, thou didst become a traveler upon the sea, obeying the divine commandment; and like the sun, O Apostle, thou didst arrive from the east unto the west, enlightening it.

*Theotokion:*
He Who is full empties Himself, the Pre-eternal One begins, being born of the Virgin Maiden of God *(Fol. 131r)*

...Whom thou didst preach as existing in two natures, yet in one Hypostasis, O God-blessed one!

*Another Canon of the Holy Apostle Titus, the acrostic whereof is: "I wisely sing of Paul's disciple Titus."[^845] Composition of Theophanes. Same Tone.*
*Ode 1. Same Heirmos:*
Having enriched thee with such spiritual gifts, the grace of the Spirit sent thee forth as a divine preacher and Apostle unto the ends of the earth, to preach the incarnation of the Word, O most glorious Titus!

Having received the radiance of divine grace, thou didst shine forth like the sun, O all-wise one, upon the souls that were in darkness, which thou didst enlighten, removing the ignorance of deceit and delivering them from servitude, O blessed one.

The all-wise Paul, a son filled with spiritual gifts, laid his hands upon thee; and he appointed thee as a minister, a divine disciple, and a preacher of the faith, O most marvelous Titus.

*Theotokion:*
Delivering the ends of the world from deceit, the Lord shone forth from thy womb, O Bride of God: Him do thou ever entreat to save those who honor thee in faith, O Virgin!

*Ode 3 [of St. Bartholomew]. Heirmos:*
Establish thy choir-singers, O Theotokos, thou living and plentiful fountain, who have gathered a spiritual company; and in thy divine glory vouchsafe unto them crowns of glory!

*Troparion:*
They who were held fast by deceit and mentally harmed by being enveloped in the darkness of godlessness found thee to be a strong consolation, a light, and deliverance from cruel things, O all-wise Bartholomew.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast the mouth of God the Word; from the mouth of the deceitful wolf thou didst deliver the people, O glorious one, leading them unto thy Creator through the font of baptism, O Apostle Bartholomew.

*Troparion:*
Having consumed the bitter matter of deceit with thy fiery tongue, O all-wise one, thou didst renew hearts that had grown cold, and didst prepare them to bring forth heavenly understanding.

*Theotokion:*
Beyond understanding, God the Word was pleased to be born of thee in the flesh, O pure Virgin Mother, choosing the glorious Bartholomew as His disciple and constant minister.

*Another [Canon of St. Titus]. Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Not in wisdom, nor in power, nor in wealth do we boast, but in Thee, the hypostatic Wisdom of the Father, O Christ; for there is none holy save Thee, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Not with foolish wisdom for those who gave heed to it, but with the understanding of the truth, O blessed one, thou didst dispel the blindness of the souls of those who fled unto thee *(Fol. 131v)*

The furrow of the inheritance of the nations, grown cold and overgrown with the thorns of godlessness,[^846] thou didst clear with a divine sickle, having sown the seeds of understanding.

Preaching one Godhead in three Persons, O blessed one, thou didst dispel the gloom of polytheism, bringing the ends of the earth unto divine understanding.

*Theotokion:*
He Who was born of thee in the flesh made thee holier than the angels and higher than all creation; therefore we honor thee as the Sovereign Lady of all.

*Kontakion of St. Titus, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ ищѧ̀):*
Having been shown forth as a companion of Paul, O Apostle, with him thou didst proclaim the Word unto us, O hieromystic of divine grace, Titus ever-memorable; therefore we cry unto thee: Cease not praying for us all!

*Sessional Hymn of St. Bartholomew, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of the divine faith" (Бж҃твенныя вѣ́ры):*
Thou wast shown forth as an unstolen treasure of the Church and an unshakable pillar of piety, O Apostle Bartholomew; and making a path in the sea, thou pourest forth a deep of divine healings, O worthy of glory: entreat Christ God to grant us great mercy!

*Glory, of St. Titus, Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премꙋ́дрости):*
Even as a star with the sun, thou didst walk, O most glorious Titus; with Paul thou didst manifestly enlighten the earth and destroy the darkness; and having set by death on the island of Crete, O blessed one, thou didst leave unto all as unsetting rays, O God-wise one, thy labors and words: enlightened whereby, we piously honor thee and with one voice cry unto thee: Entreat Christ God to grant remission of sins unto those who lovingly honor thy holy memory!

*Now and ever, Theotokion:*
As the all-immaculate bride of the Creator, as the mother of the Deliverer that knew not man, as the vessel of the Comforter, O all-hymned one: me, who through iniquity am a defiled dwelling and have deliberately become a plaything of demons, make haste to deliver from their malice, and make me a bright habitation of virtues, O radiant and incorrupt one; drive away the cloud of passions, and make me worthy of communion on high and of the unsetting Light, by thy prayers!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
Beholding the Lamb, Shepherd, and Deliverer upon the Cross, the Ewe-lamb cried out weeping, and bitterly lamenting exclaimed: The world indeed rejoices, receiving deliverance through Thee; but mine inward parts *(Fol. 132r)*

...burn, beholding Thy crucifixion, which Thou didst endure for the tender compassion of Thy mercy, O longsuffering Lord! O abyss of mercy and inexhaustible fountain, have compassion and grant remission of transgressions unto those who sing of Thy divine passions in faith!

*Ode 4 [of St. Bartholomew]. Heirmos:*
Perceiving the unsearchable counsel of God, the incarnation from the Virgin of Thee, the Most High, the Prophet Habakkuk cried: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!

*Troparion:*
The prophetic sayings have received their fulfillment: for as a light for the preservation of the nations, the God-speaking disciple went forth, enlightening all the ends of the universe.

*Troparion:*
Enlightened by the thearchic radiance through divine communion, thou didst appear as a second light, having served the First Cause Who for our sake impoverished Himself in the flesh, O glorious one.

*Troparion:*
Deliver thy flock from the sinful tempest, and perils, and adverse afflictions, ever guiding us by grace unto saving harbors, O glorious one!

*Theotokion:*
Having ineffably given birth unto ineffable, surpassing wealth, O pure Mary, enrich with divine gifts us who have become impoverished through sin!

*Another [Canon of St. Titus]. Ode 4. Heirmos:*[^847]
Sitting in glory upon the throne of the Godhead, upon a light cloud, Jesus the most divine came with incorrupt palm, and saved those who cry: Glory to Thy power, O Christ!

*Troparion:*
Drawing from inexhaustible treasures, O all-wise one, thou didst satisfy minds that thirsted for true wealth, and didst enliven slain hearts, driving away the gloom of senselessness, O all-glorious one.

*Troparion:*
The net of Paul, by the providence of grace, caught thee, O Titus, who afterward wast zealously to draw forth many nations with the fishhook of the word, from the deceitful deep unto piety.

*Troparion:*
Following the divine Paul as a lamb follows the chief shepherd, O God-proclaimed voice, thou didst drive away the grievous wolves from the midst; and thou didst cast down the idolatrous temples unto the ground, and didst make the earthborn to be temples of God.

*Theotokion:*
The holy Theotokos, the undefiled tabernacle, the gate of light, the table and all-golden jar, the unhewn and overshadowed mountain, having contained the Creator, is called blessed.

*Ode 5 [of St. Bartholomew]. Heirmos:*
All things were amazed at thy divine glory; for thou, O Virgin who knewest not wedlock, didst hold in thy womb the God Who is over all *(Fol. 132v)*

...God, and gavest birth unto the timeless Son, Who bestows peace upon all who hymn thee!

*Troparion:*
Having acquired feet that were beautiful through preaching, thou didst proclaim good tidings of good things unto all; thou didst preach peace unto all, O blessed one, having alienated thyself from ancient enmity by the divine teachings of the Savior, O Apostle!

*Troparion:*
Thy word became a healing unto those who had received the deadly venom of the serpent, O Bartholomew; and the land of the ungodly fell down, as was prophesied, by the utterance of thine all-wise teachings.

*Troparion:*
Though a corpse of many days, pouring forth abundant life, borne upon the crests of the sea, O Bartholomew, together with the divine martyrs, having traversed boundless deeps, thou didst hasten unto the island of Lipari, O Apostle!

*Theotokion:*
Truly I glorify thee as the all-pure Mother of God; like the Angel I cry unto thee: Rejoice, O blessed one who hast found favor! A fearful thing it is to hear and to speak of: a marvelous receptacle for the Master of creation!

*Another [Canon of St. Titus]. Ode 5. Heirmos:*[^848]
The ungodly shall not behold Thy glory, O Christ; but we, waking at dawn from the night, hymn Thee, O Only-begotten One, the radiance of the Father's glory of the Godhead, O Lover of mankind!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst stay the foul-smelling wound of malice of the souls of the nations, O blessed one, with the sweet salt of thy divine words, as an excellent physician and as a minister of grace.

*Troparion:*
Obeying the command of Paul, meekly with him thou didst traverse the earth under heaven, O all-wise one, preaching the word of understanding, O all-honored proclaimer of God.

*Troparion:*
Having taught that the Word is co-equal in power and enthroned together with the Father, like a wise one, O God-proclaimed voice, thou didst persuade the foolish-minded to cast away all the deceit of idols.

*Theotokion:*
With a pure mind let us honor the Beauty of Jacob in a pure manner, and being adorned with divine deeds, let us piously hymn her as the Mother of our God.

*Ode 6 [of St. Bartholomew]. Heirmos:*
Celebrating this divine and all-honored feast of the Mother of God, come, ye God-wise, let us clap our hands, and glorify the God born of her!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a ray of the Sun Who shone upon the world in the flesh, by thy light-bearing teachings, O Apostle, driving away the night of polytheistic deceit.

*Troparion:*
Unto the peoples thou didst make plain the salvation of God Who appeared in the likeness of flesh because of His great goodness, O Bartholomew, preacher of the nations and Apostle!

*Troparion:*
Thou didst have a way through the sea, and didst make paths in the waters *(Fol. 133r)*

...and by the Spirit thy footsteps in them were recognized, even as David foretold, O blessed Apostle!

*Theotokion:*
Thou wast the temple of glory, and wast shown forth as the gate of light, the overshadowed mountain praised in prophetic tokens, O Virgin Mother who hast found favor with God!

*Another [Canon of St. Titus]. Ode 6. Heirmos:*
I shall sacrifice unto Thee with a voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood which flowed from Thy side for mercy's sake!

*Troparion:*
Paul, the unsetting sun of the world, sent thee forth like a radiant ray, O blessed one, enlightening the hearts that sat in the darkness of senselessness.

*Troparion:*
Being filled with living waters, by the streams of divine teachings, O blessed one, thou didst drown the torrents of senselessness, watering the gatherings of the nations.

*Theotokion:*
Generations of generations hymn thee, O all-hymned one; for beyond word thou gavest birth unto the all-hymned Word of God, Whom both human and angelic ranks glorify.

*Kontakion [of St. Bartholomew], Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Яви́лся есѝ дне́сь):*
Thou hast appeared as a great sun unto the Church, with the radiance of teachings and terrible wonders enlightening those who hymn thee, O Bartholomew, Apostle of the Lord!

*Ikos:*[^849]
Like a most radiant sun thou didst traverse the world, and didst enlighten it with the rays of thy words; having died as a martyr, thou didst set, and by the radiances issuing from thy tomb thou didst drive away the darkness of passions; therefore they who were possessed by envy, having driven thee toward the settings of the sun, O passion-bearer, let thee go traveling upon the sea. A surpassing wonder was seen in thee, who didst dreadfully sail the deep of the sea, which was illuminated by thy marvelous voyagings, O Bartholomew, Apostle of the Lord!

*Ode 7 [of St. Bartholomew]. Heirmos:*
The God-wise ones did not worship the creature rather than the Creator, but having courageously trampled upon the threat of fire, they rejoiced, singing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Mankind, which through malice had become foolish, O most glorious one, thou didst heal with the sweet salt of thy words, and didst persuade them to sing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having become an exalted heaven, thou dost preach the glory of God spiritually with a high voice, as a divine Apostle; unto Whom we cry: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
Having woven a robe of divine grace, O blessed one, thou didst clothe those who were cruelly stripped naked by the malice of the serpent *(Fol. 133v)*

...and didst persuade them to sing: Blessed art Thou, O all-hymned Lord and God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Having acquired thee as an intercessor and an impregnable rampart, O pure Birthgiver of God, we thy servants ever cry unto thee in faith: Rejoice, O blessed bridge that leads from earth unto God!

*Another [Canon of St. Titus]. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
Thou Who didst save Thy Abrahamic youths in the fire, and didst slay the Chaldeans whom justice justly ensnared: O all-hymned Lord God of our fathers, blessed art Thou!

*Troparion:*
Titus taught men to hymn the one Godhead and to honor the one Lordship, delivering them from polytheistic fury, as they piously cry: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Troparion:*
They that were slain in soul, worshiping soulless creation, came to know the God and Giver of life Who was put to death for our sake, being instructed by thee, O disciple and proclaimer of ineffable mysteries.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst appear as a minister of truth and a dispeller of falsehood; and as a disciple of Paul thou didst endure persecutions and afflictions, singing with him, O glorious one: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Theotokion:*
Adorned with virtues, thou didst stand at the right hand of the King of all, Who was incarnate of thy blood, O unwedded one, fervently entreating that we be delivered from every necessity.

*Ode 8 [of St. Bartholomew]. Heirmos:*[^850]
The childbirth of the Theotokos saved the pious youths in the furnace; then it was prefigured, but now it is fulfilled in deed, and it stirs up the whole universe to sing unto Thee: Hymn the Lord, ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Flooded with divine streams, even as a river from Eden thou didst go forth to water the earth under the sun with thy teachings, and by grace didst dry up the waters of deceit, crying: Hymn the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
The eye of the divine Theologian, having instructed thee in higher words, sent thee forth unto the world like a fiery arrow, wounding the gatherings of the enemies, O glorious one, and [healing] those who sing of the Savior in faith and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparion:*
Thou wast shown forth as a mountain dripping divine sweetness, O worthy of praise; and after death thou didst have the sea as a path, sailing by divine powers, not submerged by its deep, as they bore thee aloft as spear-bearers, O valiant sufferer!

*Theotokion:*
Having escaped the laws of nature, thou givest birth and remainest Ever-Virgin; for thou didst give birth unto the Word Who became man through His surpassing goodness; unto Whom we cry: Hymn the Lord, ye works, and supremely exalt Him unto the ages.

*Another [Canon]. The Heirmos the same:*
*Troparia:*
By the sprinkling of thy blood, the alien fire of delusion was extinguished; sicknesses were driven away, and demons vanished through thy supplication unto God, O Apostle Titus. Therefore we praise thee as a divine servant of Christ the King of all.

Wholly consecrated unto God and adorned with divine graces, O Titus, thou didst explain the saving word unto all, which delivered from irrationality us who with true faith bless and exalt Christ unto the ages, O most wise one.

Having become a most honorable receptacle of light, thou didst enlighten the world under the sun with divine radiance, O Titus worthy of wonder; and thou didst darken the oppositions of lawless enemies, singing and exalting the Lord unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
The sword that turned of old now turns its back to me, O Virgin,[^851] for thou hast borne the Word clothed in flesh and understood in two natures, before Whom the angelic ranks tremble and whom earthly beings glorify unto the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Let every earthborn mortal leap for joy, enlightened in spirit; and let the nature of the bodiless minds celebrate, honoring the sacred festival of the Mother of God, and let them cry aloud: Rejoice, O all-blessed Theotokos, pure Ever-Virgin!

*Troparia:*
Today the choir of the Apostles brings the feast of thy memory; the martyrs rejoice, and the ranks of all the righteous. And we the faithful honor thee with hymns and cry aloud: From every sorrow deliver us, O disciple of Christ!

Having received a blessed end upon the Cross, O glorious one, thou wast conformed to the sufferings of thy Master, and a partaker of radiance and divine glory, standing clearly with the angels before Him, and being deemed worthy of ineffable light.

Thy most radiant and illustrious memory hath shone forth upon us, enlightening all, O eyewitness of Christ, wise Bartholomew! In it we beseech thee: remember us all, delivering us from every opposition and adversity by thy prayers.

*Theotokion:*
Put to death our carnal mortifications, O pure Birthgiver of God; calm the tempest of our passions, quiet our turmoil, and establish with divine thoughts the hearts of those who honor thee with faith, O pure one, our unashamed Protectress.

*Another [Canon]. Heirmos:*
Eve indeed, through the disease of disobedience, brought in the curse; but thou, O Virgin Theotokos, through the sprout of the bearing in thy womb— —bearing, hast blossomed forth blessing unto the world. Therefore we all magnify thee.

*Troparia:*
Bearing both love and hope unto the Deliverer, Paul takes thee as a fellow traveler, preaching with thee the sacred Gospel unto the Gentiles, O Apostle; therefore together with him we honor thee.

Thou drivest away the diseases of the faithful and expellest demons by divine grace. The great city of Crete gained thee as a divine primate, a luminary and a heavenly radiance, O Titus wise in God and worthy of all praise.

Like the sun, thy memory hath shone forth upon us with spiritual rays, most brightly adorned, driving away the clouds of souls. Preserve us who observe it with faith, O all-blessed Titus worthy of all praise.

*Theotokion:*
Untie the fetters of my transgressions, O pure and all-immaculate one, through thy mediation; destroy the darkness of my soul, calm the tempest of my passions, crush those who battle against me, and save me, O good Ever-Virgin.

*Exapostilarion.*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Having adorned the heavens" (Не́бо ѕвѣзда́ми):*
As receptacles of light, steadfast rays, light-beaming lightnings of the day that knows no evening, we the faithful all glorify Bartholomew and Titus.

*Theotokion:*
By thy mighty protection, O pure one, preserve all thy servants unharmed from the assault of the enemy; for thee alone do we have as a refuge in tribulations.

*At the Liturgy, the Prokeimenon, Tone 8:*
Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth, [and their words unto the ends of the universe.]
*Verse: The heavens declare the glory of God.*
*The Epistle to Titus, pericope 300.*
*Alleluia, Tone 1:*
*The heavens shall confess Thy wonders, O Lord.*
*The Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 38.*
*Communion Hymn: Their sound hath gone forth into all the earth:*

*The same month, on the 26th day.*
*The Holy Martyrs Adrian and Natalia:*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Saints, Tone 4, Podoben: "As noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
Beholding the most honorable suffering and the valiant contests of the martyrs, O glorious one, thou didst voluntarily surrender thyself unto the struggles with a manly mind, not sparing the flesh for the sake of divine love. Therefore— thou didst pass through valiant contests, having brought low the pride of the adversary, O patient-minded Adrian.

Shut up in prisons, beaten with ox sinews, weighed down with irons, O glorious one, and crushed with stones together with a multitude of martyrs; with whom thou didst obtain the heavenly good things, having as thy fellow-helper thy spouse Natalia, who hoped in God, O valiant Adrian.

The spouse of Adam drove him out of paradise by the counsel of the serpent; but Natalia wisely led Adrian into paradise, instructing him with sacred, divine teachings to endure painful sufferings, mediating heavenly rewards and everlasting glory.

*Glory, Tone 6:*
O holy and chosen pair of the Lord! O couple excellent in faith and blessed unto God! O desirable and beloved pair unto Christ! Who hearing of this will not marvel at their deeds beyond human nature? How the female nature took courage against the bitter tormentor, and strengthened her peer,[^852] not to surrender unto cruel things, but out of faith to choose rather to die than to live! O God-woven words of all-wise Natalia! O divine teachings that came from the heavens and placed glorious Adrian as renowned before the very throne of the great King! But O holy pair, pray unto God for us who with love celebrate your memory, that we may be delivered from tribulations and every sorrow.

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having laid aside all" (Всѐ ѿло́жше):*
A sword, as Simeon said, pierced thy heart, O all-pure Lady, when thou didst behold Him Who shone forth from thee by an ineffable Word, lifted up upon the Cross by the lawless like one condemned, given vinegar and gall to drink, His side pierced, and His hands and feet nailed; and sobbing thou didst cry aloud, maternal-like exclaiming: What is this new mystery, O sweetest Child?

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos, Glory, Tone 1, by Ephrem of Caria:*
The zeal of the pious husband drew the God-loving wife unto bright instruction; for Adrian the most excellent, being brought forward by Natalia's words, accomplished the course of suffering. O disposition of the God-loving woman! For not as Eve brought corruption unto Adam, but she mediated unending life unto her spouse. Praising her together with her husband, let us cry aloud unto Christ: Grant us help— by the prayers of Thy saints.

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "Of the heavenly ranks" (Нбⷭ҇ныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Standing by the Cross of thy Son and God, and beholding His longsuffering, the pure Mother wept and said: Woe is me, O sweetest Child! Why dost Thou suffer these things unjustly, O Word of God, that Thou mayest save mankind?

*Troparion, Tone 4:*
Thy martyrs, O Lord, in their sufferings received incorruptible crowns from Thee, our God. For having Thy strength, they cast down the tormentors and crushed the powerless insolence of the demons. By their supplications save our souls.

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos, and of the Saints to 4, Tone 6.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Traversing the deep on foot as on dry land, Israel, beholding the pursuer Pharaoh drowned, cried aloud: Let us sing unto God a song of victory!

*Troparia:*
From the night of this life, held fast by a cruel sleep of deeds, raise me up, O God, unto the light of repentance, by the prayers of Thy martyrs, O Christ, that I may glorify Thee.

Illumined by the Orient of the spiritual Sun, O martyrs, and having become sons of the day, ye mightily destroyed all idol mist.

Delighting in lips dripping with the sweetness of divine understanding, O Adrian, thou didst truly spew out the poison of former senselessness, O divinely blessed one.

*Theotokion:*
Having received thy Creator from thy seedless womb, incarnate beyond understanding as He Himself willed, O pure one, thou hast truly appeared as the Lady of creatures.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
There is none holy as Thou, O Lord my God, Who hast exalted the horn of Thy faithful, O Good One, and hast established us upon the rock of Thy confession.

*Troparia:*
Having escaped the snares[^853] of idol madness, O martyr, thou didst bind thyself with the desire of Christ's love, and didst joyfully partake in the bonds of His passion-bearer.

Having your shins crushed with iron bars, O passion-bearers of the Lord, ye truly crushed the bones of impiety with the bars of endurance and with courage.

With the fire of divine love burning in thy soul, O spouse, thou didst inflame thy longing for Christ, having utterly hated carnal love, O Natalia.

*Theotokion:*
The wonder of thy divine childbirth, O pure one, surpasses every order of nature; for thou didst supernaturally conceive God in thy womb— and having given birth thou remainest Ever-Virgin.

*Kontakion, Tone 4:*
Having laid up in thy heart the divine words of thy God-wise wife, O Adrian, martyr of Christ, thou didst eagerly hasten unto sufferings, receiving the crown together with thy spouse.

*Kathisma, Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оу҆диви́сѧ і҆ѡ́сифъ):*
Thou didst extinguish the conflagration of impiety with the bedewing of piety, O passion-bearer; by the crushing of thy limbs thou didst grind to powder the might of the idols, having found thy spouse as a helper unto all cruel arrays of torment, O martyr; with whom thou hast settled in the highest kingdom, O stout-hearted Adrian. Pray unto Christ to grant mercy unto all who honor thee.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Tempest-tossed by passionate billows, I, conscious of my sins, cry warmly unto thee, O pure one: Do not despise me, the wretched one, so that I perish, for thou didst bear the abyss of compassion, and beside thee I have no hope; let me not appear to the enemies as an object of rejoicing and mockery, having placed my hope in thee; for thou art able to do whatever thou wilt, being Mother of the God of all.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The Virgin and Ewe, beholding upon the Cross the Lamb born seedlessly from her, pierced by a spear, wounded by the arrows of grief, cried out weeping in pain: What is this new mystery? How dost Thou die, Who art the only Lord of life? Therefore arise, raising up our fallen forefather.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
Christ is my power, God and Lord, the venerable Church sings in a manner befitting God, crying out from a pure mind, keeping feast in the Lord.

*Troparia:*
Having acquired the strength of Christ, O martyrs, ye truly trampled down the feeble cruelty of the persecutors, and received crowns of victory from heaven.

While the bodies of the saints flowed all about with gore, myrrh was truly offered unto God, mightily cleansing away the festering stench of delusion.

With thy blood thou didst dye for thyself a garment of incorruption, having stripped off the garment of corruption, and now thou standest before Christ in glory, O Adrian.

*Theotokion:*
Truly ineffable and incomprehensible to those on earth and in heaven, O Theotokos, Ever-Virgin, are the mysteries of thy divinely-magnificent childbirth.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
With Thy divine light, O Good One, illumine in love, I pray Thee, the souls of those who awake early unto Thee, that they may know Thee, O Word of God, as true— God, crying out from the darkness of sin.

*Troparia:*
A double decade of sufferers with a simple triad, now praying unto Christ, broke the bond of impiety which had no strength, through the strength of the Trinity, O valiant ones.

Standing like a bright sun in the midst of stars, O glorious Adrian, thou didst shine forth upon those suffering with thee with the light of piety, driving away the mist of impiety, and didst enlighten the ends of the world.

The dew of thy venerable words became truly a healing of pains unto thy spouse, who tasted the love of better things, O divinely-wise Natalia, companion of the martyrs.

*Theotokion:*
Confessing thee from our soul as the Theotokos, O good Lady of the world, save us; for we have thee as an invincible protection, the true Birthgiver of God.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Beholding the sea of life surging with the tempest of temptations, fleeing unto Thy calm haven, I cry unto Thee: Lead up my life from corruption, O Greatly-Merciful One!

*Troparia:*
Steered piously by the hand of Christ, ye traversed the abyss of godlessness, and unto the calm haven of the highest splendor ye hastened, O sufferers of the Lord.

Like flowers, O wise one, the supernaturally victorious struggles of thy contests smell sweetly; for thou didst appear as a sweet-smelling incense unto God, O Adrian, consumed by the fire of torments.

With the beauty of faith thy holy soul was most brightly adorned, and seeking the truly immeasurable beauty of Christ, O Natalia, thou didst not fail of thy desire.

*Theotokion:*
O all-pure Lady, who didst bear the Helmsman and Lord for mankind, calm the unsteady and cruel turmoil of my passions, and grant stillness unto my heart.

*Kontakion, Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
The divine memory of the martyrs hath shone forth, and brightly illumined all the ends of the earth, who cry aloud with gladness: Thou art the rejoicing of the martyrs, O Christ!

*Ikos:*
Having valiantly desired Christ the King, nailed in the flesh upon the mighty tree, the soldiers eagerly endured cruel pains and fearful deaths, having trampled down all the idol impiety and cast down the oppositions of the lawless—twenty-three martyrs in all;[^854] therefore Adrian and Natalia, the fair pair, beholding these, were zealous for them, and rejoicing cry aloud: Thou art the rejoicing of the martyrs, O Christ!

...rejoicing of the martyrs, O Christ!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The angel made the furnace a source of dew for the venerable youths, while the command of God, consuming the Chaldeans, persuaded the tormentor to cry out: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Troparia:*
Ye appeared as unconquerable pillars of the Sion on high, O martyrs, having cast down all the wiles of the enemy through the endurance of your suffering. Therefore we piously honor your memory evermore.

Natalia was granted by God unto Adrian as a like-minded helper, drawing out this man who was bogged down in the depth of delusion, and exhorting him to cry aloud: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

Having hidden the martyr's hand[^855] as a precious treasure, thou didst not surrender the treasure of thy chastity to those striving to steal it, O venerable one, being guided by the hand of the God of the fathers.

*Theotokion:*
The existing prophets openly proclaimed the mystery of thy honorable childbirth, O pure one; and now we, beholding the fulfillment from afar, piously bless thee.

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Out of the flame Thou didst pour dew for the venerable ones, and with water didst burn up the sacrifice of the righteous one; for Thou dost all things, O Christ, merely by willing them: Thee we exalt unto all ages.

*Troparia:*
Consumed in the fire of torment, ye quenched all the flame of impiety with your blood, O sufferers, crying aloud: Thee we exalt, O Christ, unto all ages.

Who will not marvel at the marvelous woman's love toward God? For she hated carnal desire and persuaded her spouse to honor and glorify Christ unto all ages.

Having shattered the snares of demons and desired the beauty of Christ, thou wast a pillar of piety unto the faithful, O martyr and passion-bearer, all-blessed Adrian.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst conceive the Word from the Light-giver of light, and ineffably gavest birth to the Transubstantial One; for the Spirit of God dwelt within thee, O Virgin. Therefore we sing of thee, O pure one, unto the ages.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
It is not possible for men to see God, upon Whom the angelic ranks dare not gaze; but through thee, O all-pure one, the incarnate Word appeared unto men, Whom magnifying, together with the heavenly hosts, we call thee blessed.

*Troparion:*
Neither the crushing of limbs, nor the threats of cruel tormentors, nor sword, nor fire, nor the death of the corruptible flesh— corruptible flesh could by any means separate you from the love of Christ, O glorious sufferers; therefore ye ever rejoice with Him unto the ages.

*Troparia:*
As divinely-bright candles, shining stars, and luminaries, having shed upon us the light of piety, O radiance of the Sun of glory, of the bliss that knows no evening, ye appeared as the godly-honored martyrs of Christ.

A spring filled with spiritual waters, a river full of divine gifts, and a cup pouring forth streams of suffering, deeps of salvation, ye appeared, O martyrs, delivering us from the tempest of various temptations.

*Theotokion:*
Resurrection hath now been granted unto the dead by thine ineffable and unutterable childbirth, O all-honored Theotokos; for Life clothed in flesh hath shone forth from thee upon all, and manifestly destroyed the infirmity of death.

*Exapostilarion.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Hear, ye women" (Жены̀ оу҆слы́шите):*
Let Adrian the all-wise, the confirmation of sufferers, together with venerable Natalia, be worthily honored with hymns; with whom shines the holy choir of the martyrs, whose bright festival celebrating with godly wisdom, let us sing with divine hymns.

*Theotokion:*
The tormenting serpent who of old envied my deification[^856] in Eden, O Virgin, the good Creator and Maker of nature, having humbled Himself in thy womb, destroyed in a manner befitting God by the Cross and death, pouring forth incorruption upon me.

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos, Glory, and now, Theotokion:*

*The same month, on the 27th day.*
*Our Venerable Father Poemen:*
*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "What shall we call you" (Что̀ вы̀ нарече́мъ):*
What shall we now name thee, O Poemen? Model of monks, and worker of healings; who by the wounds of abstinence didst put to death the passions of the soul; citizen and conversationalist of angels, dweller of the highest metropolis, vessel of virtues, and confirmation of the pious! Pray that our souls may be saved.

What shall we now address thee, O Poemen? Inhabitant of the desert, lover of silence, uprooter of passions, and instructor of monks— —instructor, flooding stream of the teachings of the divine Spirit, sleepless luminary of discernment, true wonderworker, healing diverse passions! Pray that our souls may be saved.

Having become a luminary of discernment, illumining the souls of those who approach thee with faith, and showing them the path of life, O wise one; therefore with praises we call thee blessed, celebrating thy holy festival, O Poemen, boast of fathers, adornment of fasters! Pray that our souls may be saved.

*Glory, Tone 6:*
O venerable father, into all the earth hath gone forth the sound of thy right deeds; therefore in the heavens thou hast found the reward of thy labors; thou didst destroy the ranks of demons, and hast attained unto the angelic ranks, whose blameless life thou didst emulate. Having boldness before Christ God, ask peace for our souls.

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "On the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Beholding Thee crucified, O Christ, she who bare Thee cried aloud: What is this strange mystery that I see, O my Son? How dost Thou die, suspended in the flesh upon the tree, O Giver of life?

*At the Aposticha of the Octoechos, Glory, Tone 8, Idiomelon:*
The multitudes of monks honor thee as instructor, O our father Poemen; for by thy path we have learned truly to walk rightly. Blessed art thou, having labored for Christ, and having exposed the power of the enemy, O converser with angels, companion of the venerable and the righteous! Together with them pray unto the Lord that our souls may be shown mercy.

*Now and ever, Theotokion or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
When the Ewe beheld the Lamb stretched out willingly upon the tree of the Cross, she cried aloud in maternal grief, mourning and weeping: O my Son, what is this strange sight? Giving life unto all as Lord, how art Thou put to death, O Longsuffering One, granting resurrection unto earthly beings? I glorify Thine abundant condescension, O my God!

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
With the streams of thy tears thou didst cultivate the barren desert, and by sighings from the depths thou didst bear fruit unto a hundredfold of labors; and thou becamest a luminary to the universe, shining with wonders, O Poemen our father! Pray unto Christ God that our souls may be saved.

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos, and of the Saint to 4.*
*A creation of Theophanes. Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos: Let us sing* Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone hath been gloriously glorified.

*Troparia:*
Warmed by the heat of the Comforter, O father, thy heart dissolved the demonic ice and the winter of passions.

Having consumed the matter of passions with the coal of divine fear, thou becamest a luminary of discernment, and a fiery dispassion,[^857] O blessed one.

Bearing thy cross upon thy shoulder, O father, thou didst follow with love Him Who called thee, and thou becamest a luminary of monks.

*Theotokion:*
The transcendent Word saves me by His goodness, having willed to become incarnate from thy womb, O pure Virgin Mother.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of those who flee unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee.

*Troparia:*
The angels marveled at thy all-night vigil, for thou didst have them as fellow-helpers in thy prayers unto God.

Thou didst shake off the blindness of passions from the eye of thy mind, O father; therefore thou didst clearly perceive the Invisible One.

Having been nourished as with milk by abstinence, O venerable Poemen, thou didst ascend unto the height of virtues, unto perfect dispassion.

*Theotokion:*
Having thee as helper, O pure one, I fear not the assaults of the enemies; having thee as protectress, I conquer their ranks.

*Kathisma, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*
Shepherded by the Lord, thou didst appear as His gentle sheep, O blessed one, meekly overcoming the opposing wolves; and having finished thy divine contest, thou didst settle in the heavenly fold, O venerable father. Earnestly pray unto Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
Not departing from the divine essence, having become flesh in thy womb; but God became man and remained God, Who after childbirth preserved thee, His mother, as an all-immaculate virgin even as before childbirth, the only Lord: earnestly pray unto Him to grant us great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled Ewe of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding suspended upon the Cross Him Who sprouted without travail from her, weeping in fitting maternal grief, cried aloud: Woe is me, O my Child! How dost Thou suffer willingly, desiring to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I understood Thy works, and glorified Thy divinity.

*Troparia:*
Having received the comforting radiance, thou becamest an undefiled mirror, O God-bearer, and a receptacle of divine ascents.

Watered with tears, thou didst appear as a high-grown tree, O father, cultivated by abstinence, and directed by divine fruits.

By the labors of abstinence thou didst cultivate the furrow of thy mind: thou didst multiply the fruitful ear of virtues, and the grace of miracles.

*Theotokion:*
O vessel of virginity, and dwelling of the incomprehensible Nature,[^858] enlighten my darkened soul, O Bride of God!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Rising at dawn, we cry aloud unto Thee, O Lord: Save us, for Thou art our God, besides Thee we know no other.

*Troparia:*
Having borne the heat of the day, O glorious one, thou wast deemed worthy of the joy of thy Lord, O venerable one.

In the valley of abstinence, thou didst blossom forth as a sweet-smelling briar-rose,[^859] fragrancing the ends of the earth with the breaths of divine knowledge.

Having walled thyself about with humility, O father, thou didst cast down unto the earth the serpent who previously boasted greatly.

*Theotokion:*
We sing of thee as the Mother of God after thy giving birth: for thou hast in the flesh borne God the Word unto the world.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Grant me a robe of light, Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, O greatly merciful Christ our God.

*Troparia:*
Having put to death carnal impulses by many ascetic struggles, thou hast departed unto the immortal life, O Poemen worthy of wonder.

Having acquired continual abstinence, prayer, and unfeigned love, O venerable one, thou didst appear as an undefiled mirror of God.

Dwelling in impassable wildernesses, thou didst strip thyself of devastating passions, and becamest a heavenly citizen.

*Theotokion:*
O thou who alone by the word hast borne the Word in the flesh, deliver our souls, we pray thee, from the snares of the enemy.

*Kontakion, Tone 4. Podoben: "Thou hast appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
Today thy holy memory has arrived, O venerable father, gladdening the souls of the pious with the radiant struggles of thine asceticism, O Poemen, God-wise father, our venerable one.

*Ikos:*
Having hated soul-corrupting pleasures and worldly turmoils with all thy soul, and having desired Christ, taking up His Cross upon thy shoulder, thou didst follow Him with unflagging desire, and didst labor in abstinence. Thou didst immaterially acquire thy manner of life, in fastings and tears and unceasing prayers. Therefore also the Savior granted thee the heavenly Kingdom, having deemed thee worthy of the unwaning Light and inaccessible radiance, O Poemen wise in God, our venerable father.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew youths boldly trampled the flame in the furnace and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.

*Troparia:*
Exercising thyself in divine contemplations, thou wast enlightened with immaterial rays; and thou becamest truly a son of the day and a light unto those in darkness, O God-bearer, unto the ages.

Lifted up unto the height of dispassion with the flesh, assimilating thyself unto the angels, thou becamest an heir to the delight of Paradise, crying aloud, O blessed one: Blessed art Thou, O God!

Shining with the radiance of dispassion, thou didst darken the demonic temptations; and deliver many from their malice who cry aloud, O venerable one: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Theotokion:*
Behold, the Virgin of whom the great Isaiah spoke in the Spirit hath conceived God in her womb and bears Him, unto Whom we sing, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O God!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Hymn the unoriginate King of glory, before Whom the heavenly hosts tremble, O ye priests; and exalt Him, O ye people, unto all ages.

*Troparia:*
Having kept the lamp of thy soul unsleeping with the oil of thine ascetic struggles, thou didst enter with joy into the incorrupt bridal chamber, and livest unto the ages.

The winds of unclean spirits shook not the pillar of thy soul: for it was established upon the rock of faith, O all-blessed God-bearer.

Thou didst strip off the unsightly garment of passions, and wast clothed, O blessed one, in the beautiful robe of divine dispassion, reigning together with Christ.

*Theotokion:*
As the honorable and exalted throne of the Most High God, let us hymn her, O ye people, who alone after giving birth was preserved a virgin.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every hearing was struck with awe at the ineffable condescension of God, how the Most High voluntarily descended even unto the flesh from the virginal womb, having become man. Therefore the all-pure Theotokos do we the faithful magnify.

*Troparia:*
Thou didst indeed set like a star from the world, yet thou didst shine forth with Christ, the truly intelligible Sun of righteousness, O all-blessed one, Sun; and as rays unto the faithful, thou hast left thy luminous virtues, which remove the darkness of souls.

Precious before God was thy death, O glorious one, for thou didst live venerable upon earth, having kept His commandments and ordinances uninjured, O Poemen; therefore the unwaning Light shone forth unto thee as unto a righteous one.

Delighting in divine beauties, O father, deified by communion, and standing now enlightened before the great Light, thou hast most clearly drawn near unto the utmost Desire; remember those who honor thee, O Poemen, and observe thy memory.

*Theotokion:*
Thou alone among women didst abolish the curse of the first-created, O Bride of God: having in the flesh given birth unto the Uncircumscribed One Who became circumscribed, thou hast renewed the laws of nature, O undefiled one, and those things formerly divided hast thou united by thy most glorious mediation.

*Exapostilarion.*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "Having adorned the heavens" (Нб҃о ѕвѣзда́ми):*
Desiring the angelic life, thou didst withdraw into the wilderness, O Poemen, and didst subdue the carnal passions, appearing as equal to the angels, O our father, bearer of God.

*Theotokion:*
By thy mighty protection, O pure one, preserve all us thy servants unharmed from the assault of the enemies; for thee alone have we gained as a refuge in needs.

*At the Liturgy, the Prokeimenon [Tone 7]:*
Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^860]
*Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?*
*The Epistle to the Galatians, pericope 213 [Gal. 5:22-6:2]. The Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 10 [Matt. 4:25-5:12]. Communion Hymn: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be [Ps. 111:6].*

*The same month, on the 28th day.*
*Commemoration of our Venerable Father Moses the Black.*[^861]
*On "Lord, I have cried," Stichera, Tone 8, Podoben: "O Thy martyrs" (Мч҃нцы твоѝ):*
Moses, renowned of many, wisely having left worldly adornment, by the strong labors of abstinence subdued the carnal passions; and having cast down the crafty one, he received the crown of victory. By his prayers, O Lord, grant great mercy unto all.

Exemplary abstinence, all-night standing, a wakeful eye, a mind contemplating divine beauties didst thou possess, O blessed Moses! Therefore thou didst receive the grace of healings, to heal grievous passions. For this reason we pray thee: ask great mercy for all, O father!

Sheltered by the divine palm, O Moses, thou didst pass unharmed through the demonic wiles, arrows, and snares, O father; and adorned with dispassion, thou wast numbered with all the venerable ones. Pray that great mercy be granted unto those who honor thee with faith.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
My thoughts are unclean, my lips deceitful, and all my deeds defiled; and what shall I do? How shall I meet the Judge? O Lady Virgin, pray to thy Son, Creator, and Lord, that He may accept my spirit in repentance, as He alone is compassionate.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
I cannot endure, O Child, beholding Thee fallen asleep upon the Tree, Who bestowest wakefulness upon all, that Thou mayest grant divine and saving wakefulness unto those who of old through the fruit of transgression fell asleep in the sleep of perdition!—the Virgin cried aloud, weeping, whom we magnify.

*On the Aposticha, from the Octoechos. Glory, and now, Theotokion.*

*Troparion, Tone 1:*
A dweller of the wilderness, an angel in the body, and a wonderworker didst thou appear, O our God-bearing father Moses; having received heavenly gifts through fasting, vigil, and prayer, thou healest the sick and the souls of those who run unto thee with faith. Glory to Him Who gave thee strength! Glory to Him Who crowned thee! Glory to Him Who through thee works healings for all!

*At Matins, both Canons of the Octoechos, and of the Saint to 4, Tone 8.*

*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea, for He alone has been gloriously glorified.

*Troparia:*
Whiten my heart, darkened by the filth of sins, with the clouds of repentance by thy prayers, O father.

Having nailed thy flesh with the fear of the Master, thou didst dry up every passionate thought from thy heart, O God-bearing, venerable, and blessed one.

Having hidden the seeds of the word in the intelligible furrows, O father, thou didst cultivate wheat preserved in inexhaustible storehouses.

*Theotokion:*
The transcendent Word by His goodness saves me wholly, having willed to become incarnate from thy womb, O pure Virgin Mother.

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
Thou art the confirmation of those who run unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee sings of Thee.

*Troparia:*
Moved by the Spirit, enduring through spiritual actions, O wise one, thou didst subdue the spirits of wickedness.

Strengthened by the divine originating power, O venerable Moses, as though bodiless thou didst cast down the strong serpent.

With the showers of thy tears thou didst quench the fiery burning of passions, and didst appear as a river of gifts filled by the Spirit.

*Theotokion:*
Having thee as helper, O pure one, I fear not the assaults of the enemies; having thee as mediatress, I conquer their ranks.

*Kathisma, Tone 3, Podoben: "Of divine faith" (Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры):*
Having grown rich in divine radiance, thou didst destroy the darkness of passions, O all-blessed one; and by thy wakeful prayers thou didst wither the flesh and its exalted carnal reasonings, and hast passed unto the highest heavenly city. O venerable father, pray unto Christ God to grant us great mercy.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion:*
He departed not from the divine nature when He became flesh in thy womb; but God became man and remained God, Who after childbirth preserved thee, His mother, as an all-immaculate virgin even as before childbirth, the only Lord: earnestly pray unto Him to grant us great mercy.

*Stavrotheotokion:*
The undefiled Ewe of the Word, the incorrupt Virgin Mother, beholding suspended upon the Cross Him Who sprouted without travail from her, weeping in fitting maternal grief, cried aloud: Woe is me, O my Child! How dost Thou suffer willingly, desiring to deliver man from the passions of dishonor?

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I understood Thy works, and glorified Thy divinity.

*Troparia:*
By unceasing prayers, O father, and by the application of painful labors, thou didst drive away the bodiless demon from thy soul, O most blessed one.

Having directed thy thought unto things beyond mind and word, O venerable one, thou didst endure the heat of abstinence as divine dew.

Borne aloft by the sail of non-possessiveness, thou didst easily sail across the sea of life, O father, steered toward a quiet haven.

*Theotokion:*
O vessel of virginity, and dwelling of the uncontainable Nature, enlighten my darkened soul, O Bride of God!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
Rising at dawn, we cry aloud unto Thee, O Lord: Save us, for Thou art our God, besides Thee we know no other.

Kindled by the coal of dispassion, O blessed one, thou didst burn up the combustible matter of passions.

Thou didst appear as a star of abstinence, shining with swift radiances,[^862] enlightening our souls, O most glorious one.

Thou didst ascend unto the height of virtues, and reached the heavenly island,[^863] O father Moses worthy of wonder.

*Theotokion:*
We sing of thee as the Mother of God after thy giving birth: for thou hast in the flesh borne God the Word unto the world.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are mine iniquities; and lead me up from the depth of evils, I pray Thee, for unto Thee have I cried, and do Thou give ear unto me, O God of my salvation.

*Troparia:*
Encompassed by the sinful night and the darkness of pleasures, enlighten me wholly by the spiritual radiances that are in thee, and steer me safely unto the haven of salvation, O father.

Like a diligent bee, having gathered the flowers of virtues in the honeycomb of thy mind, thou didst pour forth immortal sweetness, driving away demonic bitterness, O father.

Having endured in the wildernesses, thou didst inherit the city on high, subduing the flesh by fastings, O wonderful one; and thou hast departed unto the inexhaustible nourishment and the habitations of Paradise.

*Theotokion:*
An intelligible sanctuary, an inviolate propitiatory, a lamp of golden appearance, and an animate table that bore the Bread of life, do we the faithful name thee, O Virgin.

*Kontakion, Tone 4:*
Having struck the Ethiopians,[^864] and spat upon the faces of the demons, thou didst intelligibly shine forth like a radiant sun by the light of thy life, and by thy teaching guiding our souls.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
The Hebrew youths boldly trampled the flame in the furnace and changed the fire into dew, crying aloud: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God, unto the ages.

*Troparia:*
Cleansed from the mire of passions, shining with spiritual radiances, thou didst pass over unto the truly immaterial Light, where the choirs of fasters dwell unto the ages, O blessed one.

Thou didst flee from all malice, and by continual cleaving unto good things and by virtuous desire thou wast immaterially joined unto them, crying aloud, O blessed one: Blessed art Thou, O God!

By immeasurable labors of abstinence thou didst dry up painful sin, O God-bearer; therefore thou didst find the painless enjoyment of good things, blessing thy Master.

*Theotokion:*

Behold, the Virgin of whom the great Isaiah spoke in the Spirit has conceived God in her womb and bears Him, unto Whom we sing: Blessed art Thou, O Lord God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
The Chaldean tormentor furiously heated the furnace sevenfold for the godly ones; but having seen them saved by a higher Power, he cried aloud unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Troparia:*
Strengthened with sinews of prayer, elevated by humility, radiating the soul with righteousness, adorned with love, thou didst ascend unto the perfection of virtues, unto the manifest height, O father, calling unto the Master: Ye priests, bless; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

Black in body, yet having acquired a soul more radiant than the rays of the sun, he blackened the darkened faces of the demons, and by his likeness he enlightens the hearts of the faithful who fervently sing: Ye people, supremely exalt Christ unto the ages!

Walled about on all sides by humility, O father, thou didst escape the intelligible shooting of the Ethiopians;[^865] and thou becamest an example unto monks for the subjection of enemies, by deeds and words, crying aloud with them: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
Beyond nature hast thou conceived, beyond word hast thou borne the Fashioner of human nature, Who became man while remaining inseparable from the Father, O pious and pure one, unto Whom all creation sings: Ye children, bless; ye priests, hymn; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Every hearing was struck with awe at the ineffable condescension of God, how the Most High voluntarily descended even unto the flesh from the virginal womb, having become man. Therefore the all-pure Theotokos do we the faithful magnify.

*Troparia:*
Having ended thy life in truly good deeds, thou didst reach the Fountain of good things, and received the fulfillment of thy desire; and where the voice of those who feast is heard with praise, there hast thou settled rejoicing, O Moses, father worthy of wonder, our venerable one.

The streams of thy painful labors distill the sweetness of profit, O father, and drive away our passionate bitterness; thy relics pour forth healings for us, and cleanse the suppurations of evils and the defilement of souls.

With unfading crowns did Christ crown thy head, O wonderful one, who didst firmly vanquish the ranks of the ruler of the world; and unto the choirs of the venerable venerably wast thou numbered. Together with them pray that those who honor thee may be delivered from tribulations.

*Theotokion:*
Thou alone among women didst abolish the curse of the first-created, O Bride of God: having in the flesh given birth unto the Uncircumscribed One Who became circumscribed, thou hast renewed the laws of nature, O undefiled one, and those things formerly divided hast thou united by thy most glorious mediation.

*The same month, on the 29th day.*
*The Beheading of the Honorable Head of the Honorable and Glorious Prophet, Forerunner, and Baptist John.*

*At Small Vespers, on "Lord, I have cried," Stichera on 4, Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the ranks of heaven" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Come, all ye faithful, let us with one accord worthily and diligently honor the Prophet John and Baptist of Christ; for being a prophet, he foretells things to come, as a divinely-fitting minister and initiate of the ineffable wonders of Christ.

Senseless Herod unsparingly cuts off thy head, which rebuked his most defiled disposition; but Christ, the Lord and Maker of all, and Redeemer of all, makes thee, O thrice-blessed one, the head of the Church as her baptizer.

Lawless Herod commanded thy head to be brought upon a platter unto the defiled Herodias, with boldness unheard of from the beginning,[^866] O all-blessed one. O frightening wonder and most glorious hearing! Yet the Church of Christ joyfully celebrates thy suffering.

*Glory, Tone 8:*
O Prophet of the Savior, even though Herod did dissolve thee by murder as a preacher of truth, yet the radiant lamp of thy lips shone forth, calling unto those in hades with the brightness of faith; therefore pray that our souls may find mercy.

*And now: "Accept, O Lady" (Влⷣчце прїимѝ).*
*Prokeimenon of the day.*

*On the Aposticha, Stichera, Tone 2, Podoben: "House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Greater than those born of women didst thou appear, O blessed John, having baptized the Master of all, O prophet, preacher, and forerunner![^867]

*Verse: Precious* in the sight of the Lord is the death of His venerable ones.[^868]

When Herodias saw lawless Herod possessed by drunkenness and lust, she drew him unto murder.

*Verse: In everlasting remembrance shall the righteous be [Ps. 111:6].*

We celebrate with love the sacred memory of divine John and Baptist, singing of the transcendent Trinity.

*Glory, and now, Podoben the same (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
