O sole hope and pure mediatress of Christians: together with the Forerunner pray to thy Son on behalf of thy servants.

*Troparion, Tone 2:*
The memory of the righteous is celebrated with hymns of praise, but the testimony of the Lord suffices for thee, O Forerunner; for thou wast shown forth truly as even more honorable than the prophets, seeing that thou wast deemed worthy to baptize in the streams Him Whom they preached. Therefore, having suffered for the truth rejoicing, thou didst announce the good tidings even unto those in hades of God Who appeared in the flesh, Who takes away the sin of the world and grants us great mercy.

*Glory, and now, Theotokion: All above understanding... (Всѧ̑ па́че смы́сла).*

*At Great Vespers, we chant the Kathisma: "Blessed is the man," 1st Antiphon.*
*On "Lord, I have cried," we set 8 verses, and sing idiomela stichera, Tone 6, by John the Monk:*
While the birthday of shameless Herod was being celebrated, the agreement of the oath was fulfilled for the defiled dancer; for the head of the Forerunner, having been cut off, was carried upon a platter like meat unto the guests. O loathsome feast, an unholy deed, full of murder! Yet we, worthily honoring the Baptist as the greatest of those born of women, call him blessed.

The disciple of the all-wicked devil danced, and received thy head as a reward, O Forerunner! O feast full of blood! It would have been better for thee not to have sworn, O lawless Herod, offspring of falsehood! Or if thou didst swear, thou didst not swear unto good: it were better to have lied and preserved life than, having kept the truth, to have cut off the head of the Forerunner! Yet we, worthily honoring the Baptist as the greatest of those born of women, call him blessed. *Twice.*

It was not fitting for thee, O Herod, because of satanic desire and the kindle of frantic lust, to condemn unto death the rebuker of adultery; it was not fitting for thee because of the oath for the dancing, wretchedly to surrender that all-honorable head unto an all-lawless woman! O how didst thou dare to commit such a murder? And how was the defiled dancer not consumed by fire as she carried it upon a platter in the midst of the banquet? Yet we, worthily honoring the Baptist as the greatest of those born of women, call him blessed. *Twice.*

Again Herodias rages, again she is troubled! O deceitful dance, and feast of guile! The Baptist was beheaded, and Herod was troubled. By the prayers of Thy Forerunner, O Lord, grant peace unto our souls. *Twice.*

*Glory, Tone the same [Tone 6]:*
While the birthday of shameless Herod was being celebrated, the agreement of the oath was fulfilled for the defiled dancer; for the head of the Forerunner, having been cut off, was carried upon a platter like meat unto the guests. O loathsome feast, an unholy deed, full of murder! Yet we, worthily honoring the Baptist as the greatest of those born of women, call him blessed.

*And now, Theotokion [Dogmatikon, Tone 6]: Who will not bless thee... (Кто̀ тебѐ не оу҆блажи́тъ).*

*Entrance. Prokeimenon of the day, and the Readings.*

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Isaiah [Chapter 40 composite]:*
Thus says the Lord: Comfort ye, comfort ye My people, says God. Ye priests, speak unto the heart of Jerusalem, implore her, for her humiliation is multiplied, for her sin is loosed, for she has received double from the Lord's hand for her sins. The voice of one crying in the wilderness: Prepare ye the way of the Lord, make straight the paths of our God! Ascend into a high mountain, O thou that proclaimest good tidings unto Zion! Lift up thy voice with strength, O thou that proclaimest good tidings unto Jerusalem; lift it up, be not afraid! I am the Lord God, I will hear the poor in Israel, and I will not forsake them. But I will open rivers from the mountains, and fountains in the midst of the field; I will make the wilderness a meadow, and the thirsty land watery springs. Let heaven above rejoice, and let the clouds drop righteousness; let the earth shine forth, and let mercy blossom, and let righteousness spring up together! Proclaim the voice of gladness, and let it be heard; proclaim it even unto the ends of the earth! Say that the Lord has redeemed His servant Jacob. And if they thirst in the wilderness, He shall pour forth water for them out of the rock. Rejoice, thou barren that bearest not; break forth and cry, thou that travailest not; for the desolate has many more children than she that has a husband![^869]

*The Reading from the Prophecy of Malachi [Chapter 3]:*
Thus says the Lord Almighty: Behold, I send My messenger before Thy face, who shall prepare Thy way before Thee; and there shall come and the Lord Whom you seek shall come to His temple; and who shall endure the day of His coming? For He is coming like a refiner's fire, and like fullers' soap. And He shall purify, melting and refining, as silver and gold. And He shall draw near to you in judgment, and He shall be a swift witness against the sorcerers, and against the adulterers, and against those who swear falsely by My name, and against those who withhold the hireling's wage, and oppress the widow, and buffet the fatherless, and turn aside the judgment of the stranger, and fear Him not, says the Lord Almighty. For I am the Lord your God, and I change not; and you, sons of Jacob, have turned aside from the law and have not kept it. Therefore return to Me, and I will return to you, says the Lord Almighty; and all the nations shall call you blessed. And understand that I am the Lord, Who discerns between the righteous and between the lawless on that day wherein I will make provision for them that love Me. Know therefore and remember the Law of Moses My servant, according as He commanded you in Horeb for all Israel, with the statutes and ordinances. And behold, I will send you Elijah the Tishbite[^870], before the great and glorious day of the Lord comes; who shall turn the heart of the father to the son, and the heart of a man to his neighbor, lest I come and strike the earth suddenly, says the Lord Almighty, the Holy God of Israel.

*The Reading from the Wisdom of Solomon. Chapter 4.*[^871]
Though the righteous man happen to die, he shall be at rest. And the righteous man when dying shall condemn the ungodly who are living. For they shall see the end of the righteous man, and shall not understand what was counseled concerning him; for the Lord shall cast down the ungodly, speechless, prone; and He shall shake them from the foundations, and they shall be utterly desolated in anguish, and their memorial shall perish. For they shall come in terror to the reckoning of their sins, and their lawlessness shall convict them to their face. Then shall the righteous man stand in great boldness before the face of those that afflicted him and set at naught his labors. And when they see him, they shall be troubled with great fear, and shall be amazed at his glorious salvation. For they shall say within themselves, repenting, and groaning in anguish of spirit, and they shall say: "This was he whom we once held in derision, and as a proverb of reproach, fools that we were! We accounted his life madness, and his end without honor. How is he numbered among the sons of God, and his lot is among the saints? Therefore we have strayed from the way of truth, and the light of righteousness has not shined upon us, and the sun rose not upon us. We filled ourselves with the paths of lawlessness and destruction, and we walked through pathless ways, but the way of the Lord we knew not."

*At the Litiya, stichera idiomela, Tone 1. By Patriarch Germanus:*
What shall we call thee, O Prophet? An angel? An apostle? Or a martyr? An angel, for thou didst live as though bodiless; an apostle, for thou didst teach the nations; and a martyr, for thy head was severed for Christ. Beseech Him to have mercy on our souls.

We celebrate the memory of the Forerunner's most hallowed head, which then indeed poured forth blood upon a platter, but now throughout the ends of the earth pours forth healings.

Today the mother, fashioner of unseemly deeds, through the murderous counsel of her lawless intercourse, craftily set forth her all-defiled daughter against him that was divinely revealed as greater than all the prophets. For while the hostile Herod was making a feast for his defiled birthday, she contrived that she should ask with an oath for the marvelous, venerable head of the preacher of God. And the all-foolish one accomplished it, giving it as a reward, an oath-bound gift, to a woman's dancing. Yet the initiate of Christ's coming ceased not, even after his death, to rebuke their god-hated mingling; but rebuking, he cried aloud, saying: "It is not lawful for thee to commit adultery with the wife of thy brother Philip!" O prophetic-murdering birthday, and feast filled with bloodshed! But we, with pious wisdom, celebrating the beheading of the Forerunner, keep festival in bright vesture[^872], rejoicing as on a chosen auspicious day; and let us beseech him to pray earnestly to the Trinity to deliver us from shameful passions and to save our souls.

*Tone 4:*
An unseemly judgment and a most shameless feast did Herod perform: for flattered by a woman's adultery and incited by impious female frenzy, he severed the head of the Forerunner; yet he severed not the prophetic tongue that rebuked his lawlessness. He shed innocent blood, wishing to conceal his lawless sin; yet not even thus did he stifle the voice that cries repentance unto all. He indeed rejoiced in murder; but we celebrate with love the blessed slaying of the Baptist John. For he anticipated life in Hades, to preach to those sitting in darkness and the shadow of death the Dayspring from on high, Christ our God, the only greatly merciful.

Come, O you people, let us praise the Prophet, Martyr, and Baptist of the Savior: for being an angel in the flesh, he rebuked Herod, condemning him for the deed of his most lawless adultery; and for the sake of a lawless dance his venerable head was severed. Unto those in Hades he proclaimed the good tidings of the resurrection from the dead, and he prays fervently to the Lord that our souls may be saved.

Come, O you people, let us praise the Prophet, Martyr, and Baptist of the Savior: for fleeing, he dwelt in the wilderness, eating wild honey and locusts; he rebuked the king who acted lawlessly, and he comforted our faintheartedness, saying: "Repent, for the Kingdom of Heaven is at hand!"

*Glory... Tone 5. By John the Monk:*
Scheming to escape the rebuke of his lawless deed, Herod wickedly delivered thy head, O Forerunner, to a lawless woman; for the wretched one understood not that he was rebuking himself in having it carried about upon a platter. But as an active teacher of purity and a saving guide to repentance, pray thou, O Baptist of Christ, that we may be delivered from shameful passions.

*Both now... The same Tone:*
Sing, O you people, sing to the Mother of our God! For today she commends her all-radiant soul into the immaculate hands of Him Who was incarnate of her without seed; Whom also she entreats unceasingly to grant peace and great mercy to the universe.

*At the Aposticha, stichera idiomela, Tone 2:*
O John the Baptist, preacher of repentance: when thy head was severed, thou didst sanctify the earth, for thou didst clarify the Law of God to the faithful and didst destroy lawlessness. As thou standest before the throne of Christ, the Heavenly King, beseech Him to have mercy on our souls.

*Verse: The righteous shall flourish like a palm tree; he shall grow like a cedar in Lebanon.*[^873]
For the sake of the Law of the Lord thou wast beheaded, O all-holy John! With blameless boldness thou didst rebuke the wicked king who acted lawlessly. Therefore the heavenly angelic powers marvel at thee, and the choirs of apostles and martyrs glorify thee. And we also honor thine annual memory, O all-glorious one, glorifying the Holy Trinity Who crowned thee, O blessed Forerunner!

*Verse: The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him.*[^874]
He that was a prophet from a prophet, and greater than the prophets, sanctified from his mother's womb for the service of the Lord, was this day beheaded by a lawless king; and having shamefully danced, the young maid clearly rebuking both before his beheading and after his beheading, he put to shame the army of sin; and therefore we cry aloud: O Baptist John, having boldness, pray fervently for our souls!

*Glory... Tone 8:*
O Forerunner of the Savior, thou didst rebuke the king, that he should not commit lawlessness. Therefore the dancing of the lawless woman compelled Herod to sever thy head; and therefore from the rising of the sun unto the setting thereof thy name is praised. Having boldness before the Lord, pray fervently that our souls may be saved.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O unwedded Virgin...[^875]

*At the blessing of the loaves, the troparion twice, written at Small Vespers; and Rejoice, O Virgin Theotokos once, and a reading.*

*At Matins, after the first Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5:*
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-beginningless with the Father and the Spirit" (Собезначалное слово):*
Assembling together, O faithful, let us praise with one accord the mediator of the Law and grace; for he preached repentance to us, and openly rebuking Herod with boldness, he was severed in his head; and now living with the angels, he entreats Christ that we may be saved.

*Glory... Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Явился еси днесь):*
Celebrating thy radiant memory today, we beseech thee, O Forerunner, fervently to entreat the Savior and Lord to grant forgiveness of sins unto all.

*Both now... Theotokion, the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
A fervent protection to those in afflictions, and our helper, and reconciliation with God, through whom we have been delivered from corruption: O faithful, we call the Theotokos blessed.

*After the second Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 5:*
*Tone 5, Podoben: "Co-beginningless with the Father and the Spirit" (Собезначалное слово):*
Him that was revealed to us from the womb as a prophet, and that shone forth clearly from a barren woman as a luminary to the universe, in hymns let us praise: Christ's Baptist and victorious sufferer, the Forerunner John. For he entreats the Lord that our souls may be shown mercy.

*Glory... Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph was amazed" (Оудивися їѡсифъ):*
Now has appeared to us the Savior's Baptist, and spiritually gladens the minds of the faithful: the beauty of the wilderness, and the seal of the prophets. Therefore he was shown forth as the Forerunner of Christ, and an unfailing witness of His coming. With spiritual hymns therefore let us cry aloud to John with one accord: O prophet and preacher of truth, pray that we may be saved!

*Both now... Theotokion, the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
All the choirs of angels were amazed, O pure one, at the dread mystery of thy childbearing: how He Who holds all things by a single nod is held in thine arms as a man is held as a man, and the Pre-eternal receives a beginning; and He Who nourishes all breath is nourished with milk by His unutterable goodness! And praising thee as truly the Mother of God, they glorify Him.

*After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "The shepherds' pipes" (Свирѣлей пастырскихъ):*
The Forerunner, the Baptist, and the angel of the Redeemer of all, O faithful, rebuking Herod for the lawlessness of Herodias, dies, having his head severed, that with boldness he might proclaim the good tidings unto those in Hades that God descends thither to lead captivity captive. *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
The Father's Word descended upon earth, and a radiant angel said to the Theotokos: "Rejoice, blessed one, who alone hast preserved thy bridal chamber, receiving the conception of the Pre-eternal God and Lord, that as God He may save the human race from deception!"

*The Graduals: The first Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: Precious in the sight of the Lord is the death of His saints. Verse: What shall I render unto the Lord for all that He has rendered unto me?*[^876]
*Let every breath praise the Lord.*
*The Gospel according to Matthew, pericope 57.*[^877]

*After Psalm 50, sticheron, Tone 6:*
The disciple of the all-evil devil danced, and received thy head, O Forerunner, as a reward. O feast filled with bloodshed! Better had it been not to swear, O lawless Herod, grandson of lies! Even if thou didst swear, yet thou didst not swear well: better had it been to obtain life by lying than, having kept the truth, to sever the Forerunner's head! But we, worthily honoring the Baptist as the greatest among those born of women, call him blessed.

*Two Canons: the first with the Heirmos to eight. By John the Monk, Tone 8:*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Traversing the water as dry land, and escaping the evil of Egypt, the Israelite cried aloud: Let us sing unto our Redeemer and God!*
*Troparia:*
Let us hymn the sacred Forerunner, who was revealed from a barren womb as a prophet unto Him Who was borne ineffably within the virginal womb.

Transcending the bounds of nature, thou didst keep the laws of righteousness: rebuking the lawless union, thou didst not fear the king's cruelty.

Nurtured on the milk of the Law, confirming the lawful ordinances of marriage as the seal of the Law, thou didst oppose abominable adultery.

*Theotokion:*
The ranks of angels and men, O unwedded Mother, praise thee unceasingly; for thou didst bear in thine arms as an infant the Creator of these.

*Another Canon, to six. By Lord Andrew of Crete By Lord Andrew of Crete. The same Tone:*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*To Him Who shook down the tyranny of Pharaoh in the sea, and guided Israel through dry land, let us sing unto Christ: for He has been glorified unto ages!*
*Troparia:*
The fair adornment of the wilderness[^878], the fruit of Zechariah, and Elizabeth's offspring beyond all hope, commands the faithful to celebrate his venerable memory.

O birthday filled with blood! O abominable feast! For Herod mingled murder with drunkenness, and his banquet table was filled with bitter, foul slaughter.

Rebuking Herod who acted lawlessly, O faithful, and enduring the severing of his head, let us hymn the Baptist, as one put to death for zeal for God.

*Theotokion:*
Thou didst bear, O pure Mother of God, the uncircumscribed Word of God, Who took up His abode wholly within thee: twofold, as the one only Son, incarnate of thee ineffably.

*Katavasia: Moses, having inscribed the Cross...*[^879]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*O Lord, Supreme Creator of the celestial vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou establish me in Thy love, O Summit of desires, Firm Foundation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.*
*Troparia:*
As the mediator of the ancient things and the forerunner of the new evangelical proclamations, thou didst stand forth and rebuke the tyrant's lawless mingling; and rejoicing, thou didst receive a glorious death.

Instructed beforehand by her lawless mother, the maiden said to Herod who was softened with drunkenness: "Give me John's head upon a platter, that I may give it to my mother as a desired gift!"

Enduring not the rebuke of thy God-bearing tongue, O glorious Forerunner, the shameless tyrant delivers to the maiden, for a woman's dancing, thy venerable, suffering head, O all-glorious one!

*Theotokion:*
Taking up Thine abode bodily within the Virgin, O Lord, Thou wast revealed to men as it was fitting that Thou shouldest be seen; and her Thou didst show forth as truly the Theotokos and help of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.

*Another Heirmos:*
*There is none holy as the Lord, and there is none righteous as our God, Whom all creation hymns: there is none righteous above Thee, O Lord.*
*Troparia:*
"A greater has not arisen among those born of women than John," cries Christ the Truth; for in the womb the creation recognized the Creator, and proclaimed Him with his mother's voice.

First with thy finger showing the Lamb, and secondly with thy tongue rebuking Herod, thirdly with thy head thou pourest forth miracles as a restored vessel[^880] unto us.

Thy birth was glorious, O Baptist of Christ, and thy life blameless, and thy departure honorable, and thy manifestation marvelous; thy death was precious, and thy burial glorious.

*Theotokion:*
Being God, Thou didst empty Thyself, O Only-begotten, that Thou mightest save what Thou hadst created; for whose sake Thou didst become man, taking flesh from the Virgin, and having transformed all my nature, Thou didst bring it unto Thy Father.

*Katavasia: The rod is taken as an image...*[^881]

*Sessional Hymn, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премудрости):*
Having sprouted from a barren woman by the judgment of God, and having loosed the bonds of thy father's tongue, thou didst show forth the Morning Star that illumines the Sun; and in the wilderness thou didst preach to the people the Creator, the Lamb Who takes away the sins of the world. Therefore with zeal thou didst rebuke the king. In thy glorious head thou wast severed, O ever-memorable and all-praised John! Beseech Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who celebrate thy holy memory with love. *Twice.*

*Glory... Both now... Theotokion:*
As the Virgin and unique among women, who without seed didst bear God in the flesh, all we generations of mankind call thee blessed: for the fire of the Godhead dwelt in thee, and as an infant thou dost nourish with milk the Creator and Lord. Therefore, both the angelic and human race, we worthily glorify thy most holy childbearing, and with one accord cry aloud to thee: Beseech Christ God to grant remission of transgressions unto those who venerate in faith thy most holy childbearing!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who didst not leave the bosom of the Father, and didst visit our poverty. Therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!*
*Troparia:*
He that was subject to the torment of the Law could not endure the boldness of rebuke; nor could he that was corrupted with defiled pleasures endure the boldness of piety, but bound and kept him who, even before the end, was immaterially joined unto the heavenly choirs.

Afflicted with soul-destroying drunkenness and inflamed with lustful burning, the wretched one was handed over; bound by the foot-dancing of a maiden, he becomes the prophet's murderer. For drunkenness conceived the mother of fornication, and brought forth grievous lawlessness.

Truly in thee the divine voice lied not: for thou art the greatest among the prophets, as Thou wast deemed worthy of prophesying from the very womb, in an imperfect body; and having seen the God-Word Whom Thou didst foretell, Thou didst also baptize Him in the body.

*Theotokion:*
Thou art the boast of the faithful, O unwedded one; thou art the protectress, thou art the refuge of Christians, a rampart and a haven: for unto thy Son thou bearest supplications, O all-immaculate one, and dost save from afflictions those that know thee with faith and love as the pure Theotokos.

*Another Heirmos:*
*From the overshadowed mountain, O Word, the prophet perceived Thee Who wast to be incarnate of the only Theotokos, with divinely illuminated vision, and with fear he glorified Thy power.*
*Troparia:*
Thou didst make clear to us the Kingdom of Christ; thou didst show to the people the paths of repentance, and thou didst rebuke Herod who acted lawlessly, O great preacher, wise John!

Herod, unable to endure rebuke, O Baptist, was overcome with wrath, envy, and bitterness; and he waited for the time of his birthday, wherein he severed thy head, O preacher of Christ!

Rejoice, O Baptist, let thy spirit leap for joy! For even there thou dost rebuke godless Herod, and to the souls in Hades thou dost preach, saying: "Our salvation has drawn near!"

The earth marvels at thy life, O John; the heaven proclaims thine unjust slaying; and the Church, singing hymns, recounts the multitude of thy virtues.

*Theotokion:*
Born of an immaculate Virgin Mother, Thou didst preserve her purity after childbearing, Who didst clothe Thyself wholly in my nature, apart from sin.

*Katavasia: I have heard, O Lord...*[^882]

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Therefore hast Thou cast me away from Thy face, O Light never-setting, and an alien darkness has covered me, the wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my ways to the light of Thy commandments, I pray.*
*Troparia:*
By her mother's instigations, as the price of a demonic dance, the fierce lion's whelp, the fiercest offspring, [demanded] the head of the Forerunner and preacher, which even the wild beasts themselves in the wilderness revered!

O Thy unutterable and unsearchable judgments, O Lover of mankind! For him who while yet in his mother's womb became the receptacle of the Holy Spirit, and who grew up in temperance and purity, an unclean maiden danced away!

Unto him who willingly and heartily chose lawless cohabitation, at his birthday drinking, and, O Prophet, to add murder; and through voluptuousness to mingle a cup filled with holy and prophetic blood.

*Theotokion:*
Having a mother's boldness toward thy Son, O all-pure one, despise not, we pray, a kin-like care for us, since thee alone do Christians present unto the Master as a merciful propitiation.

*Another Heirmos:*
*Having enlightened the ends of the earth from the night of ignorance through the knowledge of God, enlighten me with the morning dawn of Thy love for mankind, O Lord.*
*Troparia:*
The adornment of the wilderness and possession of the universe, the Forerunner, today prepares a spiritual banquet for us.

Thou didst rebuke Herod, O John, as a slave of pleasures and a transgressor of the laws, a bitter paramour of Herodias.

Herodias dances, John is bound and slain: O Herod's drunkenness! O the senselessness of his soul!

*Theotokion:*
Rejoice, O divine Mountain, from which a stone was cut without hands: O all-pure and all-immaculate one, who didst bear God the Word in the flesh!

*Katavasia: O thrice-blessed Tree...*[^883]

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Cleanse me, O Savior, for many are my lawless deeds, and raise me up from the depths of evils, I pray; for unto Thee have I cried, and hear me, O God of my salvation!*
*Troparia:*
Witnessing concerning the commandments of the Law, O blessed one, by rebukes thou dost chastise him that acted lawlessly; for thou wast not a reed shaken by the blast of the enemy's winds.

Thy head, dripping with the blood of slaying, was brought as a reward for a lustful deed, rebuking Herod even after thy death, for having confounded nature.

Walking in the wilderness, clad in camel's hair: in both ways thou didst live radiantly as the Church; and in both ways thou didst carry about the beauty of the Church: thou didst reign over passions.

*Theotokion:*
That we may be delivered from grievous transgressions through thy prayers, O pure Mother of God, and that we may obtain the most radiant divine illumination of the Son of God, Who was ineffably incarnate of thee.

*Another Heirmos:*
*As Thou didst deliver the prophet from the nethermost depths, O Christ God, so also deliver me from my sins, as Lover of mankind, and direct my life, I pray.*
*Troparia:*
The severing of thy venerable and all-praised head, O Forerunner of Christ honoring, O Baptist of Christ, we glorify thy ever-praised and worldwide memory, O most blessed one!

O defiled banquet! O bitter birthday! O defiled drunkenness of lawless Herod! Bound unto lawlessness and rebuked, he was moved to slay the prophet.

The new Egyptian woman, dancing defiledly in the midst of the banquet, asked for the head of him that rebuked her mother and Herod's concubine.

*Theotokion:*
He Who is now the Son of the Virgin was previously revealed as the Son of the Father, not understood as another from the visible one, though He remained one and perfect in both.

*Katavasia: In the belly of the watery beast...*[^884]

*Kontakion, Tone 5:*
The glorious beheading of the Forerunner was a divine dispensation, that he might preach the Savior's coming even to those in Hades. Let Herodias therefore weep, having asked for a lawless murder; for she loved neither the Law of God nor the living age, but a simulated, fleeting one.

*Ikos:*
Herod's birthday appeared unholy unto all, when in the midst of the feasters the head of the faster was set forth as food; sorrow was joined to joy, and bitter weeping was mingled with laughter; as carrying the Baptist's head upon a platter, the maiden entered before all, even as she had said. And because of the oath, weeping fell upon all who were then reclining with the king; for it gladdened neither them nor Herod himself: for it was said that they were grieved with a sorrow that was not true, but simulated and fleeting.

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The fire was ashamed before God's condescension in Babylon of old; therefore the Children in the furnace, dancing with joyful step as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*
*Troparia:*
Held by lustful desire and inflamed[^885] with drunkenness, lawless Herod dashed against and broke himself upon the unbending pillar of self-control and the indestructible citadel of temperance, Christ's Baptist.

She was not terrified, O Forerunner, she was not ashamed, she faltered not, who was instructed by the devil, bringing thy venerable head shamelessly upon a platter, having seduced her mind by her mother's instigation.

Thou didst shine forth as a luminary, and wast sent beforehand as an angel; as a prophet thou didst preach the Lamb of God, Christ Who appeared; as a martyr thou wast severed in the head by the sword, preannouncing Him also unto those in Hades unto the dead in Hades.

*Theotokion:*
Having been delivered by thy childbearing from the condemnation of the ancient fall, O Virgin Mother, we ever glorify thee as the manifest cause of freedom, together with thy Son, Who gave Himself as a ransom for us.

*Another Heirmos:*
*Thou Who in the beginning didst found the earth, and by the Word didst establish the heavens: Blessed art Thou unto the ages, O Lord God of our fathers!*

*Troparia:*
Preaching the Law of God, John was not afraid to bring to chastity Herod the wicked, who acted lawlessly; for the fire of his sin was within him.

From the head that clearly legislated, and from the earth crying aloud unto Herod: It is not lawful for thee who actest lawlessly to wear the purple as a tyrannical garment!

Armed with defiled weapons, lawless Herod fulfilled his lawlessness through an oath, having there the drunkenness of gluttony that pandered unto him.

*Theotokion:*
Being bodiless, He was unchangeably incarnate; being timeless, He was recognized in time from the Virgin: the Creator of all, remaining unconfusedly as He was, and becoming what He was not.

*Katavasia: The senseless decree...*[^886]

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*The Chaldean tyrant furiously kindled the furnace sevenfold for the godly; but having seen them saved by a higher power, he cried unto the Creator and Deliverer: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparia:*
He who went before Thy Nativity and Thy divine Passion, by the sword becomes in the nethermost regions a prophet and messenger of Thy coming there, as John the voice of the Word, crying aloud: Ye dead, [exalt] the Giver of life; ye blind, the Giver of light; ye captives, the Deliverer: supremely exalt Christ unto all ages!

The forerunner from a barren woman of the virginal childbearing, has now by the beheading of his head become the predecessor of the voluntary crucifixion of Him Who made all things, crying unto those in Hades: Ye children, bless; ye priests, sing praises; ye people, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

When thy head was severed from the body, O Forerunner, by thy flesh the Master's soul was separated therefrom; but the Godhead was in no wise separated from the flesh of Emmanuel, nor was a bone of God and Master broken: therefore we supremely exalt Him unto all ages.

*Theotokion:*
O all-pure Mother of God, the wounds and festerings of my soul, by thy maternal supplication, O Virgin cleanse, and raise me up who have fallen! Save me, O all-blameless one, the prodigal, save me! For thou art my protectress and helper, only pure and blessed unto all ages.

*Another Heirmos:*
*Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain, and in the bush by fire revealed to Moses the mystery of the Ever-Virgin: Sing ye to the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparia:*
Thou didst preach Christ's coming unto those in Hades, announcing with faith: The Lord of glory comes to loose pains, Whom we supremely exalt unto all ages.

Herod shut thee up in prison, O John, the second Elijah; and having bound thee with iron bonds, he slew the sacred preacher of repentance and the eternal Kingdom.

O table filled with blood! O food mingled with murder! O banquet filled with sacrilege! O the inhumanity and foul murder of lawless Herod!

*Theotokion:*
Wholly on high and wholly below, O Word, in the bosom of the Father and of Thy Mother: wholly God and wholly man by nature, Thou art one in Hypostasis and twofold in nature, O Merciful One!

*Katavasia: Bless, ye children...*[^887]

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Every ear was terrified at God's ineffable condescension, that the Most High voluntarily came down even unto the flesh, becoming man from the virginal womb. Therefore, O all-pure Theotokos, we the faithful magnify thee.*

*Troparia:*
The evil host was terrified, and its leader the devil, of thy divinely-speaking tongue, O prophet, which preached Christ; but through the defiled maiden, he compelled Herod to sever thy head. Yet we magnify thee, the Baptist!

The lowly valley of nature was exalted, and the hill of mortal pride was brought low; for hearing the voice of him that cried in the wildernesses of light, he cried aloud in the dwellings of Hades: "Lift up the gates, for the King of all enters!"

Human passions tremble, and demons flee in fear at the overshadowing of the grace given thee by God. But from surrounding temptations deliver thy flock, O Forerunner of the Lord, which ever magnifies thee in faith!

*Theotokion:*
Prosper, and proceed, and reign, O Son of the Mother of God, subduing the Ishmaelite people who fight against us, granting victories unto the Orthodox King over the barbarian enemies, by the supplications of her who bore Thee, O Word of God!

*Another Heirmos:*
*The childbearing of the Ever-Virgin, which was prefigured on the mountain to the Lawgiver in fire and the bush, for the salvation of us the faithful: with unceasing hymns we magnify.*

*Troparia:*
Again Jezebel exults against Elijah, again the Egyptian woman seeks Joseph: against the Prophet and Baptist of the Savior, now has Herodias appeared, the defiled one!

Having been beheaded in the head by Herod, O John, thou wast sent unto the souls in Hades, to preach Him Who is to come, and to save them all, the faithful who had fallen asleep before, from Adam.

Being the lamp of the Light, and the voice of the Word, the preacher of the Lamb of God, the sheep of the wilderness, O John, honorable martyr of the Lord: pray that they who hymn thee may be saved!

*Theotokion:*
Alone among women didst thou appear as a virgin after childbearing; alone among women wast thou shown forth as the Mother of God; alone didst thou loose Eve from pangs, and dry up the primal curse.

*Katavasia: Thou art a mystical Paradise, O Theotokos...*[^888]

*Exapostilarion:*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "As the disciples were looking on" (Оученикѡмъ зрящым):*
Recognized as greater among the prophets, and having been pre-chosen above the Apostles, with laudatory hymns we honor the Forerunner of grace; for he was beheaded in the head for the sake of the Law of the Lord.

*Glory:*
Defiled Herod deceitfully severed thy head, O planter of purity, Baptist of the Savior; yet the rebukes of thy tongue he could in no wise cut down.

*Both now... Theotokion:*
Having destroyed the curse of the world by thy divine childbearing, O pure one: deliver thy flock, which faithfully prays unto thee, from all afflictions by thy prayers, O Maiden!

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4, Tone 8:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O most marvelous wonder" (Ѽ преславнагѡ чудесе):*
O most marvelous wonder! * The sacred head, reverenced even by the angels, * an unclean, adulterous maiden carried, * which rebuked the lawless deed, * and brought it to her adulterous mother. * O Thine ineffable long-suffering, O Lover of mankind! * Whereby, O Christ, save our souls, * as Thou alone art compassionate! *Twice.*

O the hardness of Herod's heart! * Having dishonored God by transgressions of the Law, * He deceitfully hypocrites under pretense of keeping an oath, * and adds murder unto adultery, * feigning to grieve! * O Thine unutterable mercy, O Master! * Whereby, O Christ, save our souls, * as Thou alone art compassionate!

O the wonder beyond understanding! * The seal of the prophets, the earthly angel an earthly angel * is shown forth as the price for lustful dancing; * the divinely-speaking tongue, * Christ's herald, is sent beforehand even unto those in Hades! * O Thine unutterable providence, O Master! * Whereby, O Christ, save our souls, * as Thou alone art compassionate!

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Again Herodias rages, again she is troubled! O deceitful dancing, and banquet of deceit! The Baptist was beheaded, and Herod was troubled. By the prayers of Thy Forerunner, O Lord, grant peace unto our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
*O Theotokos, thou art the true vine...*[^889]

*The Great Doxology. After the Trisagion, Troparion of the Forerunner. Glory... Both now... All beyond understanding... Litanies and dismissal. And holy oil is given to the brethren from the lamp of the holy Forerunner. And 1st Kathisma reading. At the 1st Hour, Troparion and Kontakion of the Forerunner. And final dismissal.*

*At the Liturgy: The Beatitudes from the 1st Canon, Ode 3, to 4; and of the 2nd [Canon], Ode 6, to 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 7: The righteous man shall be glad in the Lord, and shall hope in Him [LXX Ps. 63:11].* *Verse: Give ear, O God, unto my prayer, when I pray unto Thee [LXX Ps. 63:2].* *Epistle: Acts of the Apostles, pericope 33 [Acts 13:25–32].[^890]* *Alleluia, Tone 4: The righteous man shall flourish like a palm tree [LXX Ps. 91:13].* *Verse: They that are planted in the house of the Lord shall flourish in the courts of the house of our God [LXX Ps. 91:14]. Gospel according to Mark, pericope 24 [Mk. 6:14–30].[^891]* *Communion Hymn: In everlasting memory shall the righteous be [LXX Ps. 111:6b]. At the trapeza there is consolation for the brethren, except cheese, eggs, and fish; and we eat twice a day, even if it be Wednesday or Friday.*

*Mark: Be it known that if this feast occur on a Sunday, its entire service takes precedence at Vespers, at Matins, and at the Liturgy.*

*Mark: Concerning this fasting rule for the Beheading of the honorable Head of the Forerunner, ordained by the ancient Holy Fathers on this day.*
*There are indeed some who prescribe that there be a quality of foods then, and not fasting; for concerning this we were able to find nothing written in the various Typika. But it is rather fitting on that day in every way to abstain by fasting, for the sake of the Prophet who suffered for the truth and preached the Lord in Hades. And let the dispensation be only for olive oil, and the rest from various orchard fruits, and by measure a single cup of wine unto the glory of God, on account of the labor of the vigil. For in every way it is fitting for us on that day to be with downcast lamentation, and not to indulge in gluttony, for the sake of the abstemious life of the saint, and his foul murderous bloodshed by Herod for the head of the Forerunner was severed at a banquet through the dancing of a lustful dancer, and gluttony, and the slaughter of various animals and bloodshed. But unto us it is not fitting to taste even one of such animals from which blood is poured out, nor even fish (for they have blood, even if they live in the water, and are of the creaturely nature of animals), lest we be shown forth as partakers of Herod's gluttony. For how shall we wish to honor the honorable beheading of the Baptist? Shall we wish to eat meat, or of other various costly foods? Yet he lived in a waterless and grassless wilderness: he ate no bread, neither did he have a table! Shall we drink wine? Yet he never drank wine, nor any other worldly drink; neither did he live in houses, but lived dug in beneath a rock. A table, and banquet, and bed for him was the earth; only for the sake of natural necessity, once a day he tasted locusts and wild honey: his cup was his hands cupped together, and his drink was water flowing from the rock. Therefore, brethren, let us fervently honor this day with prayer and fasting in chastity, and let us flee from this evil disposition of drunkenness; for if anyone be held by unbelief, and keep not this ordained, most discerning command, such a one shall evilly suffer ruinous afflictions. But those that rightly maintain this contest, O Lord, bless Thou, and guide by the prayers of the holy Forerunner!*

*In the same month, on the 30th day.*
*Our Holy Fathers the Patriarchs of Constantinople: Alexander, John, and Paul the New:*[^892]

*At "Lord, I have cried," Stichera of the Forerunner, 3; and of the Saints, 3. Stichera of the Forerunner, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Called from on High" (Зва́нный свы́ше):*
The divine mediator of the Old and the New [Covenants], * the Prophet and Forerunner, * the angel in the flesh, * and rebuker of lawlessness, * the offspring of a barren womb, * the fire-breathing mouth: * thou didst rebuke Herod, * who did lawless things; * who, unable to endure rebuke, * commands thy head to be severed; * and it was brought as the food of temperance, * a divine delight upon a platter, * all the more rebuking him who committed adultery!

Instigated by her lawless mother, * the lawless daughter * asks for thy most glorious head, revered even by the angels; * for unable to endure these rebukes, * she brought it upon a platter; * and dancing with her feet, * she turned the joy of the rejoicers into weeping! * But all the more having rebuked * the adulterous abomination, O blessed one: * pray unto Christ, * that our souls may be saved!

Herod, made foolish through drunkenness,[^893] * is stolen away by lust through the tunes of dancing; * and unto an unrighteous oath * he works thy murder, * O glorious foremost prophet! * But [thou wast sent] unto death, * to manifestly announce immortality * unto those who died before; * for thou becamest a preacher * and forerunner even unto those in Hades, * preannouncing the saving coming * of Christ our God: * Whom beseech, * to save and enlighten our souls!

*Other Stichera of the Saints, Tone 1:*
*Tone 1, Podoben: "O all-lauded martyrs" (Всехва́льнїи му́ченницы):*
O all-blessed Alexander, * thou wast a shepherd of the Church, * a champion of Orthodoxy. * Having wisely cast down the heresy of Arius * by thy prayers, O all-wise one, * thou didst pray that his soul be evilly cast out * in foul places, O glorious one! * Therefore with one accord we honor thee.

O honorable John, * thou didst hate the corruption of the flesh * by temperance and faith, * and by vigilance and confession; * thou wast found to be an earthly angel * and a heavenly man. * Pray unto Christ, * to grant great mercy * unto our souls!

Unto the divine teachings of Paul, * O wise one, thou didst truly submit thyself, * and as unto the third heaven thou didst ascend, O glorious one, * unto the height of virtues; * and thou didst hear ineffable * and divine voices of the Spirit, * O blessed Paul, * unshakable degree of the Orthodox! * Make Him gracious unto us by thy prayers!

*Glory, Tone 6:*
Again Herodias rages, again she is troubled! O deceitful dancing, and banquet of deceit! The Baptist was beheaded, and Herod was troubled. By the prayers of Thy Forerunner, O Lord, grant peace unto our souls!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 6, Podoben: "Having triumphed on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ):*
Beholding Thee crucified, O Christ, * she that bore Thee cried aloud: * What strange mystery is this that I behold, O my Son? * How dost Thou die in the flesh, suspended upon the Tree, * O Giver of life?

*At the Aposticha, Stichera of the Octoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 4:*
An unfitting birthday and a most shameless feast did Herod celebrate; for being sweetened by womanish wantonness, and spurred on by unclean female frenzy, he severed the head of the Forerunner; yet he severed not the prophetic tongue that rebuked his folly! He poured out innocent blood, wishing to cover the lawless sin; yet not even thus did he hide the voice crying unto all repentance! He indeed rejoiced in murder; but we celebrate together with love the blessed slaying of the Baptist John: for he went before Life into Hades, to preach unto those sitting in darkness and the shadow of death: Christ our God, the Dayspring from on high, the only plenteous in mercy!

*Both now... Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
When she who bore Thee, the ewe-lamb, * beheld Thee upon the Tree, * the Lamb and Shepherd, * she lamented and maternally spoke to Thee: * O Son most desired! * How art Thou suspended upon the Tree of the Cross, O Long-suffering One? * How were Thy hands and feet nailed by the lawless, O Word, * and Thou hast poured out Thy blood, O Master?

*Troparion [of the Forerunner], Tone 2:*
The memory of the righteous man is celebrated with praises, but the testimony of the Lord is sufficient for thee, O Forerunner; for thou wast shown forth truly more honorable than the prophets, since thou wast deemed worthy to baptize in the streams Him Whom thou didst preach. Therefore, having suffered for the truth with joy, thou didst proclaim the good tidings even unto those in Hades of God Who appeared in the flesh, Who takes away the sin of the world and grants us great mercy.

*Glory... of the Saints, Tone 4:*
O God of our fathers, Who dealest ever with us according to Thy meekness: take not Thy mercy away from us, but through their supplications guide our life in peace!

*At Matins: One Canon of the Octoechos; and of the Forerunner to 4, of yesterday; and of the Saints to 4. Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Let us sing unto the Lord, Who led His people through the Red Sea; for He alone is gloriously glorified!*

*Troparia:*
The thrice-radiant dawn of the fathers, their all-glorious memory, has shone forth today; enjoying which faithfully, let us leap for joy!

Thou didst destroy the fortifications of the heretics, O hierarch, with pious boldness, having preached the Trinity, O Alexander!

Having divinely made thy tongue the pen of the grace of the Spirit, O Father John, it clarified the Faith.

Having partaken of the calling of the marvelous Paul, O blessed one: thou wast shown forth as an unchanging image of his virtues!

*Theotokion:*
Let us sing *unto the Lord, Who was well-pleased to be incarnate of the Virgin without seed, for our salvation and deliverance!*

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*Thou art the firm foundation of those that run unto Thee, O Lord; Thou art the light of the darkened, and my spirit sings of Thee!*

*Troparia:*
Excellently didst thou shepherd the flock of thy Master; therefore, as a hierarch, it glorifies thee forever, O Alexander!

Thou didst pour forth saving waters with thy tongue, pious teachings unto the flock, O blessed John!

O all-blessed Paul, thou wast a radiant vessel of Christ's election, manifestly like [unto him] both in calling and in life!

*Theotokion:*
We all have thee as our refuge and rampart, O Christians; we praise thee unceasingly, O unwedded Bride!

*Kontakion of the Saints, Tone 8:*
Kindled with the love of Christ, O glorious ones, and having taken up His yoke, the honorable Cross, ye were shown forth as imitators of His life, and became partakers of His divine glory, O divinely-wise Alexander, with marvelous John and glorious Paul! Therefore, standing before His throne, fervently pray for our souls!

*And the Sessional Hymn of the Forerunner.*
*Glory of the Saints, Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Speedily anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предвари́):*
The thrice-radiant lamp of the fathers has shone forth, ever illuminating the assemblies of the faithful unto divine knowledge, destroying all the impiety of the godless, defending [them] together with the teaching of wisdom: Alexander and John, and with them glorious Paul!

*Both now... Theotokion:*
O all-immaculate Virgin, who didst bear the pre-eternal God: together with the bodiless hosts, unceasingly pray unto Him to grant remission of transgressions and amendment of life before the end unto us who hymn thee with faith and love as is meet, O only all-hymned one!

*Stavrotheotokion:*
O all-immaculate Virgin Mother of Christ God, a sword pierced thy most holy soul when thou didst behold crucified voluntarily Thy Son and Thy God: Whom unceasingly beseech, O blessed one, to grant us forgiveness of transgressions!

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*I have heard, O Lord, the mystery of Thy dispensation; I considered Thy works, and glorified Thy Divinity!*

*Troparia:*
In a foul place didst thou cast down, like unto Judas, Arius the betrayer of Christ, O glorious one, together with the effusion of his entrails, as was fitting, O blessed Alexander!

*Troparia:*
Standing in the house of God as a fruitful olive tree, thou didst bring unto those believing in Him ripe fruit, O John!

On the wings of humility thou didst escape the snares of the enemy; therefore, being ever exalted, O Paul, thou wast a citizen of heaven!

*Theotokion:*
Let us hymn the Virgin, who remained a virgin after childbearing, as having born Christ God, Who delivered the world from deceit!

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Early in the morning we cry unto Thee, O Lord: Save us! For Thou art our God, and we know no other besides Thee!*

*Troparia:*
Adorning the throne of Metrophanes, O Alexander, even before the throne thou didst shine forth in hierarchic teachings.

Though thou didst labor in humility, O father, thou wast shown forth marvelous in Nicaea by divinely-speaking teachings.

The grace of thy words strengthens the Church, which knows thee, O John, as a pillar of Orthodoxy.

*Theotokion:*
We sing of thee as virgin after childbearing, O Theotokos; for thou didst bear in the flesh God the Word unto the world!

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*Grant me a radiant robe, Thou Who clothest Thyself with light as with a garment, O plenteous in mercy, Christ our God!*

*Troparia:*
Manfully fighting for the Church of Christ, O father, with the arrows of prayer thou didst pierce Arius the betrayer of Christ, O glorious one!

As a vine of the Vineyard of Life, O glorious John, thou didst grow abundant fruit for thy Master, O all-blessed one!

Piously thou didst choose to flee from the imperial city, when it lawlessly rejected the veneration of icons, O all-honorable Paul!

*Theotokion:*
O thou who alone by a word didst bear the Word in the flesh: deliver our souls, we pray, from the snare of the enemy!

*Kontakion [of the Forerunner], Tone 4:*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
Having acted lawlessly, O Forerunner, Herod gave thy venerable head unto a wedding, which we venerate and rejoice; but Herodias weeps and wails, with Herod and the dancing girl!

*Ikos:*
Let us prepare ourselves, brethren, for the divine banquet; for lo, the god-bearing head of the Forerunner is set forth for us as pure food in the church, which sheds blood, yet rather pours forth healings; and healing those who are sick from sinful drunkenness by fasting remedies. Let no one therefore be held by corruptible food, and be deprived of the divine nourishment of the Forerunner now The world indeed rejoices, but Herodias weeps and wails, with Herod and the dancing girl!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
*The youths who went down from Judea in Babylon of old, through faith in the Trinity trampled the flame of the furnace, singing: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!*

*Troparia:*
Thou didst appear as an initiate of the truth of the Most Divine Trinity, O exceedingly rich one! Thou didst serve the incorruptible Unity, singing from the heart, O Alexander: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

Christ showed thee forth as a treasure of teachings and a fountain of wonders, pouring forth the wealth of the knowledge of God, O John, unto those that cry: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

An angelic life equal to the divine Paul, who shares thy name, and in the breadth of heart the downpouring of wisdom, and in unutterable understanding, glorified thee throughout all the earth, O divinely-wise one!

*Theotokion:*
Incarnate of a virginal womb, Thou didst appear for our salvation; therefore, knowing Thy Mother to be the Theotokos, we cry thankfully: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
*Him Who was glorified on the holy mountain, and in the bush by fire revealed to Moses the mystery of the Ever-Virgin: Sing ye to the Lord, and supremely exalt Him unto all ages!*

*Troparia:*
Having slain the wicked Arius by thy fervent prayer, O divinely-wise Alexander: thou didst choke him in foul places like a second Judas, who had evilly apostatized in like manner!

The fighter against God, having torn the garment of Christ as a betrayer, burst asunder in the midst within; and now in Gehenna he is subject to torment, like a second Judas, even the senseless Arius, O thrice-blessed Alexander!

By temperance thou wast shown forth as one bodiless, exceedingly so by vigils; in teachings thou wast ever a rich fountain of Paradise, O John, unto all ages!

*Theotokion:*
How shall the human choir hymn thy fearful childbearing, O Theotokos? For thou remainest a virgin even after giving birth, truly beyond word and higher than cause, O Mother of God of all mysteries!

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
*Thee, the unwedded Mother of God the Most High; thee, who beyond understanding didst bear by the Word the true God, higher than the most pure hosts: with unceasing praises we magnify!*

*Troparia:*
Having been a lawful forerunner, thy disciple Alexander submitted himself unto thy life-depicting words; and having become a hierarch, O Metrophanes, meek and chaste, he is now worthily called blessed is magnified.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a sure initiate of unutterable mysteries, O father, divinely blessed John, all-wealthy one, and wast appointed a mystagogue of things unspoken; therefore, O father, thou didst proclaim the divine Trinity.

*Troparion:*
Thee, an innocent and venerable shepherd, blameless, pure and undefiled, chaste and renowned, rightly dividing the word of truth,[^894] do we worthily honor, O father Paul.

*Troparion:*
O divinely blessed trinity of marvelous fathers,[^895] mortal human nature is now divinized in the heavens before the Godhead, Whom ye devoutly served on earth; cease not to pray for your flock.

*Theotokion:*
O holy and all-immaculate one, vessel of sweet fragrance, pray unto Christ Whom thou didst bear, granting victory unto the king,[^896] and relief from the yoke of servitude unto Thy people; for thee do we unceasingly magnify.

*Exapostilarion of the Forerunner.*
*At the Aposticha, the stichera of the Octoechos.*
*Glory, Tone 8:*
O Forerunner of the Savior, thou didst rebuke the king not to commit lawlessness; therefore the dancing of a lawless woman compelled Herod to cut off thy head; and for this cause, from the rising of the sun unto the west, praised is thy name.[^897] Having boldness before the Lord, diligently pray that our souls may be saved.

*Both now, Theotokion, or Stavrotheotokion:*
*Tone 8, Podoben: "O Most Marvelous Wonder!" (Пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ):*
When the ewe-lamb beheld the Lamb* outstretched of His own will upon the tree of the Cross,* suffering maternal agony, she cried aloud with weeping:* "O my Son, what is this strange sight?* O Thou Who givest life unto all as Lord,* how dost Thou die, O longsuffering One, granting resurrection unto the earthly?* I glorify Thy great condescension, O my God!"

*In the same month, on the 31st day.*
*The Deposition of the Precious Sash of the Most Holy Theotokos.*
*At Small Vespers, at "Lord, I have cried," we set 4 stichoi.*
*And we sing stichera, Tone 1. Podoben: "Joy of the Heavenly Ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):*
Most glorious art thou throughout generations of generations,* O Virgin Mother, Maiden Mary,* protectress of the world, who didst bear in the flesh the Son of the unoriginate Father,* co-eternal with the Spirit:* Him do thou implore that we may be saved.

Held fast by unexpected sorrows, O pure one, having acquired thee as our only protectress, O Virgin,* we cry aloud with thanksgiving: save us, O all-immaculate Bride of God;* for thou art the refuge of the world and the defense of our race.

The world was renewed at thy nativity, O Theotokos,* salvation of the faithful and sleepless intercessor;* cease not therefore diligently to pray for those who devoutly offer supplications, O all-pure one,* on behalf of all who faithfully celebrate the deposition of thy precious sash.

*Glory, Both now, the same Tone:*
A divine solemnity hath arrived today, brethren! Let creation dance, and let mankind rejoice! For the Theotokos summons us, the undefiled treasure of virginity, the noetic paradise of the Second Adam, the composition uniting the two natures, the triumph of saving reconciliation, wherein the Word espoused flesh unto Himself: a truly light cloud,[^898] bearing Him with the flesh Who is borne upon the Cherubim. By her intercessions, O Christ, save our souls.

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме є҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):*
Rejoice, O Chalkoprateia,[^899] divine repository of the only Theotokos,* wherein was deposited the precious sash of the Queen of all.
*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou, and the ark of Thy holiness.*[^900]
The Mother of God,* the only Theotokos, all-blessed one,* having gloriously reposed,* left unto us her sash.
*Verse: The Lord hath sworn truth unto David, and will not turn aside from it.*[^901]
O church doors, be opened wide!* For lo, the sash of her who was the Mother of the Creator, the all-venerable Maiden,* is brought unto the church!
*Glory, Both now, the same Podoben (Подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Glory in heaven,* and gladness on earth!* For lo, the sash of the Mother of the Creator of all* was deposited in the church by hierarchal hands.

*After the Trisagion: Troparion, Tone 8:*
*O Theotokos, Ever-Virgin, protection of mankind:*
*Look for this at Great Vespers.*

*At Great Vespers we sing "Blessed is the man," the first Glory.*
*At "Lord, I have cried," we set 8 stichoi; if there is no vigil, then 6. And we sing stichera, Tone 4. Podoben: "As Noble" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
The reliquary containing Thy sash, O Theotokos,* is ever known to Thy servants as an ark of sanctification,* and a sacred bulwark,* and a glory and praise,* and a fountain of healings;*

today, as we assemble in holiness, we hymn Thine abundant majesties and an abyss of wonders. *(Twice)*

Behold the most glorious place!* Behold the ever-radiant temple* wherein the treasure was deposited: the sash of the Maiden of God, precious through grace!* Draw near, ye people; draw forth clearly illumination and cleansing,* and cry aloud with a grateful heart:* "O all-holy one, we bless thee, O Virgin, who art saved by thy childbirth!" *(Twice)*

The holy deposition of thy sash, O Theotokos,* is the festival we have acquired, rejoicing, for today thou hast deigned to bestow upon thy city a morning girding, an unstolen treasury,* a precious gift, an inalienable wealth, a river of healings,* filled with spiritual gifts. *(Twice)*

*Glory, Both now, Tone 2:*
As with an all-radiant crown, O all-pure Theotokos, the Church of God hath arrayed itself with thy holy sash, and shines, rejoicing today; and mystically it keeps festival, O Sovereign Lady, crying unto thee: Rejoice, honorable diadem and crown of divine glory! Rejoice, thou who alone art the fullness of glory and eternal joy! Rejoice, haven and protectress and our salvation for them that flee unto thee!

*Entrance. The Prokeimenon of the day, and three readings. Look for these on the 15th day of this month.*[^902]

*At the Litiya, stichera, Tone 2:*
Who shall worthily praise and bless thee, O Maiden Bride of God, for the deliverance that came through thee unto the world? Giving thanks therefore, we cry aloud unto thee, blessing thee: Rejoice, thou who hast divinized Adam and gathered the divided into one! Rejoice, thou who hast illuminated our race by the most radiant resurrection of thy Son and our God! For the Christian race unceasingly blesses thee.

*Tone 3:*
The renewal and life of human nature are known afresh through thee, O all-pure Mary, who in thy womb didst contain the Creator of creation, Who raised us from hades and death and bestowed upon us eternal life. Therefore we cry aloud unto thee, O Ever-Virgin: Rejoice, thou who hast joined earthly things unto heavenly! Rejoice, hope of all the ends of the earth, protectress and defense! Rejoice, thou who hast illuminated all things by thy most pure childbirth, and dost bestow upon the world great mercy!

*Tone 4:*
He Who is glorified in the highest with the Father and the Spirit by the Seraphim, desiring to renew the first-created, God Himself emptied Himself unutterably in thy womb, O all-hymned Theotokos; and having shone forth from thee, the whole world He illuminated with His divinity, delivering it from the madness of idolatry; and having in Himself divinized mankind, He led it up unto the heavens: Christ God and Savior of our souls.

Thou didst conceive without seed, and didst bear unutterably Him Who casts down the mighty from their thrones and exalts the humble,[^903] Who raises up the horn of His anointed who glorify the Cross of Christ, His burial and resurrection. Therefore, knowing thee, O Theotokos, to be the cause and intercessor of so many good things, we bless thee with unceasing hymns, and we celebrate the deposition of thy precious robe.[^904]

*Tone 6:*
Come, all ye nations, in voices of gladness let us praise the all-holy Virgin and Theotokos, the furnace of human nature,[^905] the worker of unutterable mysteries; for in her a new and most glorious mystery was accomplished: the unoriginate One came, the Word grew corporeal; God becomes man, that He might make man god.[^906] Her therefore do we diligently beseech, that our souls may be saved.

*Glory, Both now, Tone 4:*
O all-hymned and all-glorious Queen Theotokos, Mother of the Creator of all creation, Christian hope and defense, consolation of the sorrowing, swift hope of the despairing and hopeless: we pray thee, be merciful unto us sinners; forsake not thy servants, and cast not away the supplication of the unworthy; we pray thee as we gaze upon thine honorable icon. May thy mercy abide with those who sail and travel by land. O Sovereign Lady, protect with thy precious sash, and in every place preserve thy cities and people from the invasion of barbarians; and pray unto Christ our God Who was born of thee, that He may save our souls from sins.

*At the Aposticha, stichera, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Given a Sign" (Да́лъ є҆сѝ зна́менїе):*
Thou hast granted thy sash unto thy city, O most glorious one,* as a firm rampart,* embracing it from every affliction* by divine operations, and preserving it unconquerable by enemies,* while in love it cries aloud:* "My strength and dominion and beautiful rejoicing* is Thy Son and Lord; He alone is compassionate."
*Verse: Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou, and the ark of Thy holiness.*[^907]
Those who piously reign with an honorable crown,* being brightly girded with thy sash, O all-pure one,* wherewith they boast in divine majesties,* are ever known as terrible unto warring enemies,* praising thee and crying aloud* unto Him Who beyond word was born unto Him Who beyond word was born of thee:* "O all-glorious Jesus,* save all, as Thou art compassionate!"
*Verse: The rich among the people shall entreat thy countenance.*[^908]
Gird us with the power of thy sash, O Virgin,* strengthening us against enemies,* subduing the passions* that ever torment and wage war against us;* and ever granting unto us victorious dispassion,* purely to glorify thee, and diligently to cry unto thy Son:* "O all-powerful Jesus,* save all, as Thou art compassionate!"

*Glory, Both now, Tone 2:*
Having cleansed our intent and mind,* like the angels let us also keep festival,* brightly beginning David's song unto the Maiden, the Bride of Christ the King of all, our God:* saying: "Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest; Thou, and the ark of Thy holiness!"[^909] For having adorned her as a fair palace, and counted her worthy for Thy city, O Master, to order and protect it from adversarial pagans by Thy sovereign power, through her prayers.

*Troparion, Tone 8:*
O Theotokos, Ever-Virgin, protection of mankind, thou hast granted unto thy city the robe and sash of thine all-pure body as a sovereign rampart, remaining incorrupt through thy seedless childbirth; for in thee both nature and time are renewed; therefore we beseech thee: grant peace unto thy commonwealth, and unto our souls great mercy.

*At Matins. At "God is the Lord," the troparion of the Theotokos, thrice.*

*After the 1st Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, Tone 3.*
*Tone 3, Podoben: "The Beauty of Thy Virginity" (Красотѣ̀ дѣ́вства):*
By the beauty of thy virginity having given birth unto the most comely Word, and Him hast thou swaddled as an infant with thy sash, O pure one; this therefore hast thou granted unto thy servants, O Sovereign Lady, as protection, help, and a fountain of sanctification; whose honorable deposition, O all-holy Virgin, we all faithfully celebrate.
*Glory, Both now, the same.*

*After the 2nd Kathisma, Sessional Hymn, the same Tone and Podoben (Гла́съ и҆ подо́бенъ то́йже):*
Childbirth incorrupt and virginity thou alone didst possess, O blessed one; and as a saving garment for mankind thou didst grant thy holy sash, which even until now remains incorrupt, O Bride of God, for whose sake we, thy servants, O pure one, receive great mercy from God.
*Glory, Both now, the same.*

*At the Polyeleos, Megalynarion:*
We magnify thee, O all-holy Virgin, and we honor thy most precious sash, and we glorify its all-glorious deposition!
*Then the selected Psalm: "Lord, remember David and all his meekness"*[^910] *After the Polyeleos, Sessional Hymn, Tone 4.*
*Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou Hast Appeared" (Ꙗ҆ви́лсѧ є҆сѝ):*
The universe celebrates today the honorable deposition of thy sash, O pure one, and in love cries unto thee: Rejoice, O Virgin, helper of the faithful!
*Glory, Both now:*
Thy people celebrate today the deposition of thy precious sash, O all-hymned one, and earnestly cry unto thee: Rejoice, O Virgin, boast of Christians!
*Gradual Hymns: 1st Antiphon of Tone 4. Prokeimenon, Tone 4: I will remember Thy name in every generation and generation.*[^911] *Verse: Give ear, O daughter, and see...*[^912] *Let every breath... Gospel according to Luke, pericope 4.*[^913]
*After Psalm 50, Glory: Through the intercessions of the Most Holy Theotokos...*
*Both now, the same.*
*Then: Have mercy on us, O God...*
*Sticheron, Tone 6:*
The marvelous solemnities of the Orthodox having assembled today, let us faithful sing in psalmody, saying: "Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest, Thou and the ark of Thy holiness,"[^914] for Thou hast granted unto us, O Word, victory to honor the sash of Thy Mother; by her prayers, O all-powerful One, pacify the world and save our souls.

*Two Canons: the first with its heirmos on 8; its acrostic:*
*With Thy strength gird me, O pure Virgin.*[^915]
*A composition of Joseph.*[^916] *The heirmos twice, the troparia on 6. Tone 8.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*The wonder-working staff of Moses, striking crosswise and dividing the sea, once drowned the chariot-driving Pharaoh, but saved the fleeing people of Israel, traveling on foot, as they sang a hymn unto God.*[^917]

*Troparion:*
Devoutly gird me with divine strength as I praise in hymn thy sacred sash, O pure and all-praised Virgin, who art divinely a girding, dominion, and unshakable bulwark for thy city, O Maiden.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst bear the mighty God Who girds all things with piety by His power, O all-immaculate one; therefore we bless thee, and kissing thy divine sash with joy, we draw forth grace and mercy.

*Troparion:*
Rejoicing today, we dance around the deposition of the sacred sash of the venerable Maiden of God, from whom was woven for us the girding of incorruption, the seamless garment, and the robe of sanctification.

*Troparion:*
Thy people flee unto thy Son, O all-immaculate one, taking refuge beneath thy protection always; be a help unto all, and grant unto all petitions unto salvation, delivering from afflictions our souls.

*Second Canon, on 4. Tone 4.*
*Ode 1. Heirmos:*
*Having traversed the Red Sea's deep on foot with dry feet in ancient times, Israel of old, by the hands of Moses stretched crosswise, conquered the power of Amalek in the wilderness.*[^918]

*Troparion:*
Having found the radiant temple of the Virgin like heaven, the all-radiant sash thereof, as a beacon illuminated and never-setting, shines today upon the universe with the rays of miracles.

*Troparion:*
Noetically girded with thy divine sash, O all-pure one, as a bond of strength and confirmation, thy city possesses an unbroken dominion; therefore it also boasts in thee.

*Troparion:*
Of old, like the truly all-venerable Ark,[^919] thy reliquary, O Mother of God, appeared unto those on earth: not bearing the tablets of stone,[^920] but faithfully preserving the knowledge of truth.

*Troparion:*
Mystical aromas pour forth unto the honorable temple from the precious reliquary today, filling with spiritual sweet fragrance all who flee unto it with love.

*Katavasia: Tracing the Cross...*[^921]

*Ode 3. Heirmos:*
*O Lord, Supreme Fashioner of the heavenly vault and Builder of the Church: do Thou confirm me in Thy love, O summit of desires, confirmation of the faithful, O only Lover of mankind.*

*Troparion:*
The heart is renewed as with fervent faith it touches the sacred sash of the Virgin; and it is girded with invincible power against impure passions and bodiless foes, remaining unharmed.

*Troparion:*
Thy sacred sash still remains incorrupt, which girded thy pristine body, O Maiden, granting sanctification unto those who draw near with piety, and delivering from the corruption of diseases and afflictions.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast a most beautifully adorned house, O pure one, for the Word incarnate of thee; and in thy holy temple thou wast well pleased to deposit thy sash, kissing which we are sanctified.

*Troparion:*
We all honorably kiss thy precious sash with a glad heart, which is the honor of all the faithful, O Theotokos, for it touched thine all-honorable body.

*Second Canon. Heirmos:*
*Thy Church rejoices in Thee, O Christ, crying aloud: Thou art my strength, O Lord, and refuge, and confirmation!*

*Troparion:*
Let us faithful honor today the sash of the pure one as a bond uniting us unto God, and let us faithfully worship: perpetual streams flowing grace from the all-pure reliquary bestowals of gifts, O pure one, filling all the faithful. *(Twice)*

*Troparion:*
Thy precious sash, much-hymned for us today, pours forth healings unto the faithful by grace, O all-pure one.

*Troparion:*
Like morning dew, pouring forth thy joy, O pure one, it extinguishes the furnace of passions for those who ever hymn thee.

*Kontakion, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "In Prayers" (Въ моли́твахъ):*
Thy precious sash which encompassed thy God-receiving womb, O Theotokos, is an unassailable bastion for thy city and an inexhaustible treasure of good things, O thou who alone didst give birth, Ever-Virgin.

*Then the Sessional Hymn, Tone 2.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Speedily Anticipate" (Ско́рѡ предварѝ):*
As a divine offering in the temple of God, O pure one, thou didst leave thy most venerable sash wherewith thou didst gird thine immaculate loins, having conceived God. Touching it therefore with faith and honorably kissing it with fear, we are sanctified.

*Glory, Both now, another, the same Tone.*
*Tone 2, Podoben: "Thou Who Wast Raised Up" (Вознесы́йсѧ):*
Thy Church brightly celebrates the deposition of thy precious sash, and diligently cries unto thee, O pure Virgin: Array all against the hostile power, cast down the arrogance of godless barbarians, and direct our life to do the Lord's divine desires.

*Ode 4. Heirmos:*
*Thou art my strength, O Lord, Thou art my power, Thou art my God, Thou art my rejoicing, Who didst not leave the bosom of the Father, and didst visit our poverty; therefore with the Prophet Habakkuk I cry unto Thee: Glory to Thy power, O Lover of mankind!*[^922]

*Troparion:*
Having reposed and departed unto the unsetting Light, thou hast left thy precious sash unto those who bless thee, in place of thy body, O pure one: an existing fountain of wonders, a place of salvation, and a rampart for this thy city that honors thee, O all-pure one.

*Troparion:*
As into a new heaven do we enter into thy temple, where thy divine sash is deposited as a treasure, shining brightly like the sun, emitting rays of wonders and illuminating the hearts of all, O Maiden, driving away the mist of passions.

*Troparion:*
Thou art the Ark of noetic sanctification, O pure and all-hymned one; and this honorable reliquary holding the sash which thou didst wear upon thyself hast thou given unto thy people as a great refuge and an inexhaustible fountain of healings.

*Troparion:*
An abundant gift was brought unto thy city: thy precious sash, O all-immaculate one; and it was brightly deposited on this day in this divine temple in thy temple, and it hath become the cause of a great feast, O Theotokos, for those who fervently love thee.

*Troparion:*
The shadows of the Law and the riddles of the Prophets foretold that thou, O all-pure one, wouldst be the true Mother unto God, through whom the curse vanished and complete blessing blossomed forth, and saving grace unto those who sing unto thee with faith and love.

*Second Canon. Heirmos:*
*Beholding Thee lifted up upon the Cross, O Sun of Righteousness, the Church stood in her station, worthily crying aloud: Glory to Thy power, O Lord!*[^923]

*Troparion:*
Having established thee as an all-honorable confirmation, O Theotokos, the Maker and Creator adorned thee, as with stars, with divine splendors wherewith thou illuminest the ends of the earth.

*Troparion:*
Having thee as its dominion and firm foundation, O Theotokos, thy city is upheld by thy divine sash, being an unbroken bond in the battles that wage war against it.

*Troparion:*
From earth unto heaven doth thy divine sash lift us up, O Mother of God, who are led upward by thy love and by it; therefore we glorify thee as the cause of a better glory.

*Troparion:*
Lo, an inexhaustible grace! Come, all ye who love the feast, with a zealous heart draw forth the streams that abundantly flow from the precious reliquary of the all-pure Mother.

*Ode 5. Heirmos:*
*Therefore hast Thou cast me away from Thy presence, O Light never-setting, and an alien darkness hath covered me, wretched one? But turn me back, and direct my paths toward the light of Thy commandments, I pray Thee.*[^924]

*Troparion:*
The deposition of the divine sash is today in thy temple, O full of divine joy; with it hath been deposited every gift of good things for those in need, and everyone is filled who comes for sanctification, faithfully receiving petitions.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast adorned, O Virgin, having given birth unto the most comely Word; and in thy beautiful house thou wast well pleased that the comely sash be deposited, which beautifully girded thy comely body, O Theotokos.

*Troparion:*
Thy holy temple is known unto all as another paradise, having in its midst thy sash like a fragrant rose, filling the hearts of those who draw near in faith with divine fragrance, O all-immaculate one.

*Troparion:*
Thou wast the cloud of divine rain, O all-immaculate one, distilling the water of salvation, bringing the earth made sterile by sins unto the bearing of the fruits of piety; therefore we faithfully glorify thee.

*Second Canon. Heirmos:*
*Thou, O my Lord, didst come into the world as Light, a holy Light turning from gloomy ignorance those who hymn Thee with faith.*[^925]

*Troparion:*
Thou, O Lord, hast magnified Thy Mother; Thou hast exalted her above all noetic powers, in the incomparable glory thereof.

*Troparion:*
The grace of God is now abundantly bestowed, O all-pure Theotokos, from thine honorable reliquary, unto those who worship thee with faith.

*Troparion:*
By thy power faithful rulers are arrayed; thy city, praising thee as a bond, O Theotokos, is honored with thy sash.

*Troparion:*
Truly known was thy grace revealed, O all-pure one, working miracles and signs throughout all the earth most gloriously.

*Ode 6. Heirmos:*
*The abyss of sins and tempest of transgressions confounds me, and hurries me into the depths of dire despair; but stretch forth Thy strong hand unto me, as unto Peter, O Guide, and save me!*[^926]

*Troparion:*
Anew didst thou bear for us as an Infant Him Who is before the ages; and thou renewest hearts grown old with sins, having received renewal by the deposition of thy precious sash, O Maiden, Ever-Virgin.

*Troparion:*
Thy holy church is marvelous in truth, which hath acquired thy marvelous sash that gushes forth miracles; it appears unto the afflicted as an abyss of healings, O Mother of God, Virgin Mother.

*Troparion:*
Let every soul rejoice, having gathered in thy holy temple, and beholding therein thy sash, O Theotokos, shining forth like the bright sun the light of the gifts of the Creator and divine Spirit.

*Troparion:*
Strengthen our hearts, O pure one, overlooking all transgressions; and gird with power those who have faithfully acquired thy sash as a precious and inalienable treasure.

*Second Canon. Heirmos:*
*I will sacrifice unto Thee with the voice of praise, O Lord, the Church cries aloud unto Thee, having been cleansed from the blood of demons by the blood that flowed through mercy from Thy side.*[^927]

*Troparion:*
The Lord hath preternaturally glorified thee: having exalted thee alone entirely, He honored thee entirely beyond word, O Theotokos, as a temple with sash and divine reliquary.

*Troparion:*
Streams of grace flow from the deep, from the all-pure reliquary, sprinkling and watering all creation, for those who faithfully worship thy childbirth.

*Troparion:*
Having acquired thee as strength and praise, the faithful are arrayed with glory; thy precious sash, O Theotokos, having [it] as a most radiant and precious adornment.

*Kontakion. Tone 4, Podoben: "Thou hast appeared today" (Ꙗ҆ви́лся є҆сѝ дне́сь):*
The deposition of thy precious sash thy temple celebrates today, O all-pure one, and fervently cries out to thee: Rejoice, O Virgin, boast of Christians!

*Ikos:*
