Enlighten me with thy light, O Virgin Theotokos, and disperse my darkness lying grievously upon my mind, that I may purely come to honor thee who hast deified mortals,[^928] and praise thy divine sash, O all-undefiled and spotless one, brighter than the sun; which this world has as a firm protection and hope, overcoming the counsels of lawless enemies, scattering their wiles, and ever preserving thy servants: Rejoice, O Virgin, boast of Christians!

*Ode 7. Heirmos:*
At the condescension of God, the fire was ashamed of old in Babylon; therefore the youths in the furnace, dancing with joyful step as in a meadow of flowers, sang: Blessed art Thou, O God of our fathers.[^929]

*Troparion:*
Having departed unto the heavenly hosts, the Queen of all left her sash as wealth unto the reigning city of all cities,[^930] whereby they are saved from the assault of visible and invisible enemies.

*Troparion:*
Let us now draw near unto the fountain dropping grace and mercy, unto the precious reliquary that possesses the all-honored sash of the Virgin and Mother, who honored the human commixture through her precious childbirth.

*Troparion:*
Praise the Lord, all heavenly hosts, and all human nations glorify her who bore Him; for she gave her sash as a true refuge and salvation for the faithful.[^931]

*Troparion:*
Let the clouds drop down righteousness from above today at the deposition of thy sash, O cloud full of divine grace,[^932] and let every soul sing sweetly, rejoicing: Blessed is the God of our fathers!

*Another Canon. Ode 7. Heirmos:*
In the Persian furnace, the youths of Abraham's seed, inflamed by love of piety rather than by the flame, cried aloud: Blessed art Thou in the temple of Thy glory, O Lord![^933]

*Troparion:*
As the Sun of righteousness, Thou who alone art the Giver of light, Thou didst divinely illumine the temple of the pure one with Thy manifold gifts, wherewith her sash, shining, shoots forth rays.[^934]

*Troparion:*
Surrounding thy sash as a golden urn,[^935] O only pure one, we truly partake now of divine and gracious sweetness, O all-blessed one, and since thou art higher than the tablets of the Law,[^936] we venerate this [sash].

*Troparion:*
Thy grace, O pure Maiden, truly pours forth marvels as a chalice unto the faithful, and it proceeds bountifully from thy reliquary, as rivers from another Eden.[^937]

*Troparion:*
Draw near now with rejoicing, all ye upon earth; come, the reliquary mystically proclaims, saying: Embrace the all-glorious one, who has treasured her sash within me!

*Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Sevenfold did the Chaldean tyrant frantically kindle the furnace for the godly ones; but seeing them saved by a higher power, he cried aloud to the Creator and Redeemer: Youths, bless; priests, praise; peoples, supremely exalt Him unto all ages![^938]

*Troparion:*
As a holy stream, the ark was placed radiantly within the inaccessible holy places, containing the restful sash of the only Maid of God and pure Queen of all, from which complete rest comes bountifully to those who labor in many afflictions.

*Troparion:*
Thou didst holily give flesh upon earth to the Lord, and with holy hands didst truly gird Him who girds the godly with might;[^939] and now having ascended higher than the heavens, thou hast left to men thy precious sash, O Virgin Theotokos, as sovereign power and protection.

*Troparion:*
Healing of the ailing, support of the stumbled, and divine encouragement of the despondent, rudder of those who sail, return of the straying: such is truly the divine grace that attends thy precious sash, O pure Virgin, which we worship in faith unto all ages.

*Troparion:*
Celebrating today the sacred festival of the holy deposition of thy divine sash, we thy servants honor it, and with joy cry aloud to thee: Rejoice, O Theotokos, joy of angels and of all men who sing in faith: Youths, bless; priests, praise; peoples, supremely exalt Him unto all ages!

*Another Canon. Ode 8. Heirmos:*
Daniel, stretching out his hands, shut the gaping mouths of the lions in the pit;[^940] and the youths, lovers of piety, girded with virtue, quenched the power of the fire, crying aloud: Bless the Lord, all ye works of the Lord![^941]

*Troparion:*
The ark of old contained the divine tablets written by the hand of God, O all-pure one;[^942] but thy honored and precious reliquary, O pure Sovereign Lady, contains the sash which contains the dread mystery of her who held their Lawgiver within herself.[^943]

*Troparion:*
The angels, O all-pure, pure one, dance together now in thy precious temple, and embrace thy precious and holy sash, O Sovereign Lady, worshipping it with love and joy and much gladness, singing praises unto thee, the glory of our God.

*Troparion:*
O rod that budded forth the Flower of life,[^944] thou art the joy of all: priceless, pure vessel of the myrrh of the Spirit, treasury of good things, fountain of aromatics, from which the divine reliquary pours forth myrrh of healings.

*Troparion:*
The noetic hosts sing of thy greatness, O all-pure, pure one; all patriarchs and prophets proclaim it clearly, with apostles, the sacred ranks, and choirs of martyrs; with whom we worship thee.

*Ode 9. Heirmos:*
Heaven was dismayed at this, and the ends of the earth were astonished, that God appeared unto men in the flesh, and thy womb became more spacious than the heavens; therefore the hierarchies of angels and men magnify thee as the Theotokos.[^945]

*Troparion:*
Unto the divine bed of Solomon, which sixty valiant men surround, according to the words of the Scriptures,[^946] as upon a royal bed in an honored ark today, she lays her sash for the restoration of all the faithful and the protection of the pious.

*Troparion:*
O city of the heavenly King, of which glorious things have been spoken,[^947] place within thy city an honored and holy gift, thine all-pure sash, for the confirmation of all the faithful, whereby crowned rulers conquer adversaries, shining with Orthodoxy.

*Troparion:*
Drop down sweetness now, O mountains, and eternal rejoicing, O hills;[^948] assemblies of patriarchs, and ranks of martyrs, company of prophets, honored gathering of the divine apostles: rejoice with us all at the deposition of the divine sash of the Maid of God!

*Troparion:*
Thou art all-holy through thy childbirth, O most holy Virgin, and now thou hast granted us exceeding illumination, thy most holy sash, by whose deposition all the earth dances and honors thee, who hast filled humanity with ineffable joy.

*Troparion:*
From deceitful love of passions, and from the enemy who ever tempts with the heaviness of sins, and from the grievous abduction of despondency, and captivity and sin, quickly deliver me who flee unto thy prayer, O merciful and pure one.

*Another Canon. Ode 9. Heirmos:*

A stone cut without hands was cut from the uncut mountain, from thee, O Virgin, even Christ the Chief Cornerstone, who joined together the divided natures;[^949] therefore rejoicing, we magnify thee, O Theotokos.

*Troparion:*
Thy reliquary, O Mother of God, has truly appeared as an all-pure bridal chamber, worthily containing and preserving thy holy sash, as a virginal and bridal garment, as a treasure of life.

*Troparion:*
Arrayed in the divine diadem of beauty, the church, O Theotokos, royal-like rejoices today with thy divine and all-pure sash, and is adorned with thy glory.

*Troparion:*
The all-fair temple of the Virgin today, wholly befitting the heavens, shines brightly; the faithful, having received the beams of gifts, are arrayed as with stellar beauties.

*Troparion:*
Thy city possesses thy holy sash, O Mother of God: a bond of peace and a wall, the divine unity of dogma, the boast of the Orthodox, and the victory of rulers.

*Troparion:*
We hymn thine ineffable glory and thy boundless grace; for thou art the fountain of wisdom, from which the Word proceeds unto all who honor thee, O all-pure one, and magnify thy childbirth.[^950]

*Exapostilarion, Podoben: "Give ear, ye women" (Жены̀ ᲂу҆слы́шите):*
Venerating thy precious sash, wherewith thou didst gird thy sacred body, O Maid of God and Virgin, we now celebrate its deposition, whereby we are delivered from corruption, and perils and tribulations, O Mother of God the Most High.
*Glory, and both now, the same.*

*At the Praises, Stichera on 4. Tone 4, Podoben: "As one valiant" (Ꙗ҆́кѡ до́блѧ):*
*Sticheron 1:*
As with a most radiant crown, O all-pure Theotokos, the church is girded with thy holy sash; and rejoicing, it shines today and mystically dances, crying aloud to thee, O Sovereign Lady: Rejoice, crown and divine diadem! Rejoice, only fullness of my joy, glory and eternal gladness! *(Twice)*

*Sticheron 2:*
Thou, O all-pure Sovereign Lady, being sovereign protection, unshakable foundation, and salvation unto thy people and thy city, hast given thine all-pure sash as a radiant garment, saving from all misfortunes those who honor it with faith and warm devotion, O Bride of God.

*Sticheron 3:*
Thy temple, O all-pure one, has appeared today as an inexhaustible fountain of marvels, pouring out rivers of grace bountifully from thy holy from thy holy reliquary; and they gladden the minds of the faithful who cry aloud to thee in faith and love: Thou art our joy, gladness, and life!

*Glory, and both now. Tone 2:*
Having purified our intent and mind, let us also celebrate with the angels, brightly beginning the Davidic song unto the Maiden Bride of Christ God, the King of all: Arise, O Lord, saying, into Thy rest; Thou, and the ark of Thy sanctuary;[^951] for having adorned her as a fair palace, He granted her unto Thy city, O Master, to protect and shield it from adversary heathens by Thy sovereign power, through her prayers.[^952]

*Great Doxology. And Dismissal.*
*At the Liturgy. The Beatitudes from the Canons, Ode 3 and Ode 6.*
*Prokeimenon, Tone 3: Song of the Theotokos:*
My soul magnifies the Lord, and my spirit has rejoiced in God my Savior.[^953]
*Verse:* For He has regarded the lowliness of His handmaiden.[^954]

*Epistle to the Hebrews, Pericope 320 [Heb. 9:1–7].*[^955]

*Alleluia. Arise, O Lord, into Thy rest; Thou, and the ark of Thy sanctuary.*[^956]

*Gospel according to Luke, Pericope 54 [Luke 10:38–42, 11:27–28].*[^957]

*Communion Hymn: I will take the cup of salvation, and call upon the name of the Lord. Alleluia.*[^958]

---

### Notes

[^6]: John the Monk (Saint John of Damascus, c. 676–749), author of numerous festal stichera and liturgical idiomena in the Byzantine rite.
[^7]: Cosmas the Monk (Saint Cosmas the Melodist / of Maiuma, c. 675–752), foster-brother of Saint John of Damascus and prominent Byzantine hymnographer.
[^8]: Deut. 28:66 (LXX): "And thy life shall be hanging before thine eyes."
[^9]: The Troparion of the Cross here petitions for "our orthodox King" (*благовѣрному королю нашему*), referring to King Augustus III of Poland-Lithuania.
[^10]: The acrostic of the Canon of the Cross in Slavonic is: Кр[е]сту покланяюся вѣрный спасенію ("I, the faithful one, worship the Cross unto salvation"), formed by the initial letters of the odes and troparia.
[^11]: An allusion to the budding rod of Aaron (Num. 17:8), prefiguring both the priesthood and the wood of the Cross.
[^12]: Ps. 147:1–2 (LXX 147:12–13): "Praise the Lord, O Jerusalem; praise thy God, O Zion. For He hath strengthened the bars of thy gates; He hath blessed thy children within thee."
[^13]: Like the Troparion on fol. 2v (p8), the Kontakion of the Cross here petitions for "our orthodox King" (*благовѣрному королю нашему*), referring to Augustus III of Poland-Lithuania (reigned 1734–1763).
[^14]: 2 Cor. 12:2–4: "I knew a man in Christ... caught up to the third heaven... into paradise, and heard unspeakable words, which it is not lawful for a man to utter."
[^15]: Gal. 6:14: "But God forbid that I should glory, save in the cross of our Lord Jesus Christ, by whom the world is crucified unto me, and I unto the world."
[^16]: Gen. 48:13–14: Jacob crossed his hands to bless Ephraim and Manasseh, historically interpreted in patristic and liturgical hymnography as a type of the Cross.
[^17]: Ps. 44:3 (LXX 44:2): "Thou art fairer than the sons of men; grace is poured into thy lips."
[^18]: Exod. 15:23–25: Moses cast the wood into the bitter waters of Marah, and the waters were made sweet, prefiguring the saving power of the Cross.
[^19]: Gen. 22:1–14 and 4 Macc. 13–16: The mother of the Maccabees exhorts her sons to emulate Abraham's readiness to offer Isaac in obedience to God.
[^20]: An allusion to Prov. 9:1 ("Wisdom hath builded her house, she hath hewn out her seven pillars") and 1 Cor. 10:4 ("and that Rock was Christ").
[^21]: Rom. 8:35–39: "Who shall separate us from the love of Christ? shall tribulation, or distress, or persecution, or famine, or nakedness, or peril, or sword? ... For I am persuaded, that neither death, nor life, nor angels... shall be able to separate us from the love of God."
[^22]: A further petition for King Augustus III of Poland-Lithuania ("our faithful King").
[^23]: The rubrics for the solemn Elevation and Veneration of the Precious and Life-Giving Cross at the end of the Great Doxology on August 1 represent the ancient Constantinopolitan tradition of carrying forth the True Cross through the city for sanctification.
[^24]: Ps. 73:14 (LXX 73:14): "Thou didst crush the heads of the dragons in the water."
[^25]: Isa. 50:6 ("I gave My back to the smiters, and My cheeks to them that plucked off the hair: I hid not My face from shame and spitting") and Matt. 27:26–34.
[^26]: Isa. 53:2 ("He hath no form nor comeliness; and when we shall see Him, there is no beauty that we should desire Him") and Luke 2:35 ("Yea, a sword shall pierce through thine own soul also").
[^27]: The Lesser Blessing of Water (*Malyi Vodosvyat*) is appointed on August 1st throughout Rus' and Byzantine codices, commemorating both the ancient Constantinopolitan tradition of processing with the True Cross through the city streets and streams during August epidemics to sanctify the waters, and the traditional baptism of Kievan Rus' under Saint Vladimir.
[^28]: Ps. 142 (LXX 142) and Ps. 50 (LXX 50).
[^29]: Old Testament types applied to the Mother of God: the golden urn (*stamnos*) holding the manna (Heb. 9:4; Exod. 16:33), the golden lampstand (Exod. 25:31), the unhewn mountain (Dan. 2:34–35), the burning bush (Exod. 3:2), the shut gate (Ezek. 44:1–2), the ladder reaching to heaven (Gen. 28:12), and the table of showbread (Heb. 9:2).
[^30]: Ps. 26:1 (LXX 26:1).
[^31]: Heb. 2:11–18, quoting Ps. 21:23 (LXX 21:22) and Isa. 8:17–18.
[^32]: Ps. 44:2 (LXX 44:1).
[^33]: John 5:1–4.
[^34]: A petition for King Augustus III of Poland-Lithuania ("our Great King"), standard in Polish-Lithuanian Uniate codices of the mid-18th century.
[^35]: In the physical codex, the name of Saint Hieromartyr Josaphat Kuntsevych, Archbishop of Polotsk (d. 1623, beatified 1643), has been erased or struck through in red ink, a physical trace of 19th-century post-Union Orthodox censorship at Pochaiv Lavra following its transfer from the Basilian Order to the Russian Orthodox Church in 1831.
[^36]: Saints Boris and Gleb of Kyiv (martyred 1015), the first saints canonized in Rus', commemorated here under their holy baptismal names, Roman and David.
[^37]: The petition for the civil sovereign (*вели́каго короля на́шего*, "our Great King") again refers to Augustus III of Poland-Lithuania (reigned 1734–1763). In the physical text, the name space following *короля на́шего* exhibits erasure and red ink rubrication for inserting the sovereign's name.
[^38]: Matt. 10:1, 8.
[^39]: Liturgical scriptural pericope appointments for August 1: Cross Epistle (1 Cor. 1:18–24, Pericope 125); Maccabean Martyrs Epistle (Heb. 11:33–12:2, Pericope 330); Cross Gospel (John 19:6–11, 13–20, 25–28, 30–35, Pericope 60); Maccabean Martyrs Gospel (Matt. 10:16–22, Pericope 38).
[^40]: Typikon rubrics detailing the order of service when August 1st falls on a Sunday, combining the Sunday Resurrectional office with the Feast of the Procession of the Honorable Wood of the Life-Giving Cross and the Holy Maccabean Martyrs.
[^41]: The Translation of the Relics of the Holy Protomartyr and Archdeacon Stephen from Jerusalem to Constantinople occurred c. 415 under Emperor Theodosius II, following the miraculous discovery (*inventio*) of his tomb at Caphargamala by the priest Lucian through a revelation of Gamaliel.
[^42]: An epigraphic wordplay on Saint Stephen's name (*Stephanos*, Greek for "crown" or "wreath"): "Having received a crown (*Вѣне́цъ*) of graces matching thy name (*тезоимени́тнѡ*)..."
[^43]: Acts 7:55–56: "But he, being full of the Holy Ghost, looked up steadfastly into heaven, and saw the glory of God, and Jesus standing on the right hand of God, and said: Behold, I see the heavens opened, and the Son of man standing on the right hand of God."
[^44]: The rubric specifies the conclusion of the veneration of the Precious and Life-Giving Cross (brought forth on August 1): at the end of Vespers on August 2, the Cross is solemnly returned to the altar and placed upon the Holy Table.
[^45]: Acrostic (*краегранесіе*) of the Matins Canon: "With hymns I crown the protomartyr. Theophanes" (*Пѣ́сньми первомꙋ́ченика вѣнча́ю. Ѳеофа́нъ*), authored by Saint Theophanes the Branded (Graptos, c. 778–845).
[^46]: An allusion to the miraculous finding of the relics of Saint Stephen hidden in the earth at Caphargamala in 415.
[^47]: Hab. 3:2: "O Lord, I have heard Thy speech, and was afraid: O Lord, revive Thy work in the midst of the years."
[^48]: 1 Cor. 15:45 ("The first man Adam was made a living soul; the last Adam was made a quickening spirit") and Rom. 5:14.
[^49]: *Heirmos of Ode 5:* Cf. Psalm 42:2 (LXX); Isaiah 26:9.
[^50]: *Heirmos of Ode 6:* Cf. Jonah 2:3–7.
[^51]: *Heirmos of Ode 7:* Cf. Daniel 3:49–50 (Song of the Three Holy Children).
[^52]: *Heirmos of Ode 8:* Cf. Daniel 3:19–25.
[^53]: *Heirmos of Ode 9:* Cf. Jeremiah 2:12; Luke 1:48.
[^54]: *Prokeimenon of the Liturgy:* Psalm 18:4 (LXX) [19:4 MT].
[^55]: *Epistle:* Acts, Pericope 31 (Acts 12:1–11).
[^56]: *Alleluia:* Psalm 88:5 (LXX) [89:5 MT].
[^57]: *Gospel:* Matthew, Pericope 37 (Matthew 10:16–22).
[^58]: *Communion Verse:* Psalm 18:4 (LXX) [19:4 MT].
[^59]: *Heirmos of Ode 1:* Cf. Exodus 14:21–22; 15:1.
[^60]: *Heirmos of Ode 3:* St. John Damascene; cf. 1 Kings 2:2 (LXX) [1 Samuel 2:2 MT].
[^61]: *Sessional Hymn:* Podoben: "Of Wisdom" (Премудрости / Τὴν Σοφίαν καὶ Λόγον).
[^62]: *Heirmos of Ode 4:* Cf. Habakkuk 3:2.
[^63]: *Heirmos of Ode 5:* Cf. Psalm 42:2 (LXX); Isaiah 26:9.
[^64]: *Heirmos of Ode 6:* Cf. Jonah 2:2–9; Psalm 141:2 (LXX) [142:2 MT].
[^65]: *Kontakion:* Podoben: "In prayers" (Въ молитвахъ).
[^66]: *Heirmos of Ode 7:* Cf. Daniel 3:49–50.
[^67]: *Heirmos of Ode 8:* Cf. Daniel 3:19–25.
[^68]: *Heirmos of Ode 9:* Cf. Habakkuk 3:2; Luke 1:48–52.
[^69]: *The Holy Seven Youths of Ephesus:* Saints Maximilian, Jamblicus, Martinian, John, Dionysius, Exacustodian (Antoninus), and Antoninus (Constantine), commemorated August 4 and October 22.
[^70]: *Troparion of the Martyrs:* Common dismissal troparion for multiple martyrs, Tone 4.

[^261]: Tone 6, Podoben: "Having triumphed on the third day" (Трїдне́венъ): Соѡбра́жсѧ за млⷭ҇рдїе хрⷭ҇тѐ...
[^262]: Doxastikon at the Aposticha, Tone 2: Ре́вности бж҃твенныѧ и҆спо́лненъ...
[^263]: Theotokion / Festal sticheron at the Aposticha, Tone 2: И҆́же на горѣ̀ ѳавѡ́рстѣй преѡбрази́ся во сла́вѣ...

[^266]: *Podoben note*: The melodic model *Of Wisdom* (Slavonic: *Премудрости*, Greek: *Τὴν Σοφίαν καὶ Λόγον*) is the standard automelon for Tone 8 sessional hymns throughout the Menaion.
[^267]: *Gospel Pericope*: Slavonic *зач. 67* corresponds to John 21:15–25 (the dialogue between the risen Lord and Peter, standard matins Gospel reading for apostolic commemorations).
[^268]: *Acrostic note*: The Greek acrostic reads ᾌσμασί σε μέλπω, Ματθία, Χριστοῦ φίλε (Poem of Theophanes), preserved in Slavonic translation as: *Пѣсньми тя пою матѳіе христо́въ друже*.
[^269]: *Theological note*: The Ikos text exhorts the soul to shake off earthly sloth and ascend Mount Tabor with Peter, James, and John to witness the divine Transfiguration.
[^270]: *Podoben note*: The model *Thou hast appeared today* (Slavonic: *Яви́лся еси́ днесь*, Greek: *Ἐπεφάνης σήμερον*) is the Tone 4 kontakion model composed by Romanos the Melodist for Theophany.
[^271]: *Podoben note*: The model *In the spirit in the sanctuary* (Slavonic: *Духомъ во святилищи*, Greek: *Γυναιξὶν ἀκουτίσθητε* / *Ἐν πνεύματι τῷ ἱερῷ*) is the standard exapostilarion melody for apostles and martyrs.
[^272]: *Podoben note*: The model *O House of Ephratha* (Slavonic: *До́ме Евфра́ѳовъ*, Greek: *Οἶκος τοῦ Εὐφραθᾶ*) is the automelon for Tone 2 prosomoia.
[^273]: *Podoben note*: The model *As one valiant* (Slavonic: *Я́ко до́бля*, Greek: *Ὡς γενναῖον ἐν Μάρτυσιν*) is the standard Tone 4 prosomoion model.
[^274]: *Epistle Pericope*: Slavonic *зач. 2* corresponds to Acts 1:12–17, 21–26 (the election of Apostle Matthias to replace Judas).
[^275]: *Gospel Pericope*: Slavonic *зач. 40* corresponds to Luke 9:1–6 (the sending forth of the twelve disciples).
[^276]: *Podoben note*: The model *Thou hast given a sign* (Slavonic: *Да́лъ еси́ зна́меніе*, Greek: *Ἔδωκας σημείωσιν*) is the Tone 4 automelon.
[^277]: *Tone 1, Podoben: "Joy of the heavenly ranks" (Небе́сныхъ чинѡ́въ):* The tone indication (Tone 1) was specified in the preceding rubric on fol. 53v (`Page_0110.jpg`). The podoben incipit corresponds to Byzantine Greek Τῶν οὐρανίων ταγμάτων.
[^278]: *Tone 4, Automelon (Самогла́сенъ):* Feast of the Transfiguration of our Lord.
[^279]: *Tone 2, Podoben: "O House of Ephratha" (До́ме Е҆ѵфра́ѳовъ):* Byzantine Greek Οἶκος τοῦ Ἐφραθᾶ.
[^280]: *Tone 1, Automelon (Самогла́сенъ):* Transfiguration idiomelon/automelon hymn.
[^281]: *Troparion of the Holy Martyr and Archdeacon Laurence, Tone 4:* Common Troparion for a martyr (`Мꙋ́ченикъ тво́й гдⷭ҇и...`).
[^282]: *Troparion of the Feast of the Transfiguration, Tone 7:* `Преѡбрази́лсѧ є҆сѝ на горѣ̀ хрⷭ҇тѐ бж҃е...`.
[^283]: *Tone 4, Podoben: "Joseph marveled" (Оу҆диви́сѧ І҆ѡ́сифъ):* Byzantine Greek Κατεπλάγη Ἰωσήφ.
[^284]: *Canon of the Feast of the Transfiguration, Tone 8, Ode 1 Heirmos:* Composed by St. John of Damascus (`Во́дꙋ проше́дъ ꙗ҆́кѡ сꙋ́шꙋ...` / Ὑγρὰν διοδεύσας ὡσεὶ ξηράν).
[^285]: *Canon of the Holy Martyr Laurence, Tone 4, Ode 1 Heirmos:* Attributed to St. Theophanes the Branded (`Трїста́ты крѣ̑пкїѧ...` / Τριστάτας κραταιούς). Acrostic in the Theotokia and Troparia: "I sing of the mighty and all-wise Laurence" (Λαυρέντιον σθένοντα μέλπω τὸν πάνσοφον / Лаѵре́нтїа держа́внаго пою̀ премꙋ́дра).
[^286]: *dancing in chorus with the angelic powers:* In the Pochaiv 1761 print, the text reads `санко́вїтсѧ` at the bottom of fol. 54v with catchword `кови́тствꙋѧй` at the top of fol. 55r (`Page_0112.jpg` / `Page_0113.jpg`), an epigraphic corruption/misprint for `соликовствꙋѧй` (Greek συνχορεύων).
[^287]: *Canon of the Feast, Tone 8, Ode 3 Heirmos:* `Нб҃нагѡ крꙋ́га верхотво́рче...` / Οὐρανίας ἁψῖδος ὀροφοποιέ.
[^288]: *Canon of the Holy Martyr Laurence, Tone 4, Ode 3 Heirmos:* `Лꙋ́къ си́льныхъ и҆знемо́же...` / Τόξον δυνατῶν ἠσθένησε.
[^289]: *statutes of the ungodly:* The 1761 Slavonic print reads `законоположе́нїю бл҃гочести́выхъ` (statutes of the pious), an apparent typographical slip for `злочести́выхъ` / `нечести́выхъ` (the impious torturers).
[^290]: *Kontakion of the Holy Martyr Laurence, Tone 2, Podoben: "Seeking the highest" (Вы́шнихъ и҆щѧ̀):* Byzantine Greek Τὰ ἄνω ζητῶν.
[^291]: *Ikos of the Holy Martyr Laurence:* Acclamation from Rom. 8:35, 39: "Nothing shall separate me from the love of Christ."

[^618]: St. Andrew Stratelates (the General) was an officer in the Roman imperial army in Syria under Emperor Maximian Galerius (c. 300). Converted with his entire military detachment of 2,593 soldiers after a miraculous victory over the Persians, they were beheaded together in the Taurus Mountains of Cilicia.

[^756]: Anatolian Sticheron on Glory: The name Agathonicus (Greek: Ἀγαθόνικος) is formed from ἀγαθός (*agathos*, "good") and νίκη (*nikē*, "victory"). The hymnographer Anatolius explicitly plays on this etymology: "namesake of good victory" (Slavonic: *благі́ѧ побѣ́ды тезоимени́тъ*).

[^821]: August 23: The commemoration of the Holy Martyr Luppus of Thessalonica is transferred or subsumed because the Apodosis (Apodosis) of the Dormition falls on this day, whereon the entire service of the Feast is repeated.
[^822]: Canon of the Feast, Ode 1, Heirmos: Tone 4, *Преꙋкраше́ннаѧ бж҃е́ственною сла́вою...* (Greek: Πεποικιλμένη τῇ θείᾳ δόξῃ).
[^823]: Typikon rubric detailing the combination of the Sunday Resurrection order of the Octoechos with the Apodosis (Apodosis) of the Dormition when August 23 falls on a Sunday.
[^824]: August 24: The Holy Hieromartyr Eutyches (1st c.), disciple of St. John the Theologian and fellow-laborer with the Holy Apostle Paul.
[^825]: Stichera at "Lord, I have cried," Tone 8, Podoben: *Ѡ҆ пресла́внагѡ чꙋдесѐ!* (Greek: Ὢ τοῦ παραδόξου θαύματος).
[^826]: "the honored disciple, the blessed preacher": St. John the Theologian, the Beloved Disciple.
[^827]: Mark 9:48: "Where their worm dies not, and the fire is not quenched" (cf. Isaiah 66:24).
[^828]: General Troparion of a Hieromartyr, Tone 4: *И҆ нра́вѡмъ прича́стникъ...* (Greek: Καὶ τρόπων μέτοχος).

[^830]: Philippians 3:10: "being made conformable unto His death."
[^831]: Canon of St. Eutyches, Ode 3, Heirmos: Tone 8, *Оу҆твержде́й въ нача́лѣ нб҃са̀ ра́зꙋмомъ...* (Greek: Ὁ στερεώσας κατ' ἀρχάς).
[^832]: According to the Synaxarion, St. Eutyches, having been a disciple of St. John the Theologian, subsequently met the Holy Apostle Paul in Sebastopol and labored alongside him in preaching the Gospel.
[^833]: Sessional Hymn of St. Eutyches, Tone 3, Podoben: *Бж҃твенныѧ вѣ́ры* (Greek: Θείας πίστεως).
[^834]: Canon of St. Eutyches, Ode 4, Heirmos: Tone 8, *Ты́ моѧ̀ крѣ́пость гдⷭ҇и...* (Greek: Σύ μου ἰσχύς, Κύριε / Habakkuk 3:2).
[^835]: Canon of St. Eutyches, Ode 5, Heirmos: Tone 8, *Вскꙋ́ю мѧ̀ ѿри́нꙋлъ є҆сѝ...* (Greek: Ὢ τῆς ἀφράστου σου / Ἵνα τί με ἀπώσω / Psalm 42:2).
[^836]: Isaiah 52:7 / Romans 10:15: "How beautiful are the feet of them that preach the gospel of peace."
[^837]: Canon of St. Eutyches, Ode 6, Heirmos: Tone 8, *Ѡ҆чи́сти мѧ̀ сп҃се...* (Greek: Ἱλάσθητί μοι Σωτήρ).
[^838]: St. Eutyches was tried and condemned to death in Ephesus, then sent bound to his native city of Sebaste in Samaria (or Sebastopol), where he completed his martyrdom by being beheaded.
[^839]: Kontakion of St. Eutyches, Tone 3, Podoben: *Дв҃а дне́сь* (Greek: Ἡ Παρθένος σήμερον).
[^840]: The hymn refers to the tradition in the Life of the Hieromartyr Eutyches (August 24) where a lioness loosed against him in the amphitheater was miraculously endowed with human speech, praising the God of the Christians before the assembled crowd.
[^841]: Slavonic: *мункѡвъ*, a printer's mechanical contraction/error for *мученикѡвъ* (of martyrs).
[^842]: August 25 in the Byzantine calendar commemorates both the Translation/Return of the Relics of the Holy Apostle Bartholomew (to the Lipari Islands) and the Memory of the Holy Apostle Titus, Bishop of Gortyna in Crete.
[^843]: Slavonic: *му́нкѡмъ*, another recurring contraction in this edition for *мученикѡмъ* (martyrs), referring to the relics of the four martyrs (Lucian, Gregory, Acacius, and Eugene) whose sarcophagi accompanied that of Bartholomew.
[^844]: The Slavonic acrostic of the first Canon (to St. Bartholomew) by St. Joseph the Hymnographer reads: *Пѣ́сньми почита́ю до́браго моего̀ предста́теля* ("With hymns I honor my good protector").
[^845]: The Slavonic acrostic of the second Canon (to St. Titus) by St. Theophanes Graptos reads: *Па́ѵлова оучени́ка ті́та пою̀ премꙋ́дреннѡ* ("Wisely do I sing of Paul's disciple Titus").
[^846]: Slavonic: *бг҃обо́жїа*, an apparent misprint for *безбо́жїа* (godlessness) or *многобо́жїа* (polytheism).
[^847]: Ode 4 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Titus is based upon the theophany of Habakkuk (Habakkuk 3:3; Septuagint: "God shall come from Teman...").
[^848]: Ode 5 Heirmos of the Canon of St. Titus draws upon Isaiah 26:9-10 ("With my soul have I desired Thee in the night...").
[^849]: The Ikos of St. Bartholomew recounts the tradition of his leaden reliquary being cast into the sea by pagans in Anastasiopolis/Albanopolis and miraculously sailing westward across the Mediterranean Sea to Lipari.
[^850]: Ode 8 Heirmos of St. Bartholomew (*Ѻ́троки благочести̑выя въ пещѝ...*) is the standard Tone 4, Ode 8 Heirmos from the Heirmologion.
[^851]: The Slavonic text reads "плещи̑ дбо́ ны́нѣ мнѣ̀ дае́тъ", where *дбо́* is a printed contraction/misprint for *дѣ́во* (O Virgin). The phrase *плещи̑ дае́тъ* (literally "gives [its] shoulders/back") translates the Greek idiom νῶτα δίδωσι ("turns its back / flees"), referring to the turning flaming sword of Genesis 3:24 withdrawing from the faithful through the Incarnation.
[^852]: Slavonic: *свере́тника* (variant form of *све́рстника* / *сверѣ́тника*, peer or spouse of equal standing). Saint Natalia, though a woman in the eyes of the pagan magistrates, spiritually fortified her husband Saint Adrian during his interrogation and torture.
[^853]: Slavonic: *свѣ́тства*, a typographical error in the 1761 Pochaiv print for *сѣ́тства* (snares/entanglements) or *сꙋ́етства* (vanities). The standard Church Slavonic Menaion reads *сѣ́тства* (*сѣ́тей*, snares).
[^854]: Slavonic: *десѧ́тица сꙋгꙋ́баѧ и҆ тро́ица мꙋ́ченикъ* (literally "a double decade and a triad of martyrs"). This refers to the twenty-three Christians in Nicomedia whose unwavering confession under torture moved Saint Adrian to profess Christ and join them in martyrdom.
[^855]: According to the hagiographical account, Saint Natalia secretly retrieved Saint Adrian's severed hand after his martyrdom, wrapped it in costly cloths, and kept it with her until her peaceful repose in Argyropolis.
[^856]: Slavonic: *ѡ҆боже́нїѧ* (deification / theosis). The hymnographic tradition identifies the fall of man in Genesis 3 as the serpent's deceitful envy of man's calling to likeness unto God.
[^857]: Slavonic: *безстра́стїе ѻ҆́гненно* (fiery dispassion/apatheia). In the ascetic vocabulary of the Desert Fathers and Saint Poemen the Great, *apatheia* is not passive stoicism or cold apathy, but the luminous, burning purity of love purged of all disordered passions by the divine fire.
[^858]: невмѣсти́магѡ є҆стества̀ селе́нїе: Gk. τοῦ ἀχωρήτου φύσεως οἰκητήριον, "the dwelling-place of the uncontainable nature," referring to the divine nature of the Logos.
[^859]: ши́покъ: rose / sweet-briar (Gk. ῥόδον or κρίνον).
[^860]: Ps. 115:6, 3 (LXX).
[^861]: мѡѷсе́а мꙋ́рїна: Venerable Moses the Black (the Ethiopian / Murin, from Gk. Μαῦρος).
[^862]: быстрота́ми: radiances / flashes (Gk. μαρμαρυγαῖς).
[^863]: во ѻ҆́строкъ нбⷭ҇ныи: into the heavenly haven/isle (or settlement, Gk. νῆσον / χῶρον).
[^864]: Мꙋ́рины: the Moors / Ethiopians (symbolizing both his former robber companions / ethnic origin and demonic hordes).
[^865]: є҆ѳїо́пѡвъ мы́сленныѧ стрѣла́нїа: the intelligible shooting of the Ethiopians, a patristic metaphor for demonic arrows/temptations.
[^866]: ѿвѣ́ка со дерзнове́нїемъ: lit. "with boldness from of old / unprecedented boldness" (Gk. ἀναιδείᾳ ἀπ' αἰῶνος).
[^867]: прⷪро́че проповѣ́дниче и҆ прⷪро́че: the second прⷪро́че is a typographical slip in the Slavonic text for и҆ предте́че ("and forerunner").

[^871]: Wisdom 4:7-14; 4:16-20; 5:1-5 composite Paremia for martyrs and righteous prophets.
[^872]: Slavonic *бѣлообразюще*, translating Greek λευχειμονοῦντες, denoting keeping festival clad in bright or festive white vesture.

[^896]: The printed Slavonic witness reads *коро́лю* (*unto the king*), reflecting liturgical usage under the Polish-Lithuanian Commonwealth wherein the Pochaiv Lavra was situated, petitioning victory for the reigning sovereign (*rex / król*).
